- **GY6 Mesh evidence says what a lone file is (controller ruling R2 on the final review).** The scan reaches the engine only as `BatchRequest::mesh_shaders`, `mesh_alpha` (both slot 0, keyed by `shader::colour_mesh_key`) and `gras_diffuses`, and before this item it typed a file only through the `member_subtype` insert over an existing suggestion, which a waiting file never has. Now `decide_subtype_with` counts a `mesh_alpha` key for the file (any slot-0 shape) or `gras_diffuse` as saying it is a diffuse, so it gets the ordinary colour suggestion (`SuggestContext::may_wait` false) and the GUI records it as any High pick; `suggest_grass` needs no earlier suggestion, so a GRAS diffuse that passes the grass tests is `complex_grass`. Only slot 0 counts: the engine gets no other slot's evidence, and `sdc` reads no scan (a saved project carries the recorded type). Tests `unknown_suffix::a_mesh_diffuse_does_not_wait`, `complex_grass::a_lone_underscored_gras_diffuse_does_not_wait` and the GUI's `unknown_suffix::a_mesh_that_uses_the_file_settles_it`.
- **GY7 An Error wins over waiting (controller ruling on the final review).** A waiting file with an Error-severity warning (an invalid `[files]` field) has status `Error`, so `waits_for_type` is false: it shows as an error with its set's card and writes nothing. Tests `unknown_suffix::an_error_wins_over_waiting` (engine) and `unknown_suffix::an_error_keeps_the_card_and_writes_nothing` (GUI).

## Note HP — Sets column, second UI-proof round (main team, 2026-11-07)

Source: the second UI-proof report `.superpowers/ui-proof/sets-column-hn/REPORT.md` (findings SC1-SC9, M1, M2) and the design spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-07-sets-column-hp-design.md`. Owner answers (2026-11-07): badge spacing option B; the card combo stays on its own row under the title; every count means what its click shows. Each item names what it amends; the replaced wording is in DECISIONS-ARCHIVE.md. Code: `crates/gui/src/ui/{columns,sets_column,mod}.rs`.

- **HP1 Badges straddle the chip corner (SC6).** A badge is drawn 16 px (`BADGE`) centred on its chip's top-right corner (position 0) or top-left corner (position 1). Its hit rect starts at 20 px (`BADGE_HIT`) and `clamp_hits` (a pure function: compute all rects, then split each overlap at its midline) cuts it to the card and to the shared edge of any other interactive rect, never under 18 px at 1055x770 and 1400x790. A selected badge has a 2 px stroke. Chips are `DOCK_GAP_X` 16 px apart across and `DOCK_GAP_Y` 8 px down, `DOCK_INSET` 2 px from the card edge, and `SETS_MIN` = 2 × `MEMBER` + `DOCK_GAP_X` + 2 × `DOCK_INSET` + `PILL_CHROME` + `SCROLL_ALLOWANCE` + `PANEL_CHROME` = 133 (was 121); a 260 px column still holds 5 chips per row (measured: five chips of 32 px and four gaps of 16 take 224 px of the 243 px card). The numbers follow from the badge: facing badges across 16 px overlap 4 px (18 wide each), a badge on a lower row reaches 2 px into the chip above (18 tall), at the card edge 1 px would leave the card (19 wide). **Amends HN1** (12 px, top-right inside), **FM32** Heights ("chip + item spacing" is now "chip + `DOCK_GAP_Y`") and FB2's "~120 px" Sets minimum (FB2 is in DECISIONS-1.x.md, which stays frozen; the digest line cites HP).
- **HP2 One wording for a fill (SC7).** `fill_text(t)` is the target's chip letter (the suffix in capitals; "D" for the colour map) + a space + `fill_channel(t)`: "RMAOS R", "RFAOS A", "CNR alpha", "D alpha", "M G" (3.0 has no layer fills, GW3). Badge name, badge hover, trailing-row name and the helper panel's Fills rows all use it. The output file is `target_file(h, t)` (the planned output's stem, "crate_rmaos"; the base name for the colour map); plain text joins them with two spaces ("RMAOS R  crate_rmaos"), and the helper panel paints the file in the weak colour. "fills to" is gone. **Amends HN1** (badge name) **and HN3** (trailing role).
- **HP3 The card combo keeps its own row under the title (SC1, SC9; owner answer 2).** It is right-aligned and sized to its value (`combo_w` returns the capped and the full width, measured in `TextStyle::Button` because the ComboBox lays out in Button), capped at the card's inner width less 48 px (`COMBO_FREE`; owner, 2026-11-08, HP12) and cut with `…` only past that; the full value is in the hover and the AccessKit value. At least 48 px on the left of that row is free card area and opens the set. The combo is named "Shader for <set id>". The titles keep their row. **Amends CD7** ("the shader combo at full width").
- **HP4 Every count in the column is what its click would show (SC5; owner answer 3).** A pill's count and a shader-menu entry's count are the keyed sets that pass the filter as it would be after that click (the search, the other pills, the shader), through one predicate. A zero pill is weak (greyed) only while it is off and stays clickable; a selected zero pill paints as a normal selected pill, because weak text on the selected fill risks 4.5:1 (HP8). A selected pill counts the sets shown with it on (the list itself), not what turning it off would show: a selected facet whose number jumps to the wider total reads as wrong (owner, 2026-11-08). **Amends CD12** ("Not ready plus Ready count every keyed set") **and HN7** ("keyed sets per shader").
- **HP5 A changed filter starts the list at its top (SC3).** On the frame `model.sets_filter` differs from last frame's copy (`DropState::filter`) the list scrolls to the top. A pending palette reveal (`nav.reveal_set`) wins over the reset; any other change of the list (a set added, a rescan) keeps FM32's anchor. **Amends FM32** Anchor for that case only.
- **HP6 The set editor's room comes from Textures first (SC2).** When the editor needs its floor (`set_editor::ROWS_MIN`), the shortfall comes from Textures down to its minimum, then from Sets. `TEXTURES_W` 380 and `SETS_W` (266, JJ2) replace the literals in `ui/mod.rs`. While Textures is above its minimum, Sets gives up only what rounding Textures up to whole tile columns takes (HP12); once Textures is at its minimum, Sets yields the rest of the floor, down to `SETS_MIN` (at 1055x770 with a set open, 266 to 172 px, HP12). In a narrow window opening a set moves the Sets column left once, by the room Textures gives up (owner accepted, 2026-11-08). **Amends FB2** (the split) **and HN4** ("FB2's narrowing is unchanged").
- **HP7 A cut title keeps the search match (SC4).** While a search is on and its folded text lies inside one middle segment (not the first, not the leaf), the title tries `…/<that segment>/<rest>` right after the whole id, and uses it only when it ends with the set's unique tail (JR5); a match in the first segment or the leaf, or across a `/`, uses HN6's order. **Amends HN6** while a search is on.
- **HP8 Readable and named (SC8, M1).** The status pill text uses `widgets.inactive.text_color()` (at least 4.5:1 on the card fill in both themes). The empty chip's dim letter (`dim_letter_colour`) is the same colour, not `weak_text_color()`: it measures 9.55:1 dark and 11.03:1 light on `extreme_bg_color`, so it reads at 4.5:1 or more in both themes at the cost of a smaller step from a filled chip's letter. The texture settings checkboxes are named by their row label (follows FM42, as "Mipmaps" is).
- **HP9 Labels sit in one place (M2).** Empty letters sit dimmed (HP8) in the floating strip at the same bottom-left offset as on a filled chip. The card and its stand-in expose the letters together in one description (Note JW1). A helper’s trailing tile paints no tag (Note JW7). Any other trailing tile paints its slot letter when the slot is taken, otherwise its member tail ("_m"), or "—" when the tail is empty. A tag wider than the tile is cut with `…`; the hover keeps the whole text. **Amends HN2 and HN3.**
- **HP10 Test support.** The `overlap` frame check spares a badge centred on its chip's corner: a pair is not a finding when the smaller rect is at most 24 px a side and its centre is within 1.5 px of a corner of the larger on each axis; a badge centred mid-edge is still flagged. `tests/ui_proof.rs` has `replay_with(fixture, before, lines)` and `replay_mid` (narrows the window between a set's open and the later lines, because narrowing before the click leaves the card unclickable for the driver); `sets_column_pins.rs` has `sets_width(h, w)`, which persists the Sets column's chosen width and settles 3 frames, for pins that need a narrow column without opening a set (an open set's floor also narrows Sets, HP6).
- **HP11 An off-screen card honours ScrollIntoView (SH1).** A card outside the clip rect answers an AccessKit ScrollIntoView request as if it had taken the focus, and its node lists the action (a screen reader or the test driver can bring it into view). No `Response` is added. **Amends FM32** Stand-ins.
- **HP12 Proof fixes (UI proof `sets-column-hp`, SH3, SH4, SH6; owner answers 2026-11-08).** (a) SH4 (owner): when an open set takes Textures' room, Textures' shrunk width rounds up to whole tile columns (`columns::round_up_to_tiles`, called from `ui/mod.rs`), the extra room coming from Sets and never taking Sets below `SETS_MIN`; when the next whole column is out of Sets' reach the width is left as it is. HP1's "5 chips per row" holds for the default 266 px Sets column (JJ2: beside the bar's 6 px gutter, 260 px holds four). Since the editor's floor is 724 px (JI5), at 1055x770 with a set open Sets measures 172 px (266 px with no set open). **Amends HP6** (the split). (b) SH6 (owner): the card's shader combo is capped at the row's inner width less 48 px, not at half; "Environment Map (auto)" shows whole on a 260 px column, and the 48 px on the row's left stay free card area. **Amends HP3** (owner answer 2's "half"). (c) SH3 (controller): a card's one-row heading needs `NAME_MIN.min(<the title's whole width>)` for the name, so a whole title that fits keeps its status pill on the title row (props/crate at 1055x770: pill y 251 below the title's 229.5, now on its row). **Amends FI13** (80 px, or the name's whole width when that is less). (d) Names match the painted text (egui-ui-rules 8.2; controller): the Sets shader filter button is "Filter, Sets shader filter" ("Filter (1), Sets shader filter: <shader>" with one chosen), and the trailing-row helper tile's name and hover say "unused: <reason>", with no painted tag (Note JW7). The Textures helper rows and the helper panel paint "not used: …", so they keep it. **Amends HN7** (the filter's name) **and HN3** (the tile's hover).

## Note GZ — MATO layer: PBR coat shapes and rotated statics (NIF team, 2026-11-08)

Source: the owner's "fix 1 and 2 on v3" on two of GG's OPEN items, settled from the pinned CS source, with no capture. Code: `crates/render3d/src/material.rs` (`projected_eligible`, `write_projected`, `write_material`); tests in `crates/render3d/tests/projected.rs`. GG's third OPEN item (replay of blended subjects) stays open.

- **GZ1 A PBR shape with SLSF2.24 gets the layer.** On a PBR shape SLSF2.24 is not multi-layer parallax: CS's TruePBR.cpp turns it into `PBRFlags::TwoLayer` (the coat) at load and clears the flag, and its PBR permutation list has TruePbr | VC | ProjectedUV with technique None. The coat reads `PBRParams2` and `MultiLayerParallaxData`; the layer writes `MaterialObjectRGBScale`, `ParallaxOccData` and `SparkleParams` and samples t3, t8, t10 and t11, so nothing collides. `projected_eligible` now skips SLSF2.24 only on a non-PBR shape (GZ3 does the same for types 3 and 11); a PBR coat toggle stays a cbuffer edit. **Amends GG6** (DECISIONS-1.x.md, frozen; the digest line cites GZ1).
- **GZ2 `TextureProj` row 2 is the shape's model row 2.** Lighting.hlsl:254-255 computes `TexProj = mul(World3x3, TextureProj[2].xyz)` and the PS projects along `normalize(TexProj)`. With row 2 = the model's row 2 (nifly 3×4, the rows `World` gets; the camera shift changes only the translation), World3x3 · row 2 = s² · world up for any rotation and uniform scale, so a rotated static takes snow on its world-top faces. Rows 0 and 1 stay as captured (they feed TexCoord0.zw, which the triplanar path does not read). An identity model writes the captured matrix unchanged. `write_material` takes the layer with the model (`Option<(&ProjectedLayer, &Mat3x4)>`), from `ShapeDraw::model` on an edit and from the shape's world at build. **Amends GG2** (DECISIONS-1.x.md, frozen) where it fixed the whole captured matrix.
- **GZ3 A PBR shape of type 3 or 11 gets the layer.** CS drops the parallax and MLP techniques under TruePBR (`permutation::defines`; the captured Arch_Eye_Moisture, a PBR shape (SLSF2.23) of type 11, has technique 0), so the `PROJECTED_UV` exclusion at Lighting.hlsl:1748 does not apply to it. A coat (SLSF2.24) or fuzz (SLSF2.25) shape keeps type 11 because its MultiLayer floats, which carry the coat and fuzz values (`write_pbr`), load only for type 11, so GZ1 alone missed every such coat. `projected_eligible` skips types 3 and 11 only on a non-PBR shape; FaceGen (4, 5), sparkle snow (14) and eye (16) keep their technique and stay skipped. Tests `eligible_shapes` (a PBR type 3 or 11 shape, with or without SLSF2.24, compiles the type 0 program on both stages) and `render_projected_rotated_static_and_coat` (a type 11 coat draws as a type 0 one; only the coat was rendered).

## Note IA — Model-space normal baker conversion (NIF team for the owner, 2026-11-08)

Source: the owner's MSN convention table and "add a small msn conversion feature … reusing the existing invert and channel swapping; no automated checks". Code: `engine::config::MsnSource`, `ChannelSpec::msn_source`, `engine::ops` (`Domain::Msn`); GUI `model::fields::MSN_SOURCE`, `ui::settings_panel::options_of`. Tests: `ops::tests::msn_source_reaches_the_skyrim_layout`, `crates/gui/tests/msn_source.rs`.

- **IA1 `channels.msn_source` turns a baker's model-space normal into Skyrim's layout.** Values: `skyrim` (default, no change, not serialised), `swap_gb` (Blender object-space bake), `invert_b` (Marmoset right-handed, or Substance, whose DirectX and OpenGL MSN are the same), `invert_gb` (Marmoset left-handed). Preprocess applies it right after the `msn` decode to [-1, 1]: a swap exchanges G and B, an invert negates the channel (the same as 1 − v on the stored value). Only `msn` reads it; every other sub-type ignores it, and no check guesses the baker (owner: none wanted). An unset field leaves every output byte as before, so no `encode_version` arm moves. It is a per-file key (`FIELD_PATHS`), settable in `[files]`, rules, presets and `--set`.
- **IA2 The GUI row is "Baked in", in the Type group, shown only for `msn`.** Options are named by the baker with the change in brackets: "Skyrim MSN (no change)", "Blender object-space (swap G and B)", "Marmoset right-handed or Substance (invert B)", "Marmoset left-handed (invert G and B)" (owner: one row for the two invert-B bakers). It writes the key like any field row (undo, ↺ reset).
- **IA3 A set's `_msn` keeps `msn` when its diffuse goes PBR or Advanced skin (owner: the re-type was a bug).** A model-space normal keeps its type and its `channels.msn_source` setting (IA1) on the diffuse's Switch. JS6 governs tangent-space members. An eligible `glow` still switches to `pbr_emissive`. Test: `pbr_members::a_msn_member_keeps_msn_when_its_diffuse_goes_pbr`.

## Note HL — Target mod and in-development guard rails (builder, 2026-11-07)

- **HL1** The target mod is the mod folder the modder is working on. It is read only and GUI-only: `[project] target_mod` in `sdc-project.toml` holds its absolute path (`sets_doc::target_mod`, `set_target_mod`; the CLI doesn't read it, and undo doesn't change it). `AppModel::set_target_mod` refuses a folder that is, sits inside or holds the export folder (`TARGET_IS_EXPORT`, compared on canonical paths without case), and a Blocks row of the same text stops the export when an export folder picked later, or a hand-edited toml, overlaps it (`pbr_export::build_blocks`). A worker (`target_mod::read`, on a target change, `open_folder` and a finished scan) lists the root-level plugins, the slot paths of each `meshes/**/*.nif` shape's lighting texture set, and the `textures/**/*.dds` files grouped by base and suffix slot (`suffix_slot`). Loose files only: a BSA-packed target lists records only. Its records are the scan's records whose defining plugin is one of its plugins (`target_records`; all records with no target mod, none while a set target is unread); a target absent from the scan has none and no Problems row. A read target with no Record row in the dialog says why above the list: `RECORDS_NOT_IN_SCAN` when it has plugins the scan lacks, `RECORDS_NO_PLUGIN` for a folder with no plugin; the dialog's rows are rebuilt when the stored path changes, not only when the read lands. Nothing is ever written into it. Rulings 1, 3 and 7 of the target-mod design.
- **HL2** `AppModel::used_by(path)` counts the target's mesh paths (one per distinct slot-1 path, as `read` groups them, judged by the slots of the first shape that names it) whose slots name the path, plus the target records whose slots name it (ASCII case ignored). A row's own mesh path or record counts toward its own number; it is the count the dialog shows beside a texture.
- **HL3** A mesh path or a file base of the target mod is an HG mapping with a `base` (`[sets.txst] base = "textures/…"`, no plugin, FormID or EDID; `TxstMap::is_base`). The stored slot paths are the source's own; a slot the source lacks goes to the base's folder with the file's default name on the base's stem (`renamed_output`, the same rule as `output_name`). The "Record … changed" and "isn't in the latest scan" rows skip it, and one set holds one mapping: picking another source replaces it, and the same base in another case is no new mapping. A base mapping on a PGPatcher PBR set (albedo) moves its match key, not its outputs.
- **HL4** The picker lives in the mapping dialog. **Map to target…** (**Change mapping…** once mapped, with **Remove mapping**) opens one dialog: the target mod row (read only, **Choose…** / **Clear**; a refused folder shows its text in red) and one virtual list with a kind column (`Record`, `Mesh`, `File`), the EDID or path and `used by (N)` (`used_by` of slot 1, counted for the rows drawn). With no target mod the list is the scan's records as in HG. The preview keeps `record_preview` for a record and lists the slot paths for a mesh path or file base. The menu item is greyed ("Scan your mods first") with no scan and no target mod, and for an albedo PGPatcher PBR set on Community Shaders with no target mod (HG8c's reason; nothing could be mapped). With a target mod that set keeps the item, and its record rows are the disabled ones (HG8c's reason as their hover). Enter maps the chosen row while no widget has focus (a typed Enter in the search box, or Enter on a focused button, maps nothing).
- **HL5** Every PBR set gets its PGPatcher JSON entry on Community Shaders, also with no PBR albedo. `SetView::json` = `pbr`, or a set whose shader pick is `Shader::Pbr` and that is no skin or shared set and has no `pbr_albedo` diffuse; `pbr` keeps its meaning (the albedo set, the prefix owner) and the JSON readers (`json_entries`, blocks, asks, `is_json_set`, auto flags) read `json`. `derived_match`, and `match_from` (`MatchFrom::{Albedo, Mapping, BasePath}`), come from the first that exists: the mapping's base, a record's slot 1 (not for an albedo set), the albedo or diffuse, the set id's own path. A no-albedo set keeps its meshes' textures: `slot1` is the mapping's slot 1 else `textures\<derived path>.dds` (PGPatcher skips a shape whose slot 1 would point at a missing `textures\pbr` copy), every filled slot names its output path, an empty slot 2 or 6 takes the mapping's stored path else `lock_normal` / `lock_rmaos` is written; an explicit `slotN` or lock still wins. A filled slot no path names (a kept DDS with no job) is locked too (`lock_empty_slots`), so PGPatcher keeps the mesh's own. The slot paths are game paths: an output path is under the export folder, so the JSON names it as it is (HQ6), and the entry is left out, with its "mesh keeps" row, when a typed `match1` matches nothing. A set with a PBR map and no filled slot has nothing to write, so `json` also needs at least one filled slot (`finish_view` in `model/sets.rs`; an all-DDS set gets no entry). On a non-CS target such a set stays "PBR, no JSON", badge "PBR set (no JSON on this target)"; on CS a PBR pick with no filled slot has the badge `BADGE_PBR_NOTHING_TO_WRITE`, since the target is not the reason. Tests `target_mod::a_no_albedo_set_without_a_mapping_keys_on_its_base_path`, `a_mapped_no_albedo_set_keys_on_the_mapping`, `pgpsim_patches_the_written_no_albedo_entry`, `the_json_slots_name_where_the_files_were_written`.
- **HL6** A missing link is an Info row and the set still exports; a conflict stays amber. Info rows (on the set and in Problems, `bottom::NO_ALBEDO_KEEPS`, `REPLACES_TARGET`): a PBR set with no albedo ("the mesh keeps textures\<path>.dds; a vanilla diffuse isn't sRGB, so it can look washed out", owner), a diffuse no mesh of the scan uses yet (`StaticsStatus::Unused` now writes the entry: `pgp_entry_wanted` is true; LOD meshes still do not count, no scan on a non-landscape set still drops it), no texture set of the scan using the texture (`NO_TXST_USES`, weak colour, not amber), and each output the target mod already ships (`target_replaces`). Stay Warning or amber: output-path collisions and a typed match key matching nothing (the Matching header, the `pitfalls` "left out" line, no entry in the JSON). Tests `target_mod::a_diffuse_no_mesh_uses_still_writes_the_entry`, `no_texture_set_uses_this_is_info`, `a_typed_match_that_matches_nothing_stays_amber`, `a_no_albedo_set_gets_the_info_row`, `a_mapping_onto_a_shipped_file_says_it_replaces_it`, `collisions_stay_amber`.
- **HL7** The owner confirmed (2026-11-08) that a PBR shape reusing a vanilla diffuse as slot 1 works with PGPatcher 2.3.1 and CS; it often looks washed out because the diffuse isn't sRGB, which the no-albedo info row says. No in-game proof was run (the plan's Task 7 was dropped as known behaviour).

## Note HF — Set groups, partial export and textures in no set (builder, 2026-11-08)

- **HF1 A texture can be in no set; any set can be deleted (owner's words in the set-groups spec; the code calls are agent rulings; amended by the owner's B2-6 answer and the controller's rulings B2-7 and B2-8, 2026-11-17).** `[project] no_set` in `sdc-project.toml` lists `[files]` keys (`SetsDoc::no_set`, `set_no_set`). Remove member puts the file there in every case: automatic, moved in (its `add` line goes too) and the only member (supersedes the 1.x DU5 refusal; a packed output still refuses), except a file other sets share, which goes to the first of them that can take it, unless the set keeps another file in that slot (Note JN5). Delete set works on every set: the files that are not packed outputs go to `no_set`, except a file other sets share (Note JN5), the entry and its order slot go, and the toast reads `Deleted set {id} ({n} textures now in no set)` (`1 texture` for one; no count when no texture goes to `no_set`, as for an empty entry or when every file was handed over: `Deleted set {id}. {name} went to {heir}, which shared it.`), followed by where a shared file went, and by which sets were left with an empty shared slot, when either applies (Note JN4, JN5), one undo step. Dropping a file on a card or on the empty area (`add_member`) and **Back to its automatic set** (item menu, `back_to_automatic_set`) take it out of `no_set`. An older `remove` line keeps meaning a one-file set that exports. A no-set file deleted from disk clears its entry with an Info line (`clear_deleted_lines`, as HE2). Tests `crates/gui/tests/no_set.rs`.
- **HF2 The engine side is one edit, `SetEdit::NoSet { file }`.** `engine_inputs` appends one per `no_set` entry after the other edits. `apply_set_edits` treats it as `Remove` (a one-file set), and `prepare` collects its paths (`sets::no_set_paths`) and leaves them out of the `counts` closure with `waiting_images`, so a no-set file blocks no same-named file with `IN-DUPLICATE` (`SetEdit::Remove` still does). A `no_set` change changes `set_edits`, so it is always a full plan. A key names every file the `[files]` rule names (the path, or the path without its last extension, case-insensitively: `sets::no_set_names`), and every hit is in no set; the GUI reads the same rule (`Plan::no_set` for the plan's jobs, `no_set_names` for the document), so a hand-written extension-less key behaves the same on both sides. The engine itself never writes a no-set file: `plan_sets` records the paths in `Plan::no_set`, and `Engine::run` drops their jobs first, whatever `write_only` says (`a_no_set_file_is_never_written`, with `write_only: None`). Since it never writes, `plan_sets` leaves its job out of the BV2 writer dedupe, which stays the backstop for every file that writes, so the twin is not `IN-DUPLICATE`. Test `crates/engine/tests/no_set_twin.rs`. The no-set drop is by set since HH4.
- **HF3 What counts a no-set texture (agent rulings).** `card_hidden` hides a set whose non-packed members are all no-set (so `views`, `has_card`, `carded_sets` agree), and a packed output of it stays out of the Textures column (only of such a set, `no_set_sets`: an excluded or out-of-scope set's packed outputs stay listed, as its raw maps do). `AppModel::in_run(set_key)` is false for such a set (and for one outside the scope or excluded, HF10, HF11), `item_in_run(item)` also asks `is_no_set`, and every counter or lister of undecided, warnings or written files asks it: `undecided`, `undecided_keys`, the Problems Decide, warning and check rows, `count_export_files`, the folder size, and the Textures column's Undecided and Warnings pills and filters. The *Not in a set* pill still counts a lone texture in a one-file set and also counts a no-set one. The GY1 guard lives in the GUI: `PlanView::build` sets `waiting` false for a `NoSet` input (the engine keeps it waiting; exempting it there would give it the colour suggestion at High, which the GUI records into `[files]`). The item stays `NeedsDecision` and is never asked. A no-set tile's border is never amber, and its row says `No set` in place of a type or a role. Everything that opens or reveals a texture's set goes through `set_of_item` / `set_key_of_item`, which answer `None` for it. The preview's "suggested settings" notice asks `item_in_run` too, so a no-set texture shows none. Two readers are left as they are, on purpose: the Problems export-file dedupe (`bottom.rs`, the live item beside a last-export row) only decides whether a row of the last export still stands, and `problems_inner` is a cache key; neither counts or lists a question.
- **HF4 Set groups live in `[[groups]]` and the views decide what shows (owner's spec; agent rulings for the code).** `[[groups]]` holds `name`, `sets` (set ids, as `[project] order` does; a set a hand edit lists again, in its own or a later group, is read in its first group only, `SetsDoc::groups`) and `excluded` (left out when false; `SetsDoc::groups`). Membership is kept there whatever the cards do: what a group shows reads `views`, so a set with no card (Note GY5's waiting lone file, or no set) is neither shown nor counted in it, membership never keeps a card, and a group whose members all lack a card has no header (`AppModel::groups`, `group_of`) while its entry stays and the header comes back with a card. A group goes with its last set only when that member leaves the membership (Delete set, Move to group or No group, Ungroup), not when its cards hide. Names are unique ignoring case; Rename refuses a taken one with the reason, and a group's own name in another case is its own. `move_to_group`, `rename_group`, `ungroup` and `set_group_excluded` return `Result` (the read-only reason, or the refusal), are one undo step and do not re-plan. Delete set takes the set out of its group in its own undo step, so undo brings back `no_set`, the entry and the membership together. `rename_set` and `retarget_auto` carry the set's place in `[project] order`, other sets' borrows and membership (Note HS13), also for an automatic set that is only in a group and has no entry, and for one whose new id already has an entry (the entries stay put; order, borrows and membership still follow). A collapsed group is a `gui.toml` key by project and group name (`group_collapsed`, same key as `match_slots`), stored only while collapsed, with no undo step.
- **HF5 The view order pulls a group behind its first member.** `build_views` does it right after the saved order (`pull_groups`), so `index`, Move up, Move down and Move to read what is on screen; the saved `order` is not rewritten by it, and a member with no card cannot place the block. `reorder_set` on a group's first member moves the whole block; any other set moves alone, and one that would land inside another group's block jumps it: stepping up from just below a group it lands before the block, stepping down from just above it lands after the block. Reordering never changes membership.
- **HF6 The Sets column draws groups as rows (agent rulings; the look is the owner's spec).** `draw` walks `Row::Header | Row::Card` built from `visible` and the stored groups (`build_rows`): a header comes before its first member that passes the filter, so a group with no passing member, or whose only member has no card, shows no header (its `[[groups]]` entry is untouched). The header reads the views: `n` or `n of m` (`m` counts the members that have a card), the worst dot of the members shown (blocks, then warning, then ready), an `Excluded` tag; its AccessKit name is `Group Armor, 7 sets, warning, collapsed`. A collapsed group draws no member card, and a filter leaves the fold as it is, also when it scrolls to the one open set. Members sit 12 px in (`GROUP_INDENT`). A header is one row of a fixed height (the body text height, at least `interact_size.y`, plus 6) and always drawn, so it needs no remembered height: the `top` sums in `jump` and `anchored` add it for each header above the target, and an off-screen card still answers ScrollIntoView. Its widget id is the group name, so a fold above never moves an open menu.
- **HF7 A reveal opens the group of what it reveals.** `nav.reveal_set` (Move to…, the palette) and a selection that moved since last frame (a click on a texture, Go to set; `DropState::selection`, the first frame only records it) open the group of the set they lead to, when a filter doesn't hide that set. A fold made by hand while the selection stays put stays folded, and Select all sets on a header is no reveal. Cost if wrong: arrow keys through the Textures column open each group they pass.
- **HF8 Group menus and dialogs.** A header's right-click menu holds Export group (HF10), Exclude from export / Include in export, Rename…, Ungroup and Select all sets (the sets that pass the filter). The set menu and the bulk menu get **Move to group** (each group, off when every set is in it; New group…; No group; one source, `group_entries`; `MenuAction::MoveToGroup`, `ToGroup`, `ToNewGroup`, `ToNoGroup`). New group… and Rename… open one name dialog whose OK shows the model's refusal (taken name, empty) and stays open; every other refusal is the `Refused` dialog. A read-only `sdc-project.toml` disables the edits with its reason. A set dropped on a header joins the group, and only while the ≡ handle is on (no filter), because the handle is what carries the set; a texture dropped there does nothing.
- **HF9 The engine writes only the listed sets: `BatchRequest.write_only` (spec; the details are agent rulings).** `Option<BTreeSet<String>>` of set keys (`sets::set_key`, so a key holds its output root). `None` (the CLI, and a GUI whole-project export with nothing excluded and an empty `no_set`) is today's run. `Engine::run` filters first, before any event or count: `jobs`, `skipped`, `failed` and `fill_ends` keep the listed sets only, so the summary, the events and `--json` cover the run (the spec named three lists; `failed` is filtered too, else a blocked set outside the run would count as a failure of it). The files cancelled while planning are filtered in `plan`, where their sets are still known. A skipped or failed entry with no set stays. Planning ignores it: it is destructured in `sig_without_files` and left out of the hash, so a scope change re-plans nothing. The manifest is append-only with compaction, so records of sets outside the run stay as they are; there is no prune (pinned, `manifest_records_outside_the_run_stay`). The Also phases plan under other roots, so `run_also` rebases the keys onto each phase's root (`rebase_write_only`), and the Vanilla copies skip jobs outside the list. A not-run file gets no Files row: the GUI drops the plan's discovered rows of textures outside the run (`ExportModel::outside_run`), and on a clean end a row still queued whose key, or an Also phase's `<folder>/<key>`, is outside the run is dropped rather than failed "not run"; a queued row of a texture in the run still ends "not run". Tests `crates/engine/tests/write_only.rs`.
- **HF10 Export set, Export n sets and Export group set a scope (owner's spec; agent rulings for the code).** `AppModel::export_sets(keys, label)` follows slot borrows and resolved slot links transitively, adding each lender as a whole set. Under HF11, a lender in an excluded group stays out unless explicitly selected, so its existing file serves the borrower. Export opens Review scoped to the resulting files, unless the project's Export without review is on and nothing needs a look. `export_group` takes the group's shown sets, excluded or not (asking by name is explicit), and leaves the flag. `in_run` answers the scope, so every reader of it (Problems rows, Review's columns, the undecided count, blocks, asks, the file count, the per-set JSON) covers the scope only; project-wide blocks (path collisions, JSON validation, the JSON name, the target mod, a missing export folder) still block. The scope is in `ViewKey` (`scope_rev`), which keys every view cache, so no cache serves a scoped answer outside it. Review's heading and button carry the label (`Export group Armor`, `Export set <id>`, `Export 3 sets`). The scope ends when Review closes, when the export ends, when a plain Export is clicked, and whenever no Review is open, no export runs, none is queued and no dialog is open (`end_idle_scope`, called after `export_sets` and every frame from `poll`; a dialog may park a scoped export, so its answer comes first), so a queued scoped export that is dropped or cancelled takes its scope with it. A queued export resumes through `export_go` at both plan-landing sites, so the scope stays. Every cache whose builder reads `item_in_run` keys on `scope_rev` and the sets-document revision: the view key, the Textures column's pill counts and lists, and the folder size. A scoped Review omits the Also options (JY5). **Export set** is off with "This set has no files to export" on a set with no key (all its files gone). While an export runs, Export set, Export group and Export n sets are off with "Wait for the export to finish", and `export_sets` does nothing: a scope set then would recount the running export's lists. Also export runs only unscoped (`also_runs`; owner: "Also export can be left to a whole project export"): a scope writes the PBR folder only, and a tick neither opens Review for it nor gates it. The *Not in a set* pill keeps its meaning (HF3): a set outside a scope is not "not in a set". Export set and Export group sit outside the editing lock, as Show in Explorer does: they edit nothing. Tests `crates/gui/tests/partial_export.rs`.
- **HF11 Exclude from export leaves a group's sets out of every whole-project export.** `in_run` is false for a set of an excluded group while no scope holds it, so a whole export sends `write_only` = every shown set's key that is in the run. A host in an excluded group is not pulled into a scope unless it was chosen: its file on disk serves the borrower. The Vanilla and CM phases leave them out through the rebased `write_only` (HF9), and `also_spec` adds their ids to `[export] skip` too (`also_run_config`): no export job reads the list then, since an excluded group always sets `write_only`, but `also_grass` does (an excluded PBR grass set gives the CM folder no grass stack), and so would a later reader. Their files are left exactly as they are (bytes and mtimes, pinned).
- **HF12 Files of sets outside the run (agent rulings).** Per-set files (CS PBR, complex material, XPMF, `.csskin.json`, the Vanilla borrowed-slot copies) are prepared for sets in the run only, and Review's JSON lines list only those. The PGPatcher JSON is one file, so it keeps every entry: on a run that leaves sets out (`untouched_root`), a dep that the run did not touch passes while its file is under the plan's output root, else the entry is left out with `not exported yet` (`pbr_export::dep_ok`, used by `finish` and `copy_files`); a dep the run touched needs its row done or up to date, as before. An edited PGPatcher JSON still asks. Orphan notes ("no set binds this EDID any more" and the per-set kinds) are not made on a scoped run or while a group is excluded (`reports_orphans`), because a set left out would read as gone; a no-set texture alone does not suppress them, and the PGPatcher JSON path-move note is unchanged.

## Note HQ — the export always has the textures folder (main team, 2026-11-08)

Owner (2026-11-08): "the export should have the textures folder always." Owner ruling on set ids (2026-11-08): "Move the export root".

- **HQ1 The output root moves into the export folder.** When the input root is at or inside a `textures` folder, a request plans under `<export>\<the root's own folders from that textures folder down to the root, each named as its parent folder lists it>`: root `Mod\textures\armor` plans under `<export>\textures\armor`. `discover::root_prefix` gives the folders and `discover::effective_output_root` the root; `BatchRequest::new` and `BatchRequest::set_export` apply it from the first input folder, so the CLI, the GUI's plan and every Also phase form it in one place. A moved request keeps the export folder in `BatchRequest::export_root`: explicit `output_paths` are relative to it, not to the output root, so a PGPatcher `textures\pbr\...` prefix still lands at `<export>\textures\pbr\...` for a root inside `textures` (fix round 1). Discovery skips the whole export folder (`skip_root`) unless that folder holds the input root (HQ5). Outputs then sit at their game paths below the export folder. Things that live beside the export folder itself keep the plain folder: the PGPatcher, CS PBR, CM and XPMF JSON locations, the Also location and **Open export folder**. Tests: `crates/engine/tests/export_layout.rs`, `crates/gui/tests/export_layout.rs`.
- **HQ2 A root with no `textures` in its path plans under the export folder itself.** No folder is invented. Cost if wrong: such projects still export without `textures`; the owner is told.
- **HQ3 Writing in place keeps the input root as the output root.** With the export folder equal to the input root (the CLI allows it, Note BC; the GUI's `export_folder_problem` refuses it), `root_prefix` is empty and a DDS lands beside its source.
- **HQ4 Existing projects whose root is a `textures` folder, or inside one, write to new paths.** Their outputs and their manifest (`_sdc`, which lives in the output root) move to `<export>\textures\...`; the old outputs and records stay where they were, since nothing deletes what an earlier export wrote elsewhere without a ruling. CHANGELOG 3.0.0 says so. No `ENGINE_VERSION` or `encode_version` change: paths are not bytes or verdicts, and the goldens are unchanged.
- **HQ5 An export folder that holds the input root writes beside the source (agent ruling).** `root_prefix` starts below the export folder when that is the root or an ancestor of it (`discover::holds`, on resolved paths when both exist, so `..` or a junction cannot hide it): `sdc convert Mod\textures\armor --out Mod\textures` or `--out Mod` writes `Mod\textures\armor\x.dds`. Discovery then skips nothing, since the export folder holds every input. Only the CLI can reach it; the GUI's `export_folder_problem` refuses such a folder. Test: `export_layout::in_place_writes_beside_the_source` plans all three through `BatchRequest::new`.
- **HQ6 Consumers (agent ruling).** Job keys stay relative to the plan's output root (`sets_root`); explicit output paths are relative to the export folder (HQ1). `sets::engine_inputs` builds them that way: a PGPatcher prefix is one already, an output name's path and a record path (`record_entries`) go through `AppModel::export_rel`, and a mirrored job key is compared as `AppModel::export_key`. Read back as they are by `pbr_export::slots_of` (the HL5 `root_is_textures` workaround is gone), `member_game_path` and the PBR path-collision messages; the collisions compare job keys as `export_key` too. The Meshes "This project" paths (`output_game_paths`) use `export_key` and are rebuilt after the plan's output root lands, so they list a `textures` root's outputs. `xpmf_json::data_rel`, `cs_skin_json::output_game_path` and `cm_json` read absolute outputs against the plain export folder and need no change. The Also walk (`note_unwritten_files`) puts the prefix on the phases' job keys and skips `_sdc` at any depth (test `also_export::a_textures_root_notes_none_of_its_own_files`); the CLI's Also location is `-o` (`cli_convert::also_from_a_textures_root_writes_its_folders_in_o`). The skipped-DDS check in the settings panel joins job keys to `sets_root` (`reencode_toggle::an_export_folder_above_the_textures_root_is_still_its_source_folder`).
- **HQ7 Set ids do not change.** `set_key` and `set_id` stay relative to the request's output root, so `[sets.<id>]` in `sdc-project.toml`, `--set set:<id>`, XPMF profile names and `rel` are what they were for every project. Test: `export_layout::outputs_move_and_set_ids_stay`.
- **HQ8 One output root per request (agent ruling).** The first input folder sets the prefix; later input folders and a first input that is a file add none of their own (`shortcut:` on `set_export`). A root at or inside `textures` that holds a folder named `textures` itself or one level down gets no prefix, since its game paths already start at that nested folder (BQ8). The rule is all or nothing: files beside such a nested folder export without `textures`, and deeper nesting is not looked for (`shortcut:` on `root_prefix`). It is worked out each time a request is built, not cached: the read is the root and its sub-folders' listings and stops at the first hit, and a cache would go stale when the user adds or renames a folder between plans.

## Note IB — UV checker leaves the GUI; sdc uv and sdc dilate (NIF team for the owner, 2026-11-08)

Source: the owner's plan to move the UV-island check out of the GUI (Tessalith is for finished textures; the check belongs earlier in an artist's pipeline). Code: removed `gui::model::uv_coverage`, `gui::model::overlay`, `engine::codes::Code::ChkUvGutter`; the removed-code row in `engine::migrate::REMOVED`. Tests: `crates/engine/tests/presets.rs::chk_uv_gutter_in_allow_is_refused`, `crates/gui/tests/checks.rs::removed_check_keys_in_gui_toml_are_ignored`.

- **IB1 The GUI has no UV coverage checker; `CHK-UV-GUTTER` is removed.** Gone: the preview's "UV coverage" toggle and its overlay (so the whole preview overlay module, `ViewState::overlay` and the overlay worker message), "Check all UV coverage" and "Stop checking UV coverage", the Advanced → Checks "UV coverage" box (three boxes remain), the Problems row, `Check::Uv`, the UV worker messages and `v2_jobs_pending` (the overlay and UV reads were its only jobs), the `uv_reads_on_this_thread` test hook and the `code_text` entry. `ChecksSettings` has no `uv`; an old `[checks] uv` in `gui.toml` loads without a message and is dropped on the next save. `Code::parse` returns `None` for `CHK-UV-GUTTER`, and `migrate::REMOVED` has an `allow` row, so an `allow` list, `--allow` or `--set` that names it is refused with "removed in 3.0" and a hint to use `sdc uv`. The schemas lose the string. This supersedes the UV items of Note CL (CL28's UV use, CL30 to CL34, CL39 and CL44; the frozen 1.x file is unchanged).
- **IB2 `engine::dilate::push_pull_fill(img, valid)` fills masked-out texels with a smooth pull.** It runs a push-pull on all four channels where `valid` is false (valid texels are never written; no valid texel returns false and changes nothing). The push is the same 2x2 mean as `dilate_under_zero_alpha`; the pull takes the bilinear sample of the parent at `((x + 0.5) / 2 - 0.5, (y + 0.5) / 2 - 0.5)`, clamped to its edges. The existing nearest-parent pull copies one parent texel into a 2x2 block, so a wide fill shows blocks; `dilate_under_zero_alpha` keeps it because its bytes are pinned by the goldens. `sdc dilate` uses the new function.
- **IB3 `sdc uv <PATTERN> (--s N | --w W --l L)` writes `<stem>_uvmask.png`, an 8-bit grayscale image of a texture's UV islands.** Island texels are 255, texels within the padding of one island 127, texels within the padding of two or more different islands 191 ("too close", always on), everything else 0. The pattern is a texture path as typed (`\` or `/`, `textures\` and `.dds` optional, any case, `*` and `?` inside a segment; a folder matches the textures directly in it, `--recursive` the ones below), and a shape uses a texture in any lighting slot or as an effect source. Meshes come from the GUI's scan database (`modscan::store::default_path()` or `--scan <db>`; game-winning copies only, loose or BSA) or, with `--meshes <folder>`, every `.nif` below it.
- **IB4 The padding is `max(2, max(W, L) / 256)` texels (512 to 2, 1024 to 4, 2048 to 8, 4096 to 16, 8192 to 32), as a Chebyshev window clamped at the image edges.** The GUI's check measured 8 texels at full size and could not resolve it at 4096; the CLI scales the padding with the image, so the 191 marker means the same thing at every size. The window does not wrap, so an island across the 0/1 edge of a tiling texture is two islands and gets no padding on the far side (`// shortcut` in the code).
- **IB5 Coverage is conservative, and islands are 8-connected texel components.** A texel is an island texel when a triangle overlaps its square with nonzero area (a separating-axis test), so a sliver thinner than a texel is drawn and touching alone does not count. UVs wrap into [0, 1) with a tile cap of 16 per axis, and a zero-area, non-finite or out-of-range triangle is skipped. Meshes that touch or overlap are one island, because the image shows texels, not meshes.
- **IB6 Output mirrors the matched path below the pattern's literal folder part, and an existing file is kept.** `FS6_Arrow_IR` writes `out\fs6_arrow_ir_uvmask.png`; the folder `Harry2135` with `--recursive` writes `out\fantasyseries6\fs6_arrow_ir_uvmask.png`; `--out` defaults to the current folder. A file that exists is kept and reported unless `--overwrite` is given; writes use `write_atomic`. One stdout line per written file: path, islands, pad, too-close texels, meshes (NIF files read). Exit codes follow the CLI table: 0 all written; 1 a texture wrote nothing (no UV triangles, or the file exists without `--overwrite`); 2 a usage error, including a pattern that matches no texture and a missing scan database (the message names the GUI's Meshes scan and `--meshes`); 3 a write failed, and the other textures are still written (as in `sdc dilate`).
- **IB7 modscan gained two read-only queries and a default-path function for `sdc uv`.** `Store::used_texture_paths()` lists the texture paths for which `winning_shapes_of_texture` finds a shape (one shared query body, so the list never names a texture the lookup cannot find); `Store::winning_shapes_of_texture(norm)` returns (copy to read, NIF path, block) for the game-winning copies (a derived winner on its source copy, as `nifs_using_canonical_texture` does) whose shape uses the texture in any lighting slot or as an effect source (slot 0, not the greyscale slot). The pattern is matched against the first list in the CLI, so no new index is needed. Test: `crates/modscan/tests/uv_queries.rs`.
- **IB8 `sdc dilate <PATTERN>...` is `engine::dilate::push_pull_fill` on a texture under a mask; the engine module is now `pub`.** A PATTERN is an image file, a folder (`--recursive` for what is below) or a glob on disk, and every input, literal or glob, must be a PNG, TGA or TIFF (a pattern with no image is exit 2); `*_matid.*`, `*_uvmask.*` and anything inside a `dilated` folder below the named file, folder or glob base are never inputs (a `dilated` above it does not count). The mask is `--mask`, else beside the input `<base>_matid.*` then `<base>_uvmask.png` for the engine's set base and then the full stem; a `_uvmask` is valid at 255 only, any other mask where R, G or B is above 0. Inputs that map to one output path write the first and refuse each later one by name, `--overwrite` or not. Test: `crates/cli/tests/cli_dilate.rs`.
- **IB9 The fill kind comes from the engine's own name reading, and a name with no sub-type is data.** The stem goes through `group::split_vocab`/`split_suffix` and `suggest::suggest` (vanilla, no set) and the first sub-type decides: Normal-category except `coat_normal` and `skin_wet` is a normal map, an sRGB `sdc-base` colour content is colour, everything else is data, and a stem with no known suffix is filled as stored with "no type from the name" on stdout. Colour is filled in linear light (alpha never decoded); a normal map is filled as stored and each filled texel is renormalised over all three channels (a zero vector becomes 0,0,1).
- **IB10 Masked texels are written back from the decoded sample code, never from the filled buffer.** `engine::load` returns k/255 or k/65535 as f32, so `round(v * max)` is the original code for every 8- and 16-bit sample (the test checks every channel layout at both depths); filled texels are rounded from the filled float. L and LA images fill with the L value in all three lanes and write back lane 0 (and alpha for LA). A float input (a 32-bit TIFF; EXR and HDR are not input formats) is refused for that input (`// shortcut` in the code: add them when a float push-pull is wanted).
- **IB11 The output is `<same file name>.png` in a `dilated` folder beside the input (`--out` overrides), same bit depth and channel layout.** The name stays so Tessalith still reads the suffix. An existing output is kept and reported unless `--overwrite`; an output that would replace any input of the same run, or the `--mask` file, is refused, `--overwrite` or not (Note HS10). Exit codes as `sdc uv` (IB6): 0 all written; 1 an input wrote nothing (no mask, size mismatch, float, no valid texel, exists, would replace an input); 2 no image matches; 3 a write failed. One stdout line per file: path, kind, mask, filled texel count.

## Note HH — Sets share a helper (main team, 2026-11-09)

- **HH1 A share is not membership (owner's spec; agent rulings for the code).** `SetEdit::Share { file, to }` puts the file in `TextureSet.shares` of set `to`; it stays a member of its own set, and a share into that set, or a second one, does nothing. A shared file is read as a member everywhere a helper is read: its role joins the set's suffixes (`apply_set_edits`, `predict_suffixes`, `drop_typed_roles`, `refresh_suffixes`), it is a raw input of `plan_packing` (the first by path still takes a role, HE7) and a helper of `plan_fills` (never a target there), with its own `helper.*` keys. A helper is used when any set uses it, so it is consumed and not `PACK-UNUSED-INPUT` unless every set leaves it out. `HelperReport` stays one per file: `fills` and `options` are its own set's, `outputs` adds the sharing sets' after its own, and `shared_with` lists their keys. A sharing set and the own set of each shared file are fast re-plan partners. Test `crates/engine/tests/helper_share.rs`.
- **HH2 A shared file stands in under the sharing set's name (agent ruling).** A recipe names and places its output from its first source, so in the sharing set `shared_stand_in` gives the file the folder, stem and game folder of the set's member whose base is the key's (else its first image), as `joined_helper_names` does for a hand-set helper: a shared roughness into `textures/c/pbr/slab` writes `<export>/textures/c/pbr/slab_rmaos.dds` with its game path under `textures/c/pbr/` (HQ1's export root holds, since the output comes from a member's). `path` stays the real file's, so sizes, `consumed` and the job removal see one file. A share adds no job and writes no file of its own.
- **HH3 What a share changes (agent ruling, 2026-11-09).** The own set plans as it did. A shared roughness makes a cs set PBR, as its own would (`set_resolved.pbr`). A share into a set with no member of its own is a `SET-EDIT-STALE` ("set '<id>' has no texture of its own"), and that set is dropped as an empty one is; a share naming a missing file or set is stale as `Move` is.
- **HH4 The no-set drop is by set (agent ruling, 2026-11-09).** `plan_sets` records `Plan::no_set_sets`, the keys of the sets whose members are all no-set (the GUI's `all_no_set` rule), and the BV2 dedupe and `Engine::run` test a job's `set_key` against it, not its input: a recipe of another set whose first source is a shared no-set helper writes, and the no-set file's own one-file set (its `_rmaos` too) still writes nothing. `Plan::no_set` stays for the GUI. `write_only` and `may_wait` (GY1, members only) are unchanged.
- **HH5 Dropping a helper on a set shares it (owner's rulings; agent ruling for the code, 2026-11-09).** A shareable helper is an `ItemKind::Helper` with a `HelperRole::Raw` role. `drop_choice(Share)` on one writes `share = ["<key>"]` in the target's `sdc-project.toml` entry (`SetsDoc::add_share`, one undo step "Share <name> with <set>", no file, no `occupant` check) and refuses a target with no member with "<set> has no texture of its own; move one in first"; on its own set it does nothing, and neither does a no-set helper on the set its name puts it in (`dir/base`, where **Back to its automatic set** returns it). A no-set helper dropped on a set asks (`AskChoice { from: "no set" }`, owner), and its dialog leaves Duplicate out; every other no-set texture still moves (HF1). Never offered or allowed Duplicate (Note JW5). The dialog's Share reads "Share (use this file here too, no copy)" for a helper, and a finished texture still borrows (`borrow.slotN`). The Textures column's helper row is a drag source (`Sense::click_and_drag`, payload `DragItem`), so a no-set helper can reach a card; a click still selects. `engine_inputs` emits `SetEdit::Share` after the `NoSet` edits. Test `crates/gui/tests/helper_share.rs`.
- **HH6 The share line follows the file (agent ruling, 2026-11-09).** The HE2 clear (`clear_deleted_lines`, through `drop_entry`) covers `share` lines as it covers `add` and `remove`: a deleted shared file's line goes with one Info line in Messages. `add_member` drops the target's own `share` line for the file it moves in, since the engine ignores a share of a member.
- **HH7 What the views show (agent ruling, 2026-11-09; final review 2026-11-09).** `SetView.shared_helpers` holds the item keys of the set's `share` lines that match an item (`path_key_matches`: any case, or without the extension) and are not the set's own members, which the engine ignores (a line matching no item is a `Missing::File`, as `add` is). The set editor draws one row per shared helper under the slot rows, outlined with `shared_stroke`, with the link mark before the name (Note JW3), named `<file> (shared)` so the user sees why it is not a slot (controller ruling); a click anywhere on the row selects the helper, and its ✕ ("Stop sharing <file>", `AppModel::stop_sharing`) removes every line that matches the file the same way. Only the ✕ is off on a read-only document, with the reason on hover. `stop_sharing` refuses a file the set does not share ("<set> does not share <file>", no undo step) and a read-only document. The row keeps clear of the editor's floating scroll bar. The helper panel lists every set in `shared_with`, used or not, under "Shared with" as links to those sets. `channel_helpers` offers a helper whose `shared_with` holds the set, and a pick writes the file's one `[files]` row, so it applies wherever the file is used. `HelperReport.used_by` (engine) holds the sharing sets whose jobs it lands in; `items::used_by` gives their base names when its own set fills nothing, and then `target_line` and the Textures row read `used by <names>`. A helper shared but used by no set still reads "not used: <reason>".
- **HH8 A borrowed slot answers the shader's "needs" warning (controller as the owner's delegate, 2026-11-17; owner's bug report "borrowed normals don't fill the set").** In the GUI a slot that a resolved `borrow.slotN` fills (`SlotFill::Borrowed` after `entry_view`) answers `SHADER-MISSING-MAP` for that slot's map (`map_slot`), as an HG7 fill does: `entry_view` drops it from `SetView::set_warnings`, so the Problems row, the card badge and readiness follow, and Clear brings it back. A borrow that does not resolve (the lender set is gone, or has nothing in that slot) keeps the warning. **The kind rule (B1-5, controller; B1-6, owner, 2026-11-17: "a cnr, or vanilla normal, has everything a pbr normal would need").** The borrowed file answers when its sub-type fits the role the set's shader needs for that map by the engine's `MapRole::matches` on `spec_for(target)` (sub-type only, so a role matched by suffix, such as `_i`, is never answered by a borrow), or when that role takes a tangent-space normal (`normal` or `pbr_normal`) and the file is any tangent-space normal (a normal-category sub-type that is not `msn`; `sets::tangent_space_normal`, the twin of the engine's `policy::takes_green`), since the borrower only names the lender's exported file. So a vanilla `normal` borrowed into a `pbr` set answers "needs _n"; an `msn` there, or an `_rfaos` for an `_rmaos`, keeps the warning. The engine, its `set_resolved` warnings and the output are unchanged. `shortcut:` `sdc convert` still warns, because it never reads `sdc-project.toml` (DV2); upgrade when the borrows are written where the CLI reads them. Tests `a_borrowed_normal_answers_the_missing_map_warning`, `a_borrowed_packed_rmaos_answers_the_missing_map_warning`, `a_borrowed_vanilla_normal_answers_the_pbr_missing_normal`, `a_borrowed_model_space_normal_keeps_the_missing_normal`, `a_borrowed_rfaos_keeps_the_missing_rmaos` and `a_borrow_from_a_set_without_that_slot_keeps_the_missing_map_warning` in `sets_model.rs`.

## Note HI — Texture panel tidy (main team, 2026-11-09)

- **HI1 One label column, one control column (owner).** Every texture-panel row draws its label in a `LABEL_W` (150 px) cell, cut short with the whole text on hover, and its control in a `CONTROL_W` (200 px) cell (`settings_panel::field_label`, `control_slot`, `control_cell`). Where a grid is narrower than both columns plus ↺, a hint and a margin, the note column (hint and ↺) keeps `NOTE_MIN` (120 px) and the control gives way first, down to `CONTROL_MIN` (the set editor's `COMBO_W`, 96 px), then the label, down to `interact_size.x` (`column_widths_at`, JI3), so no row runs past the centre (rule 3.1). A combo with no name of its own is named after its row (`labelled_by`), keeping its value. The Mipmaps tick sits in the control column, named "Mipmaps", with the level count or provenance in the hint column. The helper panel's rows and the cubemap rows use the same two widths; the helper's controls also give up the width of the Reset beside them where the row would not fit (`column_widths_beside`, agent ruling). Test `crates/gui/tests/texture_panel_layout.rs`.
- **HI3 The preview strip's controls are `COMPACT_CONTROL_W` (140 px) (agent ruling, owner to confirm).** Its labels take the room left (cut short, whole text on hover), so no control ends past the 280 px strip. The strip shows no Levels or DDS header row (JI3).
- **HI4 Helper is a tick under Texture type (owner).** An image's Helper row sits on the first screen, right under Texture type; ticking it puts a "Helper role" combo ("Pick a role…") in Texture type's place, and picking a role writes `helper.role`. The helper panel draws the same tick, ticked, under its Role row. A non-image has no Helper row; an image with no job (failed, skipped, packed) draws the Helper row alone, with the role combo once ticked.
- **HI5 Ticking writes nothing until a role is picked (agent ruling).** No default role is right for every file, so the ticked state is egui temp data keyed `("helper-tick", key)`, per file; any `helper.role` write from either panel clears it. Cost: one more click.
- **HI6 Unticking replaces "Not a helper" (agent ruling).** The role combo lists every role but "Not a helper"; unticking a helper writes `helper.role = "none"` (DY2), and such a file reads unticked with "set by you" and ↺ "Reset role" on the Helper row. Supersedes the 1.x DY17a (Not a helper in the combo), DY17c's "Not a helper (set by you)" wording and DY17f's "Helper role" combo under every image; Reset stays.
- **HI7 The alpha question has a Confirm button (owner).** `settings_panel::alpha_question` draws the dropdown on the proposal and **Confirm**, which answers with the shown type (the `Answer::Alpha` a pick gives); picking from the list still answers at once (FM35), and Enter on a Problems row is unchanged. Confirm's accessible name is "Confirm alpha" after the surface ("Problems confirm alpha", "Slot confirm alpha"), so the sub-type's "Confirm" stays unique; it waits for the plan as the dropdown does. The button is `alpha_confirm`, shared with the slot row's `alpha_choice`, which shows it only while the alpha is asked (JI5).
- **HI8 Problems and Review get Confirm too (agent ruling).** They ask the same question from one shared row (`review::fix_buttons_on`); on a Problems row, laid out right to left, Confirm still reads after its dropdown.
- **HI9 A `?` and a tooltip on every settings row (owner).** Every field `all_fields` lists (the Effort row's search fields and the Size and DDS header rows included, JI3, JI4), Levels, Filter, Edge, Straight alpha, the Mipmaps tick and the Helper tick (texture and helper panels) has help: `fields::field_help` (or `MIPMAPS_HELP`, `HELPER_HELP`), shown on the label's hover, also while the row is off (`edit_off`, a lock). In the texture panel, the 3D settings window, the bulk panel and the helper panel's Helper row the label cell also ends with a weak `?` named "Help: {label}" with the same hover (not in the strip, HI18). Aligned, the `?`s line up at the label column's right edge; in the bulk panel the `?` follows the label. The checkbox hovers (parallax, BC1 alpha, Perceptual, EXR straight) stay on the control and are those rows' help too.
- **HI10 The cube section gets no `?` (agent ruling).** It has its own wiki page (`Cubemaps.md#settings`), and the owner's screenshot doesn't show it.
- **HI11 The texture panel's Format names the format alone (owner).** `fields::format_choices` lists each format once, "BC7", with `NOT_VALID` on a kept stray pair.
- **HI12 Other format pickers keep the tag (agent ruling).** The bulk panel, the Presets window and the set editor still list "BC7 · linear", since the owner said "on this page". A Format pick on the texture panel keeps the current DDS tag when the new format allows it, else takes that format's first allowed pair.
- **HI13 The Alpha row and the Undecided box line up and agree (owner).** The Undecided box sits on the first screen, above the groups. In the texture panel the box asks the alpha as one row, "Alpha" in the label column, the dropdown in the control column at the Alpha row's x, then Confirm; the label's hover is the SUG-LOW-CONFIDENCE reason. The sub-type question keeps its sentence, its combo and Confirm in the control column. Where the box is too narrow for a full-width dropdown and Confirm (1100 px), the dropdown gives up the difference. The strip keeps FM35's line, with Confirm added.
- **HI14 While the alpha is asked, every row reads the proposal (agent ruling).** `draw` sets the proposal on its own copy of the job, so the Alpha row, Threshold, Comparison, Coverage and the filter locks show what Confirm will give, and the Alpha row's hint is "proposed: confirm above". Nothing is written, the document stays clean and the export gate still counts the texture undecided until an answer; a pick on the Alpha row is an answer, and picking the shown value there writes nothing (Confirm accepts it). The folded "Where these settings come from" section shows the unconfirmed profile (JI3).
- **HI15 A texture in no set gets the same panel (agent ruling).** That covers its Undecided box with Confirm and its Helper tick; a role picked there makes a no-set helper, which reads "not used" until it is shared (HH5) or moved into a set.
- **HI16 Unticking a shared helper writes `helper.role = "none"` on its one `[files]` row (agent ruling).** Every set that shares it stops using it, and the `share` lines stay; the tick's hover says "Also used by {n} other sets: they stop using it too." ("1 other set: it stops …" for one). Undo takes it back.
- **HI17 The 3D settings window is aligned (agent ruling).** It draws the texture panel, so its rows use the same two columns; at its default 460 px they stay inside it.
- **HI18 The preview's settings strip shows no `?` (agent ruling).** The strip's labels are about 90 px and already cut short; there the label's hover carries the help (with the full label where it is cut short).

## Note HJ — Rename outputs through slots (main team, 2026-11-09)

- **HJ1 Rename outputs is for PGPatcher PBR sets only (owner).** `AppModel::renames_outputs` (`v.pbr` on the Community Shaders target, the old `record_ignored`) decides it. The Outputs entry is absent, not greyed, from the set menu, the texture menu and the palette when no selected set qualifies; a mixed selection lists the qualifying sets with their count in the label. F2 on a texture selection opens Inputs mode, and the mode switch hides Outputs when no selected set qualifies; F2 on a set selection with no qualifying set opens no dialog and says where to rename inputs (LJ3, W19). `set_output_name` refuses a name on any other set ("output names are for PGPatcher PBR sets"); only `None` clears.
- **HJ2 An `output_name` already saved keeps working and can be cleared (agent ruling).** Dropping it would silently move an old project's outputs. The set editor's Clear button stays. Cost if wrong: an old project keeps a name the user can no longer set.
- **HJ3 A rename that cannot be a PGPatcher `rename` writes slot commands (owner).** `prefix::rename_form` is `Key` (written as `match_diffuse` + `rename`, AN1, AA8) for a file-name `rename` on a set with at most one match row (an empty + row counts) and no `path_contains`, and `Slots` for any other rename: `json_rename` is then `None`, the entry keeps its active matches and `path_contains` and writes no `rename` or `match_diffuse`, and `slots_of` compares each filled slot against `default_prefix` (where PGPatcher looks by default) so every filled slot names the renamed file (a user's `slotN` wins, a locked slot stays unnamed). AN1 and AN2 hold for `Key` form only, so no edit is refused for a rename, the "not written" markers and pitfalls are gone, and `cs_pbr::set_key` keeps every match; the "match slots point at different textures" pitfall still fires on a renamed set (agent ruling), since a slot the set leaves empty comes from the hit's prefix.
- **HJ4 A plain rename keeps `match_diffuse` + `rename` (agent ruling).** Existing JSON doesn't change for no reason: `Key` form writes the same bytes as before (pinned by `a_plain_rename_keeps_its_json`), and AF8's pitfall still applies to it. Cost if wrong: two JSON shapes for one feature; switching every rename to slots would be a one-line change in `rename_form`.
- **HJ5 Rename outputs can change the whole path, through slot commands (owner).** A `rename` with a `\` or `/` is a whole path relative to `Data`, which is relative to the export folder: `prefix_of` returns it backslashed, with `textures\` added when missing, and `rename_form` is always `Slots`. It is checked by `check_rel_path` (no absolute path, no `..`) and `check_named` on its last component (no map suffix, no `_detail`/`_detailnorm`). The rename dialog offers *Whole path* in both modes; in Outputs mode the recipe runs over the output base path (the suffix and extension stay out of it), and unticked it runs over the last component only.
- **HJ6 The path is stored in the set's `rename` (agent ruling).** No new key, so the JSON editor's box and undo keep working; it is never written to the JSON in that form. `set_output_name` stores the last component alone when it gives the same prefix (`rename_value`), so a dialog rename that keeps the folder stays in `Key` form (HJ4), and a path otherwise. Cost if wrong: a typed `textures\pbr\<matched folder>\x` path is stored as `x`; the outputs land on the same files either way.
- **HJ7 A moved set's files stay where the user put them (agent ruling).** Not under `textures\pbr\`. Known texture paths under `textures\pbr\` are still the only ones kept out of the input-name suggestions (AO8; a `shortcut:` in `input_name_candidates`). Cost if wrong: a moved output shows up in the input-name suggestions when the export folder is scanned as a mod.
- **HJ8 A no-albedo JSON set gets no Rename outputs (agent ruling).** A set with `json && !pbr` (Note HL5) keeps its outputs at the mesh's own paths and its slot 1 at the mapping's; a vanilla set renames through Map to target (Note HG). Cost if wrong: the owner wanted those sets renamed too; `renames_outputs` would then read `json` and the slot rows would need the HL5 paths.
- **HJ9 A whole path is relative to the export folder (agent ruling).** That is the frame HQ6 gives a PGPatcher prefix, so `armor\plate` lands at `<export>\textures\armor\plate.dds` whatever the input root, and outside the plan's output root when that root is deeper. The Outputs dialog shows and compares outputs in that frame for file names too, so its rows read as game paths: the old value is the set's prefix with `/`, the outputs are `output_rel(<new prefix>, suffix)` for each output `output_candidates` gives the set, and a collision is checked, lower case, against the sources of `pbr_export::path_collisions` for every set not renamed (owned, record and mirrored `export_key` paths) and between the renamed sets. Cost if wrong: a user expecting a path relative to the input root gets a different folder; the row shows the full game path before Apply, so it is seen, not silent.
- **HJ10 A shared helper's recipe output follows the sharing set (agent ruling).** A shared helper (Note HH) writes nothing of its own; a recipe output it feeds belongs to the sharing set and moves with that set's rename only. Selecting a shared helper's tile and pressing Rename outputs renames its own set, as for any member. Cost if wrong: the user wanted the sharer renamed; they select its card instead.
- **HJ11 A rename never changes a set key (agent ruling).** A no-set file (Note HF1) has no set and offers no Rename outputs (pinned by `rename_keys_skip_textures_that_are_in_no_set_in_outputs_mode`); a rename never changes a set key (HQ7), so Export set, Export group, Exclude and `write_only` treat a moved set as before, and a scope that leaves it out keeps its PGPatcher entry while its files exist (HF12). Cost if wrong: a scoped export drops a moved set's entry as "not exported yet" until it is exported once at the new path.

## Note HR — seven OPEN items closed (main team, 2026-11-09)

- **HR1 A `remove` written by an override is opaque colour to the rules (agent ruling, owner confirmed 2026-11-09).** On a sub-type whose alpha the mesh decides (`Subtype::alpha_from_mesh`), when the source alpha varies and the winning `alpha.policy` override (`[files]` or `--set`, the last that applies) is `remove`, `layered_profile_with` matches the rules on alpha `none`, as for the mesh's own `remove` (owner O1), so cs-compact gives BC1. A `remove` from a preset or config table is unchanged: the rules still see the real alpha. Tests `mesh_alpha::an_explicit_remove_on_varying_alpha_matches_the_mesh_remove`, `a_recorded_remove_in_the_project_file_is_bc1_under_compact`, `a_later_override_over_a_remove_keeps_compact_off_bc1`.
- **HR2 The GUI records a mesh `remove` (owner).** `record_auto_decisions` writes every mesh-alpha verdict, `remove` included, as `alpha.policy` in `sdc.toml`, a mesh `remove` on a `complex_grass` file too (`tests/complex_grass.rs`) (amends FC1; closes OPEN [FC]); the threshold is still written only when the mesh set it, and the automatic verdict survives Undo of a user edit (JY6). With HR1 the CLI plans the same format from the text. Test `auto_accept::a_mesh_remove_is_recorded_and_survives_undo`.
- **HR3 A file link into the output folder is skipped (agent ruling).** Discovery resolves a file link as it does a folder link (Note CV6): unless the input lies inside the output folder (an in-place run), a link whose target is under the output root is not an input and nothing is reported. A hard link is not a link and is still read; an in-place run skips nothing. Closes OPEN [CV6]. Tests `discover_group::a_file_link_into_the_output_folder_is_skipped`, `an_in_place_run_still_reads_a_file_link` (both print `skipped:` where file links cannot be made).
- **HR4 OPEN [R-fills-1] and OPEN [U] are closed by a pin test each (agent ruling).** `rfaos` is in `group::SUFFIXES`, so a pre-packed `_rfaos` joins its set without a prediction, and `Code::PackConflict` is raised for it; a mesh-evidence read error is already a Messages warning ("Scan database: ... keeping the previous mesh evidence") and the evidence is kept. Neither needed code. Tests `skin_subtypes::a_prepacked_rfaos_joins_a_forced_rfaos_set_without_fuzz`, `edges::a_mesh_evidence_read_error_warns_and_keeps_the_evidence`.
- **HR5 The late restore-Undo line names the keys (agent ruling).** After the restored edits were undone or edited over, the toast's Undo says "The restored edits were already undone or edited since: press Ctrl+Z to undo or Ctrl+Y to redo". It supersedes FB14's wording; closes OPEN [FB14]. Test `recovery::toast_undo_after_another_edit_leaves_everything`.
- **HR6 A resized cutout texture keeps its source's coverage (agent ruling, owner confirmed 2026-11-09).** When `resize::target_dims` changes the size (a `resize` mode or the power-of-two step) and coverage preservation applies, `preprocess_with` records the alpha-test coverage just before the resize (`Work::source_coverage`), and the pipeline fits every level, mip 0 included, to it (`coverage::preserve_coverage_to`); that is also the base the coverage check compares with. Their outputs from before the move are of an older encode version: `if-changed` with `--ev` writes them again (only resized cutouts change) and without it keeps them (Note JA). A texture that is not resized is unchanged byte for byte, and complex grass and cubemaps keep their path. Supersedes S-mips-8 (closes its OPEN line). The `encode_version` arms of `diffuse`, `pbr_albedo`, `ui_sky` and `skin_albedo` moved to the engine version: the sub-types a built-in layer can make a cutout (the mesh layer and alpha suggestion, `Subtype::alpha_from_mesh`, and the hair shader's diffuse); `complex_grass` is the other mesh sub-type and keeps its own path. A hand-written cutout on any other sub-type that is also resized keeps its old bytes under `if-changed` until that arm moves and a run has `--ev`. No golden, `encode_inputs.json` or `identity.json` entry changed. Tests `coverage::preserve_coverage_to_fits_level_0`, `preserve_coverage_to_at_the_ends`, `ops::a_resize_records_the_source_coverage`, `pipeline::a_resized_cutout_keeps_its_source_coverage`, `encode::a_resized_cutout_export_keeps_the_source_coverage`.
- **HR7 A cache hit keeps its entry from the prune (agent ruling).** A `get` on an analysis-cache entry whose modification time is more than a day old (`TOUCH_AFTER`) sets it to now, so the prune, which still deletes oldest first, keeps what is used. At most one small write per entry per day; a failed touch (read-only entry) is still a hit. Amends DZ8 (closes its OPEN line). Tests `analysis_store::a_hit_on_an_old_entry_survives_the_prune`, `a_recent_hit_leaves_the_time_alone`, `a_read_only_old_entry_is_still_a_hit`.

## Note HT — audit 2026-11-10: one guarded write, per-crate test runs (main team, 2026-11-10)

- **HT1 Every JSON finisher writes through `json_finish::guarded_write` (agent ruling).** CS PBR and XPMF call it as complex material and CS skin do, so the never-overwrite order (drop the own case twin, `may_replace`, atomic write, record the hash) has one copy; notes and files are unchanged (extends FW2). Two pins came with it: `presets_creator::a_new_preset_is_not_saved_over_a_file_that_did_not_load` (AM8) and `cm_json::without_a_cm_phase_the_cm_json_follows_the_pbr_phase` (the fall-back of FW1).
- **HT2 A per-crate test run adds `-p sdc` (owner, 2026-11-10).** `cargo test -p <crate> -p sdc ...` resolves features as `--workspace` does, so the gate reuses its build. Measured with `cargo tree`: `-p engine` alone builds 15 packages under other features and `-p sdc-gui` alone 3; with `-p sdc` added, none. For this the CLI's dev-dependency on `sdc-gui` turns on `test-hooks` (no CLI test reads a GUI hook). It amends the Gates rule of 2026-10-29 in CLAUDE.md.

## Note HU — one GUI test binary (main team, 2026-11-10)

- **HU1 The GUI test files are modules of one test binary, `it` (owner, 2026-11-10).** `crates/gui/tests/it/main.rs` names each `tests/<name>.rs` with `#[path]`, so no file moved. Each keeps its own `mod support;`, and a few their own copy of another crate's fixture builder, so the binary allows `clippy::duplicate_mod`; it allows no other lint. `crates/gui/Cargo.toml` sets `autotests = false`: only the targets it lists are built. A test is named `<file>::<test>`, and one file runs with `cargo test -p sdc-gui -p sdc --test it <file>::`. Measured for the 154 merged files: 19.9 GB of exes and PDBs became 0.23 GB, and building the GUI and CLI test binaries went from 454 s to 180 s. The gate's `render_` filter still selects GPU tests only, because no test file has `render_` in its name; a file named so would move all its tests to the GPU step.
- **HU2 A test file that changes process-wide state stays its own `[[test]]` target (agent ruling).** `it::process_state_needles_match_the_files_kept_apart` pins the list against the manifest. `app_no_local_store`, `doc_shots`, `meshes_header`, `nif_edit`, `replan_timing` and `ui_proof` point `%LOCALAPPDATA%` or `%APPDATA%` at a temp folder. `crash_recovery` and `fonts_crash_hook` install the panic hook. `fonts_env` sets `SDC_FONT`. `device_lost` sets the device-lost flag, which nothing clears: the next `SdcApp` frame in the process would open the restart dialog of FC4, wait for a click, then start the test exe again and exit. A trial run with `device_lost`, `nif_edit` and `meshes_header` all merged hung for hours on tests of the last two, and its log ends with the restart's `--after-device-loss`; the same run without those two passed, so which of the two causes stopped it was not separated. `ui_drive` keeps its own harness.
