- **HU3 `it::every_test_file_is_built` fails when a test file is in neither place, or in both (agent ruling).** With `autotests = false` a forgotten file is never compiled, and its tests would pass by not running. A file in both places would run a second time beside tests it can disturb. It checks each `tests/<name>.rs` for its `#[path]` and `mod` lines or a `[[test]]` path, and each folder with a `main.rs` for a `[[test]]` path.

## Note HV — analysis cache: compressed thumbnails and a limit the user sets (owner, 2026-11-10)

- **HV1 A cache entry's thumbnail is stored deflated (owner: "add compressed caching"; the format is an agent ruling).** `AnalysisStore::put` writes the RGBA8 bytes through zlib at the fast level when that is smaller, else as they are. `get` tells the two apart by length alone: bytes as long as width × height × 4 are not deflated. So the header has no new field, `ANALYSIS_VERSION` does not move, and an entry written before this note still reads. A deflated thumbnail must be one whole zlib stream, with its checksum and nothing after it, that inflates to exactly the header's size, or the entry is a miss (DZ: every failure is a miss). A header that names a thumbnail larger than `THUMB_MAX` a side is a miss too, so a damaged header never sizes the buffer. An older Tessalith that opens the folder reads a deflated entry as a miss and writes it again. Measured with zlib on 600 entries of the owner's cache: an entry goes from about 67 KB to about 33 KB (the thumbnail to 47.8% of its bytes at level 1; level 6 gives 45.6%). A hit now costs one inflate more, about 0.19 ms a thumbnail (about 3 s of CPU over 15,000 entries); a miss costs 17 ms to decode the image again. `flate2` moves from the engine's dev-dependencies to its dependencies; it was already in the lock through `esp`. Tests `analysis_store::a_thumbnail_is_stored_compressed_and_reads_back`, `an_entry_with_an_uncompressed_thumbnail_still_reads`, `a_thumbnail_that_does_not_shrink_is_stored_as_it_is`, `a_packed_thumbnail_never_has_the_length_of_its_texels`, `a_damaged_compressed_thumbnail_is_a_miss`, `a_thumbnail_larger_than_any_made_is_a_miss`.
- **HV2 The cache limit is a GUI setting, 500 MB unless changed (owner).** `Session::with_disk_cache(dir, max_bytes)` takes the limit, and the GUI passes `GuiSettings::analysis_cache_bytes()`: `analysis_cache_mb` in `gui.toml`, 500 when unset (an unset limit is not written), one MB being 1024 × 1024 bytes. **Settings → Cache** (JI1) has one number box, *Analysis cache limit*, from 50 to 5,000 MB (agent ruling: the prune walks the whole folder before the window opens, and 5,000 MB is about 150,000 entries; its time at that size is not measured). A whole number typed into `gui.toml` outside that range is used and shown as it is, and drawing, entering or leaving the box does not change it; 0 keeps nothing between runs. Any other value is a file that does not load, as for every key. The prune still runs once, at start (DZ8), so a new limit applies the next time the app starts and the box's hover says so (agent ruling: lowering the limit deletes nothing while the app runs). It replaces DZ8's fixed 200 MB; HR7 is unchanged. With HV1, 500 MB holds about 15,000 entries, where 200 MB held about 3,100. Tests `analysis_disk_cache::a_session_prunes_to_the_given_size_at_start`, `app_no_local_store::with_analysis_cache_stores_under_the_given_folder_only`, `settings::the_analysis_cache_limit_is_500_mb_until_set`, `a_hand_edited_cache_limit_survives_the_next_save`, `settings_tab::app_settings_save_to_gui_toml_at_once`.

## Note HS — trace-debug fixes 2026-11-10 (main team, 2026-11-10)

The trace-debug report of 2026-11-10 (I1 to I20) and two quality-audit items (QA2, QA6). Task 10 (I19, I20: the Meshes edit panel and model, which CONCURRENCY.md gives the NIF team) was done by the main team on the owner's word, 2026-11-10, with a line in the NIF team's handoff file.

- **HS1 A scope follows an output-root change (agent ruling).** (I3) A plan under a new output root moves the export scope's keys to it (`AppModel::rekey_scope`). Before, the keys kept the old root (HF9 keys hold their root), so Export set after choosing or changing the export folder, with its Review open or the export queued, wrote nothing. Extends HF10. Tests `partial_export::export_set_survives_choosing_the_folder_from_review`, `export_set_survives_a_folder_change_while_review_is_open`, `a_scoped_set_gone_after_the_folder_change_does_not_stop_the_others`.
- **HS2 A packed output's `[files]` key is the first source's folder plus the stem of the job's own output name (agent ruling, owner confirmed 2026-11-10).** (I4) `engine::pack::plan::files_stem(job, kind)` gives it from the job's `game_path`, so it also follows `[inputs] read_as`; the GUI item key (`packed_key`) and the CFG-FILE-UNMATCHED check use it, as policy already did, and the GUI's `by_input` skips packed items. So a shared helper's packed output is keyed in the helper's folder (`a/slab_rmaos` for set `c/pbr/slab`), and a joined helper's under the set's name (`x_rmaos`). A stem that differs from the output name only in letter case keeps the source's own spelling, non-ASCII capitals included (both sides fold alike). An output path never moves the key: `game_path` comes from the source name, `read_as` or the stand-in, never from an explicit output path or output name. The GUI's default output (`DefaultOutputs::key`, which an output name or base record mapping renames) names such a stand-in's packed output after the set member it stands in for, so it stays in the set's folder (`c/maple_m`, not `a/maple_m`). Existing entries under the old wrong keys are not migrated. Tests: engine `pack::plan::tests::files_stem_keeps_the_own_case_and_takes_a_set_name`, `helper_share::a_files_key_on_a_shared_output_is_not_reported_unmatched`, `an_output_path_keeps_the_packed_files_key`, `helper_roles::a_files_key_on_a_joined_helpers_output_is_not_reported_unmatched`; GUI `helper_share::a_shared_roughness_gives_each_set_its_own_packed_item`, `a_fill_on_a_sharing_sets_packed_output_reaches_the_engine`, `a_shared_roughness_exports_both_packed_outputs`, `an_output_name_on_a_sharing_set_names_its_packed_output`, `output_names::a_fill_on_a_renamed_pbr_sets_packed_output_reaches_the_engine`, `helper_panel::a_joined_helpers_packed_output_is_keyed_as_the_set`, `a_joined_helper_differing_in_case_keeps_one_item`.
- **HS3 A packed source's fill string names its inverted channels when they are mixed (agent ruling, owner confirmed 2026-11-10).** (I5) `pack::source_fill` writes `ch:<fed>,inv:<inverted>` when the channels one source feeds do not share one invert flag; a uniform source keeps `ch:<fed>`. The record and if-changed read one list of recorded sources (`manifest::recorded_sources`, QA6), so an invert toggle on such a source re-encodes. Extends DY21 and FM12; the digest's fill line gains `inv:`, and `schema/manifest.schema.json` describes it. Cost, accepted by the owner: every mixed-inversion packed output (a Complex Material from an ORM, a cs `_rmaos` with a gloss in R and A) re-encodes once on the first if-changed run after 3.0. Test `helper_packed::if_changed_sees_an_invert_toggle_on_a_mixed_source`.
- **HS4 The export folder is not source layout (agent ruling).** (I6) When the export folder lies strictly inside the input root, `discover::root_prefix` ignores it and every folder in it, so its own `textures` folder no longer flips the output root on the second run (HQ8 reads the source tree only). Only the current export folder is ours: an old export folder left inside the root is source layout again. A project that already flipped flips back; the flat copy and its manifest stay behind. `shortcut:` paths are compared as written, so an export folder reached through a junction or `..` is not recognised. Test `export_layout::an_export_folder_inside_the_root_does_not_flip_the_output_root`.
- **HS5 Leaving Blocked pushes the set preview's scene too (agent ruling).** (I7) A Blocked member changes pgpsim's existence check, so its rows must be simulated again when it comes back; before, "will be skipped: … missing" and the unpatched material stayed until another push. Amends GQ5 in place ("a member the edit newly blocks, or unblocks, pushes the scene"); GQ5's old wording is in the archive. Test `set_preview_model::a_member_that_stops_being_blocked_pushes_the_scene_and_clears_the_skip`.
- **HS6 A PBR set with an Error job is left out of Also (agent ruling, owner confirmed 2026-11-10).** (I8) `derive_sets` counts the set's Error jobs as failed maps (FV1's note, "not derived: N map(s) failed in the PBR export"), and a PACK-SIZE-MISMATCH job's sources join the dropped files. Before, the Vanilla and CM maps were derived from one raw map read as a packed `_rmaos`. Extends FV1 and CN99. Cost, accepted by the owner: a set with `packing.prefer = raw` and mismatched raw maps is left out too, although its pre-packed `_rmaos` converts. Test `derive_plan::a_failed_rmaos_recipe_leaves_a_pbr_set_out_of_also`.
- **HS7 A scoped export with a tick is checked against the folder it writes (agent ruling).** (I9) `export_stopped` checks `output_root()` (the PBR folder) for any run that skips Review's Also gate. Before, a scope was checked against the export folder and wrote the PBR folder unchecked. Extends HF10. Tests `partial_export::a_scoped_export_with_a_tick_checks_the_pbr_folder_it_writes`, `a_scoped_export_with_a_tick_never_writes_into_the_source_mod`.
- **HS8 Copy settings plans each ticked field against the target as the copy's other written fields leave it (agent ruling).** (I13) A skipped field is checked again against a projection of the target's profile with the fields that write (`bulk::projected`), until nothing changes, so a field another copied field unlocks is written (the alpha threshold with a cutout policy, the mip edge with a Tent filter, BC1 1-bit alpha with a BC1 format). A slot lock, a target with no job and an invalid value still skip. A single-field bulk Set keeps Z2. `shortcut:` BC7 weighting is not re-checked, and a copied sub-type projects only `subtype`, not the preset values it brings. Tests `bulk_apply::copy_settings_writes_a_field_that_another_copied_field_unlocks`, `copy_settings_writes_the_edge_with_a_non_box_filter`, `copy_settings_lands_a_whole_unlock_chain`, `copy_settings_lands_a_two_step_unlock_ticked_in_reverse`, `copy_settings_still_skips_a_field_nothing_in_the_copy_unlocks`.
- **HS9 The centre tab strip commits a focused field on press (agent ruling).** (I14) A tab click goes through `commit_click`, so a commit-on-blur field commits before the switch hides its view; the digest's FC8 line adds the tab strip. Test `centre_tabs::a_tab_click_commits_the_focused_field_before_switching`.
- **HS10 `sdc dilate` never replaces an input of the run (agent ruling, owner confirmed 2026-11-10).** (I16) An output path that is any input of the same run, or the explicit `--mask` file, is refused ("would replace an input of this run"), `--overwrite` or not, and that input exits 1. Paths are compared canonicalised, so an output that does not exist yet is never refused. Amends IB11 in place; IB11's old wording is in the archive. Tests `cli_dilate::an_output_that_would_replace_another_input_of_the_run_is_refused`, `an_output_that_would_replace_the_mask_file_is_refused`.
- **HS11 Rename refuses an automatic set's name while the engine still makes that set (agent ruling).** (I17) A name any file's automatic set id would take (`auto_id`: `dir/base_of(stem)`) is refused for a set that was not created, card or not, with "<id> is still the automatic set of a file; pick another name". The id is taken from the name a file is read as (`[inputs] read_as`), so a name an input name left is free. Packed outputs are left out: their key names a set, not a file. Before, Rename checked the cards only, so it took the id of an automatic set deleted to no set or emptied by a move; the engine refused the Rename and the entry was left naming no files. A created set may take such a name. `shortcut:` the id is predicted with `base_of` (no preset aliases, no `_detail` join); upgrade to `engine::group::group_sets_with` over the file list if it mis-predicts. Tests `sets_model::rename_refuses_the_name_of_a_deleted_automatic_set`, `rename_refuses_the_name_of_an_automatic_set_emptied_by_a_move`, `a_created_set_may_take_a_hidden_automatic_name`, `rename_may_take_an_automatic_name_an_input_name_left`, `helper_share::a_shared_packed_item_does_not_block_a_rename`.
- **HS12 A touched Meshes edit refuses an export folder that holds its source copy (agent ruling, owner confirmed 2026-11-10).** (I20) `NifEdit::retarget` returns an error, so the root is not taken and the row is blocked (GD12) with "<folder> holds the mesh this edit applies to (<origin>); an export there would replace it. Choose another export folder." The "could not open the edit document in <folder>: " prefix now comes from `retarget`, on its three document and path errors, so the row shows the guard's text without it. Folders are compared by `same_dir` (ASCII case, `/` or `\`, a trailing separator), not through junctions or 8.3 names. Extends the digest's edit-source line. Cost, accepted by the owner: a mod author who edited their own mod's mesh cannot export into that mod (an untouched session already refuses it). Test `nif_edit::an_export_folder_holding_the_source_copy_is_not_taken`.
- **HS13 A retarget carries order and borrows as well as membership (agent ruling, owner confirmed 2026-11-10).** (I10) `retarget_auto` with no entry at `from`, or with an entry already at `to`, also renames the id in `[project] order` and in other sets' `borrow.slotN` (`SetsDoc::rename_refs`, which `rename_set` shares; the order keeps one place for `to`, the first). Onto an existing entry the borrows move to the new id too (owner, 2026-11-10). Before, an input-name change on a set with no `sdc-project.toml` entry left the order and the borrows on the old id. Amends HF4 ("membership" → "order, borrows and membership"); HF4's old wording is in the archive. Tests `gui_tweaks::retarget_with_no_entry_moves_the_order_and_other_sets_borrows`, `retarget_onto_an_existing_entry_moves_the_order_and_borrows`, `retarget_leaves_a_created_set_alone`.
- **HS14 A dragged set lands where its insertion line is drawn (agent ruling).** (I11) Dragging a group's first member down past its block subtracts the block (`reorder_target`), and a drop on a member of its own block does nothing. Before, the group landed (group size − 1) cards past the line, and a drop inside its own block moved it. Extends HF5. Tests `sets_column::tests::a_groups_first_member_dropped_below_counts_without_its_block`, `set_groups_ui::a_group_dragged_down_lands_just_after_the_card_it_is_dropped_on`.
- **HS15 A typed texture path is committed by any click that ends the typing (agent ruling, owner confirmed 2026-11-10).** (I19) In the Meshes edit panel the path commit has its own slot (`Act.path`), applied before the click's own edit, and a tab click draws the old tab once more so its field commits. When the click is that same slot's own Fix, the Fix is dropped (the typed path wins, as before), so no Conflict is created (owner, 2026-11-10). A click that ends the typing and makes its own edit is two undo steps. Tests `nif_edit::a_path_typed_without_enter_survives_a_tab_click`, `a_path_typed_without_enter_survives_a_click_on_a_later_control`, `a_same_slot_fix_while_a_path_is_typed_is_dropped`.
- **No ruling** for I1 (DT22's quarter cap now also holds for a cube DDS preview), I2 (restores Z11 on the fast re-plan: `apply_partial` keeps `type_base` as `apply_plan` does; a full plan that fails after a partial landed leaves the cleanup to the next settled plan), I12 (FM31: a filled job reads the filled image's analysis), I15 (rename and the input name read preset suffix aliases), I18 (DS12: `sdc validate` reads the alpha of every cube face), QA2 (`FileError` and a failed preview read as text) and QA6 (one list of recorded sources).

## Note HW — Tessalith 3.0: version 3.0.0 and one licence (owner, 2026-11-11)

- **HW1 The workspace version is 3.0.0.** The 2.1 work never shipped on its own, so it ships in 3.0 (Note DM8: MAJOR, because v3 removes features). The `encode_version` arms that read `ENGINE_VERSION` (the 2.1.0 and HR6 arms) now name 3.0.0, so a 2.0.0 record of those types is of an older encode version: re-encoded with `--ev`, kept and counted without it (Note JA). `RELEASED_ENGINE_VERSION` stays 2.0.0 until the release commit sets it.
- **HW2 The engine is GPL-3.0-or-later.** From 3.0, `engine` and `encoders-sys` take the workspace licence like every other crate; their MPL-2.0 `LICENSE` files are gone. This supersedes the 1.x digest line from DC41 (dropped). Code already released under MPL-2.0 (2.0.0 and earlier) stays available under it. The vendored encoders keep their own licences (`THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`). Not legal advice.
- **HW3 Release commit (owner, 2026-11-12).** `RELEASED_ENGINE_VERSION` is "3.0.0", so `bumped()` is false for every sub-type and `encode_versions` is strict from here on; `identity.json`, `encode_inputs.json` and `profile_hashes.json` are unchanged. CHANGELOG's `## 3.0.0 (Unreleased)` is `## 3.0.0`. Every built-in preset is `version = "3.0.0"`, with one entry in `docs/presets-changelog.md`: `sdc-base` gained `skin_detail` and the two compact presets halve it (the 2.1 work, which never got a preset version). The preset version is not part of `profile_sha256`, so the bump re-encodes nothing; the pin `compact_and_fast_preview_resolve_as_designed` expects "3.0.0". Screenshots were not re-rendered: since the last render (bafe4b86) the only GUI or render3d source change is GZ3.
- **HW4 OPEN: encoder byte-identity on hosted CI runners (owner, 2026-11-12: "release without it").** Four tests (`bc6h::shipped_levels_are_byte_identical_to_the_recorded_objects`, `level_identity::every_shipped_level_is_byte_identical_to_the_recorded_objects`, `encode::exact_opaque_off_still_fixes_rotated_blocks_and_nothing_else`, `encode_versions::encode_inputs_are_pinned_or_their_type_was_bumped`) failed on main da8b8445 and 885ab05a and passed on the same tree on other runners, with the same wrong BC7 and BC6H hashes both times. CI prints the runner's CPU since d69d1f11; the one run since passed on an AMD EPYC 7763. Unverified guess: the CPU vendor (the ISPC objects use `rcpps`; the hashes were recorded on an AMD CPU). 3.0.0 ships as it is; the fix is chosen once a failing run shows its CPU.

## Note IC — Envmap cube orientation (NIF team, 2026-11-11)

Source: Texalith's cubemap spike drew an envmapped ball whose top read the cube's −Z face, against DS13 (cube +Z = world up, measured in game). Probed with the installed CS shaders; no code change. Test `crates/render3d/tests/envmap.rs`.

- **IC1 render3d reads cube +Z as world up.** A black type 1 sphere (envmap scale 1, glossiness 1, white ambient, no direct light) with +Z red and −Z blue reads red on its upper half and blue on its lower half from the side, red at the centre from above and blue from below. render3d's world, camera and CS's envmap lookup all agree with DS13. Test `render_envmap_reads_cube_plus_z_as_up`.
- **IC2 The spike's flip was a byte-order slip.** `Format::Rgba8` UNORM is DXGI 87, B8G8R8A8 (Note S4), so `dds_bytes_cube` expects BGRA texels. The spike passed RGBA, which swaps red and blue, so a red +Z face showed as blue. Pack BGRA for `Format::Rgba8`; the test builds its cube that way and first checks that an all-red cube draws red. Packed RGBA, as in the spike, the all-red cube draws blue and the test fails.

## Note HX — texcore, the encoder-free base crate (main team, 2026-11-12)

- **HX1 BC1 at the NVIDIA model is decoded in Rust.** `texcore::decode::bc1_block_nvidia` (re-exported as `engine::decode::bc1_block_nvidia`) is a port of rgbcx's `unpack_bc1` (`cBC1NVidia`, with alpha), and `decode_level` uses it instead of the FFI. It is proven equal to `encoders_sys::bc1_decode_block(.., Bc1Model::Nvidia)` on all 2^32 endpoint pairs with all four selectors (`rust_bc1_nvidia_equals_rgbcx_on_all_endpoint_pairs`, `#[ignore]`, 12.5 s wall on this machine) and on the structured set plus one million random blocks that runs in every test run (`rust_bc1_nvidia_equals_rgbcx_on_structured_blocks`). The FFI `bc1_decode_block` stays in `encoders-sys` as the reference those tests compare with. FM17's decoder model is unchanged.
- **HX2 The crate `texcore` is the encoder-free base.** `crates/texcore` holds `image` (`Rgba32F`, `SourceInfo`, `luma`, `pot_dims`) and `transfer` (the sRGB pair, `GAME_GAMMA` and the game-gamma pair); it never depends on `engine` or `encoders-sys`, and its `load` feature (off by default, on in engine) gates the `image` and `bytemuck` crates. Engine keeps every old path by re-export: `pub use texcore::image;` in `lib.rs`, and `pub use` lines in `ops.rs` (the curves) and `resize.rs` (`pot_dims`), so `engine::image::…`, `engine::ops::srgb_to_linear` and `engine::resize::pot_dims` still resolve. The dev profile builds texcore at `opt-level = 3`, because these per-texel helpers were same-crate calls in engine and are `#[inline]` for the same reason.
- **HX3 The DDS container and the decoders live in texcore.** `texcore::dds` (the reader, writer, `DdsPixelFormat`, `write_atomic`, `same_file`, `absolute_or_same`) and `texcore::decode` moved from engine, and `engine::dds`, `engine::decode`, `engine::config::{Format, HeaderForm}` are re-exports. `Format` and `HeaderForm` are now plain enums in `texcore::dds` with the derives, `rename_all`, `bc6h_uf16` rename, variant order and doc-comment-free variants the `cfg_enum!` macro gave them; `schema/` regenerated with `sdc presets schema all` is byte-identical, and the `schemas`, `encode_versions` and `golden` tests pass unchanged.
- **HX4 Image loading and the PSD reader live in texcore behind `load`.** `texcore::load` (`load_image`, `analyse`, `decoded_header`, `dds_header_size`, `dds_header_facts` and the rest) and its private `psd` module moved from engine; `engine::load` is `pub use texcore::load;`, so `engine::load::…` still resolves. With `load` off, texcore builds without the `image` and `bytemuck` crates; engine turns the feature on and no longer names `bytemuck` itself.
- **HX5 The cube maths lives in texcore (amends DS1).** `texcore::cube` holds `FACES`, `cube_dir`, `Cube`, `dir_at`, `dir_to_face` (all hot ones `#[inline]`) and the modules `sample` (`build` is `pub`), `latlong`, `rgbe` and `world`; there is one copy, and engine and render3d both use it. `engine::cube` re-exports all of them, keeps `CubeJob`, `CubeSource` and the pipeline stages (`checks`, `filter`, `layout`, `marker`, `plan`, `run`), so `engine::cube::…` paths are unchanged.
- **HX6 `render3d` depends on texcore, not on the engine.** Its normal and build dependency closure holds `texcore` and neither `engine` nor `encoders-sys`, so other programs can build the 3D preview alone; its source uses `texcore::{dds, decode, image, transfer, cube}` and its tests also `texcore::load` (a dev-dependency with `load` on). `crates/render3d/tests/deps.rs` reads `cargo metadata` and fails if either crate returns, and the `render3d-alone` step (`cargo check -p render3d`) in `tools/gate.ps1` and CI builds it alone. A `nif/*` branch that added an `engine::` use in render3d must take the texcore path for it after merging main, or `tests/deps.rs` fails; if texcore lacks the item, move it into texcore.
- **HX7 What texcore may depend on, and who uses it.** texcore depends only on pure-Rust crates already in `Cargo.lock`, never on `engine`, `encoders-sys` or any C or C++ build. Programs outside this workspace (the owner's FaceGen Composer and Texalith) depend on `texcore` and `render3d` directly. An item such an outside program needs from `engine` moves into texcore with an engine re-export, and is not copied.
- **HX8 Engine code reads images only through texcore; what the split costs.** No engine source opens an `image` reader itself: `bake::decode_peak` sizes a decode with `texcore::load::decoded_header`. A release build shares no generic code between crates, so a second crate that opens a reader compiles every `image` decoder again; that made `sdc.exe` 600 KB larger until the call moved. Measured on 2026-11-12 (Ryzen 7 9800X3D, release, both from `cargo build --release -p sdc`; the shipped 3.0.0 exe is built with `-p sdc-gui -p sdc` and is not the baseline here): `sdc.exe` is 15,876,096 bytes against 15,852,032 for commit 1665ad28. Built the release way, `tessalith.exe` is 43,151,872 bytes against 43,123,200 and `sdc.exe` 16,274,432 against 16,250,368 (0.07% and 0.15% larger). 300 PNGs of `textures-10k` (278 converted) give 278 DDS files whose SHA-256 equal 3.0.0's. Five alternating runs took 49.5 to 50.7 s (median 50.1) against 49.8 to 51.7 s (median 50.9) for 3.0.0, so the speed is the same. `bc1_block_nvidia` takes 9.84 ns per block against 16.79 ns for rgbcx through the FFI (`bc1_nvidia_decode_timing`, `--release`), so the Rust decoder stays. A clean release build of `render3d` alone compiles 60 crates in 14 s, against 116 crates in 56 s on 3.0.0, where it pulled in `engine`, `encoders-sys` and `image`. To find where an exe grew, link with `-C link-arg=/MAP:<file>` and sum the code per object.

## Note HY — bcenc, rgbcx's block encoders in Rust (main team, 2026-11-13)

- **HY1 `bcenc` holds the Rust BC4-family search; the C++ is the reference (except for the `_max` search).** `crates/bcenc` has no dependencies, and `unsafe` only to call its four SSE2 kernels (HY9 and HY13 for BC1, HY11 for BC4, HY24 for the bound's table fill) and, holding a token only CPU detection creates, the AVX2 frames and kernels (HY21). `bcenc::bc4` is the BC4 search that lived in `encoders-sys/tests/support/bc4ref.rs` (moved; behaviour unchanged, branch counters added), and `bc4_encode_block`, `bc5_encode_block` and `bc4_encode_alpha` (BC3's alpha half, with the cut) wrap it. They are proven byte-equal to `encoders_sys` on generated block classes at radii 4, 5 and 10 (`crates/bcenc/tests/bc4.rs`) and, in the `#[ignore]` test, on 20.02 million blocks at radius 5 (57.9 s on 16 threads, 0 mismatches). The vendored C++ stays the specification (except for the `_max` search, which it does not have; Note LI2) and is never edited. A `hit!` counter marks each branch and compiles to nothing outside the crate's unit tests; `every_ported_branch_is_taken` fails if a generator stops reaching one.
- **HY2 The BC1 total-ordering tables are generated from the vendored text and tested against it; the dual palettes equal the two decoders.** `crates/bcenc/src/bc1/tables.rs` is written by `tools/gen-bcenc-tables.py` (`py -3.13 -I`, same bytes on every run) from `rgbcx.cpp` and `rgbcx_table4.h` (the large 969 x 128 table), under `#[rustfmt::skip]`. `tests/bc1_tables.rs` parses the vendored text again and compares every entry. `bc1::palette` ports `bc1_get_dual_palette`, `bc1_dual_err` and the four dual single-colour tables (built once in a `OnceLock`); the tests compare both palettes with `encoders_sys::bc1_decode_block` for NVIDIA and AMD in 4- and 3-colour order, and every single-colour entry with `dual_err`. No stored single-colour error reaches the C++ clamp at 255; the test fails if one does.
- **HY3 `bcenc::bc1::encode::encode` with `allow_3color = false` is rgbcx's level-18 four-colour BC1 encode, byte-equal to `encode_bc1(18, .., false, false)` in `cBC1Dual`.** Only what level 18 (two least-squares passes, full MSE, 128 likely total orderings twice, 6 power iterations, both initial-endpoint rounds, 256 endpoint-search rounds) reaches without 3-colour blocks, black handling or forced selectors is ported (`encode.rs`, `initial.rs`, `fit.rs`, `sels.rs`, each function naming the C++ it ports); `precise_round_565_noscale`, the 2D-LS and integer bounding-box initial arms and the non-dual selector searches are left in C++ only, and weights are a parameter (HY4). The tie rules are the C++'s: a texel's selector is the first minimum except that selector 3 wins a tie and the search stops at error 0, round 1's endpoints replace round 0's on an equal error, every other replacement needs a strictly lower error, and the selector search returns its partial total and partly written selectors once the total reaches the current best (callers take selectors only when the total is lower). The `m_hist[0] == 16` arm of `lookup_total_ordering_index` is ported because a review block reaches it, and a branch on the reachable path is deleted only with a proof from the code, never because tests do not reach it; the byte equality is proven on the block classes, 2^24 solid colours (which also proves the 4-colour single-colour tables against rgbcx) and 100 million generated blocks, with 0 mismatches (`crates/bcenc/tests/bc1.rs`).
- **HY4 `bcenc::bc1_encode_block(px, allow_3color, perceptual)` is rgbcx's level-18 BC1 encode in `cBC1Dual`, byte-equal to `encode_bc1(18, .., allow_3color, false)` with the shim's weights; it replaces `bc1_encode4_for_tests`, and `encode4` became `encode(px, allow_3color, weights)`.** With `allow_3color` it ports the 3-colour solid block, `try_3color_block` (started from the taken round's initial endpoints, with its own two least-squares passes and 32 likely orderings) and the 3-colour arms of the endpoint search and packing; the perceptual weights (54, 183, 19) are a value passed down and enter only `bc1_dual_err`, the one weighted sum on the dual path, while `try_3color_block_useblack`, `any_black_pixels` and every `use_black` arm stay C++ only because the call never sets `cEncodeBC1Use3ColorBlocksForBlackPixels`. The 3-colour selector search keeps rgbcx's strict `<` and early return, `hist3`'s selector 1 and 2 arms keep rgbcx's pairing with orderings 15 and 89 (a named block reaches the selector 1 arm), and byte equality is proven on 30 block classes in all four flag combinations, 2^24 solid colours in both modes and 100 million blocks, with 0 mismatches (`crates/bcenc/tests/bc1.rs`).
- **HY5 `bcenc::bc1_encode_block_alpha(px, transparent, perceptual)` is rgbcx's `encode_bc1_alpha(18, ..)` and `bcenc::bc3_encode_block(px, search_radius, cut, perceptual)` is `encode_bc3_hq` as the shim calls it, both byte-equal to rgbcx.** `bc1/alpha.rs` ports `encode_bc1_alpha` and `bc1_alpha_find_sels` whole: mask 0 is `bc1_encode_block(px, true, perceptual)`, mask 0xFFFF the all-transparent block, otherwise the opaque texels' statistics, principal axis (4 power iterations), extreme texels, up to 8 least-squares refits and up to 64 rounds of +-1 endpoint steps, then `bc1_encode3`, with masked texels on selector 3 and read nowhere; the selector-3 skip of the 3-colour least-squares fit is ported because this path reaches it, nothing on the path is left out, and the weights enter only through `bc1_dual_err` in the selector search (rgbcx.cpp 980, 1012). `bc3_encode_block` is `bc4_encode_alpha` of the alpha channel then `bc1_encode_block(px, false, perceptual)`, and `bc4_encode_alpha` reads a cut of 0 as 128, as `encode_bc4_hq` does. Named blocks reach every early path and both loop bounds (`gen::LONG_WALK` runs all 64 search rounds; in `gen::DEFAULT_AXIS_TIE` two colours tie on the default axis and the texel order picks the low endpoint, which needs a third opaque colour: two colours alone never tie there), and byte equality is proven on every mask class over the colour classes, BC3 at radii 4, 5, 10 and cuts 0, 1, 128, 201, 255, each cut from 1 to 254 against its neighbour on blocks where the cut changes the bytes, and 50 million blocks of each, with 0 mismatches (`crates/bcenc/tests/bc1_alpha.rs`, `tests/bc3.rs`).
- **HY6 The proof that `bcenc` equals rgbcx has three parts, each with a limit: class generators, branch counters and corpus tests gated on `SDC_BCENC_CORPUS`.** The generators (`crates/bcenc/tests/support/gen.rs`, BC1 colour classes in `bc1_classes.rs` beside it) compare tens of millions of constructed and random blocks per format with the C++, and the `hit!` counters in the unit tests fail when a branch of the port is never taken (they count per thread, so only the coverage test's own blocks count for it, and it builds the once-built tables itself); both prove only what their blocks reach: the `m_hist[0] == 16` arm of `lookup_total_ordering_index` (HY3) was missed by 100 million generated blocks and reached by one reasoned block. The `#[ignore]` tests in `crates/bcenc/tests/corpus.rs` compare every 4x4 block of every PNG under `SDC_BCENC_CORPUS` (`SDC_BCENC_CORPUS_EVERY=n` takes every n-th file) for BC1 (3-colour on, plain and perceptual), BC1 alpha (images with a texel below 128, mask = alpha below 128), BC3 (radius 5, cut 128), BC4 (channels 0 and 3, radius 5) and BC5 (radius 5), and cover only the images, radii and cut given.
- **HY7 The engine encodes BC1, BC3, BC4 and BC5 with `bcenc`; no output byte changes, so `versions()` and `encode_version` do not either.** `crates/engine/src/encode.rs` takes the five block functions from `bcenc`; rgbcx stays in `encoders-sys` as the reference the `bcenc` tests compare with, and its source hash stays in the manifest (`rgbcx_patch`) as the name of the algorithm the bytes follow. A change to `bcenc` that alters a byte is an engine change like any other: it sets the type's `encode_version` arm (Note DM), except the `_max` search (Note LI2, LI5). A format moves to `bcenc` only when its bytes equal rgbcx's and its median time is not above rgbcx's; all moved at once: on 1,048,576 blocks of 16 textures, one thread, five alternating runs, a block took 0.69 to 0.70 of rgbcx's time for BC1 (plain, perceptual, one-bit alpha) and BC3, and 0.81 to 0.82 for BC4 and BC5, with equal bytes. `bcenc::bc3_encode_block` takes the alpha cut as a `NonZeroU8` (owner, 2026-11-13): rgbcx's flags read a cut of 0 as 128, `encode::alpha_cut` gives 1..=255, and `encode::bc3_cut` maps a 0 to that same 128, so no input changes its bytes; `bc3_encode_block_raw_cut` keeps the flags' form for the tests that compare every value with rgbcx. End to end, `sdc convert` of a 300-PNG slice with the 3.0.0 build and with this one wrote the same bytes (SHA-256) for every DDS, all with mips, in 13 configurations: forced BC1 (251 files), BC1 perceptual (75), BC1 cutout with one-bit alpha plain (294) and perceptual (113), BC3 (278), BC3 perceptual (75), BC3 cutout at threshold 200 (294), BC4 (124), BC5 (124) and three presets; the forced BC1 and BC3 runs took about 28% less wall time, BC4 and BC5 about 16%.
- **HY8 BC1 scores each endpoint pair once per block, one set per block type; the bytes do not change.** `bc1/seen.rs` holds the pairs scored so far (a 1,024-slot table of 32-bit keys); `total_orderings`, the 3-colour ordering loop and `endpoint_search` skip a pair already in the block's set for their block type (a skipped search step still runs its stop test), while the round starts and the least-squares passes only record, because round 1's `round_err` starts fresh and a pair round 0 scored can be taken there. The error a trial must beat only falls and starts at most at the error of every pair scored before it, and a pair scored before was taken (error at least the present best) or rejected against an error at least the present best, so it is never strictly lower and a trial is taken only when strictly lower. On the 16 test textures 65.6% of all selector searches were repeats and none of 158 million skipped ones would have been taken; in the unit tests every skipped pair is scored anyway and asserted not below the error it had to beat (`shadow4`, `shadow3`).
- **HY9 BC1 scores all 16 texels of a candidate at once, in kernel units (twice rgbcx's); the bytes do not change.** `bc1/sels.rs` takes a `Texels` built once per block with the channel weights and the two decoder weights (`DUAL` = (1, 1), the only value `encode` passes; both at least 1 and summing to at most 4, the kernel's integer bound) and returns the full block error times their sum, writing the selectors only when that is below the cutoff; every caller only compares errors with each other, with 0 and with `u32::MAX` (a cutoff, never scaled), so a common factor and a full total in place of rgbcx's partial one decide the same, and the 3-colour ordering trial now passes `trial_err` as its cutoff because it reads the selectors only when the total is below it. The tie rule is the C++ loop's: the first minimum wins and in a 4-colour block selector 3 wins a tie unless the least error is 0. The kernel (`bc1/sels/sse2.rs`) is SSE2 integer intrinsics, one 16-bit product per channel and palette entry (`pmaddwd`), because the squares need a 32-bit multiply under the perceptual weights and plain loops were left scalar by the vectoriser; `unsafe` is only the call of a `#[target_feature(enable = "sse2")]` function from a module compiled only where the target has SSE2, and any other target runs `portable`, the scalar loops without a cutoff, which the unit tests run on every target against rgbcx's loops; in the unit tests every real call of the public searches also runs rgbcx's loop with the same cutoff and asserts the same answer, total and selectors (the `#[cfg(test)]` shadow check).
- **HY10 One-bit alpha scores the opaque texels on the same kernel; the bytes do not change.** `Texels::masked(px, w, dec, mask)` adds a `keep` row (all ones for an opaque texel, zero for a transparent one) and `find_sels3_masked` is the 3-colour search with no cutoff: a transparent texel adds no error and gets selector 3, an opaque one is scored as in `find_sels3`; `encode_alpha` builds one `Texels` per block and compares its kernel-unit errors only with each other and with 0. rgbcx's loop (`bc1_alpha_find_sels`) moved unchanged into `sels::scalar`, which the unit tests and the shadow check run against every real call, and the counters `AlphaSelTransparent` and `AlphaSelBetter` went with it; repeated endpoint pairs are not skipped on this path, because it can score 777 pairs, more than `Seen::MOST`, and how often one repeats has not been counted.
- **HY11 BC4 scores all 16 texels of an endpoint pair at once; the bytes do not change.** `bc4::pair` returns the block's error under a palette and the selectors, or `None` when the error is not below the best so far; `Best::try_pair` replaces itself only on `Some`. The kernel (`mod sse2` in `bc4.rs`) takes the least of the eight absolute differences per texel and sums their squares (`pmaddwd`), choosing the first nearest palette value by running the selector blend from the last value to the first; squaring keeps the order of differences and the scalar loop's early return fires exactly when its total reaches the cutoff, so the strict-`<` replace and the first-nearest tie rule are unchanged. `pair_scalar` (rgbcx's loop, minus the counters) is the test reference and what a target without SSE2 runs, and in the unit tests every real call also runs it and asserts the same result; the kernel's call is the crate's second use of `unsafe`, under HY9's rule.
- **HY12 BC4 searches each mode's candidates once; the bytes do not change.** `v0_modes` runs rgbcx's candidates per mode on a fresh best (`[0]` 8-value, `[1]` 6-value); `v0` takes `[1]` only when its error is strictly lower, so a tie keeps the 8-value block, and `refit_both` takes the other mode's best from the same pair instead of searching those candidates again, since both are the first pair to reach the mode's least error. `v0_reference` and `other_mode_reference` are the old loops, kept for the tests, and in the unit tests `search` runs the old loop for both modes on every block and asserts the same pair.
- **HY13 A BC1 total-ordering pass, 4-colour or 3-colour, fits all its orderings at once, on SSE2 vectors, float code included; the bytes do not change.** `bc1/order_keys.rs` returns the endpoint pair of each of a pass's orderings (at most 128 in a 4-colour pass, at most 32 in a 3-colour one; HY15 gives each level's counts) as its `Seen` key, and `total_orderings` scores them in the old order: a trial's pair depends only on the ordering, the pass's prefix sums and the block's totals and average, which are fixed before the pass starts. `try_3color_block`'s 32 orderings go the same way (`q10` is two prefix sums, `uq00` two times the total minus it). The kernel (`bc1/order_keys/sse2.rs`) holds R, G and B in one vector and is the crate's one float kernel (HY21 stamps it a second time for the AVX2 path). It gives the scalar code's bits because only integers are rearranged (in the 4-colour pass `q10` is the sum of three prefix sums and `uq00` is three times the total minus it, in the 3-colour pass two prefix sums and two times the total minus it, all below 2^24, so their conversion to `f32` is exact; a single-colour ordering is fitted too, with all-zero factors, and its result replaced by the single-colour pair), each float result is the same two products and one sum in the same order (`mulps` and `addps` are the scalar operations per lane, and Rust never fuses them), the truncation and the per-lane clamp equal `precise_round_565`'s, and the rounding step is still the compare with the `MIDPOINT` tables. `ls_endpoints4_ordered`, `ls_endpoints3_ordered` and `precise_round_565` stay as the reference and as what a target without SSE2 runs; in the unit tests every real call also runs them and asserts the same keys, and directed tests cover every rounding threshold with the floats beside it, out-of-range fits per channel, and every ordering on the largest sums. The call is the crate's third use of `unsafe`, under HY9's rule. Measured on the 16 test textures on a quiet machine (two passes of five runs, Rust time over C++ time): BC1 0.144 and 0.149 against 0.200 and 0.195 before, so a BC1 block takes about 0.74 of the time before.
- **HY14 A BC1 palette entry is computed, not read from a table.** Compile-time tables of the 5-bit and 6-bit interpolants (`PAL5`, `PAL6`) were built and measured: the bytes were the same and the quiet run showed no gain. Rust time over C++ time, pass 1 and pass 2, with the tables against without: BC1 0.146 and 0.148 against 0.144 and 0.149; perceptual BC1 0.155 and 0.149 against 0.143 and 0.146; 4-colour-only BC1 0.139 and 0.137 against 0.143 and 0.139 (faster with the tables). The tables were to be kept only if BC1 and 4-colour-only BC1 were both faster in both passes and no format was more than 2% slower; BC1 was not faster and perceptual BC1 was slower, so they were removed again. Do not add them back without a measurement that shows a gain.
- **HY15 BC1's search depth is a set of six numbers per rgbcx level; at levels 10, 12, 13, 15 and 18 `bcenc` is byte-equal to rgbcx.** `bc1/params.rs` holds power iterations (4 or 6), initial-endpoint rounds (1 or 2), ordering passes (1 or 2), 4-colour orderings (20, 32, 56, 128), 3-colour orderings (8 or 32) and endpoint-search rounds (0, 20, 32, 256), from rgbcx's `encode_bc1(level, ..)`; every other flag resolves the same at these levels and `cEncodeBC1UseFullMSEEval` has no effect under `cBC1Dual`. The level is a const generic, so each level is its own code and level 18 keeps its loops and speed. `Seen` skips at every level: HY8's argument needs only that the error to beat never rises, which one round, one pass or no search keep; the unit tests run the shadow checks at all five levels. `Seen::MOST` is the most over the five sets (518). Proven against rgbcx's level wrappers in every mode for BC1, opaque one-bit alpha and BC3 (`tests/levels.rs`), with the branches each level must and must not take. This amends the port spec's "the other levels stay C++ only": levels 10, 12, 13 and 15 are ported because a setting will let the engine reach one of them.
- **HY16 `Bc1Search::Fast` is rgbcx level 12, the fastest of 10, 12, 13 and 15 whose mean RGB PSNR still beats NVTT 3 `-highest` on both the NVIDIA and the AMD decoder (owner's rule).** On the 93 `rgb_real` images, scored by `crates/bcenc/examples/quality.rs` (mean of per-image dB), level 12 scores 39.41 / 39.65 dB against level 18's 39.51 / 39.74 and NVTT's 39.38 / 39.62 (paired delta against 18: -0.097 +/- 0.018 / -0.099 +/- 0.020 dB, lower on all 93 images); level 10 scores 39.37 / 39.61 dB and does not beat NVTT. The fast level takes about 0.36 of level 18's time per BC1 block and about 0.34 per BC3 block in the Rust encoder (quiet run, 2026-11-14). Method and table: `docs/research/encoder-benchmark.md`, "Fast BC1 search"; the `corpus_*_fast` tests compare the fast level with rgbcx on the real-image corpus.
- **HY17 `encoder.bc1_search` (`default` | `fast`) picks the BC1 search depth; `fast-preview` sets `fast`.** `default` is rgbcx level 18 with today's bytes; `fast` is HY16's level for BC1, BC3's colour half and the fully opaque blocks of one-bit-alpha BC1 (rgbcx's `encode_bc1_alpha` passes its level only there). Blocks with a transparent texel, BC4, BC5, BC7, BC6H and uncompressed formats ignore it without a warning, as BC1 ignores `bc45_search_radius`; in the GUI the Effort row writes it on BC1, and with the radius on BC3, only (JI4). Not serialised at `default`, so no profile hash, encode-input hash or `encode_version` arm moves; a project or preset that sets `fast` cannot be read by 3.0.0 (`deny_unknown_fields`). `fast-preview` profiles now carry it: their `profile_sha256` pins changed and nothing else did. This is the setting through which the engine reaches a level other than 18 (HY15's port of levels 10 to 15).
- **HY18 BC1 keeps decoder weights (1, 1): counting NVIDIA's error 2 or 3 times AMD's was measured and did not pass (plan D, 2026-11-15).** On the 93 `rgb_real` images against today's default (39.51 dB NVIDIA, 39.74 dB AMD), (2, 1) moved the means by +0.0113 / -0.0173 dB and (3, 1) by +0.0146 / -0.0266 dB; both fail "below the gain floor" (0.02 dB), though both pass the speed bar and budget. The rule, fixed before the run, is in `docs/research/encoder-benchmark.md`, section "BC1 decoder weights"; the per-image reading of "both lower is a fail", the half-of-the-gain "slight loss", the shrinking gap, the speed bar and the budget were pinned by the controller and confirmed by the owner on 2026-11-13, and only the 0.02 dB floor, the candidates and dropping endpoint refinement (+0.002 / +0.001 dB within 1 code, ruled out before the run) were the acting owner's. Three caveats (rgbcx's NVIDIA 3-colour model gap, worse one-bit alpha under both candidates, 2 images with both decoders falling in each perceptual row) are in that section's limits list and report-only table. The weight parameter and the measurement tool stay on branch `feat/bcenc-weights` (tag `bcenc-plan-d-measured`), unmerged: no shipped call passes weights other than (1, 1). HY19 and HY20 are unused.
- **HY21 `bcenc` compiles the whole encode of a block a second time for CPUs with AVX2 and picks the path per call; the bytes do not change.** `Path::best()` reads std's cached detection: `Avx2` when the CPU and OS have AVX2, else `Base`. The hot functions take a token first and are `#[inline(always)]`, so `#[target_feature(enable = "avx2")] fn encode_avx2` compiles them again; one dispatch per call. On the AVX2 path the selector searches run the 256-bit kernels of `bc1/sels/avx2.rs`. The SSE2 ordering kernels did not inline into the frame (each was a call at every level), so a `macro_rules!` stamps them a second time with `enable = "avx2"` (`keys4_avx2`, `keys3_avx2`) and `order_keys::keys4`/`keys3` pick the stamp by the token. Only `Avx2::require`, which checks the detection, creates the token; every hidden `_on` entry asserts its path is available. The set is exactly `avx2` (Rust 1.99 adds `avx`, `sse3`, `ssse3`, `sse4.1`, `sse4.2`; `+avx2` alone never enables `fma`). This lifts the spec's "no float code inside a `target_feature` function" (HY13: one kernel) for the AVX2 set, with the same bits: Rust gives each float operation one result whatever the instructions, never fuses a multiply and an add, never reorders; the hot path has no float `min`/`max` (`maximum` and `clampf` are compares and selects, defined for -0.0); no NaN arises; every differential test and shadow check runs on every path the machine has (`SDC_BCENC_PATHS` makes a skip fail). Calling a frame or AVX2 kernel while holding the token is the crate's second kind of `unsafe` (HY1). BC4's search (so BC5's two channels and BC3's alpha block) has an AVX2 frame too, `search_avx2`; HY22 has its kernel. Measured on the 16 test textures (controller's record t4, 2026-11-15, Ryzen 7 9800X3D, ratio of minima over 3 rounds, 3 of 3 clean), AVX2 `rust/cpp` over base `rust/cpp` of the same build, E1 and E2 together: `bc1` 0.898, `bc1p` 0.897, `bc1-4` 0.900, `bc1a` 0.884, `bc3` 0.913, `bc1-fast` 0.904, `bc3-fast` 0.959; `bc4` and `bc5` 1.00 (E3 came later).
- **HY22 Kept kernels of plan E; the bytes do not change.** Three candidates are kept. E1 is the AVX2 frame and the stamped ordering kernels (HY21), E2 the 256-bit selector kernels (`bc1/sels/avx2.rs`), E3 BC4's search in an AVX2 frame (`search_avx2`) with a `Batch` that scores two endpoint pairs per 256-bit vector (`avx2::pair2`) and a lone last pair through `try_pair`. BC1's kernels are exact because each lane runs the SSE2 kernel's integer operations and the sums wrap the same, and every float operation gives one result (HY21). E3 is exact by BC4's batch order: each pair gets `try_pair`'s verdict against the best of its moment, a strict `<` on the full total, in the order written (a pair whose bound is at or above that best is skipped before scoring, which is the verdict `try_pair` would give; HY24); no candidate of a loop is computed from that loop's best, so scoring the second pair before the first is decided changes neither the pairs nor their order, and each loop flushes before anything reads its best (the `bc4.rs` module doc says so). The Max search (Note LI2) runs in its own AVX2 frame, `search_max_avx2`, where its radius-10 search and its window's pairs go through the same `Batch` (no window pair depends on the best), with no speed figure of its own here (the Max figures are in HY24). Figures are ratios of minima over 3 rounds, each against its own reference: E1 and E2 together on the 9800X3D are in HY21 (AVX2 over base of the same build); E1 alone, AVX2 over base of the same build, is 0.905 to 0.960 on the BC1 rows (record t3, 1 of 3 clean rounds on avx2); E2 alone, against the AVX2 path before it, is 0.950 to 0.978 (record t4, 3 of 3 clean). E3 was judged on an i9-13900K host (a Proxmox KVM guest with 6 vCPUs pinned to P-cores, `speed.exe` under Wine 11.0), 3 of 3 clean rounds, after over before on the AVX2 path: `bc3` 0.988, `bc4` 0.901, `bc5` 0.904, `bc3-fast` 0.965, the BC1 rows 0.995 to 1.001, the base path 0.952 to 1.003 on every row. On the 9800X3D E3 has one clean round of three, so its figures are indicative only: `bc4` 0.975, `bc5` 0.976, `bc3` 0.990. So the BC4 and BC5 gain is about 10% on that Intel CPU and about 2 to 3% on the Zen 5 desktop. The shipped head (204fe8d3) on the VM, AVX2 over base: `bc1` 0.897, `bc1p` 0.896, `bc1-4` 0.896, `bc1a` 0.882, `bc3` 0.911, `bc4` 0.945, `bc5` 0.947, `bc1-fast` 0.896, `bc3-fast` 0.933; every row `same_bytes yes`. The full tables are in docs/research/encoder-benchmark.md. This replaces the spec's plan-B line "There is no AVX2 clone". `Path::best()` takes AVX2 on every CPU that has it, with no CPU-family gate. `bcenc` has no AVX-512 path (E6, HY23). CPUs with split 256-bit units (Zen 1) are not measured.
- **HY23 Measured for plan E and not shipped.** E5 (BC4 palettes from a 4 KB table indexed by endpoint difference; 9800X3D, clean round 3): `bc4` `rust/cpp` went from 0.063-0.064 to 0.067-0.070 (+6 to 10%) and `bc3-fast` from 0.225-0.232 to 0.242-0.253 (+5 to 8%); it missed the rule that a targeted row gain at least 3% and the rule that no row lose more than 2%, because the lookup is slower than computing the palette. E4 (BC1 ordering fits two at a time in 256-bit vectors on the AVX2 path; 9800X3D, 3 of 3 clean rounds), after over before on AVX2: `bc1` 0.989, `bc1p` 0.988, `bc1-4` 0.983, `bc1a` 0.986, `bc3` 0.985, `bc1-fast` 0.991, `bc3-fast` 1.001; it missed the 3% gain, no targeted row reaching 0.97 (a steady 1 to 1.7%). E6 (an AVX-512 path with a frame and a BC4 kernel scoring four pairs per vector) was dropped on the owner's word on 2026-11-22 with no quiet record: the only reading came from a busy machine, which is not a figure, and had BC4 slower on AVX-512. A `Bound` whose four tables are not zeroed (`MaybeUninit`, only the window written and read, which would be a third kind of `unsafe` under HY1) was measured on the Linux test VM on 2026-11-23 against the head before it (`9a2f70fc`; the prototype was never committed) (`speed.exe --no-ref`, 5 clean rounds, ratio of minima): `bc3` 1.000, `bc4` 1.002 and `bc5` 0.999 on base, 1.000, 0.995 and 0.994 on avx2, with the untouched `bc1-fast` at 1.003 and 0.999, all inside the drift, so zeroing is not the bound's cost and the tables stay zeroed. E5 and E4 were measured on the Ryzen 7 9800X3D only.
- **HY24 The BC4 pair search skips pairs by a lower bound; the bytes do not change.** `Batch::push` (`bc4.rs`) does not score an endpoint pair whose lower bound is at or above the error of the `Best` it would be tried against, as that `Best` stands when `push` is called, or when that `Best` has error 0; `search_on` and `search_max_on` build one `Bound` per block on the stack, except that a solid block (all 16 values equal) shares the static `bound::EMPTY`, which never prunes on its own (`bc4/bound.rs`; nothing is kept between blocks; measured on the Linux test VM on 2026-11-23 against `c10c5693` (2 clean rounds, ratio of minima): every `speed.exe` row inside 0.98 to 1.02, so no speed claim). The bound counts the texels outside the pair's endpoint range, `A[e1] + B[e0]` for the 8-value palette and `C[e0] + D[e1]` for the 6-value one, from four 256-entry tables that the SSE2 fill (the fourth SSE2 kernel; a scalar twin elsewhere) computes over the block's range widened by the pad. A pair's error is at least its bound, so `try_pair`'s strict `<` would have refused it: no verdict changes, so no output byte does (on `Avx2` a waiting pair may still lower the best, and a stale best only prunes less). Tests: `the_bound_never_exceeds_the_error` and `the_bound_is_zero_outside_its_window` (`bound.rs`); `a_perfect_block_skips_the_pair_search`, `the_window_skips_and_scores_the_rest`, `a_pair_whose_bound_is_below_the_best_is_tried`, `a_pair_whose_bound_equals_the_best_is_skipped` and `a_skip_keeps_the_pending_pair` (`bc4.rs`); and `crates/bcenc/tests/bc4.rs` against `encoders_sys` on both paths. Measured on the Linux test VM (i9-13900K host, 6 pinned vCPUs, `speed.exe` and `max_speed` under Wine 11.0, 3 clean rounds, recorded 2026-11-22 to 2026-11-23), after over before (`fca6cb41` and `f78c9a6b`), every `speed.exe` row `same_bytes yes`: at the default radius 5 `bc3` 0.948 base and 0.942 avx2, `bc3-fast` 0.842 and 0.829, `bc4` 0.981 and `bc5` 0.973 on avx2, but `bc4` 1.011 and `bc5` 1.006 on base, where building the bound costs about what it saves; the bound therefore stays at every radius (no row above 1.02). Radius 4 was not measured. Max on `Path::best()`: BC4 and BC5 Max take 0.388 and 0.384 of their old time, BC3 Max 0.831, Slowest 0.718, 0.701 and 0.923. The figures and the Max/Slowest ratios are in LI6 and docs/research/bc4-family.md#max-near-exhaustive.

## Note HZ — AVX-512 ISPC target for BC7 and BC6H (main team, 2026-11-13)

- **HZ1 The ISPC objects have an AVX-512 target, `avx512skx-x16`, and its two objects are built with ISPC 1.32.0.** Every other object and both headers are ISPC 1.31.0 builds, and `versions().ispc` stays `"1.31.0"`. ISPC 1.31.0 has a known AVX-512 miscompile (ISPC issue 3891); both compilers' AVX-512 objects wrote the same bytes in the measurement, and the fixed compiler's are shipped. `tools/regen-ispc.ps1` takes the second compiler as `-Ispc512`. ISPC's dispatcher picks the target on a CPU with AVX-512 (seen on a Ryzen 7 9800X3D, which has F, DQ, CD, BW and VL; `IspcTarget::Avx512.supported()` asks for those five). On that CPU it takes 0.61 to 0.81 of the AVX2 time: [docs/research/avx512-ispc.md](../docs/research/avx512-ispc.md). `avx512skx-x8` and `avx512icl-x16` write the same bytes and are not faster, so they are not built.
- **HZ2 BC7 and BC6H bytes depend on whether the CPU has AVX-512 (owner, 2026-11-13: "yes that is fine, pinning tests to avx2 is the smart thing to do").** This extends CB3. Quality is equal (PSNR within 0.002 dB). A manifest record names the path in `isa` (`"avx512"`). `isa` is not part of the up-to-date check, so a project moved between CPUs is not re-encoded and one folder may hold both paths' bytes. No `encode_version` arm moves: bytes on every CPU without AVX-512 are unchanged, and a moved arm would mark every BC7 and BC6H output on every machine as written by an older encode version (Note JA). This is an exception to DM1.
- **HZ3 Test builds encode on the AVX2 path at most.** The `test-hooks` feature of `encoders-sys` (forwarded by the `test-hooks` features of `engine` and `sdc-gui`; `sdc` reaches it through `engine`) caps `encoders_sys::ispc_target()` at AVX2; `SDC_TEST_AVX512=1` lifts the cap. A release build of `sdc` or `tessalith` has neither the feature nor the variable. A `cargo test --release` or a `cargo build --release` with `--tests`, `--examples` or `--all-targets` does turn the feature on and leaves a capped `sdc.exe` and `tessalith.exe` in the release folder, so `tools/package-release.ps1 -NoBuild` after one would ship a capped build. Two CLI tests (`sdc_encodes_on_avx2_unless_the_cap_is_lifted`, `a_rerun_on_the_other_path_keeps_every_output` in `crates/cli/tests/cli_convert.rs`) deliberately spawn a lifted `sdc convert`, so on an AVX-512 machine a default `cargo test` does encode that fixture on the AVX-512 path. The AVX-512 pins (`level_identity`'s AVX-512 row, `RECORDED_AVX512` in `tests/bc6h.rs`) are checked only with the cap lifted; `tools/gate.ps1` does that in its `avx512` step. `identity.json` never holds an `@avx512` field (FM1).
- **HZ5 HW4 is still open, and one of its four tests now checks more.** `bc6h::shipped_levels_are_byte_identical_to_the_recorded_objects` checked the AVX2 path only and skipped on a CPU without AVX2. It now also checks the forced SSE2 path against five hashes recorded on an AMD Zen 5 CPU, and a CPU without AVX2 checks its own path. So if HW4's cause is the CPU vendor, this test can fail there in more rows. `level_identity` already checks its forced rows this way; it gains the AVX-512 row, read only with the cap lifted. The two engine tests encode on the same path as before on every CPU (the cap keeps them on the AVX2 path on a CPU with AVX-512). The new plain-call tests read no pin. The AVX-512 pins were recorded on an AMD Zen 5 CPU only; whether an Intel AVX-512 CPU writes the same bytes is not known.
- **HZ6 `engine::manifest::dispatched_isa()` returns `encoders_sys::ispc_target().name()`**, so the name in a record and the path that encoded it come from one place.
- **HZ7 Two BC7 probe tests return early on the AVX-512 path.** `encoders-sys` `tests/bc7_fixup.rs` `bc7e_opaque_noise_rotated_blocks_fixed_losslessly` and `engine` `tests/encode.rs` `exact_opaque_off_still_fixes_rotated_blocks_and_nothing_else` look for blocks that bc7e writes from their noise input on the AVX2 path and does not write on the AVX-512 path, so they return when `ispc_target()` is `Avx512`. No guarantee broke: the fix is path-independent Rust, and on the probe inputs the AVX-512 path emits no block that needs it (the raw bc7e output decoded alpha 255 with 0 blocks needing the rotated-slot fix). The engine-equals-raw-plus-fix check was confirmed once by hand on that path; the two neighbour tests `exact_opaque_covers_rotated_mode4_and_mode5_on_opaque_noise` and `remove_guarantee_holds_on_task41_noise_with_exact_opaque_off` pass there, but their inputs may give the AVX-512 path no rotated block either.

## Note JA — if-changed keeps outputs of an older encode version or a lower target; --ev re-encodes them (main team, 2026-11-14)

Owner, 2026-11-13: "if-changed needs an extra argument --ev (encoder version) to rencode an image if only our encoder changed, by default it only re encodes if the image itself changed. A different encoder type, a different weighting, or a higher quality target (fast > max) still rencodes, a lower quality target does not". Code: `api::currency`, `engine::ev_scope`. Narrows the DM6/DM7 digest line, FM17, FM19, FM21, FM42, FM43, HR6, HW1 and HZ2 (edited in place; their old wording is in the archive).
- **JA1 The rule.** Under `--on-existing if-changed` (`api::currency`) an existing output with a manifest record is encoded again when its source, recorded sources, pack roles or output file changed, its validation failed (a record not validated, `validation.skipped`, has not failed; a validating run checks it in place, LG10), a settings field other than a quality target changed, or a quality target rose. It is kept, its record untouched, when only its encode version differs and `--ev` does not cover it (JA2), or a quality target fell (JA5); a check in place never touches such an output (LG10, LG11). Otherwise it is current, and a validating run may append its record again with only `validation` and `ts` changed (LG10). `isa` is not compared; the engine version is read only by LI4, for a record whose radius is `max`.
- **JA2 What `--ev` compares.** `--ev` is named after `engine::manifest::encode_version`. A record's encode version is its `encode_version`, its encoder tags (`bc7enc_rdo`, `ispctc`, `ispc` and `bc7e_patch` on every format, `rgbcx_patch` on BC1, BC3, BC4 and BC5; a missing patch tag counts) and, on BC1 and BC3, `bc1_model` compared with `BC1_ENCODE_ID` (the constant, never the literal `dual`, so a later change of it marks the older outputs). An `encode_version` arm records any change to a type's bytes or verdict, an encoder change or a pipeline fix, so the outputs of a pipeline fix are kept too without `--ev`; every run that keeps some says how many and how to re-encode them (JA6). No `bcenc` build id is added: a `bcenc` change that alters bytes moves an arm or `BC1_ENCODE_ID` (HY7, Note DM), and a same-bytes change needs no mark. User text says "written by an older encode version", never "only the encoder changed".
- **JA3 Readings of the request (acting owner).** "Our encoder" is JA2's encode version: the vendored encoders' tags and `bc1_model` count with `encode_version`. "A different encoder type" is a different format or encoder choice in the profile, and "a different weighting" is `encoder.perceptual` or `encoder.bc7_weights`; both re-encode through JA5's profile check.
- **JA4 Not comparable.** A record without encoders or a preset, of a newer schema, or with a missing or empty `encode_version` (1.x, FM9) is stale with or without `--ev`.
- **JA5 Quality targets: `encoder.effort`, `encoder.bc45_search_radius`, `encoder.bc1_search`.** When the profile hashes differ, the record's own profile (`raw["profile"]`, which must hash to the record's `profile_sha256`) with the job's three targets copied in must hash as the job's profile, else the output is stale (format, weighting, size, alpha, mips or any field added later). Then per field, for the job's format: effort on BC7 by bc7e level (`ultrafast` to `max`) and on BC6H by ISPC level in the order `veryfast`, `fast`, `basic`, `slow`, `veryslow`, peak (no effort reaches `slow` today) (`slowest` and `max` are one level); the radius on BC3, BC4 and BC5 by its rank (`fast` < `default` < `slowest` < `max`, not the declared order and not the radius, since `slowest` and `max` both start at 10; LI4); `bc1_search` on BC1 and BC3 (`fast` < `default`). A field the format does not read never differs. Any rise is stale; else any fall keeps. `bc7_weights` unset against `linear` re-encodes: equal bytes, different settings.
- **JA6 Reporting.** A kept output counts in `BatchSummary::up_to_date` and in `kept_older_encode` or `kept_lower_target` (both when both apply), and its event is `FileSkipped { kind: UpToDate }` with the reason "kept: written by an older encode version (--ev re-encodes it)" (under `--property`: "(outside the --property scope)") or "kept: encoded at a higher quality target" (joined by "; " when both apply). Before `batch_done` the run emits at most two run-level Info events, one line per run however many files: `OUT-KEPT-OLDER-ENCODE` (data `count`, `by_format`, `by_subtype`, the maps in id order since serde_json keeps no insertion order; the message lists the per-format counts in `Format` order and names `--ev`, or under `--property` says the outputs are outside its scope) and `OUT-KEPT-LOWER-TARGET` (data `count`; the message names `--on-existing overwrite`). The exit code does not move. Kept outputs stay `SkipKind::UpToDate`, so a JSON consumer tells kept from current by those events and the summary counts (accepted by the acting owner). The GUI shows the counts in its export summary in its own words (no `--ev`). The Also export's Vanilla and CM phases drop run-level warnings (`NoBatchWarnings`), so their kept counts reach only the summary.
- **JA7 Where `ev` applies.** `BatchRequest.ev` is read only under `IfChanged`: the engine ignores it under `Skip`, `Overwrite` and `Reencode`, and only the CLI refuses `--ev` with another mode. A PBR twin is copied into the Vanilla folder only when it is current (TA25): a kept twin is not copied, and the Vanilla job then takes the run's verdict on its own record, which is stale when that record is a copy (it names the twin's profile, Note CN33), so the Vanilla output is encoded.
- **JA8 `--property` (owner, 2026-11-13: "could we bundle a subflag to --ev --property and recommend in changelog for such pipeline cases, rather than build a second id system"; then "I meant more specific to the bc family that changed actually changed, for example our fix targetted alpha mipmaps").** `--ev --property <w>[,<w>...]` (repeatable) limits `--ev` to the outputs a fix touched: within one value every word must hold (AND), across values any value may hold (OR); older outputs outside the scope are kept and counted. The words (`ev_scope::Property`), decided per output from the job's profile: a format id as the config spells it (`bc1` ... `rgba16f`); `alpha` (the policy is not `remove` and the format stores alpha, on BC1 only with one-bit alpha); `cutout` (`alpha`, and the policy is `cutout` or `cutout_blend`, or `blend` on one-bit-alpha BC1); `mips` (the record's `validation.levels`, the written file's header mip count, above 1; a record without it is in scope; the profile's `mips.levels` is never read); a texture type id (`Subtype::from_id`, the parser of `--set subtype=`). Words are trimmed and ASCII case-insensitive; an empty or unknown word is an error, and so is a list holding two formats or two texture types ("a comma list must all hold; repeat --property for alternatives"). No texture type id equals another word (`no_property_word_is_ambiguous`); a future clash is spelled `type:<id>` for that type only.
- **JA9 `EvScope` is canonical.** `Off`, `All` or `Matching(PropertyGroups)`, built only through `PropertyGroup::new` and `EvScope::matching` (both types keep their list private; deserialisation goes through them too), which reject empty lists and sort and deduplicate, because `BatchRequest::sig_without_files` hashes the `Debug` form: equal scopes hash equal.
- **JA10 The release rule.** A CHANGELOG entry for a change that moves an `encode_version` arm, `BC1_ENCODE_ID` or an encoder tag ends with the command that picks it up, scoped to what the change touched (`sdc convert … --ev --property cutout,mips` for an alpha-mip fix, `--ev --property bc1 --property bc3` for a BC1 encoder change), and says those outputs are otherwise kept. It replaces a second id system; its home is `docs/DOCUMENTATION.md` §6.
- **JA11 This plan's additions beyond the request.** `OnExisting::Reencode` (serde `reencode`) encodes every output that has a record again and keeps one without (`OUT-UNKNOWN-EXISTING`); the Also export treats it as `IfChanged` (the twin was just encoded, so its copy is redone). It backs the GUI's one-shot "Re-encode every output" item (see JA12 for its location), which does not use `Overwrite` because that replaces files Tessalith did not write. The CLI has no value for it; `--encoder-version` is a visible alias of `--ev`.
- **JA12 Re-encode every output is a one-shot item in Tools ▾ (JY12).** With a project open, ticking it keeps the menu open. It starts off and is never saved. Starting an export with it ticked clears it, including Export set and Export group. A tick during a running export applies to the next one.

## Note JB — Three ISPC targets: SSE2, AVX2, AVX-512 (owner, 2026-11-13)

- **JB1 The bc7e and ISPC TC objects hold three ISPC targets: SSE2, AVX2 and AVX-512 (owner, 2026-11-13: "dropping the base avx and the sse4 objects would be fine, sse2 or 4 left for old processors"; after Gate A measured the SSE2 path at 1.50 to 1.76 times the AVX path, owner, 2026-11-14: "Drop both as planned").** The four SSE4 and AVX objects (1.5 MB) are gone. SSE2 runs on every x86-64 CPU, so a CPU without AVX2 runs the SSE2 path; AVX2 and AVX-512 CPUs are unaffected. Measured on a Ryzen 7 9800X3D with forced paths: the SSE2 path takes 1.76 times the time of the AVX path for BC7 and 1.50 times for BC6H (a CPU with AVX but no AVX2), and 1.29 and 1.09 times the time of the SSE4 path (a CPU with SSE4.2 but no AVX): [docs/research/avx512-ispc.md](../docs/research/avx512-ispc.md#without-avx2-the-sse2-path). No old CPU was measured.
- **JB2 No CPU's bytes change.** The SSE2, SSE4 and AVX paths wrote the same BC7 and BC6H bytes (`level_identity` rows, `RECORDED_PRE_AVX2`, and the 16 test textures in four configurations), so a CPU that ran SSE4 or AVX writes the same bytes on SSE2. `tools/regen-ispc.ps1` printed MATCH for the six kept per-target objects before the update; only the two dispatcher objects changed; the headers did not (in that update; JB5's trim then changed the four kernel objects and `kernel_ispc.h`, which now declares only the BC6H export). No `encode_version` arm moves and the version is not bumped (as HZ2).
- **JB3 `IspcTarget` keeps its numbers (`Sse2 = 0`, `Avx2 = 3`, `Avx512 = 4`; 1 and 2 unused),** because the shim's switches take them and its `default:` dispatches on any other; `tests/target.rs` pins them. Records that say `"isa":"sse4"` or `"avx"` still load and are not re-encoded (`isa` is never compared, HZ2); a CPU without AVX2 now records `"sse2"`.
- **JB4 The forced SSE4 and AVX test cases are gone; the pre-AVX2 pin stays, on forced SSE2** (`level_identity`'s SSE2 row, `RECORDED_PRE_AVX2`). Nothing has run on a CPU without AVX2.
- **JB5 Trimming the ISPC TC kernel (owner, 2026-11-13: "add trim to plan f").** The ISPC TC kernel objects hold only the BC6H encoder (ISPC TC patch 0003, `-DSDC_BC6H_ONLY`): the four objects went from 923 to 397 KB, `sdc.exe` from 15359 to 14896 KB and `tessalith.exe` from 41560 to 41097 KB. BC6H bytes are unchanged on SSE2, AVX2 and AVX-512: the five-level and peak-mode pins on one synthetic 64x64 HDR image, and 18 Poly Haven HDR files at all six levels. The file's own `clamp` must stay unguarded: guarded, ISPC's standard-library `clamp` takes its place, the build passes and the synthetic pins still pass; only the regeneration check (10 MATCH) and the real-file hashes catch it. The keep rule was bytes: the trim stays only if every BC6H pin and every real-file hash is unchanged on SSE2, AVX2 and AVX-512; a build that failed after two rounds of guard corrections would also have dropped it. The patch is number 0003 because 0003 is the next number not used anywhere under `vendor/` (bc7e has 0001 and 0002, ISPC TC has 0001). Merging bc7e into the kernel was not done: a few KB at most, BC7 bytes at risk under `--opt=fast-math`, different ISPC flags, upstream tracking lost.
- **JB6 The BC6H peak mode has a hash pin per ISPC path** (`tests/bc6h.rs`, `RECORDED_PEAK`: SSE2, AVX2, and AVX-512 only with the test cap lifted), recorded before the BC6H-only trim was tried; `the_peak_pin_is_not_the_veryslow_pin` checks the test image engages the peak mode.

## Note JC — Byte pins skip on Intel CPUs (owner, 2026-11-14)

- **JC1 An Intel CPU encoded other BC7 and BC6H bytes than the AMD CPU the pins were recorded on, on the same ISPC path.** This extends HZ2: BC7 and BC6H bytes depend on the CPU vendor too. The evidence is one Intel model on one path: GitHub CI run 43681871945 on `main` 2109c952 ran on an Intel Xeon Platinum 8573C (AVX2 path, the test build's cap) and failed five tests, all BC7 or BC6H byte checks; runners with an AMD EPYC 7763 passed the same tree before (run 43213197055 on d69d1f11). The SSE2 and AVX-512 paths on Intel were not measured. BC1, BC3, BC4, BC5 (`bcenc`, integer code) and uncompressed outputs are the same on both. The likely cause is `rcpps` in the vendored ISPC objects, whose results differ between Intel and AMD; that is inferred, not verified. The manifest's `isa` does not name the vendor and the up-to-date check ignores it (HZ2), so moving a project between an Intel and an AMD CPU re-encodes nothing.
- **JC2 On an Intel CPU the checks of BC7 or BC6H bytes against recorded AMD bytes skip (owner, 2026-11-14: "skip byte parity tests when we encounter intel").** `encoders_sys::intel_cpu()` (feature `test-hooks`) reads the CPUID vendor; `SDC_TEST_AS_INTEL=1` makes it true on any CPU, to test that path. `skip_byte_pins()` is the same check and prints the reason. Skipped whole: `encoders-sys` `bc6h::{shipped_levels_are_byte_identical_to_the_recorded_objects, the_peak_mode_is_byte_identical_to_the_recorded_objects}` and `level_identity::every_shipped_level_is_byte_identical_to_the_recorded_objects`, so `SDC_RECORD_LEVEL_HASHES=1` records nothing on an Intel CPU. Partly: engine `encode::exact_opaque_off_still_fixes_rotated_blocks_and_nothing_else` drops its probe (a broken rotated block in AMD bytes) and still checks that only rotated blocks change; engine `encode_versions::encode_inputs_are_pinned_or_their_type_was_bumped` drops the `payload_sha256@<isa>` fields before comparing, so `identity.json` keeps the recorded ones and every other field is compared, and a by-ISA hash with no recorded value fails ("record it on an AMD CPU"). The pins stay AMD's; per-vendor hashes or objects without `rcpps` were not chosen.

## Note JE — Advanced Skin re-types untouched recorded members (owner, 2026-11-15)

Hostile UI review 2026-11-15, finding 1: loose skin maps (`body_albedo`, `_normal`, `_roughness`, `_ao`, `_f`, `_sk`, `_wet`) open as a PBR set, and FC1 records `pbr_albedo`, `pbr_normal` and `pbr_fuzz`. Choosing Advanced Skin then changed almost nothing: the albedo stayed `pbr_albedo`, so the set kept its PGPatcher JSON and wrote no `.csskin.json` (HB1), and the `_f` stayed a texture of its own. Owner: "ok to both" to the proposal below.

- **JE1 An untouched recorded diffuse is switched (amends DU6, DU9).** Choosing Advanced Skin makes the set's own diffuse `skin_albedo` also when its sub-type is an untouched recorded one (FC1 R5: equal to what auto-accept picks, as `pbr_albedo` is in a set that opened as PBR). `skin_follow` (`model/sets.rs`) counts it as inferred. `auto_skin` keeps the recorded value, so leaving Advanced Skin writes it back (DU7), and DQ1 writes `packing.recipe = "rfaos"`, so `_rmaos` becomes `_rfaos`. DV3 is unchanged: the engine still keeps any written sub-type other than plain `diffuse`, and the GUI writes the new one. Test `auto_accept::advanced_skin_re_types_an_untouched_recorded_pbr_albedo`.
- **JE2 An untouched recorded `pbr_fuzz` is cleared.** In the same step `untouched_for_shader` clears it, so the `_f` packs into the `_rfaos` G channel as the skin fuzz mask (DR1, DR2) and is not exported alone; `sdc convert` on the saved text plans the same. A `pbr_fuzz` that an explicit PBR-family pick wrote on a `_fuzz`/`_sheen` map (Note JQ9) is cleared by that pick's leave step, so under Advanced Skin the fuzz map is the skin mask again (JQ54). Leaving Advanced Skin: the next settled plan records `pbr_fuzz` again (FC1). A pinned diffuse keeps the set from turning skin (DW1), so then nothing is cleared. shortcut: a set with a `_fuzz` or `_sheen` map keeps its `_f` as `pbr_fuzz` (DR7), so there the clear is followed by a recording step of its own; check the set's raw maps in `untouched_for_shader` if that shows up. Test `auto_accept::advanced_skin_packs_an_untouched_recorded_fuzz_into_rfaos`.
- **JE3 What stays.** `pbr_normal` stays: the engine suggests it in a skin set too. A value that differs from the auto pick, and any value under accept `None`, is the user's and keeps its type (R5, DU9; test `auto_accept::advanced_skin_keeps_hand_types_and_a_pinned_diffuse`). As everywhere under R5, a value the user picked by hand that equals the auto pick cannot be told from a recorded one and is switched (owner, 2026-11-15: accepted). A `_wet` is never recorded while it waits alone (GY2); once JE1's written `rfaos` recipe makes the set Advanced skin, the `_wet` joins it and is recorded `skin_wet` (`join_skin_wet`).
- **JE4 `_wet` and `_sk` keep their skin types after leaving (owner, 2026-11-15: "they could be shared").** Leaving Advanced Skin takes the `_wet` out of the set again, but its recorded `skin_wet` stays, so it becomes a lone set that exports a skin wet map another Advanced skin set can share (HH, HB2). The `_sk` keeps its recorded `skin_sk` in the now PBR set the same way. Leaving clears neither.
- **JE5 No engine change.** No `encode_version` arm moves; `golden` and `encode_versions` are untouched.

## Note JD — Assign on the skin wet and detail slots (owner, 2026-11-15)

The hostile UI review of 2026-11-15 (finding 6) found that "Assign…" on an Advanced skin set's empty Skin wet or Skin detail slot (9, 10) offered only the pin, so a `_wet` left in a set of its own (HG3, the DV7 limit) could reach the set only by a drag. The owner chose the entries on 2026-11-15 ("go" on the proposal). `AppModel::slot_menu` builds them in `skin_slot_entries` for an empty slot 9 or 10 of a set that is Advanced skin or shared; a filled or borrowed one keeps Clear, Show and the pin. Tests in `crates/gui/tests/wet_assign.rs`.
- **JD1 Files from the set's folder move in.** Each file in the set's own folder (a directory one of its files is in, or its id's, since a renamed set's id may name another; compared as set ids are, Note BF) whose type is the slot's (`skin_wet`, `skin_detail`), or which waits for a type (GY5) and whose stem ends in `_wet` or `_detail_n`, and which is in no set or in a set of its own the user has not touched (`is_real_set`, the test a drag uses before it asks), is listed by name; an undecided one reads "(undecided: make it Skin wet _wet)". The pick (`MenuAction::SlotMove`, `move_into_slot`) moves it in as a drop would, and types an undecided one for the slot first, as one undo step. GY2 is unchanged: the file still gets no suggestion and waits alone until the user picks it here; an undecided one is off while `sdc.toml` is read-only.
- **JD2 Other sets' maps are borrowed.** "From another set" lists every other set's file of the slot's type, those JD1 lists too (a lone `_wet` set may be one other skin sets share); the pick (`MenuAction::SlotBorrow`, `borrow_into_slot`) is Share (`borrow.slot9`/`slot10`, HB2), never a slot command, which slots 9 and 10 do not have. Off with "no other set has a …" when none.
