- **JD3 Left out (agent ruling).** No "Path…" (it writes `slotN`) and no "Browse…" (a file outside the open folder is not an item). Files in other folders come only through JD2. Known limit: beside a set that is Advanced skin only by its chosen shader, `x_detail_n` is typed `normal` in a set of its own (`_detail_n` is a suffix only in a skin set by its maps, HB4, HG3), so it is neither a `skin_detail` nor waiting and is not listed; the user types it Skin detail first, then it is listed. Widening JD1 to retype a decided normal is left to the owner.

## Note JF — Review's file count and the Acknowledge offer (owner, 2026-11-15)

From the UI review of 2026-11-15 (commit b724800b): four small defects of the Review panel and the Problems tab.

- **JF1 With an Also tick, the count is the PBR folder's and the other folders are named without a number (owner, 2026-11-15: "1a").** An export with "Also export as Vanilla" said "24 DDS files" and "Export 24 files to 2 folders" and wrote 45 (24 in the PBR folder, 21 in the Vanilla folder). The Vanilla and CM file counts exist only after the engine plans those folders (`engine::also::run_phases`), and that plan assembles every PBR set's derived maps, so Review cannot know them before the export without planning twice more on every change (option B, not chosen). The texts are now: Review's button `Export {n} files + Vanilla folder` (`+ Vanilla and CM folders` with the CM tick, which writes Vanilla too, TA30); the footer's `Will be written` group and currency summary (JY8) keep the folder wording `in the PBR folder, plus the Vanilla folder`. While a currency check is due or pending, the summary says `checking which changed…`. With no check due or pending and no verdict, it reads `{n} DDS files in the PBR folder, plus the Vanilla folder` (`AppModel::written_text`; the line is cut when the panel is narrow and keeps its text on hover); the header Export hover `{n} files + Vanilla folder, {m} problems`. `{n}` is `export_files()`, the PBR plan's files. This replaces the button text of re-check A7 (`to {k} folders`) and CN82's hover text; `also_folder_count` is `also_extra_folders`. A GUI-side estimate of the other folders was not chosen: it would repeat the engine's derive and copy rules and drift from them. Test `also_export::the_count_is_the_pbr_folders_and_the_other_folders_are_named` compares the count with the DDS files on disk.
- **JF2 The export count leaves out a texture whose plan status is an error.** `count_export_files` counted an `ItemStatus::Error` item (an incomplete cube, a cube whose faces differ in size). Such a job runs and fails, so the header button, Review's button and What will be written named one file more than the export wrote. A `Failed` item never has a job and was never counted. No gate was added: an error is a red Problems row and the export still writes the rest. Test `review::a_texture_that_will_fail_is_not_in_the_export_count`.
- **JF3 Acknowledge is offered only where it ends the block (owner, 2026-11-15: "3 yes").** The engine ignores `allow` for `FMT-CS-SKIN-LOADER`, and for `CFG-UNAVAILABLE` it only accepts a fallback sub-type (`policy.rs`, the `--allow` loop of `resolve`), so the GUI's Acknowledge wrote `allow` and the question stayed. `items::ack_clears` decides it: never for `FMT-CS-SKIN-LOADER`; for `CFG-UNAVAILABLE` only when `policy::availability` names a fallback (`pbr_albedo`, `pbr_emissive`, `pbr_normal` outside cs), where Acknowledge converts the file as the fallback and stays. Any other blocking code keeps Acknowledge. A block Acknowledge cannot clear is `Decision::Change(code)`, shown in Problems, in Review's Undecided column and in the texture panel's Undecided box:
  - `FMT-CS-SKIN-LOADER`: no button, and in practice no row. Note JG3 resets a format the type does not allow on every settled plan of a writable project, and every pair `format_rule::allowed` lists for `skin_rfaos`, `skin_wet` and `skin_detail` is one Community Shaders loads, so the question does not stay in the GUI. Where JG3 leaves a pair (a layer that still overrides the pin), the row has its sentence and no Acknowledge. A **Use BC7** button the owner first accepted ("3 yes") was dropped when this note met JG at the merge (agent ruling, 2026-11-15): the reset made it unreachable.
  - `CFG-UNAVAILABLE` with no fallback: the type picker (**Set type…** on a row, the sub-type dropdown and **Confirm** in the panel), and the sentence "This type isn't available in this mode — set another type, or change the mode in Settings…" (`code_text::change_line`; JI1). When the texture already asks for its type, that question is the answer and no second one is added.
  - The engine is unchanged, and `sdc.toml` files that hold such an `allow` entry are read as before. Tests in `crates/gui/tests/ack_offer.rs`.
- **JF4 The `Kinds ▾` hover says what a hidden row still does.** It said "Export's review still lists every warning"; since FM24 Review lists none. It now reads "Hide kinds of row you don't need in this project. Blocks and questions always show. A hidden row still counts for Ready, and the tab shows how many are hidden." (JO6). HC4's reason ("Review's safety rules read every warning") predates FM24 and is not the reason any more: Ready ignores the hiding because a hidden row is not fixed; the tab counts hidden rows apart (JO6). Test `bottom_panel::the_kinds_hover_says_what_a_hidden_row_still_does`.

## Note JG — Grey maps are BC4 or R8 in the GUI; a format the type does not allow resolves itself (owner, 2026-11-15)

- **JG1 The rule (owner, 2026-11-15: "a bad format shouldn't be accepted or visable in the gui at all, a grayscale map is always either bc4 or r8, no bulk edit or setting should override that"; "not a rule in the engine, but a hard rule in the gui").** In the GUI a grey map, every sub-type whose `Subtype::category()` is `Grayscale` (envmask, height, msn_specular, skin_sk, projected_noise, gray), is BC4 or R8, UNORM. `model::format_rule::allowed` is the engine's `policy::allowed_encodings_for` narrowed to those two pairs for a grey type, and every GUI use of the engine list now reads it: `fields::encoding_options` (the texture panel's Format and DDS tag rows, the set editor's slot combo, the bulk Format row, the Presets tab's Format combo), `bulk::skip_of` (bulk Set and Copy settings: "not valid for this map") and the settle cleanup (JG3). The engine is unchanged: the CLI and a hand-written `sdc.toml` still encode BC7 on a `skin_sk` (FM37's list of the other grey types stands there), and no output byte, golden or `encode_versions` pin moves. Why: the owner found a `_sk` encoded as BC7 from a pin the GUI had accepted (Copy settings, or a sub-type change that kept an old pin).
- **JG2 Write guards.** `AppModel::set_format` and `set_field` of `format` or `color.tag` refuse a write whose resulting pair the texture's sub-type does not allow (`format_write_ok`; the sub-type is the document's when it names one, so a sub-type written earlier in the same Copy settings batch counts). `PresetsModel::set_field` and `set_branch_field` refuse the same for the preset's sub-type and target.
- **JG3 A pair the type does not allow resolves itself, on every type (owner, 2026-11-15: "not valid for this map should resolve itself automatically in the gui as an info message > (resolve to the best compressed version for that map type, usually bc7)"; recommendations 1 to 3 accepted).** On every settled plan of a writable project, a texture whose resolved (format, tag) is outside `allowed` (and whose list is not empty) is fixed: an own `[files]` format or tag is cleared, so the type's default applies; with no own pin, or when the default is not allowed either (a preset, `[defaults]` or `[[rules]]` layer), the texture is pinned to `format_rule::best`: BC7, then BC5, BC4, BC3, BC1, then the rest in list order, among the allowed formats, keeping the tag where that format allows it. A grey map thus gets BC4. At most two rounds per chain (clear, then pin); a pin a later layer still overrides is left after the second. Each fixed texture is a Problems Info row (Note FC3): "<name>: <old pair> is not valid for <type>; format reset to the default (<pair>)." or "…; format set to <pair>, the best this type allows.", with the type change as the head when one caused it. This amends Z11: it runs whether or not the sub-type changed, also on the plans of an open, reload, undo or redo, and a slot lock (Z10) or field lock no longer keeps a pair the type does not allow. The fix is folded into the user step that caused it (one undo restores both, as before); one no step caused is its own step "Encoding follows type", marks the project unsaved (the close and open prompts ask; this overrides FI4 for it) and its row offers no Undo, since the rule would fix it again: Ctrl+Z restores the old pair for one plan, then the fix lands again as a new step, which clears Redo. A read-only project (FM10) is never rewritten; Review already refuses to export it. The `NOT_VALID` stray in `encoding_options` stays only for the moment before the plan settles and for a read-only project.
- **JG4 Presets tab.** Selecting, reloading, reverting or duplicating a user preset whose sub-type row resolves to a pair outside `allowed` fixes the draft the same way (own value dropped, else `best` set) and shows the lines as a note in the editor; the draft is then unsaved. A new preset, or a delete, clears the note. Built-in presets already give every sub-type a pair `allowed` lists, so a healthy project opens with no fix. `shortcut:` suffix branch rows are not fixed on load (their combo and setter are guarded); upgrade if a user preset branch gives a grey type another format.
- **JG5 Tests.** `grey_format::{every_grey_picker_lists_bc4_and_r8_only, a_bc7_pin_on_a_skin_sk_resolves_to_bc4_on_open, a_layer_that_gives_a_grey_map_bc7_is_answered_with_a_bc4_pin, a_direct_format_write_of_bc7_on_a_grey_map_is_refused, a_bulk_bc7_skips_the_grey_map, copy_settings_never_lands_bc7_on_a_grey_map, retyping_a_bc7_texture_to_a_grey_type_drops_the_pin, a_disallowed_pair_on_any_type_resolves_on_open, the_presets_tab_never_keeps_bc7_on_a_grey_type, revert_fixes_again_and_a_new_draft_drops_the_note}`, each failed before its fix; `{every_built_in_default_is_a_pair_the_gui_keeps, a_healthy_grey_map_is_left_alone, clear_then_pin_is_one_step_and_one_row, undoing_the_open_fix_fixes_it_again}` pin what stays. `encoding_follows_type::{a_texture_in_a_locked_slot_is_reset_too, the_verdict_clears_or_pins}` replace the Z11 lock tests. Older tests that wrote a pair their type does not allow (BC1 or sRGB on a landscape diffuse, sRGB on complex grass) now write an allowed one: `bulk_apply`, `bulk_ui`, `bc7_weighting`, `fields::the_one_bit_alpha_box_shows_only_for_bc1`, `complex_grass::a_scan_vote_retype_cleans_up_in_its_own_step`, `encoding_follows_type::an_explicit_encoding_the_new_type_allows_is_kept`; `texture_panel_layout::a_long_value_does_not_widen_the_column` checks every allowed format, since the "not valid" label is no longer reachable; `unknown_suffix::an_error_keeps_the_card_and_writes_nothing` makes its error with `alpha.threshold = 0`; `presets_creator::the_format_list_follows_allowed_encodings` pins the GUI list.

## Note JH — A PBR set's match key follows the albedo's input name (main team, 2026-11-15)

The owner saw the set 3D preview fall back to "Mesh: Auto (Sphere)" for PBR sets. `set_match` built the match key from the albedo's file name on disk, so an `[inputs]` alias (the input name box, `Rename inputs…`) left it behind: `teeth_albedo2.png` named `textures/arch/mouth/teeth_02` kept `match_diffuse = arch\mouth\teeth_albedo2`, which no mesh uses, while `arch\mouth\teeth_02` matches 4 meshes of the owner's scan. The exported JSON patched nothing either.

- **JH1 The albedo's match key is built from the name it is read as (agent ruling, on the owner's report).** `sets::read_name` takes the diffuse's `[inputs]` alias when the plan applied it (the job's `game_path` without extension ends with the alias without extension, as `input_name_plan` checks), else its key; `set_match` and `mirrored_stem` read that. So `derived_match`, `match1` / `match_diffuse` (with a Key-form `rename`, HJ4), the set preview's mesh query and the default prefix follow the alias, and the set's outputs under `textures\pbr\` move with the prefix (`rock02.dds` for a set named `landscape/rock02`), as the alias moves every other output (AA1). A refused alias (IN-ALIAS-INVALID) leaves the file name (`a_refused_alias_leaves_the_match_key_on_the_file_name`). The key takes the alias's case; the input name box writes lower case. Cost if wrong: an existing project whose PBR albedo has an input name writes its outputs and entry under the new name on the next export, and the old outputs stay on disk. Tests `set_preview_model::an_input_name_moves_the_match_key_and_the_meshes_follow`, `a_key_form_rename_of_an_input_named_set_matches_the_input_name`.
- **JH2 What does not move (agent rulings).** `Rename…` (the set) and `Rename outputs…` change no alias (HE5), so a renamed set still matches its source path (F1 Review Focus 1, `sets_model::a_renamed_set_still_matches_its_source_path`, unchanged). A renamed or created set whose albedo gets an input name follows it (`an_input_name_on_a_renamed_set_moves_its_match_key`, `an_input_name_in_a_created_set_rebuilds_its_view`); a created set keeps its id, so `sets::view_reads` now includes the diffuse's `game_path`, or the fast re-plan kept the old view (the created-set test fails without it). HE4's lookup is still the written name; for an aliased PBR set that is now the input name, so HE4's "an input name is not asked" is edited and `sets_model::an_aliased_set_looks_up_the_written_name` swapped its two paths.
- **JH3 GUI only.** PGPatcher JSON is GUI-only (P14), so the CLI has no match key to change. No engine change: no `encode_version` arm moves.

## Note JI — Settings tab, and no Advanced drawer (owner, 2026-11-15)

The owner approved the design on 2026-11-15 ("doc approved, yes add a this project section"): spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-15-settings-and-no-advanced-design.md`, whose survey corrections 1-25 win over its goals, and plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-15-settings-and-no-advanced.md`. Each section of the Advanced drawer moves to the panel that owns its scope, and the drawer goes. Nothing in the engine, the presets, the goldens or `encode_versions` changes: the same `[files]` keys carry the same values. "Owner" marks the owner's word; the rest are controller or agent rulings.

- **JI1 A Settings tab (owner; layout accepted 2026-11-15).** `CentreTab::Settings` is a third centre-column tab with Pop out, beside Presets and the TruePBR JSON editor (FB7). The header's **Settings…** opens it (`AppModel::open_settings_tab`); it sits after the view tabs, where the column eyes were, and stays outside ⋯, also with no folder and in the Meshes view (JY13).
  - **This project.** Heading "This project: saved in this folder's sdc.toml and sdc-project.toml", then Mode (moved whole from the Presets tab, which now only edits presets), Preset (a combo built from the header's menu body, `header::preset_entries`, accessible `Preset (this project): {value}`) and Export folder (the path cut in the middle at 48 characters, whole on hover and in the accessible name, with **Choose…** and **Open**). Mode and Preset save with Save and are off while `sdc.toml` is read-only; neither waits for a plan, as in the header. The export folder saves as `set_export_folder` does (at once while `sdc-project.toml` is clean, else on Save); Choose… and the header share `header::export_folder_off`. The Export without review row follows the folder row (JY3). With no project the section reads "No project open" and holds no controls.
  - **App settings.** Heading "App settings: these apply to every project", then five groups in this order, each saved to `gui.toml` at once: Preview (Live encode, accessible "Live encode setting"; the preview toolbar and the command palette keep their copies), Checks (Low detail, Green direction, Parallax), Export (Validate exported DDS, LG6), Cache (Analysis cache limit, HV2) and Columns (ticks "Textures" and "Sets", ticked = shown, keeping BH15's accessible names "Textures column" / "Sets column").
  - **Names and text.** The tab's Open is named `Settings: Open export folder`, so it differs from Export to ▾'s "Open export folder" (rule 8.4; agent ruling over the plan's fixed name). The header's Preset hover ends "Change the mode in Settings…", and the CFG-UNAVAILABLE sentence says "change the mode in Settings…". `open_export_folder()` errors are discarded alike in the header, the Files tab and this tab. The popped-out tab's heading "Settings" can share the screen with the expanded preview's collapsed settings strip, so the strip's button is named `Preview settings` (rule 8.1); its painted text stays "Settings".
  - **Header width.** Settings… stays visible on the 1055 px row when optional controls move into ⋯ (JY13).
  - Amends BH15 (the eyes are the Columns ticks), FB7 (Settings is a third tab), FS5 (a docked Settings tab owns the keys), CD2 (header width, JY13), HV2 (Cache lives here), FM25 and FM26 (Preview and Checks are groups here) and JF3 (the sentence). Tests in `crates/gui/tests/settings_tab.rs`; `header_layout::the_header_is_one_row_from_1280`.
- **JI2 The set editor's last tab is "Also export" (owner).** It replaces the Advanced tab and keeps its place; the first tab stays **Material** on a set with PGPatcher JSON and **Set** on any other. The chosen tab lives in egui memory only, and `advanced_open` is gone from `gui.toml` (review finding 9).
  - **This set.** On a PBR set, the one tick "Skip vanilla/CM for this set", moved from the Material tab's Also export group (gone) and still the one `[export] skip` list Review's "Skip these sets" edits. It is greyed with "Turn on Also export as Vanilla or as Complex material below first" while both project ticks are off. On a set that is not PBR the block reads "Also export reads PBR sets only; this set is not one".
  - **All sets in this project.** On every set: "Also export as Vanilla" and "Also export as Complex material" (accessible `All sets: also export as …`), the same `[export] also` values as Export to ▾ and Review, then the 9 `[export.legacy]` recipe numbers, greyed with "Turn on Also export as Vanilla or as Complex material to use these" while both ticks are off. The recipe stays project-wide (owner). The ticks take their disabled reason from `also::tick_off` (export running, no folder, read-only `sdc.toml`, then cs only), shared with Export to ▾ and Review; the Skip tick and the recipe are off while an export runs or `sdc.toml` is read-only. None waits for a plan (agent ruling: the header edits the same values without one, and the replan absorbs an edit). A recipe value outside its box's range (a hand-edited `sdc.toml`) is shown as it is: the box does not clamp it on draw, so opening the tab writes nothing (`clamp_existing_to_range(false)`, as the cube section's boxes).
  - **Selection.** A set key holds the output root, so an Also tick or a new export folder changed every set key and closed the editor: `apply_plan` rekeys `Selection::Set` when the root moves (a key not under the old root is left as it is).
  - **Review's link.** Review's "Recipe settings are in Advanced" is **Recipe settings…** (hover "Brightness, AO, specular and the other [export.legacy] values, in a set's Also export tab"). It opens the Also export tab of the selected set if that is PBR, else of the first PBR set in list order that is not skipped, else of the first PBR set (`AppModel::recipe_set`), calling `collapse()` before `select_set`; in 3D it switches to 2D first. The tab row scrolls into view once, so the recipe shows. With no PBR set the link is not drawn.
  - Amends CC3 and CC4 (the tab and `advanced_open`) and HG4 (a shared set keeps the Also export tab). Tests in `crates/gui/tests/also_export.rs` and `set_panel::a_pbr_set_has_material_and_also_export_tabs_and_the_choice_is_not_saved`.
- **JI3 The per-texture fields are in the texture panel (owner: "resize is a per texture setting, but it can also be defined by a preset").**
  - **Rows.** Mipmaps gains **Levels** (the mip count, with its note and ↺; the tick row above it then has no note and no ↺, so an edited count shows one ↺, while the strip, which has no Levels row, keeps the ↺ on the tick row); **Size** is a group (order in LQ2) with Resize, Relative to and Size floor (`PanelGroup::Size`, `panel_groups::panel_group`); Compression gains **DDS header**. `ADVANCED_FIELDS` is folded into `FIELDS`, minus its second BC7 weighting row, so `all_fields()` still lists every field (`field_view`, the bulk labels, Copy settings and the item menu keep them); `SEARCH_FIELDS` keeps the two search fields there (JI4). Copy settings lists fields in `all_fields()` order, so DDS header and the Size rows come before Levels, Filter and Edge.
  - **Strip and bulk.** The preview's compact strip shows neither Levels nor DDS header. Because `PanelGroup` also drives the bulk panel, bulk edits Size and DDS header too (this amends digest Z4/Z8).
  - **Widths.** The note column keeps `NOTE_MIN` (120 px) and the control gives way first, down to `CONTROL_MIN`, which is the set editor's `COMBO_W` (96 px: the format combos' minimum, above which they widen into free room; the source pickers have their own `SOURCE_W` 130, JR4), then the label (HI1, review finding 11), down to `interact_size.x`; only once the label is at that floor does the note give way below `NOTE_MIN`. That happens in the 3D settings window at its narrowest (262 pt), where the note column is about 60 px. `hint_and_reset` keeps a right margin of `bar_width + bar_outer_margin` for the panel's scroll bar, which senses its full width over the content's right edge (JI7).
  - **Inspector.** "Where these settings come from" is a section at the bottom of the texture panel holding the read-only preset inspector, minus its Batch preset row, its user-presets list and its Presets… button (the header has both) and the "Built-in presets are read-only here…" hint. Whether it is open is a texture-panel group state (Note LQ5). The "only what differs" tick is one value for the whole app, kept in egui memory (`inspector-only-differences`) across selections and panels. It is not in the strip.
  - Amends HI1, HI3 (the strip), HI9 (help covers `all_fields`), HI14 (the inspector shows the unconfirmed profile), HI18 (no drawer), FS1 (the moved rows wait for the plan), FM29 and FM44 (where the Size rows live). Tests in `crates/gui/tests/texture_panel_layout.rs`, `inspector_section.rs` and `resize_field.rs` (`moved_rows_write_the_same_files_keys`).
- **JI4 Effort drives the search fields (owner: "should be driven by the existing controls").** The Compression group's Effort row writes the field the texture's format reads (`fields::effort_field`): `encoder.effort` on BC7 and BC6H (the seven levels), `encoder.bc45_search_radius` on BC4 and BC5 (Fast, Default, Slowest, Max), `encoder.bc1_search` on BC1 (Fast, Default), and both on BC3 (`BC3_CHOICES`: Fast = both `fast`, Default = both `default`, Slowest = radius `slowest` and Max = radius `max`, each with search `default`) in one undo step "Change Effort" / "Reset Effort". A single-texture edit of any field the row writes reads "Change Effort" / "Reset Effort" on every format, as a bulk edit does (`field_step_label`); a field outside the row names its key. On other formats the row is greyed with "this format has no search setting". Provenance and ↺ follow the field the format reads; the separate "BC4/BC5 search radius" and "BC1/BC3 search" rows are gone.
  - **BC3's mixed pair.** A pair no choice names shows a read-only entry `Custom: search {bc1}, radius {radius}` (hover "Mixed values from a preset or [files]; pick Fast, Default, Slowest or Max to set both"); its provenance is the radius's when the radius is not `default`, else the search's when that is not, else the radius's.
  - **Before.** On BC1, BC3, BC4 and BC5 the row wrote `encoder.effort`, which those encoders ignore (correction 11); it now writes the search field there, the same key the old drawer rows wrote. An `encoder.effort` already pinned on such a file stays, still ignored, and a value written for another format stays in `sdc.toml`.
  - **Bulk.** When every selected texture reads the same field (BC1 and BC3 count as different), the row offers that field's choices; otherwise it reads "Formats differ: edit one format at a time". A texture in a locked slot or with no search does not count, so BC7 plus an uncompressed texture edits `encoder.effort` and skips the uncompressed one with "this format has no search setting", before the per-field format locks; Clear is not blocked. Two BC3 textures with different Custom pairs read "— mixed". Every bulk combo is `labelled_by` its row label (review finding 16). A bulk write of any `EFFORT_FIELDS` member is named "Effort" on every format: one step "Change Effort on {n} textures" / "Reset Effort on …", and the report "Effort: written to …". `field_label` keeps the search fields' own names for Copy settings and the inspector.
  - Amends HY17 (the GUI row). Tests in `crates/gui/tests/effort_search.rs`.
- **JI5 The diffuse slot row's alpha is a dropdown (owner).** The set editor's "Opaque" icon toggle is replaced by `settings_panel::alpha_choice`: a dropdown on the texture's alpha (the proposal while it is asked, else the resolved type, a preset's included), accessible `Slot alpha type: {label}`, then **Confirm** ("Slot confirm alpha", the shared `alpha_confirm`) only while the alpha is asked, then ↺ ("Reset slot alpha") while the texture has its own `alpha.policy`, which goes back to the inherited value. A pick writes `alpha.policy` at once as one step "Alpha"; picking the shown entry writes nothing. The controls wait on `fix_off()`. The control's role changes from a selectable Button (CC2) to a ComboBox (rule 8.5); "Match on this" stays a toggle, painted as the link mark (JR4). The dropdown's label is the "Alpha" heading over the Flags column (JR4).
  - **Width.** The labels stay `Remove`, `Cutout`, `Blend`, `Cutout + Blend` and `Data`, and `ALPHA_W` (114 px) is measured on "Cutout + Blend"; this replaces spec correction 14's one-word labels and ~80 px, which rested on a wrong premise. `ROWS_MIN` = 540 + `ALPHA_W` + Confirm (~54) + two gaps = 724, so while a set is open in 2D and the Selection tab is in front (JR5) the Textures and Sets columns give up about 184 px more; at 1100 px they keep their minimums.
  - Amends the CC1/CC2 digest line (Flags), FM35 (a fourth surface) and HI7 (Confirm is shared). Tests in `crates/gui/tests/slot_alpha.rs`.
- **JI6 Unmatched `[files]` entries are a Problems row.** `CFG-FILE-UNMATCHED` entries from the last full plan are one Info row at the project, "{n} saved settings name no file in this mode ({mode})" (n = 1: "1 saved setting names no file in this mode ({mode})"), with every entry on hover, an `Entries ▾` menu that lists each entry still in `[files]` with its own **Remove** (accessible `Remove {key}`), and **Remove all**. Unmatched means in the mode of that plan (`model.target`), since some planning is cs-only. Like any note it counts in the tab's `Notes (m)`, not in its problems, and takes nothing from "ready"; having buttons, it also stays in the default list after the problems (JO3).
  - **Gates.** Each Remove waits on `fix_off()`, as every Problems row's fix does. Remove all is on when `project.target().is_none_or(|t| t == model.target)`, no full plan is pending and no partial plan is out, and its hover lists what it deletes. One entry is one step "Remove entry"; Remove all is one step "Remove {n} entries". While no entry is left in `[files]` (a read-only `sdc.toml`'s empty document), `Entries ▾` is off too, with Remove all's reason on hover.
  - **Code.** The Kinds ▾ name of the row is "Unmatched [files] entries" (FB8: no raw codes). `ProjectDoc::target()` reads the raw `target` key without a full parse, since the row asks every frame. `target_name` moved to `model/code_text.rs`.
  - It replaces the drawer's last section. Amends nothing: no live ruling described that section (FM16's drawer wording is edited under JI7). Tests in `crates/gui/tests/problems_rows.rs` (`unmatched_entries_show_as_one_info_row` and the tests after it).
- **JI7 The Advanced drawer is gone.** `ui/advanced.rs`, its body and the window-edge "Advanced" strip are deleted, and nothing is drawn at the window's right edge in any state. In 3D nothing replaces it: the preview takes the width, and the recipe and the Also ticks are reached in 2D through the Also export tab, or through Export to ▾ for the ticks.
  - **Helpers.** `settled_drag`, `held`, `held_value` and the test-only `gesture` module live in `ui/number_input.rs` (FN2, FY1), used by the set editor's fill boxes, the cube section, Review and the Settings and Also export tabs. `set_editor_shown` is `pub` for its test pin.
  - **Scroll bars.** The moved rows make the centre's panels taller, so the texture panel, the bulk panel and the set editor each scope `ScrollStyle::thin()` on their scroll area (rule 3.2, as CC21 and CN86): the bar shows without hover. The set editor's had been the floating default since FB2's one column. `hint_and_reset`, the set editor's File cell and the shared row's ✕ keep their `bar_width + bar_outer_margin` reserve: the thin bar takes 6 px beside the content and senses 10 px, so 4 of them lie over it. Tests in `crates/gui/tests/scroll_bars.rs` read the paint (a bar has no AccessKit node).
  - **Old keys.** A `gui.toml` with `advanced_open` or a `[layout.widths] advanced` width loads without a message, and the keys are dropped on the next save. The column widget ids shift once, since the drawer's `skip_ahead_auto_ids(1)` branch is gone.
  - Amends CC5 (3D routing), CD18 (the start screen), CE17 (the drawer's 260 px minimum is gone with it), FN2, FY1, HE6 (no strip to skip), FM14, FM15, FM16, FM45 and FO3 (their drawer wording). Tests `set_panel::no_window_edge_strip_in_any_state` and `settings::old_advanced_keys_load_and_are_dropped_on_save`.

## Note JJ — The 3D toolbar's mesh combo wraps, and the Textures and Sets columns show their scroll bars (main team, 2026-11-16)

Two layout bugs from the Settings plan's UI proof (2026-11-15), B4 and B5, both older than that plan. Agent rulings.

- **JJ1 The mesh combo sits in an exact-width slot (rule 3.5).** At 1100x800 the set preview's toolbar drew "Mesh: Auto (Sphere)" from x 1020 to 1163 in a centre that ends at 1092. egui's `ComboBox` lays itself out in a child `horizontal` ui, and the toolbar's `horizontal_wrapped` row places that child before its width is known, so the combo never took the next row (View ▾ after it did). `set_preview::mesh_picker` now measures the whole text (Button style, plus the icon and the button padding) and puts the combo in `allocate_ui` of that width, which the row wraps like any sized widget. A row narrower than the text gets a slot as wide as the row; the text truncates there, with the whole of it on hover and, as before, in the combo's accessible value. A scanned NIF's label (path, origin, shape count) is wider than the row at 1100 px, so truncation is the usual case once a project has meshes. Tests `set_preview_layout::the_mesh_combo_stays_inside_the_window_at_1100` (failed before the fix) and `a_long_mesh_label_truncates_inside_the_window` (an injected candidate list; it fails without the row cap, where the combo ran to x 1649).
- **JJ2 The Textures and Sets columns scope `ScrollStyle::thin()` (rule 3.2, as JI7 for the centre's panels).** egui's default floating bar is transparent until hovered, so a column longer than the window gave no cue. Thin draws the bar at rest; hovered it grows to 10 px. The Textures tiles already leave `SCROLL_ALLOWANCE` (18 px) on the right, so the bar never covers a tile. In the Sets column the bar takes a 6 px gutter, so the cards end before the bar at rest; hovered, it reaches only the cards' frame margin (7 px). The default Sets width (`SETS_W`) goes from 260 to 266 px: a 260 px column's cards held exactly five chips a row (243 px; 48 px a chip with its gap), and the gutter left four, so every card of five chips or more grew a row. A width the user dragged to is kept as it is. The FM32 chip-row estimate takes the cards' width beside the gutter whether or not the bar shows: a card is estimated only while off screen, and then the list overflows and the bar shows. A width that followed the bar would make its appearing a re-flow, which scrolls back to the open set. Tests `scroll_bars::{the_textures_column_shows_its_bar_at_rest, the_sets_column_shows_its_bar_at_rest}` read the paint, as JI7's do. Both failed before the fix (bar alpha 0). Each also checks that the reserves cover the bar: beside the bar no control reaches its hovered 10 px, and no set card reaches it at rest. The Sets check fails with thin and a 0 px gutter. Unit test `sets_column::tests::the_default_sets_width_holds_five_chips_beside_the_bar` (failed at 260). `set_order::a_number_past_the_end_never_picks_a_set_named_with_it` told the Move to dialog's row from the set card by rect, and the wider column took in the dialog's row; it now leaves out the card by its name ("Set …").

## Note JK — The Also export tab's recipe names its scope (owner, 2026-11-16)

- **JK1 One sentence under the recipe (owner pick: "Sentence only").** A blind reader of the Also export tab on 2026-11-15 could not tell who the 9 `[export.legacy]` boxes apply to: they are greyed while both Also ticks are off, and the "All sets in this project" heading did not read as covering them. Under the boxes the tab now reads "Saved in this project's sdc.toml; used when Vanilla or Complex material is ticked" (`also_tab::RECIPE_SCOPE`). It is drawn outside the greyed block, so it is never greyed, and as a plain label, not weak text, so it does not look greyed beside the boxes. It wraps, and at 1100 px it fits on one line. The boxes' greying and their hover reason are unchanged (JI2): the owner chose the sentence over keeping the boxes editable while both ticks are off, and over doing both. Test `also_export::the_recipe_says_where_it_is_saved_and_when_it_is_used`.

## Note IF — The display readback converts on the GPU (owner's "do the gpu conversion", 2026-11-16; details are agent rulings)

Measured on 2026-11-16 (`docs/research/render3d-backends.md`): 72% to 74% of the readback was the CPU's half-to-8-bit table lookup, not the copy from the GPU.

- **IF1 The bytes do not change.** A compute pass (`crates/render3d/src/convert.rs`) takes each colour channel's half bits with `f32tof16` and looks them up in a 256×256 `R8_UINT` texture that holds the same 65536-entry table the CPU used (`unorm8` or `srgb8` in `device.rs`); alpha is written as 255, as the display readback always did. I chose a table over shader arithmetic because `pow` and float-to-UNORM rounding may differ by a step between GPUs, and tests pin frame bytes. Test `device::gpu_conversion_matches_the_cpu_table_for_every_half` compares the pass with the old CPU loop (`Target::readback_display_cpu`, test-hooks only) for every half bit pattern in each colour channel, forward and deferred, sRGB on and off, on the default device and on WARP. The test writes the bits raw, so every NaN payload and denormal is in the target. `deferred::a_frame_reads_back_as_the_cpu_conversion` does the same comparison on frames drawn through `Renderer`. Run on one NVIDIA GPU and on WARP; on other GPUs the identity rests on the table, not on a run.
- **IF2 What a target holds now.** The colour texture is a shader resource on forward targets too. The target adds an `R8G8B8A8_UINT` texture the pass writes, and the staging texture is that format (it was half float), so the copy to the CPU is half the bytes. The pass and its two tables are made once per `Gpu`, on the first readback. A failure to build them fails that readback and is not kept: the next readback tries again.
- **IF3 The pass restores the render targets and nothing else.** It must unbind them, because D3D11 gives a shader a null view of a texture still bound for output; it binds them again afterwards. A frame has always ended with its targets bound, and `deferred::recreating_the_target_unbinds_the_old_views_first` relies on that (it failed before the rebind was added); `device::readback_leaves_the_render_targets_bound` checks it directly. For the same reason the pass first clears compute UAV slots 0 to 7: the deferred composite writes the colour texture through one. It leaves the compute stage unbound (shader, t0, t1, u0 to u7); every compute pass in the crate binds what it uses before it dispatches.
- **IF4 `FrameTiming::map_ms` (test-hooks only)** is the part of `readback_ms` before `Map` returns: the pass, the staging copy and the transfer. The rest is copying the rows into the returned `Vec`.
- **IF5 Not done:** the rows are still copied into a new `Vec` each frame (3.5 ms of the 6.2 ms readback at 3840×2160), and the frame still goes to the CPU and back to the GUI's device. Sharing the texture with the GUI is the report's later step.

## Note JL — egui 0.37 (main team, 2026-11-16)

The GUI moves from egui, eframe and egui_kittest 0.36.2 to 0.37.0. A user sees no change except the accessible names of JL4 and JL5 and the egui behaviour changes of JL6 and JL7; JL10 is a bug fix found while making the test suite reliable. No output byte changes, no encode version moves and the engine version stays. Owner rulings are quoted; the rest are agent and controller rulings. egui source lines are egui 0.37.0's unless an item says otherwise.

- **JL1 The pins, the lock and the API moves (owner: "fetch ok").** `crates/gui/Cargo.toml` pins `eframe` and `egui_kittest` at `=0.37.0`.
  - **The lock.** It moves the egui family (eframe, egui, egui-wgpu, egui-winit, emath, epaint, ecolor, egui_kittest) and crates only the family depends on: accesskit 0.25.2 and its platform crates (accesskit_consumer 0.39.2 replaces 0.35, 0.36 and 0.38), glifo 0.3.0, vello_common and vello_cpu 0.2.0, phf and its three helper crates 0.14.0, kittest 0.5.0. It adds send_wrapper 0.6.0, unicode-properties 0.1.5 and accesskit_ios 0.2.2, and drops calloop, calloop-wayland-source, smithay-client-toolkit, smithay-clipboard and two wayland-protocols crates. windows-sys and every other crate keep their version.
  - **The lock rule, for the owner to overrule.** In an egui upgrade a crate outside the egui family may change version only when every crate that depends on it is in the family, so the engine and the CLI build against what they built against before. The 0.37.0 lock passes it.
  - **Fonts before the first frame.** egui_kittest 0.37 lays out text in the frame it runs inside `build_*` and panics on a missing glyph (`MissingGlyphPolicy::Panic`, egui_kittest `builder.rs:50`). Every harness therefore installs the fonts in `HarnessBuilder::with_context_setup(|ctx| { sdc_gui::fonts::install_system_fonts(ctx).expect("system fonts"); })`, never after the build. `install_system_fonts` also applies the pinned look (JL2), so a test draws the app's style.
  - **API moves.** `egui::WidgetType` is `egui::Role` (TextEdit→TextInput, DragValue→SpinButton, ColorButton→ColorWell, CollapsingHeader→DisclosureTriangle, Checkbox→CheckBox, Other→Unknown, SelectableLabel→Button). `Id::new` is deprecated and calls `Id::unique` (`id.rs:101-104`), so every stored key keeps its value under `Id::unique`. `ui.id()` is `ui.scope_id()` and `UiBuilder::id` is `UiBuilder::scope_id`; a scroll area's offset is read with `state.clamped_offset()`; `Event::MouseWheel` takes `source: MouseWheelSource::Wheel`.
  - **Debug-build ids.** In a debug build egui records the source of every new `Id` in a map it never clears (`id.rs`, `mod id_source`), so an id made per keystroke grows it. The input-name path list keys its cache with a plain std hash (`cache_key` in `crates/gui/src/ui/input_name.rs`), not with `Id::unique(..).value()`. Release builds are unaffected, and no test can see the map (it is private to egui).
  - Supersedes, in part: FM32 (the `ui.id()` and `UiBuilder::id` names; its parent sentence is re-checked on 0.37 and stands). Its old wording is archived.
- **JL2 The 0.36.2 look is pinned (owner: "pin the 0.36.2 look").** `crate::look::pin_036_style`, applied by `fonts::install_system_fonts` through `ctx.all_styles_mut`, sets `button_padding` (4, 1) and `interact_size` (40, 18), where 0.37 has (8, 2) and (40, 20). It copies 0.36.2's `Widgets::dark()` and `Widgets::light()` field by field (`egui-0.36.2 style.rs:1680-1770`): corner radius 2 (3 hovered) where 0.37 has 4 and 6; dark inactive `weak_bg_fill` 60 and `fg_stroke` 180, hovered 70 and 240, active 55, where 0.37 has 50, 215, 64, 245 and 40; `expansion` is 0.0 in both versions. It turns the scroll rubber band off, as part of the pin (the controller's reading of the owner's ruling, for the owner to overrule). `look::thin_scroll()` is `ScrollStyle::thin()` with the rubber band off and is every thin scroll area's style. `look::TEXT_EDIT_MARGIN`, (4, 2), is 0.36.2's TextEdit margin and every TextEdit takes it: 0.37 takes the margin from `button_padding`, 2 points short under the pin. Reason: the upgrade changes no layout a user sees, and the 0.37 defaults moved the header's Save to a second row at 1280 and pushed "Cutout + Blend" past `ALPHA_W`.
  - **What it keeps.** A button "Export" 44.72×19, a DragValue 40×19 (40×19.28 in edit mode), a checkbox row 52.7×18, a TextEdit 21.28 tall at rest and focused, and the same fills, strokes and radii at rest and hovered. With the rubber band off the reveal scrolls settle within their step limits; with it on, `set_order::a_reveal_lands_on_a_far_card_among_mixed_heights` and `set_groups_ui::the_revealed_set_is_on_screen_with_headers_above_it` needed 50 and 40 steps.
  - **Two widgets 0.37 rebuilt are drawn as 0.36.2 drew them (pinned; found by the doc shots while the kittest suite passed).** Every `ComboBox` is shown through `look::Combo036::show_ui_036`, inside a `ui.horizontal` row of its own as in 0.36.2 (`egui-0.36.2 containers/combo_box.rs:234`; 0.37 shows it in the caller's layout, `containers/combo_box.rs:265`). Without that row a value wider than the combo wrapped in a vertical layout (the row gave it `TextWrapMode::Extend`, `egui-0.36.2 ui.rs:589`): the set editor's "BC4 · linear (one channel)" combo was three lines tall, the Format column narrowed and the File column's slack moved it 62 px right. A combo beside a 19 pt button also sat half a point higher in a row 1 px shorter, which moved the cube panel's handle, the status dots and the row buttons by 1 to 2 px. Every submenu entry is drawn through `look::menu_button_036`, which puts 0.36.2's 13 pt "⏵" glyph back (`egui-0.36.2 containers/menu.rs:347`) through `SubMenuButton::from_button` in place of 0.37's 14 pt painted triangle (`containers/menu.rs:492-511`); the triangle made the set card's menu 1 px wider and its arrows 2 px higher. The entry's accessible name stays its text (JL4). The combo's ▼ icon and its minimum height are the same in both versions. 0.37's `theme/default_style.rs` builds the button, checkbox and separator styles with the margins, sizes and 6 pt separator spacing of 0.36.2's `widget_style.rs`, so nothing else there is pinned.
  - **Accepted, for the owner to overrule.** Every other default that differs between the two versions' `style.rs`, or that 0.37's new `theme/default_style.rs` sets, is pinned above or accepted here. A hovered read-only TextEdit rounds 3, not 2 (Tessalith has none). A TextEdit's minimum height is `interact_size.y`, which is inert: the smallest box, a one-row monospace TOML preview, is 19.22 tall with `TEXT_EDIT_MARGIN`, as on 0.36.2. TextEdit prefix and suffix colours are new (Tessalith uses neither). A checkbox box's radius is capped at 4, which is inert under the pinned 2 and 3. The kinetic drag momentum and the new text stack's glyph metrics are JL7's. `Interaction`, the rest of `Visuals`, the other `Spacing` fields and the text sizes did not change.
  - **Tests.** `look::tests::{widget_visuals_are_036s_in_both_themes, the_widgets_keep_their_036_sizes, a_focused_text_edit_and_a_checkbox_paint_as_in_036, a_drag_past_the_end_of_a_thin_scroll_list_stops_at_the_edge}` (all four fail with the pin stubbed), `header_layout::the_header_is_one_row_from_1280`, `slot_alpha::alpha_w_fits_the_longest_label`, `look_036::{a_combo_beside_a_button_sits_as_on_036, a_long_combo_value_in_a_grid_cell_stays_on_one_line_as_on_036, the_set_editors_long_format_combo_is_one_line, a_submenu_entry_ends_in_036s_arrow_glyph}` (each fails without its helper) and the two source scans `look::tests::{every_combo_box_is_shown_through_show_ui_036, every_menu_button_goes_through_menu_button_036}`.
- **JL3 Every panel is built through `ui::panel_ids`.** egui 0.37 keys a panel's `PanelState` by `parent_ui.scope_id().with_salt(salt)` (`containers/panel.rs:971-976`), not by a global id of its salt. The same salt has another id in every parent `Ui`, and a read or write under `Id::unique(salt)` silently misses. No earlier ruling named the panel-id mechanism.
  - **Building and reading.** `crate::ui::panel_ids::{left, right, top, bottom}(parent, salt)` build every panel of the app and record the id egui will use, under the salt. Readers take it from `panel_id`, `panel_rect` (the stored outer rect) and `resize_id` (`panel_id.with("__resize")`). `chosen_width_id(salt)` keeps the Textures and Sets columns' dragged width under the same key as before, and `ui::chosen_width_panel` writes and reads the panel's state under the recorded id. Tests read a panel the same way, never with `PanelState::load(ctx, Id::unique(salt))`.
  - **A restored width is never lost.** `layout::restore_ctx` hands a saved size to `panel_ids::restore_size`; the panel's first build applies it along its axis and drops it. `layout::panel_width` reads the panel's rect, else the restored size not yet applied, so a panel not built this session (the Meshes tree in a session that never opens Meshes, the preview settings) saves the width it was given. This fallback is a controller ruling, for the owner to overrule.
  - **A side panel is as wide as its content**, at least its minimum, in 0.36.2 and 0.37 alike: egui stores the frame's response (`containers/panel.rs:849-857` and `:905-912`; egui-0.36.2 `:828-837` and `:894-897`). An empty Meshes tree is drawn and stored at its 200 px minimum, and a restored width shows once the tree holds wider content.
  - **Tests.** `panel_ids::tests::{a_recorded_id_finds_the_panel_state_at_the_root_and_nested, a_restored_size_is_applied_once_when_the_panel_is_first_built, no_panel_is_built_outside_panel_ids}`, `window_layout::{saved_column_widths_come_back_in_the_real_panels, a_saved_meshes_tree_width_comes_back, a_restored_meshes_tree_width_survives_a_session_without_the_meshes_view, a_restored_preview_settings_width_survives_a_session_without_the_panel}` (the tree test gives the model one scanned mod, so the tree fills its panel), `column_widths::a_dragged_width_is_read_from_the_panels_own_handle` (it narrows the window to 1000 px, where Sets is really clamped; at 1100 the dragged width fits and the test could not fail), and `egui_gotchas::a_panel_is_stored_under_its_parents_scope`.
- **JL4 Every input widget has an accessible name (closes review finding E28).** egui_kittest 0.37 fails any `run()` that leaves an input widget (the roles egui's `accessibility::is_input` lists, `accessibility.rs:11-45`: buttons, check boxes, combo boxes, links, list, menu and tree items, radio buttons, sliders, spin buttons, splitters, switches, tabs, disclosure triangles, colour wells and every text, number, date and time input) with no name and no placeholder (egui_kittest `lib.rs:518-520`, `accessibility.rs:46-55`). The check stays on: no committed test calls `with_accessibility_check(false)`. It and `crates/gui/tests/accessible_names.rs` are the acceptance tests. There was no earlier ruling.
  - **How.** `crate::ui::name_input(response, name)` (`crates/gui/src/ui/mod.rs`) sets a control's name with `Response::accessible_name`. The name is set or linked before any hover text: 0.37 copies `on_hover_text` into an empty name (`response.rs:797-816`), and a linked control keeps its label under a hover.
  - **Names that differ from the upgrade plan's table, with the reason (controller rulings, for the owner to overrule).** The Meshes search box is "Search paths and mods", its visible hint, so a sighted user and a screen-reader user get the same words (the table said "Search meshes and mods"; the hint stays, since the upgrade changes nothing a user sees). The Presets tab's target combo is "Preset target": it picks the preset's game target beside the label "Target", so the table's "Save to" misnamed it and "Target" would collide with the label. The Presets tab's preset combo is "Preset to edit", because the header combo keeps "Preset". The export drawer's progress bar is "Export progress"; it had no row in the table.
  - **Naming rules, for the owner to overrule.**
    - Two controls on screen never share an accessible name (egui-ui-rules 8.4).
    - A value box beside a control with the same label is "{label} value" (the CS PBR card's "Specular level value" beside its tick, the complex-material card's key boxes).
    - A box whose only same-text neighbour is a plain Label keeps the label's words: through `labelled_by` where the label is at hand (the glint row, the preset field rows, the rename dialog's Find, Replace, Prefix and Suffix), else the same string (the PGPatcher and XPMF rows).
    - A Community Shaders card CheckBox and a PGPatcher card SpinButton with the same words ("Specular level", "Roughness scale") stay as they are: role and card differ, so a screen reader and a role-scoped finder tell them apart.
    - A Slider and its own value box may share one name: 0.37 names the box after its slider (`widgets/slider.rs:633-651`), and the two are one widget that edits one value, with different roles.
    - A control whose own name says more than its row label is named again after the link. 0.37's `labelled_by` clears the widget's own name (`response.rs:1065-1071`); 0.36.2 kept it, and a screen reader reads the own name first. `own_name_after_link` (`crates/gui/src/ui/settings_panel.rs`, also called from `bulk_panel.rs`) puts "Alpha threshold" back on the threshold slider of the texture and bulk panels, which would otherwise read "Threshold". Where the own name is equal or poorer (a combo's value, a DragValue's prefix or suffix), the label names the control.
  - **Names egui changed.** A submenu entry's name leaves out its ⏵: 0.37 paints the arrow as a shape (`containers/menu.rs:510-511`), and `look::menu_button_036`, which puts 0.36.2's ⏵ glyph back (JL2), names the entry its text, so "Move to group ⏵" (0.36.2) reads "Move to group". A collapsing header is a `DisclosureTriangle`. Panels, popups, menus and tooltips are nodes of their own, and each resizable panel's handle is a `Splitter` named "Resize panel" (`containers/panel.rs:1202`).
  - **Tests.** `accessible_names::{every_input_has_a_name_in_the_textures_view, every_input_has_a_name_in_the_set_editor_and_json_editor, every_input_has_a_name_in_presets_and_rename, every_input_has_a_name_in_the_meshes_view, the_header_preset_and_the_search_boxes_are_named, the_textures_filters_have_short_names, the_meshes_search_is_named, a_busy_3d_view_names_its_spinner, a_running_scan_names_its_spinner, an_export_in_progress_names_its_bar}`, `texture_panel_layout::a_linked_control_keeps_its_label_under_a_hover`, `bulk_ui::a_mixed_slider_shows_mixed_and_an_idle_frame_writes_nothing`, `ui_smoke::coverage_sits_under_alpha_for_cutout_policies_only` and `egui_gotchas::hover_text_names_an_unnamed_button`. `ui_proof::names_flags_an_unnamed_clickable_widget` keeps the check on: it feeds the proof tool's name detector a clickable `Role::Unknown` node, a role the check does not cover.
- **JL5 Undo and Redo name their step, for the owner to overrule.** On the Textures history the header button's accessible name is "Undo: {step}" or "Redo: {step}" while it is on (for example "Undo: set type of teeth_sss.png"), and "Undo" or "Redo" while it is off. On the NIF edit history it is "Undo (NIF edit history): {step}", and "Undo (NIF edit history)" while off or with no step. The button's text and hover are unchanged (`ui::header::undo_button`). Reason: a screen reader hears what the button undoes, as the hover tells a sighted user. No earlier ruling named the button's accessible name.
  - **Finder.** Tests find the button with `support::header_undo(h, verb)`: the one Button whose name is the verb, starts with "{verb}: ", or is or starts with "{verb} (NIF edit history)". It uses no panel rect: with the command palette open over the header it panics on two matches rather than click the wrong button.
  - **Tests.** `header::tests::{undo_names_the_step, nif_undo_names_its_history_and_step}`, `open_cancel::undo_label_names_the_nif_history` and `export_folder::undo_text_does_not_move_the_header`.
- **JL6 Three 0.37 behaviour changes are accepted (owner: "accept all three").** No fix was needed to keep a reveal or the View menu as before: the reveals settle under the rubber-band pin (JL2), and the View menu's colour swatch opens its picker and keeps the menu.
  - **The wheel scrolls during a drag.** 0.37 scrolls the area under the pointer while a widget is dragged (`containers/scroll_area.rs:1326-1329`; 0.36.2 skipped the wheel then, `egui-0.36.2 containers/scroll_area.rs:1215-1219`). A set dragged in the Sets column can be wheeled to a card that was off screen and dropped there. Test `set_order::the_wheel_scrolls_the_sets_during_a_drag` (it replaces `the_wheel_does_not_scroll_the_sets_during_a_drag`; a release on a card the wheel brought up moves the set there).
  - **A popup closes only on a click whose press started while it was open** (`containers/popup.rs:659-676`; 0.36.2 closed on any click, `egui-0.36.2 containers/popup.rs:608`). Test `problems_accept::choose_preset_opens_the_preset_list_and_a_pick_closes_it`.
  - **Nested scroll hand-off.** A scroll area at its end passes the wheel to its parent (`containers/scroll_physics.rs:213-225`). Tessalith's one same-axis nesting is Review's "Skip these sets" list inside Review's column. Test `accepted_037::the_wheel_passes_to_the_outer_list_at_the_inner_lists_end`.
  - **What the tests must allow for (no change a user sees).** 0.37 parents a popup's AccessKit nodes to the widget that opened it (`containers/popup.rs:641-643`), so in tree order a popup's nodes come before the opener's later siblings; `view_menu::the_background_swatch_opens_its_picker_and_keeps_the_menu` clicks the picker's own `B` box by name. Once a control is linked to its Label with `labelled_by`, kittest's label query returns the control, not the Label, so `dialog_keys::enter_right_after_typing_in_find_applies_the_rename` finds Find by role and name. `support::shown_containing` leaves out a name that 0.37 copied from hover text.
  - Supersedes, in part: 1.x CF7 (its sentence that egui ignores the wheel during a drag, so a drag reaches only the cards on screen, is no longer in force; egui still has no edge auto-scroll, and Move to… stays the way to make a long move). The digest line on the Sets column keeps citing CF7 for the rest. JA12 (it no longer names egui 0.36; its old wording is archived).
- **JL7 More 0.37 changes, accepted for the owner to overrule.** The owner did not rule on these; each is a change of egui with no switch, accepted as it is.
  1. **Arrow keys in menus** (`containers/menu.rs:233-288`). In a menu of buttons Up and Down move between the entries and Enter picks one; the menu stays open after that Enter. In a menu that starts with a filter box, Down focuses the box and every arrow key then stays in it, because a TextEdit takes both arrow axes (`widgets/text_edit/builder.rs:148-151`); the rows are reached by typing and a click, and a click on a row still picks it and closes the menu. Tests `popups::arrow_keys_and_typing_stay_in_the_input_name_filter` and `egui_gotchas::arrow_keys_stay_in_a_menus_filter_box`.
  2. **Kinetic drag momentum.** After a drag-to-scroll (on once egui has seen a touch screen) or a trackpad scroll, a scroll area coasts with `decay_time` 0.5 s and `stop_distance` 0.5 (`style.rs:878-883`); 0.36.2's linear momentum cannot be written in the new style. The mouse wheel and the scroll bars do not coast. No Tessalith test.
  3. **The scroll bar's interact rect runs along the bar only** (`containers/scroll_area.rs:1443-1459`, an upstream fix: a press beside a short bar is no longer a bar press). No Tessalith test of the rect itself; the `scroll_bars::` tests pass.
  4. **A panel's clip rect is intersected with its parent's** (`containers/panel.rs:841-844`; 0.36.2 replaced it), so a panel inside a narrower window is clipped to the window. Test `egui_gotchas::a_nested_panel_is_clipped_to_its_parent`.
  5. **The TextEdit stroke is drawn inside its margin** (`widgets/text_edit/builder.rs:696-701`), so the focus stroke shifts no layout. With `TEXT_EDIT_MARGIN` a box is 21.28 tall at rest and focused, as on 0.36.2, so nothing a user sees changes. Test `egui_gotchas::text_edit_height_with_and_without_the_036_margin`.
  6. **Glyph rasterising and colour glyphs; Latin metrics unchanged.** Both versions lay a row out in epaint's `text::text_layout::layout` (epaint-0.37.0 `text/text_layout.rs:90`, epaint-0.36.2 `:101`), shape each run with harfrust 0.12 in `shape_text` (0.37 `:1572`, 0.36.2 `:1400`) and take each glyph's advance from the shaper's `x_advance` in `layout_shaped_run` (0.37 `:252`, 0.36.2 `:253`); what 0.37 adds is `layout_raster_run` (`text/text_layout.rs:452`) for glyphs a `GlyphRasterizer` draws (colour glyphs, and a cluster in a family with no font) and vello_cpu 0.2 as the rasteriser (`text/font_face.rs:155`). For Latin text in Segoe UI the advances measured the same: a 14 pt sample line is 360.7 wide in both versions. The Sets column still holds five chips a row at 266 px (`sets_column::tests::the_default_sets_width_holds_five_chips_beside_the_bar`), and the header is one row at 1280, 1215 and 1055 px (JY13) (`header_layout::the_header_is_one_row_from_1280`). Other scripts (CJK, Arabic, emoji) were not measured.
- **JL8 What moved: four tests, and two widgets the doc shots caught; no layout pin's number.** Each layout pin that failed during the upgrade failed on a stale panel reader (JL3) or on the 0.37 style defaults (JL2), and passes with its old number: five chips at 266 px, the header's one row from 1280, the 3D view's 160 px (`nif_edit::{the_meshes_cap_keeps_160_px_at_1280x720, the_preview_keeps_160_px_with_review_open}`), the set preview's centre (`set_preview_layout::{narrow_window_expanded_columns, wide_window_expanded_columns, narrow_window_collapsed_columns, in_3d_the_viewport_takes_most_of_the_centre}`), `column_widths::a_narrow_window_keeps_each_column_at_its_minimum`, `set_cards::{a_card_shows_name_pill_and_a_right_aligned_shader, card_heights_refresh_after_a_resize}` and `checks::a_wide_set_card_keeps_its_one_row_heading`. Four tests changed, each with its cause:
  - **The doc shots moved where the suite did not.** With the suite green, 13 of the 25 doc shots showed a widget moved, resized or reshaped. Two causes, both pinned in JL2: the ComboBox row and the submenu arrow. After them every shot equals its committed 0.36.2 image, with two exceptions that are not egui's: the five popup shots equal it once aligned, because their crop (the new AccessKit nodes' union) is up to 15 px smaller on 0.37 with the same pixels inside, and review-tab differs by 106 px (0.017 %), as a render of the 0.36.2 base commit does: the committed image is older than the base. A Sets card below the fold (never drawn) gets the mean of the measured cards (`sets_column.rs` `card_h` / `est`) in both versions; the 0.36.2 test harness drew its first frame before the fonts went in, with no text size, so eight cards fitted and one kept that frame's height, which no user saw: the app installs its fonts before the first frame (`main.rs`) in both versions.
  - **Tooltips take 4 kittest steps, not 3** (`TOOLTIP_STEPS` in `crates/gui/tests/nif_edit.rs`; `nif_edit::{shader_colour_picker_is_disabled_outside_0_1, quick_export_hover_says_a_foreign_file_opens_review, quick_export_is_off_without_a_folder_and_offers_the_picker, quick_export_is_off_while_an_export_runs}`). A tooltip's first frame is an invisible sizing pass (`containers/area.rs:691-692`, as in 0.36.2), and 0.37 leaves an invisible widget out of the AccessKit tree (`context.rs:655`), so the tooltip's text reaches the tree on step 4, the step it is first painted in both versions. 3 steps fail, 4 pass.
  - **The settings window is drawn at its saved size.** `set_preview_layout::a_saved_window_size_reopens_at_the_same_size` asserted a window drawn larger than saved (706×506 for 700×500); it now asserts the saved size. The growth was a harness artefact: fonts went in after kittest's build passes, a window with no glyphs grew a point a pass, and `Resize` keeps the largest size. With the fonts in before the first frame (JL1) nothing grows. FM31 holds the measured excess and the new test of it.
  - **A link under the clip is scrolled to.** `used_by_meshes::no_scan_database_hides_the_list_and_a_database_reads_it_off_the_ui_thread` scrolls "Show all in Meshes" into view before the click: with real fonts the link sits below the panel's clip.
  - **A pick that starts a re-plan waits for it before running to idle.** `resize_field::moved_rows_write_the_same_files_keys` failed whenever it ran alone ("`Harness::run` exceeded max_steps (4)"), because its `run()` right after the pick raced the worker's plan: 0.37's `Ui::widget_style` asks for a repaint for each widget with no response from the last pass (`theme/mod.rs:32-38`; 0.36.2 has no such repaint), so when the plan landed mid-run its new widgets added steps; `pick_in` now draws one `step()` for the click, and the caller waits (`wait_idle`) before `run()`.
  - Supersedes, in part: FM31 (the Window size bullet's 0.36.2 excess of 3 points). Its old wording is archived.
- **JL9 NIF-team files.** `git diff --name-only` against the base lists `crates/gui/src/ui/meshes.rs`, `crates/gui/src/ui/meshes/{curve, edit_ctrl, edit_extra, edit_fields, edit_panel, skin_preview, txst_preview}.rs` and `crates/gui/tests/meshes_view.rs`. They changed only through the moves above: `egui::WidgetType` to `egui::Role` with the renamed variants of JL1, `Id::new` to `Id::unique`, `ui.id()` to `ui.scope_id()`, the panels meshes-bar, meshes-tree, meshes-edit, meshes-3d-anim and meshes-edit-export built through `panel_ids` and read with `panel_rect` (JL3), `look::thin_scroll()` and `TEXT_EDIT_MARGIN` (JL2), every `ComboBox` shown with `.show_ui_036` and its `use crate::look::Combo036 as _;` in `meshes.rs` and `meshes/{edit_ctrl, edit_extra, edit_fields, txst_preview}.rs` (JL2; a `nif/*` branch that adds a `ComboBox` or a `ui.menu_button` fails the matching source scan in `look::tests` until it goes through `show_ui_036` or `look::menu_button_036`), the Meshes view's names "Scanning mods", "Search paths and mods", "Mod filter", "Filter mods by name" and "Filter textures" (JL4), and, in `meshes_view.rs`, fonts in `with_context_setup` and the tree's width read with `panel_rect`. No NIF crate (`modscan`, `vfs`, `esp`, `nif`, `render3d`, `pgpsim`) changed. The NIF team's handoff file gets one line with what `nif/*` branches must change.
- **JL10 A partial plan patches the skeleton its edit was made on (bug fix, not an egui change).** A quick re-plan after an edit (the partial plan) patched whatever skeleton the session held when its worker thread started. When the edit's own full plan finished first and stored a newer skeleton, the partial found no change on it and could answer "nothing to re-encode" for an edit that needs a full plan. The GUI now reads the skeleton when the edit is made (`AppModel::send_partial` passes it to `worker::spawn_partial`), and the engine patches that one: `Session::replan_sets_on(skeleton, ..)`. `Session::replan_sets` keeps its meaning and calls it with the stored skeleton. In the race window a user now gets the right plan a moment later (the partial answers `NeedsFull` and the full plan lands). The engine API is additive; no output byte changes and the engine version stays. The fix ships with the egui upgrade by controller ruling, because the full test run must pass reliably before a merge; for the owner to overrule (the commits can move to a branch of their own). Tests `crates/engine/tests/fast_replan.rs::a_partial_on_the_edit_time_skeleton_needs_a_full_plan_after_the_edit_is_planned` (deterministic: the edit-time skeleton gives `NeedsFull`, and the control on the stored newer skeleton gives the old `Ok`) and the GUI's `fast_replan::needs_full_waits_for_full`, which failed 3 times in 8 full runs before and passed 20 of 20 after. The GUI test harness's `collect` also drops a partial whose sequence is not past the applied one, as the model does (`fast_replan::export_waits_for_full_after_partial` failed 3 times in 50 runs before). The GUI's `fast_replan::a_partial_patches_the_skeleton_from_the_edit_not_the_stored_one` pins the wiring without ordering threads: it stores the edit's full plan first, then hands `spawn_partial` the edit-time skeleton (`NeedsFull`) and, as its control, the stored one (`Ok`); it fails if `worker.rs` goes back to `replan_sets`. Files: `crates/engine/src/session.rs` (`replan_sets_on`), `crates/gui/src/model/mod.rs` (`send_partial`), `crates/gui/src/worker.rs` (`spawn_partial`, `PartialEdit`), `crates/engine/tests/fast_replan.rs` and `crates/gui/tests/fast_replan.rs` (`collect`).
- **JL11 `render3d`'s dependency test asks about the build's target only (NIF-team file; found by CI, 2026-11-17).** `crates/render3d/tests/deps.rs` runs `cargo metadata --offline`, which by default wants every package in the lock, other platforms' included. The 0.37 lock adds Linux-only crates (`accesskit_atspi_common` among them) that a machine which has only built for Windows never downloads, so the test failed on the hosted CI runner while it passed where the registry holds every crate. The call now passes `--filter-platform x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`, the workspace's one target (`.cargo/config.toml`); what the test pins is unchanged (HX6: `render3d`'s normal and build closure holds `texcore` and neither `engine` nor `encoders-sys`). A test that shells out to `cargo metadata` names its platform. The NIF team's handoff file gets one line.

## Note JM — Fonts: the system's own by family name, a built-in floor (main team, 2026-11-17)

- **JM1 Interface fonts.** `fonts::resolve` builds both families from the OS's table in `fonts.rs` (`WINDOWS`, `LINUX`, `MACOS`); the system lookup runs once per process (`system_definitions`).
  - **Linux** (`generic_first`): fontconfig's own `sans-serif` and `monospace` answers first (the desktop's configured fonts), then the first family found of Noto Sans, DejaVu Sans, Liberation Sans, Cantarell, Ubuntu Sans, Ubuntu, Adwaita Sans, Inter, FreeSans (monospace: DejaVu Sans Mono, Liberation Mono, Noto Sans Mono, Ubuntu Mono). The order is the controller's ruling of 2026-11-16. Monospace takes the proportional system font after its generic answer and its named list.
  - **Windows** (Segoe UI; Consolas): the named family first, the generic family second, then the Linux proportional list above, in that order, before the floor. Fixed-width text takes Consolas, then the Linux monospace list, then the proportional system font, then the generic `monospace` answer, so Segoe UI stands in for a missing Consolas unless a Linux monospace family is installed. The Linux names are in the Windows table because the only build is the Windows one, and under Wine it reads the `WINDOWS` table, so only that table reaches a Linux user (`fonts::tests::{windows_without_segoe_ui_or_arial_takes_a_linux_family, windows_takes_the_linux_families_in_table_order, windows_prefers_arial_to_a_linux_family, windows_fixed_width_takes_a_monospace_family_before_the_stand_in, linux_and_macos_have_no_late_lists, a_generic_first_table_asks_its_late_lists_last}`). Measured 2026-11-17 under Wine 11.0 (a Debian root with DejaVu Sans installed and no Windows font in the prefix): the build before this rule drew the built-in Ubuntu Light, and this one draws the interface in DejaVu Sans, so fontique's DirectWrite back end finds the host's families there. The tests pin the order with fake lookups; fixed-width text was not looked at under Wine.
  - **macOS** (SF Pro Text, Helvetica Neue; Menlo): the named family first, the generic family second. Fixed-width text takes Menlo, then the proportional system font, then the generic answer. On every OS, where a family finds no system font, the floor follows (JM2).
  - **Guards.** A generic answer is used only if it passes `fit_for_ui` (it draws `AZaz09`; `i` and `W` differ in advance for the proportional family and match for the monospace one; a serif face is not detected), else the next step runs. Every face found must parse (`parses`, the `skrifa::FontRef::from_index` call of epaint's `FontFace::new`) or that candidate is skipped.
  - **Windows fallbacks.** Segoe UI Symbol and Segoe UI Emoji follow Segoe UI, and Segoe UI Symbol follows Consolas, as configured fonts (keys `segoeui`, `seguisym`, `seguiemj`, `consola`). They stay configured because for a character with no script the system provider (JM3) asks only fontique's generic families, and on Windows none of those is Segoe UI Symbol (`fontique-0.11.1/src/backend/dwrite.rs:31-41`).
  - **Lookup.** Through fontique (DirectWrite, fontconfig opened at run time, CoreText), memory-mapped. This lets `fonts.rs` start on Linux; it is not a Linux build (the workspace builds for `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` only).
  - **Start time** (fonts installed plus one pass, release, median of five fresh processes, `fonts::tests::prints_the_font_start_time`) on the owner's machine: 14 ms before this note, 3 ms with it. The budget is +100 ms (controller ruling, 2026-11-16).
- **JM2 Built-in floor.** Where a family finds no system font, `tessalith.exe` uses egui's own fonts from `epaint_default_fonts` 0.37.0, embedded: Ubuntu-Light for text, Hack for fixed-width text, egui-icons after both, 674,568 bytes in all (`Ubuntu-Light.ttf` 361,676, `Hack-Regular.ttf` 309,408, `egui-icons.ttf` 3,484). Supersedes N10.
  - **Message.** `main.rs` logs `FontChoice::message()` as a Warning in Messages. It names the case: no usual font (`FLOOR_NO_FONT`), only the interface font fell back (`FLOOR_TEXT_ONLY`), or the system font list could not be read (`FLOOR_LOOKUP_FAILED`). With both families on system fonts there is no message.
  - **Licences.** In `LICENSES/` (`Ubuntu-Font-Licence-1.0.txt`, `Hack-Regular-LICENSE.txt`, `egui-icons-emoji-icon-font-MIT.txt`) and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`; `about.toml` accepts `OFL-1.1` and `Ubuntu-font-1.0`. The owner approved the floor on 2026-11-16 ("fetch ok, embed ok") and its licences the same day ("licence ok, embed ubuntu and hack").
  - **egui-icons (3,484 bytes, MIT) is the third floor font by the controller's ruling of 2026-11-16,** beside Ubuntu-Light and Hack: the floor's chain needs it for egui's own icon glyphs. The owner's approval named Ubuntu and Hack, so it is open for the owner to overrule.
  - **No egui default fonts.** egui's `default_fonts` feature stays off (eframe without its default features, `crates/gui/Cargo.toml`), so a context that never installs fonts has none.
  - **Gaps.** Of the UI's symbols the floor lacks ẞ ⓘ ⚠ ✎ ✓ ✕ ✖ 🔒 🔗 ⏵ (`fonts::tests::the_floor_parses_and_lacks_only_these_ui_symbols`); where the system provider has no font for them either, they draw as boxes.
- **JM3 Missing characters come from the system.**
  - **One provider.** One process-wide `egui_system_font_provider::SystemFontProvider` (`fonts::system_provider`), wrapped in `fonts::Guarded` so a panic answers "no font" from then on and a face that does not parse is dropped (`fonts::tests::a_provider_face_that_does_not_parse_is_dropped`), is installed by `install_system_fonts` with `set_font_providers`, which replaces the context's provider list. `NativeOptions::system_font_fallback` is false (`main.rs`) and eframe's `system_fonts` feature is off, so eframe adds no provider of its own.
  - **What is pinned.** `fonts::tests::install_replaces_other_providers` proves a provider added before an install is never asked. That a second install does not stack a duplicate cannot be pinned by a test: egui keeps its provider list private (`egui-0.37.0/src/context.rs:406`).
  - **When.** A missing glyph is looked up in the frame that needs it; the first lookup waits for the provider's font enumeration thread.
  - **Windows.** The provider turns on epaint's `color_fonts`, so a glyph drawn from Segoe UI Emoji draws in colour. No UI string reaches the provider: every UI symbol resolves in Segoe UI or Segoe UI Symbol (in fixed-width text also Consolas; checked by cmap on the owner's machine), and `fonts::tests::every_ui_symbol_has_a_configured_font_on_windows` fails when a UI symbol lacks a configured font in the proportional or the monospace family. User text (file names) may reach it.
  - **Linux.** The configured chain is one font, so a UI symbol it lacks (⚠ ✓ 🔒 …) goes to the provider. For a character with no script it asks the family's generic (`sans-serif`, or `monospace` for fixed-width text), then `system-ui`, emoji, math, serif and monospace (`egui_system_font_provider-0.37.0/src/lib.rs:137-148`); emoji-presentation symbols such as 🔒 may draw in colour there. That is unmeasured and part of the Linux proof.
  - **Tests (accepted trade-off).** Every kittest harness installs the fonts in `with_context_setup` (JL1), so it asks the shared system provider before kittest's missing-glyph panic: a test string with a character outside the configured fonts passes on a machine that has a system font for it. `fonts::tests::ui_symbols_cover_the_source` still checks every non-ASCII character in `crates/gui/src` (CJK aside) against `UI_SYMBOLS`, so each new UI character meets the floor-gap pin (JM2) and the Windows configured-font pin above.
- **JM4 No CJK font at start.** `msyh.ttc`, `YuGothM.ttc` and `malgun.ttf` (47,071,940 bytes, 47.1 MB) are not read into memory at start (`fonts::tests::cjk_comes_from_the_system_without_eager_loads`). Chinese, Japanese and Korean come from the provider, memory-mapped when first drawn, in the font the system's fallback order gives for the locale, as native apps do; for Han the pick can also depend on which lookups came first (controller ruling 2026-11-16). In the test run on the owner's machine (system locale en-US) it picked `system:Yu Gothic UI` for Han and kana and `system:Malgun Gothic` for Hangul. Supersedes BR13 (Microsoft YaHei first for Han).
- **JM5 Start never fails on fonts.** `install_system_fonts` returns `Result<FontChoice, Infallible>` and applies JL2's `look::pin_036_style` (`fonts::tests::install_still_applies_the_pinned_look`). A font file that does not parse is skipped per candidate (`a_broken_font_file_is_skipped`), and a lookup that panics falls to the floor (`a_lookup_that_panics_falls_back_to_the_floor`); no font failure shows a dialog or stops the start.
  - **A panic caught on purpose is not a crash (controller ruling 2026-11-16).** `fonts::guarded` and `fonts::Guarded` catch through `crash_log::catch_expected`, which holds its label in the `EXPECTED` thread-local while it runs. The panic hook sees the label, appends one line to the run's crash log (`<stamp> font lookup failed (caught, Note JM) in thread '<name>' at <location>: <message>`, no backtrace) and returns: it writes no recovery files, so no restore offer follows. Test `fonts_crash_hook::a_caught_font_panic_writes_no_recovery_file` (its own `[[test]]`, with the real hook installed).
  - A panic on the provider's own enumeration thread is outside `catch_expected`, so the hook logs its normal crash line there; that thread is not `main`, so it writes no recovery files either.
- **JM6 Test seam.** `SDC_FONT=embedded` (exactly that value, `fonts::floor_forced`) installs the floor; the provider stays on. Only builds with the `test-hooks` feature read it. Tests `fonts::tests::only_sdc_font_embedded_forces_the_floor` and `fonts_env::sdc_font_embedded_installs_the_floor` (its own `[[test]]`). No layout test uses it yet. Open: whether kittest's missing-glyph panic passes on the floor (for a character with no script the provider never looks in Segoe UI Symbol, JM1); the first test that adopts the seam answers it.
- **JM7 Pins.** `fonts::tests::{segoe_ui_is_the_same_file_as_before, text_is_as_wide_as_before, ui_symbols_resolve_to_the_same_fonts_as_before}` pin Windows against `legacy_definitions()`, a copy of the chain before this note kept in the test module, so they catch drift from that copy, not from the old build. Only the doc shots compare against the old build's pixels: all 25 re-rendered unchanged (0.000 %). The `UI_SYMBOLS` scan covers `fonts.rs` up to its test module, so the `FLOOR_*` messages are inside the floor-gap pin. `slot_alpha::alpha_w_fits_the_longest_label` and the Sets width pin (JJ2, `sets_column::tests::the_default_sets_width_holds_five_chips_beside_the_bar`) pass unchanged.

## Note JN — A borrow follows its texture (owner addition B2; main team, 2026-11-17)

- **JN1 Chains are followed (B2-2).**
  - `entry_view` follows a lender's own `borrow.slotN` to the set that owns the file. A `seen` guard starts at the borrower's own id, so a cycle and a self-borrow fill nothing. A cycle reads "(it has none)". A self-borrow (a hand edit only) keeps the set's own file, and its card line reads "(this set itself)" (`Missing::Borrow.itself`; t1).
  - `SlotFill::Borrowed.from` names the set that owns the file. `Missing::Borrow.from` keeps the entry's own line.
  - Own-first (planner's call): a lender with its own file and a borrow line lends its own, as a single hop did.
  - `splice_views` rebuilds every hop.
  - A project written before JN3 can hold a chain: a Share replace wrote one.
  - HH8's filter reads the resolved fill, so a chain answers "needs" only when the kind fits (B1-5, B1-6).
  - **Exports change for a project that already holds a chain (B2-10, intended).** The far end of a chain was empty and is now filled, so its PGPatcher JSON gains the `slotN` line, it gains its vanilla shared-slot copy, and `borrowed_normal` gains the entry (its roughness bakes from the source's normal). The far end also counts as a user of the file (`owners_of` counts a shared fill; final review Important 1, controller 2026-11-17): when it is a PBR set that comes before the file's other PBR users in the Sets column, the file's CS PBR output path moves to the far end's prefix. The file is written at the new path, the old file stays on disk, and the other sharing sets' JSON `slotN` lines follow the new path. A hand-edited self-borrow no longer sends `X → X` to the engine.
- **JN2 A Move carries the sharing sets (B2-1).**
  - The drop dialog's Move, a file dropped on a slot card, the wet/detail Assign, Fill from names and a new set from a file: every set sharing that slot from the file's old set now shares it from the new one, in the same undo step (`SetsDoc::retarget_slot_borrows`, as HF4/HS13 do for a rename).
