  - Only for the file the old set lends in that slot (`lent_slot`: the one a borrow of that slot number resolves to; on slots 3 and 7 one of the two kinds).
  - Locked slots are rewritten.
  - The receiving set drops its own line.
- **JN3 A Share follows the slot (B2-1b).**
  - A Share into a set's slot (replacing its file, replacing a share, or into an empty slot; planner's call for the last two) passes that slot's sharing sets to the new source's set. The source drops its own line. No chain is written.
  - A Move, Duplicate or Fill replace leaves them on the lender, with the new file.
- **JN4 The count (B2-3, B2-9).**
  - When Delete set or Remove from set leaves shares empty, the toast names the sets. The list is taken before the edit, in the function that runs it (`sets_sharing`, called by `delete_counted` and `remove_member`). The shares stay (HE3), and nothing is refused. In a toast, because `counts_status` rewrites the status line after every plan.
  - What counts: an explicit share (not a record fill, HG7) that resolves, through a chain too, to a file that leaves, while the lender keeps no other member of that slot number (B2-8). Not counted: a set with no card, and a sharing set with a file of its own in that slot number, which fills the slot again (final review Important 2).
  - Strings as built (`lost_share_text`): `{who} shared {what}; that slot is empty now.`, with `those slots are empty now` for more than one (set, slot) pair. `{who}` is the set's id, or `{k} sets`; for `{k} sets` the sentence ends `empty now: a, b and c.` (at most three ids, then `and N more`). Delete set appends it to its first sentence with `{what}` = the file names, sorted (`Deleted set landscape/rock01 (4 textures now in no set). 2 sets shared rock01_normal.png and rock01_rmaos; those slots are empty now: landscape/leaf and landscape/odd.`). Remove from set's toast is `{name} is in no set now. ` followed by it, with `{what}` = `it` when the removed file is the only one lost, else the file names.
  - Packs: a packed output stops filling its slot when its last raw map leaves its set (`last_raw_map_of`: every other raw map of the pack is in no set already). So Remove from set of that raw map counts the sets sharing the pack too, and the toast names the packed output (`rock01_ao.png is in no set now. landscape/odd shared rock01_rmaos; that slot is empty now.`). Delete set counts every member, packs included.
  - **A share through a deleted set is passed on (B2-11, controller 2026-11-17).** On Delete set, for each slot number where the deleted set has a share line to a set X and no file of its own, the sets sharing that slot from the deleted set are rewritten to X (`retarget_slot_borrows`), in the same undo step. Nothing is lost, so the toast says nothing about it. Where the deleted set has its own file in that slot number, the hand-over (JN5) covers its sharing sets instead, or, when no set can take the file, the lost-share sentence names them; the two rewrites never touch the same slot number. Only a hand edit or an older project holds this state.
  - **Clearing a share that passes a chain on (B2-12, controller 2026-11-17).** When a set's share line for slot N is cleared (**Clear** or **From this set** on slots 1 to 8, `set_slot_command`; **Clear** on the skin `_wet` and `_detail_n`, `clear_slot_borrow`) and the set has no file of its own in that slot number, the sets sharing slot N from it are rewritten to the set its line named (`passed_on`, `retarget_slot_borrows`), in the same undo step and with no toast: the far set keeps its texture. A record fill turned off (HG) has no line and passes nothing on.
- **JN5 The hand-over (B2-6, owner 2026-11-17: "Option 1, hand it over"; B2-7 and B2-8, controller 2026-11-17).**
  - A file Delete set or Remove from set would send to No set, and that other sets share by an explicit line, goes to the first of them in the Sets column's order that can take it, as its own file. The others share it from there. The lender itself is never a candidate.
  - It is one undo step, and the toast says where it went: `{name} went to {heir}, which shared it.`, or `…, which shared it; 1 other set now shares it from there.` / `…; {k} other sets now share it from there.` (`handoff_text`; the count is of entries whose line named the lender, skipped ones and sets with no view included). Delete set appends one such sentence per handed file, in slot order, then JN4's sentence.
  - B2-7, cannot take it: its slot is locked; it has its own file in that slot number (either kind of slot 3 or 7); the kinds clash (`slot_conflict`); it has no view, or no files and was not created. The heir is picked from the views, so a set with no view is never one.
  - B2-8: only when the lender keeps no file in that slot after the edit; otherwise the sharing sets stay on the lender and nothing is said.
  - With no taker, the file goes to No set (JN4). A packed map is never handed over and never put in No set: it leaves with its raw maps (HF1).
  - The lender's own share line for that slot, when it names the lender itself (a hand edit), is dropped, not rewritten to the heir: the lender would share back the file it gave away (t5 Minor 5). The count of other sets leaves it out.
  - A handed file is never a pack's last raw map (a pack's raw maps have no slot), so a Remove from set with a hand-over lists no lost shares and its toast is the hand-over sentence alone.
  - A read-only `sdc-project.toml` refuses the edit before anything is handed (`sets_editable`).
  - Exported files can change, as after any Move: the file's PBR path follows its first PBR user, the heir's JSON slot line and vanilla copy go, and a handed diffuse makes the heir write its `.csskin.json`.
  - Amends HF1.
- **JN6 Not done and known limits.**
  - B2-4 (store borrows by file) is not chosen: it is a format change, and a recipe change renames a packed key (`_rmaos` ↔ `_rfaos`) while set and slot stay.
  - `shortcut:` these leave the share stale with no count, and the card's **?** says so. Upgrade if a user loses a share that way:
    - a type change that moves the file to another slot or makes it a helper;
    - a packed output whose raw maps move;
    - files renamed on disk outside the GUI;
    - a lent file deleted on disk: after the next plan the lender no longer has it, each sharing set's slot shows the red **?** ("(it has none)") with **Remove from set** and a Problems row, and no toast names the sets.
  - A packed output whose raw maps are not members of its set keeps lending until the set's card goes; its sharing sets then go empty with no toast. No fixture reaches this today: a pack whose raw maps are moved to another set follows them (final fix wave probe).
  - **Remove from set** on a share line that fills nothing (`remove_missing`) passes nothing on, unlike B2-12 (JN4): the sets sharing through that line showed nothing before and show nothing after.
  - A written cycle (m6): a Share into a slot can point a set X at the new source S while S's own hand-edited line for that slot still names X. Only a hand edit makes a set own a file and share the same slot. The view-time walk stops on the cycle: S lends its own file, X fills from S, and S's card shows "(it has none)" for its line. Not cleared automatically.
  - A set that shares a slot from itself is reported, not cleared: JN1.
  - A lender with two files of one kind in a slot cannot be tested (their outputs collide).

## Note JO — Problems and Notes, Mark as intended, readiness (review E, plan 3a; controller for the owner, 2026-11-16)

Review E's problems plan (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-16-review-e-problems.md`). The source of the numbered rulings is spec 7a, "Controller rulings for Plan 3a" (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-16-egui037-review-e-design.md`; controller, acting for the owner, 2026-11-16; the owner may overrule), quoted here word for word: "(1) A mark follows a set rename: the subject is renamed and the sentence is unchanged, so the mark holds. No engine-code sentence of a set row names the set id (plan survey §2, round 2), so no data-keyed mark path is built. A PGPatcher-pitfall row starts with the set id (`pbr_export.rs:606`): when the row's subject is the set, the stored key sentence leaves that leading id out, so the mark still follows the rename. (2) A row holds at most two fixes, drawn main fix, second fix, **Mark as intended**, then **Go to set**. (3) DERIVE-NIF-SUGGEST keeps its last-export File row at Warning level and gets Mark. (4) The Also-only PACK-UNUSED-INPUT case runs before the general note rule and keeps today's row text. (5) A note with a button stays in the default list, after the problems, and never counts; a marked row leaves the default list even when it has a fix. (6) The codeless complex-grass row stays an Info note with **Tag as grass**; it counts in neither readiness. (7) Mark is for warnings only: an Error- or Blocking-severity code gets none, even on a last-export File row. (8) No bulk mark: hiding a kind already covers "all of these" per project. (9) Stale marks stay, and a rewrite keeps entries the app cannot read, in place; only deleting a set removes that set's marks. (10) The filter pills and the card's status hover both say what Ready means. (11) Go to set outlines nothing for SET-COMPANION-DDS, DERIVE-DDS-SOURCE and CHK-PARALLAX-SCALE. (12) The tab-count cache is tested by counting row scans. Unnumbered: SHADER-UNAVAILABLE is a Problem with **Change shader…**; SUG-CONTRADICTION offers the fix its sentence names; the app gets one reduced-motion switch (`ui/motion.rs`), which Plan 4 also reads." Items cite them as "ruling N". "Ledger" marks a controller ruling made while the plan ran (2026-11-16/17); "planner's call" marks the plan's own choice. No engine code, message, encoded byte or encode version changes. Tests: `crates/gui/tests/problems_notes.rs`, `sets_model.rs`.

- **JO1 A row is a Problem or a Note.** `code_text::row_class` classes every engine code and has no `_` arm, so a new code does not build until it is classed. Notes are the Info codes except CHK-GREEN-GUESS and DERIVE-NIF-SUGGEST, plus DIM-NPOT, ENC-WEIGHTS-IGNORED, PACK-DEFAULTED, PACK-UNUSED-INPUT and SHADER-EXTRA-MAP (Tessalith applied a documented default or choice and the output is valid); every other code is a Problem (the output may be wrong or a file is not written), Blocking and Error codes included, which keep their own rows. `model::makes_a_row` (Warning, Error or Blocking severity), plus CHK-GREEN-GUESS and DERIVE-NIF-SUGGEST, the two Info results with an action, decides whether a warning makes a row, and `counts_toward_badge` (the tile's ⚠, the "Has warnings" pill and filter) is `makes_a_row` and Problem-class by code; `counts_for` narrows it by the job (JP6). GRASS-DETECT-SOURCE is classed by its job, not its code: a Note while the written file is renormalised, a Problem under `mips.normal_base = keep` (`bottom::warning_class`, JP6).
- **JO2 A note says why the output is fine.** A Note-class warning makes an Info row with `code_text::note_sentence`, built the same way for a texture, a set and a last-export File warning: PACK-DEFAULTED from `Warning.data` (output file name, channel, the slot's default and why it is fine), PACK-UNUSED-INPUT from the message ("{name} isn't used: {reason}. Nothing else changes."), SHADER-EXTRA-MAP from `data.member` and `data.shader`, DIM-NPOT and ENC-WEIGHTS-IGNORED from their code sentence; a message or `data` without what the sentence needs falls back to the engine's text. The map notes still name the map, so they edit HC2: PACK-DEFAULTED, PACK-UNUSED-INPUT and SHADER-EXTRA-MAP. The action sentences of CHK-Z-SIGN ("… — use Rebuild Z"), DIM-FLOW-SMALL ("… or mark it as intended") and SUG-CONTRADICTION ("The file name and the contents point to different types. Set the right one.") name the buttons JO15 and JO4 add.
- **JO3 The tab label, the worklist and the Notes pill.** The tab reads `Problems` with no row at all, else `Problems (n)` or `Problems (n · h hidden)`, then ` · Notes (m)` when m > 0, so only notes read `Problems (0) · Notes (m)` (`bottom::tab_label`): n counts the problem rows not hidden, h the hidden problem rows, m the notes not hidden; a hidden note counts nowhere. A note is an Info row, a queued row (JO9) or a marked row (`Problem::is_note`); the default list and the Set filter list the problem rows, then the notes with a button after them with their info dot (ruling 5, `Problem::in_worklist`), so every SHADER-EXTRA-MAP note, which always has a button (JO15), sits there (ledger); a marked row and a note without a button are listed only under the **Notes (m)** pill. Supersedes FC3's "the Problems tab number stays the count of every row shown, Check and Info rows included", and edits JI6.
  - The Notes pill toggles Notes, or the set's notes under a Set filter, as the Checks pill does; it stays drawn while on, even at m = 0, so it can be turned off (ledger). Hover: "Rows that need nothing from you: what a default did, and rows you marked as intended. They never count against Ready. Notes with a button also show in the main list."
  - With no problem row, m > 0 and no hidden line (JO6), the first line of the default list or a Set filter reads "No problems. Notes ({m}) lists what Tessalith did for you." (m: the set's notes under a Set filter), unless every row of the list is hidden.
- **JO4 Mark as intended.** A markable row (below) shows **Mark as intended** (hover "Keep it as it is. The row moves to Notes and stops counting against Ready. Undo brings it back.", accessible name "Mark as intended: {row}"); a marked row, under Notes, shows **Undo mark** ("Move this row back to Problems."). Each is one undo step, "Mark as intended: {row}" or "Undo mark: {row}", with no plan, since a mark changes no output. NIF Warning rows get it too (planner's call). Supersedes FM24's "no Acknowledge" for warnings (FM24 edited).
  - **Storage.** `[project] intended` in `sdc-project.toml`: an array of inline tables `{ code, subject, sentence }`. The subject is `texture:{item key}`, `set:{set id}`, `file:{job key}`, `nif:{path}` or `project`; the sentence is the row's shown sentence after `sets_doc::normalise_sentence` (trimmed, whitespace runs made one space, and on a `set:` subject a leading `"{set id}: "` naming that set left out, so the PGPatcher pitfall rows' marks follow a rename too). The key part of a `texture:` or `file:` subject is folded as `[files]` keys are (Note BF, Unicode case folding) on store and on lookup; the sentence is kept verbatim (final fix wave R4). A changed sentence shows the row again (spec 7a, correction 3).
  - **Kinds.** A row with a code is keyed by its code. A codeless row is keyed by a kind named where it is built (planner's table): `NIF-WARNING`, `PGP-PITFALL`, `SET-MISSING-ENTRY`, `RECORD-CHANGED`, `XPMF-SKIP`, `SKIN-SKIP`, `READ-ONLY-FLAG`, and `CFG-PRESET-SKIPPED` for the two skipped-preset rows (ledger). A renamed kind orphans the marks stored under it, so the kinds are one list, `bottom::kind`, pinned by a test (final fix wave B3).
  - **Rename and delete.** A set rename moves the set's marks to the new id (`rename_refs`), so they hold (ruling 1); a rename onto a set that holds an equal mark leaves two equal entries (ledger). Deleting a set removes its marks.
  - **Off.** With no project open ("Open a project folder first"), with the sets document read-only (its reason), and when `[project] intended` is there but is not an array ("sdc-project.toml's intended entry isn't a list: fix or delete it in the file first", ledger).
  - **Warnings only (ruling 7).** `bottom::markable`: the row's level is Warning or Check and its code, if it has one, is Warning or Info severity. An Error- or Blocking-severity code gets no Mark, even on a last-export File row at `Level::Warning`; such a row counts until the next export.
  - **Stale marks (ruling 9).** A mark whose row is gone stays, so a file that comes back keeps its mark; a write edits the array in place and keeps the entries the app can't read where and as they were; the new first entry is tidied (`tidy_first`) only when the old first entry was removed, so a hand-written first entry keeps its spacing when a later mark goes (final fix wave A5).
  - **Marked rows in hovers (final fix wave A6).** A tile's hover, a file's warning list in the Files tab and a set card's check badge hover list the unmarked warnings or results, then the marked ones under "Marked as intended:" (`bottom::MARKED_LINE`); on a tile or a Files row with none unmarked, only that line and the marked ones (a card with every result marked has no check badge to hover, JO5). When every warning on a Files row is marked, its weak `{k} marked` label carries that hover (JY18). `bottom::marked` is false for a code that is not Warning or Info severity before it looks for a key, as `markable` is. It is false too for a warning whose row is a Note (`class_at`, JP6), which has no Mark.
  - **No bulk mark (ruling 8).** Hiding a kind already covers "all of these" per project.
- **JO5 Readiness is one rule.** `bottom::counts_for(m, at, w)`: a warning takes Ready away when it counts toward the badge (JO1), is no artist check and has no mark on the sentence its row shows, and is a Problem for its job (`class_at`, JP6); the tiles (`tile_ready`, `item_warned`: the ⚠, the "Has warnings" pill and filter, the Status sort) and the set cards' ⚠ read it, and the rows read `counts_as_ready_problem` (not a Check, Info or Queued row, not marked). A marked check leaves the card's check badge, the Checks pill and the export line's check count (`counts_as_check`, `check_listed`, `checks_summary`; ledger, final fix wave A6), and checks still never change Ready (owner answer 1). The Files count and Has warnings filter share only the mark predicate (`marked_code`, shared by `marked` and `export_warning_marked`) and continue counting Info and Note-class export warnings, which `counts_for` leaves out (JY18).
  - The codeless complex-grass row stays an Info note with **Tag as grass**: it offers a choice and asks nothing the export needs, so it counts in neither the rows' nor the tiles' readiness (ruling 6).
  - DERIVE-NIF-SUGGEST is a Problem with Mark. Its last-export File row is at `Level::Warning`, so it counts against Ready; marking it is the way to clear it (ruling 3).
  - The filter pills and the card's status hover say what Ready means (ruling 10): "Ready: no problem row in the set or its textures. Notes, rows you marked as intended and checks don't count." and "Not ready: a problem row in the set or one of its textures. Notes, rows you marked as intended and checks don't count." (`sets_column::READY_MEANS`, `NOT_READY_MEANS`), after "Only sets that are ready." / "Only sets that are not ready." on the pills.
- **JO6 Hidden kinds in the tab and the first line.** The tab leaves hidden problem rows out of n and shows them as "· h hidden" (edits HC4's "My calls"); Ready and Review still ignore hiding. While the default list or a Set filter hides rows and the project hides a problem row, its first line reads "{k kind(s)} hidden in this project ({h row(s)}). Hidden rows still count for Ready.", with the project's numbers; k counts the hidden kinds that hide at least one problem row (`TabCounts::hidden_kinds`, ledger), and `Kinds ▾ ({n} hidden)` stays this list's own count, notes included (ledger). An empty list that hid rows reads "Every problem here is hidden. Open Kinds ▾ to show them." only when it hid a problem row, else "Every row here is hidden — see Kinds" (ledger); an empty list that hid nothing reads "No problems" only when the list has no notes (with notes, JO3's "No problems. Notes ({m})…" line shows first), or "No notes" under the Notes pill.
  - The `Kinds ▾` hover reads "Hide kinds of row you don't need in this project. Blocks and questions always show. A hidden row still counts for Ready, and the tab shows how many are hidden." Supersedes JF4's hover text (JF4 edited).
- **JO7 The set editor's status line.** The editor's first line sits above its scroll area in 2D (above the heading in 3D), so it stays in sight (amends FB2's one scrolling column; the digest line is edited). It reads "Ready: nothing in this set needs you." with a green dot, or "Not ready:" with an amber dot and up to three reasons (`STATUS_REASONS`) as buttons, then a "+k more in Problems" link; a set `export_blocks` names draws the red dot, as its card does (ledger). Supersedes CA18's one line "N problems in this set - Show in Problems".
  - **Reasons.** `bottom::cached_set_reasons` takes the resolved view and reads the set's and its members' rows that count against Ready (JO5), hidden kinds included, in list order; a ready set scans no row. A SHADER-MISSING-MAP whose slot row the editor draws for the set (`sets::editor_cells`) reads "no {slot label} (Assign…)" and goes to the slot (`ReasonGo::Slot`); any other row reads its code sentence up to " — ", or a codeless row's text cut at 60 characters with "…", and goes to its Problems row (ledger). Two shown reasons with one text each add their subject, "{text} ({subject})", in the text, the accessible name and the hover (ledger); the name is "{text} — go to the fix", the hover "Go to the control that fixes this." plus "This kind is hidden; this shows it again." on a hidden kind's row. A codeless text cut at 60 characters keeps its whole sentence in the accessible name ("{full} — go to the fix") and as the hover's first line (final fix wave R1). A slot label with its own brackets loses them in a reason: "no Emissive / Skin, slot 3 (Assign…)", not "no Emissive / Skin (slot 3) (Assign…)" (`unnested`, A11). The editor caches reasons in `ViewCache` by the problems Arc, the resolved view's canonical document id and the hidden kinds. A change of views clears the cache (JV1).
  - **A set with no engine key.** A key-less editor reads its resolved view's rows, even for an empty id. Its card hover reads that card's readiness. Its badges open a Problems filter using JP5's scope key and JR11's lookup order. With a row that counts against Ready it reads "Not ready:" and its reasons, as any set does; with none it reads "Not ready: this set has no files yet." with no reason buttons, as its card's hover does (`sets_column::NO_FILES_YET`, `bottom::no_files_yet`; JP5). Its dot is amber, or red when the set blocks the export.
  - **A slot reason** runs Go to set with that slot (JO8) and gives keyboard focus to the slot's **Assign…**; clicked in the 3D view it first switches the editor to 2D, saved as the 2D | 3D switch saves it (ledger).
  - **A row reason** shows its kind again if hidden, opens Problems on the set's filter, scrolls the list to the row and gives keyboard focus to the first control the row reads among its fix, second fix and **Mark as intended** (never its link or Go to set) when that control is enabled. When it is disabled or the row has none, the row itself takes focus, unless Enter or Space on the row would decide (a texture row with a suggestion or an alpha question, `row_decides`): then nothing takes focus (ledger).
  - **"+k more in Problems"** shows the hidden kinds of the rows counted in the editor's resolved view, including slot reasons. It then opens the ordinary set filter using JP5's scope key and JR11's lookup order (JV5).
- **JO8 Go to set lands on its slot.** A Problems row's **Go to set** calls `AppModel::go_to_target(target, slot)` with the row's `SetTarget`. When the row names a slot or member, the editor receives a `model::nav::RevealSlot` with `set: SetTarget` and `target: SlotTarget`. The slot target is `Slot(kind)` for SHADER-MISSING-MAP (`sets::slot_for_shader_map` of `data.map`: albedo, diffuse and skin_albedo to Diffuse, the rest through `map_slot`, 2 Normal, 3 Emissive, 4 Height, 6 RMAOS, else none; ledger), `Item(member)` for SHADER-EXTRA-MAP when `data.member` names one (with none the row has no target for the outline: **Change shader…** is its only fix, and **Go to set** still shows), and none for every other row. With a texture preview expanded, `go_to_target` collapses it first, so the editor draws and the reveal runs at once (final fix wave A2). The plan table's DERIVE-NO-ALBEDO row (to the Diffuse slot) does not occur today: no producer reaches that arm, which is kept with a comment (ledger).
  - **The reveal** (`ui::row_reveal`). On the editor's next 2D draw of that set the row is scrolled to at once, centred, and outlined 2 px outside in the selection colour, at full strength for `REVEAL_SECS` = 2.0 s, then fading over `REVEAL_FADE_SECS` = 0.12 s, or ending at 2.0 s with no fade under reduced motion. A slot target matches the card that shows either kind of its slot (`SlotKind::shares_card`, slots 3 and 7). A target the editor draws no row for selects the set and outlines nothing; a waiting reveal is dropped by `select_item` and `select_set`, in the 3D view and on another set (ledger). A newly selected set opens at its top, and the reveal's scroll wins over that (ledger). Nothing moves keyboard focus or announces the row on Go to set (ledger).
  - **Reduced motion** (`ui::motion`, the unnumbered ruling). `reduced(ctx)` is true when the OS asks for no animations or the style's `animation_time` is 0. `install`, run from `restore_ctx` before the first frame, reads the OS value once (Windows `SPI_GETCLIENTAREAANIMATION`: animations off means reduced; a failed call reads as not reduced) and, when reduced, sets `animation_time = 0.0` and turns off egui's animated scrolling on both themes (ledger). It is true in every kittest harness (kittest sets `animation_time` to 0). FM25's "no Reduce motion setting" stands: there is no UI setting; Plan 4 reads the switch.
- **JO9 A Rebuild Z waiting for the export is a queued note.** A last-export File row of CHAN-NORMAL-LENGTH whose live item's resolved job has `channels.reconstruct_z` on (so a preset that turns it on counts too) is a `Level::Queued` row reading "Rebuild Z is on for this file: export again to rewrite it", with a green "queued" dot and no fix, so it is listed under Notes only. It never counts toward Ready, cannot be hidden (`can_hide` is false for it, ledger) and goes with the next export; an exported short normal without Rebuild Z stays a warning with **Rebuild Z**. Supersedes FC3's last-export row that keeps **Rebuild Z** greyed (DP7d).
- **JO10 A row's buttons follow its sentence.** Each Problems row is laid out in a wrapper as wide as its dot, link and sentence (`lead_width`), the gaps, the buttons' width measured on the previous frame (kept in temp data under the row's id) and an 8 px margin (`ROW_MARGIN`), capped at the list's width; a row's first measure, and any row wider than the list, is full width, where the buttons are laid out right first and the sentence is cut to the room left. When the measured and stored widths differ by more than 0.5 px the row asks for one repaint. A row measured for the first time that would be narrower than the list discards that pass (`ctx.request_discard`, as egui's `Grid` does on its first measure), so it is first shown with its buttons in place, with no one-frame jump when egui allows the extra pass (one discard a frame: a row first measured on a frame that already discarded shows at full width for that frame); when the pass is discarded, the `focus_row` hand-off it took is put back for the kept pass (final fix wave A1). The narrow-row guard is bounded by the window as well as the panel (A12). The row is drawn by `row_ui`; `lead_width` and the row share `link_text` and `status::DOT_SIZE` (C4). Edits the CA11 digest line.
- **JO11 Counts read "1 kind", "2 kinds".** `code_text::counted(n, one, many)` writes the hidden line's kinds and rows (JO6), the header Export hover ("1 file, 1 problem"; "{files} + Vanilla folder, {problems}" with an Also tick), the Files tab's per-row warning count ("1 warning") and the 3D view's notes header ("1 note"), and (final fix wave A7) the bulk panel's "{n} textures selected", the reset dialog's "Reset all settings of {n} textures?", the CS PBR "Used by {n} meshes", the CS PBR export's file count, the PBR export's "({n} entries)", the export review's counts (`model/review.rs` has no `plural` of its own; `ui/review.rs` still has one) and the file, texture and set counts in `status_line_counts` (JY8); the plan bar's "Analysing {done} / {total} files…" picks "file" for one by hand, because it keeps a thousands separator. The Sets column's "{k} of {n} sets" is unchanged: a group header is placed only for a group with a visible member, so k ≥ 1, and k = n = 1 already reads "1 set". NIF-team files keep their own plurals.
- **JO12 Shared files and the tab-count cache.** This plan changes no NIF-team file: nothing under `modscan`, `vfs`, `esp`, `nif`, `render3d`, `pgpsim`, `ui/meshes` or `model/meshes`. Two shared hook files (CONCURRENCY.md) gain lines: `crates/gui/src/ui/mod.rs` declares `motion` and `row_reveal`, and `crates/gui/src/model/mod.rs` adds `makes_a_row`, changes `counts_toward_badge` (JO1), adds the tab-count cache and the mark and Go to set methods, and clears `nav.reveal_slot` in `select_item` and `select_set`; nothing under `ui/meshes` or `model/meshes` calls the changed functions. `bottom::cached_tab_counts` keeps the counted `Arc` and compares with `Arc::ptr_eq`, so a new list at a freed address never matches (planner's call), and a test counts its row scans (ruling 12).
- **JO13 Two fixes at most.** A row holds `fix`, then `second_fix`, drawn in that order before **Mark as intended** and **Go to set** (ruling 2). The Also-only PACK-UNUSED-INPUT row keeps its text, "Read only by Also export; not written on its own", with no code and no button: its case runs before the general note rule (ruling 4).
- **JO14 Rows Go to set does not outline.** SET-COMPANION-DDS (a texture row, no Go to set), DERIVE-DDS-SOURCE (its reason names every DDS of the set) and CHK-PARALLAX-SCALE (a set value, no slot or file) select the set and outline nothing (ruling 11).
- **JO15 The fixes this note adds.** SHADER-UNAVAILABLE (a Problem; the unnumbered ruling) and SHADER-MISSING-MAP get **Change shader…**, a menu of the target's shaders by the names the Sets column's combo shows, the set's own selected (picking it again is no edit), off with `fix_off` or the sets document's read-only reason. SHADER-EXTRA-MAP gets **Remove from set** for the member `data.member` names (its output or source file name), then **Change shader…**; with no such member, **Change shader…** alone. When that member is the set's packed output, the row offers no **Remove from set** (a packed output cannot leave its set): on cs with a `packing.recipe` other than `auto` written on the set's packed keys it offers **Clear recipe**, then **Change shader…**; otherwise **Change shader…** alone (JR10). SUG-CONTRADICTION gets **Set type…**, the Undecided rows' picker with no suggestion (the unnumbered ruling); ALPHA-PARALLAX-NO-ALPHA gets **Turn parallax off** (`alpha.parallax = false`); the CHK-Z-SIGN check row gets **Rebuild Z**. Every **Rebuild Z** button is named "Rebuild Z for {file}" (accessible name; the visible text stays **Rebuild Z**), so buttons of different files differ; one texture's CHAN-NORMAL-LENGTH and CHK-Z-SIGN buttons share that name and do the same thing (final fix wave R5); once Rebuild Z is on, its disabled hover is JO9's queued sentence (`bottom::QUEUED_REBUILD_Z`).
  - **Remove from set** is disabled only by the sets document's read-only reason, then by `remove_member_refusal`, the refusals of `remove_member` (a packed output, a set or member no longer there, a locked slot: "slot {n} of {set} is locked; unlock it first"); a read-only `sdc.toml` does not stop it (ledger). It runs `remove_member`, so the file goes where Remove member sends it: to the first set that shares it and can take it, with JN5's hand-over toast, else to No set, with JN4's toast when shares are left empty. Its hover reads "Take {file} out of this set. It goes to No set, or to the first set that shares it." (final fix wave A8).
  - CHK-Z-SIGN is not raised while the job's `channels.reconstruct_z` is on (`model::checks`), so Rebuild Z clears its row (ledger).
  - GRASS-DETECT-SOURCE has no button (the engine raises it only on a texture already tagged complex grass; overrules the plan table's **Tag as grass**, ledger); it is a Note while the written file is renormalised and a Problem, which **Mark as intended** clears, under `mips.normal_base = keep` (JP6). Its sentence follows the job (`bottom::warning_text`, final fix wave A3): with the job's `mips.normal_base` renormalising, the row reads "This grass source's corner normal isn't unit length — the written file is renormalised, so Community Shaders still reads it as grass"; with `mips.normal_base = keep` the file is written as it is and the row reads "This grass source's corner normal isn't unit length, and its normal base level is kept as authored — Community Shaders won't read the written file as grass" (`code_text::GRASS_SOURCE_KEPT`). Under `keep` the row, its mark and the tile's count read the same sentence; while the file is renormalised the row is a Note, which reads no mark and no count (JP6).
- **JO16 Fixes that remove or switch off run on a pointer click only.** The missing entry's **Remove from set** or **Delete set** (RemoveMissing), the extra member's **Remove from set**, **Remove match**, **Turn off {feature}** and **Turn parallax off** are drawn with `click_only_button`, as are **Clear read-only flag** and **Remove aliases from {id}**: Enter or Space on the focused button does nothing, so a status line reason that focuses one never deletes (FC5; ledger). The fixes that add, assign, rebuild or mark (**Assign…**, **Rebuild Z**, **Use new paths**, **Keep alpha** (JS7), **Mark as intended** and the like) keep Enter and Space, and so does the unmatched-entries row's **Remove**, since a project row is never a reason (ledger).

## Note JP — A vanilla normal in a PBR set is one note, not two warnings; key-less sets and the grass source's class (owner, 2026-11-17)

Owner addition B3 (plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-17-plain-normal-in-pbr.md`) and two Plan 3a follow-ups it carries, F6 and F1. The rulings B3-1 to B3-28 are in `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-17-owner-additions.md` ("Addition B3", "B3 after the survey", "Rulings on F1 and F6 in B3"); "planner's call" marks the plan's own choice. No output byte, encode version or golden changes.

JP1 to JP4 are superseded by Note JS (archived).

- **JP5 A set with no engine key is named by its id (B3-16, B3-17, B3-19 to B3-23; F6 N1, N2, N4, N5).**
  - `bottom::scope_key` (the key, or the id when the key is empty) is what a set's Problems filter, "+k more in Problems", and the clicks of its card's **⚠ n** and check badge name it by. The editor reads its status-line reasons from the resolved view (JV1). A row-reason button opens Problems with that view's typed key or id and shows its own row, even for an empty id. JO7 governs keyboard focus (JV1). An ordinary empty filter string names no rows. For an ordinary filter, `scope` looks up the set with that key first, then the key-less set with that id, then the set whose id matches it by fold, then the key-less set whose key would be that key (`view_of`; Note BF: a keyed set's id is folded, a key-less one keeps its typed case; the fourth lookup is JR11's), so a created set whose id equals another set's key resolves to the keyed set, and a filter opened on a nonempty key-less id follows the set when a file lands unless that string resolves first as another set's key. A key-less scope matches the set rows built for that id and no other key-less set's. The `ProblemsFilter` set payload is a `String` holding a scope key (B3-19). The card's check badge still reads `set_check_warnings` by key, which is harmless: a key-less set has no check results.
  - `Flagged.keyless` holds the ids of key-less sets with a counted set row, apart from `Flagged.sets`, so an id never meets a key (planner's call). `bottom::no_files_yet` is a key-less set with no such row. `sets_column::pill_hover` takes the frame's `Flagged`, so the cards keep one `flagged` fetch a frame.
  - "Not ready: this set has no files yet." (`sets_column::NO_FILES_YET`) shows on the status line and the card's hover only for `no_files_yet`. Any other key-less set's status line reads "Not ready:" with its reasons, and its card's hover reads the "Not ready: a problem row…" line, as JO7 says (B3-17, B3-22).
  - A key-less set never reads Ready (`set_ready_in` unchanged), so a key-less set whose every counted row is marked reads "no files yet" (B3-21).
  - Known limit (B3-19): renaming a key-less set while its Set filter is open leaves that filter stale. Opening another folder clears a set filter. Same-root key transitions keep the payload, and JR11 governs a change of output root. JV1 and JV2 supersede B3-20.
  - Related wording (B3-22, B3-23): JO7's resolved-view reason cache (as amended by JV1), JO10 (N2) and JO15's mark and count sentence (N4) state these rules. `ui/messages.rs`'s `shortcut:` comment gives the true cost of `request_discard`, which is not used there. `pbr_export::the_written_note_counts_the_entries` states its fixture (N5).
  - Tests: `problems_notes::a_key_less_set_lists_and_reasons_its_own_rows_only`, `a_key_less_set_with_rows_reads_not_ready_on_its_card`, `a_set_with_no_entry_says_no_files_yet_on_its_status_line`, `a_key_less_sets_filter_follows_the_set_once_it_has_a_key`, `a_key_less_sets_filter_follows_a_mixed_case_id`, `a_key_less_sets_reason_opens_its_own_rows`, and the kept `a_set_with_no_files_yet_says_so_on_its_card`.
- **JP6 GRASS-DETECT-SOURCE's class follows the job (B3-18, B3-24 to B3-26; F1).**
  - `bottom::warning_class(job, w)` is the one home: GRASS-DETECT-SOURCE is a Problem when the job's `mips.normal_base` is `keep`, else a Note; with no job it is a Note too (B3-25), as `warning_text` reads it; every other code is `row_class`'s. `class_at(m, at, w)` looks up the job (`job_at`) only for a code whose class follows it. `warning_text` and `warning_class` share the predicates `follows_job` and `normal_base_kept`.
  - `row_class` keeps the code a Problem (its worst case), so the counts test keeps its numbers, and `counts_toward_badge` stays by code, a superset the readers narrow; `model/mod.rs`'s hook keeps its signature (B3-24, JO12).
  - Readers: `build_problems`' `note_row`, in the item, set and File loops. The File row reads the job of the one live item; a File row whose job key maps to several items has no job, so it is a Note (B3-25). `counts_for` reads `class_at`, and through it the tile's ⚠, "Has warnings", the Status sort, the card's **⚠ n** and, through the rows, the set's Ready. `marked` is false for every Note-class warning (B3-26): a note has no Mark, so a mark stored while the row was a Problem shows in no hover, and its key stays in `sdc-project.toml` (ruling 9).
  - The renormalised row is an Info note that shows the code's sentence, with no button and no Mark, listed under **Notes** only.
  - `row_class` keeps its table.
  - Tests: `problems_notes::a_grass_source_row_is_a_note_when_the_file_is_renormalised`, `a_grass_source_row_is_a_problem_when_the_normal_base_is_kept`, `the_grass_source_class_follows_the_job_on_the_card`, `a_grass_source_file_row_follows_the_live_job`, `a_grass_source_file_row_with_a_shared_job_key_is_a_note`, `the_grass_source_row_says_what_the_normal_base_does` and `a_mark_stored_while_the_grass_row_was_a_problem_holds_nothing_once_it_is_a_note`.

## Note JQ — A fuzz map follows the set's shader (owner, 2026-11-17; controller rulings Fuzz-1 to Fuzz-33)

Plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-17-fuzz-follows-shader.md`, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-17-fuzz-follows-shader-design.md`. The rulings are the controller's, acting for the owner, in `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-17-owner-additions.md` (sections "Rulings on the fuzz rule", "Rulings on the fuzz plan's hand-back", "Rulings after plan review 1 of the fuzz plan…", "Rulings after review r2 of the fuzz plan" and "Fuzz plan re-checked against B3"), copied here word for word; an "As built" line says where the code states a ruling more exactly. "Planner's call" marks the plan's own choice; "controller, during execution" marks a ruling made while the plan ran. No encoded byte, golden or `encode_version` arm changes; one `encode_versions` fixture key moves (JQ47). Tests: `crates/engine/tests/fuzz_follows_shader.rs`, `crates/gui/tests/fuzz_follow.rs`. If the letter JQ is taken at merge time, the rename covers every `JQ` in DECISIONS.md (this note, the seven index lines, the digest cites and JE2's cite), the "superseded in part by Note JQ" headings in DECISIONS-ARCHIVE.md, the `superseder` column of `tools/decisions-archive/2026-11-18-jq.tsv`, every `<!-- D:JQ… -->` marker in `docs/wiki` and every code comment that cites JQ.

The owner's words, verbatim (2026-11-17): "fuzz is grayscale only for advanced skin, it should be treated according to which shader it is in"; asked whether a `_fuzz` map under Auto still makes the set Advanced Skin: "because fuzz is a shared suffix, it should be a very low identifier for a shader whatever other maps are present would be a better solve, but if none existing, I suppose pbr would make the most sense"; on old projects whose sets were skin only because of a fuzz map: "Switch on open".

- **JQ1 Fuzz-1 (the owner's rule).** a `_fuzz`/`_sheen` map never makes a set Advanced Skin and never makes `auto` pack `_rfaos` by itself. The set's shader is picked from the other maps, with the fuzz map left out; when nothing else gives a shader, the pick is PBR — the owner's two statements — supersedes DR2's "a `_fuzz`/`_sheen` raw map" as skin evidence and R-fills-1's "`auto` is `_rfaos` when a fuzz source exists".
  - Ruling A1-2 and A1-3 are withdrawn — the owner's rule makes them unnecessary: a PBR set packs `_rmaos` because the fuzz map stops being skin evidence, not because the GUI writes a recipe.
- **JQ2 Fuzz-2 (the owner's rule).** the fuzz map is then read as the picked (or explicitly chosen) shader reads it. Advanced Skin: the greyscale mask in the `_rfaos` G channel, as today (DR1, DR2, DR7 stay). PBR, PBR Grass, PBR Landscape: the PBR fuzz texture, sub-type `pbr_fuzz`, exported as `<base>_f.dds`, and the set packs `_rmaos`.
- **JQ3 Fuzz-3.** under any other shader the fuzz map has no use; it is not converted and is listed as an unused input with the shader named — the shader reads no fuzz map, and silently converting it would write a file nothing loads — if wrong: one arm.
  - Review r2 m8: an explicit Advanced Skin with a pinned diffuse reads the fuzz map as PBR, in the GUI and the CLI alike, because DW1 keeps that set off skin. Intended.
- **JQ4 Fuzz-4.** a PBR set that holds both an `_f` and a `_fuzz`/`_sheen` map keeps the `_f` as its fuzz texture; the other is unused and listed — two files cannot both be `<base>_f.dds` (the mirror of DR7, where the real `_fuzz` wins G) — if wrong: one comparison.
- **JQ5 Fuzz-5 (rewritten after the advisor's check; the first wording counted Tessalith's own recorded `skin_albedo` as evidence, which would have kept every opened fuzz set skin).** other skin evidence keeps a set Advanced Skin: an explicit Advanced Skin shader, a written `rfaos`, an `_rfaos` member, a skin sub-type on another member, or a `skin_albedo` the user wrote. A `skin_albedo` that Tessalith recorded by itself because of the fuzz map is not evidence. It is told apart without history: the set's shader is on Auto, the set holds a `_fuzz`/`_sheen` map, no `packing.recipe` is written on its packed keys (a hand edit to `skin_albedo` writes `rfaos`, DQ1; the automatic recording writes the sub-type alone, FC1), it is not in `auto_skin`, and no other skin evidence exists. Such a value counts as untouched (FC1 R5).
  - This extends FC1 R5 (DECISIONS-1.x.md): `AppModel::untouched_subtype` also holds for such a value, in every accept mode (`fuzz_recorded_skin`; JQ55 has the predicate as built).
- **JQ6 Fuzz-6.** goldens stay byte-identical and no `encode_version` arm moves (DR6's precedent: which file is planned changes, no type's bytes do). If a golden or `encode_versions` fixture would change, the task stops and says so. The engine version is not bumped by this plan.
- **JQ7 Fuzz-7.** the CLI never reads the set's shader (DV2), so every explicit pick reaches it through written values: Advanced Skin as today (DU6, DQ1), PBR through Fuzz-9.
- **JQ8 Fuzz-8 (the owner's answer, 2026-11-17: "Switch on open").** when a project opens, each set that was skin only because of the fuzz map becomes PBR: its untouched `skin_albedo` is re-recorded as the new pick, in one undoable step, listed as a row that names every changed file (`key: skin_albedo → pbr_albedo`, the form FC1 R5 already uses when a pick changes). Sets where the user picked Advanced Skin, or that have other skin maps, stay skin. The CLI changes nothing in a project: a written `skin_albedo` stays evidence there until the GUI re-records it.
  - As built: the switch clears the Diffuse-slot `skin_albedo` (`AppModel::switch_fuzz_sets`); under High the recording of the plan that follows writes the new pick and joins the switch's step, under accept off nothing is written and the engine infers the type (`fuzz_follow::the_switch_runs_with_accept_off`). The row's lines use JQ19's form. JQ56 has the rest.
- **JQ9 Fuzz-9 (the mirror of DU6/JE2 for PBR, for CLI parity).** an explicit PBR-family pick in the GUI writes, in the shader's own step, what `sdc convert` needs to plan the same: `subtype = "pbr_fuzz"` on the set's `_fuzz`/`_sheen` map and the PBR type on its untouched members (the existing `untouched_for_shader` step, Fuzz-5's untouched `skin_albedo` included). Leaving PBR for Auto clears what that step wrote and was not changed since. The engine also learns the explicit PBR pick (a `chosen_pbr` beside `chosen_skin`), so the GUI plans it before anything is written.
  - The PBR step lists its writes in `sdc-project.toml`'s `[project] auto_pbr` (JQ54). Leaving the pick for Auto clears its untouched writes. JQ59 governs an explicit vanilla shader. Where High records the new pick on the next settled plan, ordinary recording follows JY6.
- **JQ10 Fuzz-10.** the plan's first task traces why probe P1g wrote `rock01_f.dds` with accept off and `rock01_fuzz.dds` under High, and pins the output name `<base>_f.dds` in both modes. It also names the PGPatcher and CS JSON export tests a `pbr_fuzz` made from a greyscale `_fuzz` map moves (the fuzz feature turns on).
  - As built: `policy::fuzz_f_output` renames a cs `pbr_fuzz` fuzz map's Discover default output to `<base>_f.dds`; JQ37 has its guards.
- **JQ11 Fuzz-1a (what "nothing else gives a shader" means).** cs target only. `shader::pick` runs with the `_fuzz`/`_sheen` map left out; when it would fall through to its last, Default arm and the set holds such a map, the pick is PBR. So diffuse + normal + fuzz is PBR. Vanilla is untouched.
- **JQ12 Fuzz-place.** its own plan and branch, an engine change with its own DECISIONS note, run after Plan 3a and before Plan 3b, because Plan 3b Task 5a removes the switch that is today's only way to hold a fuzz-map set at `_rmaos`.
- **JQ13 Fuzz-11 (spec 6.1, explicit Glow, Default, Hair… on a fuzz-map set).** the GUI lists the fuzz map unused; the CLI, which never reads the set's shader (DV2), plans from the maps and may convert it as `pbr_fuzz`. ACCEPTED as a known limit, pinned by a test and written in the note as a `shortcut:` with its upgrade condition (the day an explicit shader pick is written to `sdc.toml`). Why: this is not new. Today the same pick already differs from the CLI, which plans that set Advanced Skin because of the fuzz map; no existing key can say "do not convert" (`helper.role = "none"` means "convert alone"), and a new key is a config change the owner did not ask for. Cost if wrong: one more task that adds such a key.
  - "Today the same pick already differs from the CLI…" is at ruling time: since JQ1 the CLI no longer plans such a set Advanced Skin because of the fuzz map.
  - shortcut: the CLI cannot see an explicit shader chosen in the GUI, nor the scanned meshes' votes. So (a) under an explicit non-PBR shader, `sdc convert` converts a fuzz map the GUI lists unused (Fuzz-11); (b) where the meshes vote a non-`Default` shader, the GUI lists the fuzz map unused while `sdc convert` plans the set from its maps alone (PBR by Fuzz-1a when they give nothing else) (Fuzz-23; a `Default` vote is no evidence, so that cell agrees); (c) under an explicit Advanced Skin that writes nothing (no own Diffuse-slot member, or a diffuse whose user-written type DU6 leaves alone), the GUI plans the set skin while `sdc convert` plans it PBR with `<base>_f.dds` and `_rmaos` (Fuzz-24). Upgrade when an explicit shader is written to `sdc.toml` (that closes (a) and (c); (b) also needs the mesh votes in the CLI). Pins: `fuzz_follows_shader::without_the_explicit_shader_the_cli_converts_the_fuzz_map`, `fuzz_follows_shader::a_default_mesh_vote_keeps_fuzz_1a_and_another_vote_wins` and `fuzz_follow::an_explicit_advanced_skin_that_writes_nothing_splits_gui_and_cli`.
- **JQ14 Fuzz-12 (spec 6.2, an old High project whose `_sk` was recorded as `skin_sk`).** such a set STAYS Advanced Skin; the untouched rule is not widened to `skin_sk`, `skin_wet` or `skin_detail`. Why: a recorded value cannot be told from a typed one, a set with a skin map beside the fuzz map is most likely a real skin set, and the owner's "Switch on open" answered the case where the fuzz map was the only reason. Cost if wrong: one predicate; those sets keep Advanced Skin until the user changes the shader, which the card shows.
- **JQ15 Fuzz-13 (spec 6.3, a body set with raw roughness or AO maps, a fuzz map and no skin pick).** it is PBR in a new project, as the owner's words say ("whatever other maps are present"). The user picks Advanced Skin once; the pick writes what the CLI needs (DU6, DQ1). The owner is told this in plain words, because it is his own skin workflow.
  - Done: the controller told the owner with the rulings; no task repeats it.
- **JQ16 Fuzz-14 (spec 6.4, amends Fuzz-9's wording).** the existing `untouched_for_shader` step writes nothing for a PBR pick, so the plan's new `pbr_member_type` writes `pbr_albedo`, `pbr_normal`, `pbr_emissive` on untouched members, in fuzz-map sets only, and leaving PBR clears them. ACCEPTED. `member_subtype` is not changed (it would change every explicit PBR pick, `--set` included).
- **JQ17 Fuzz-15 (spec 6.5).** the switch on open gets a row of its own with **Undo this change**. This is the switch's own undoable step (JY6).
  - The row as built: JQ56.
- **JQ18 Fuzz-16 (schemas).** the reworded `PackRecipe` doc comment gives a description-only diff in five schema files; it is committed (the old text would be false). "No schema change" in Fuzz-6 means no field, code or shape change.
- **JQ19 Fuzz-17 (strings).** accepted as proposed: the suggestion reason "_fuzz/_sheen outside an Advanced Skin set: the PBR fuzz texture (_f)"; "{file}: the {shader id} shader reads no fuzz map; not converted"; "{file}: {f file} is the set's PBR fuzz texture (_f); not converted"; undo label "Switch {n} fuzz-map set{s} off Advanced Skin"; row "{n} set{s} left Advanced Skin (a _fuzz or _sheen map alone no longer picks it)"; row lines "{file}: skin albedo → {subtype_name}"; hover "A _fuzz or _sheen map alone no longer makes a set Advanced Skin. To keep a set skin, choose Advanced Skin as its shader." "Left Advanced Skin", not "became PBR": the new pick can be Glow or Default.
  - The owner is told these strings at the fuzz merge (with JQ34's and JQ56's).
- **JQ20 Settled details accepted as the planner wrote them (spec 3d, hand-back section 5).** a user-typed `pbr_fuzz` beside an `_f` keeps `<base>_fuzz.dds`; a hand-set `helper.role = "fuzz"` keeps its skin meaning (DY); two fuzz maps in one PBR set give IN-DUPLICATE (BV2); the Default arm takes the PBR arm's sub-choice (PBR Grass, PBR Landscape); a written `rmaos` is not negative evidence; the new evidence counts only in sets that hold a fuzz map; the switch runs once per open and never on a read-only project.
- **JQ21 Fuzz-18 (replaces "under High the GUI records it" in spec 3a).** under Auto NOTHING is recorded on a `_fuzz`/`_sheen` map. Its PBR reading is a resolution from the set, not a suggestion the recorder writes. Why: a recorded `pbr_fuzz` makes the map "typed", and then a later Glow pick or later skin evidence could no longer change its reading (plan review 1, Important 1), which breaks the owner's rule "treated according to which shader it is in". Only an explicit PBR-family pick writes `pbr_fuzz` (Fuzz-9), and leaving PBR clears it. Cost if wrong: the Textures column shows the type as resolved, not as a recorded line in `sdc.toml`; one recorder condition.
- **JQ22 Fuzz-19 (the full re-plan for fuzz-map sets).** a full re-plan is taken only for an edit that can change a fuzz-map set's reading (its shader, a member's sub-type, a packing recipe, a helper role, a file added or removed). An edit that cannot (format, quality, size, mips…) keeps the fast patch. If the code cannot tell these apart cheaply, the plan says why from the code, keeps the full re-plan as a `shortcut:` with its upgrade condition, and the task reports the time of one edit on a project with many fuzz-map sets. Why: old skin projects hold many such sets, and no common action may get slower without a reason on record.
- **JQ23 Fuzz-20.** the fuzz plan pins, in an engine test, that a written `rfaos` still packs `_rfaos` under an explicit PBR shader (and a written `rmaos` under Advanced Skin packs `_rmaos`), because ruling A1-7 and Plan 3b Task 5a rest on that cell.
  - Pin: `fuzz_follows_shader::a_written_recipe_wins_over_an_explicit_shader`. The cell is A1-7's: "a written `rfaos` under an explicit PBR shader keeps `_rfaos` and raises no PACK-RECIPE row, and the mirror exists for a written `rmaos` under Advanced Skin (DV3)".
- **JQ24 Fuzz-21 (string, after the planner's fix round).** the provenance text for a type the set decided changes from "decided by the texture set (variance bake)" to "decided by the texture set": that provenance now has two causes (the variance bake and the fuzz reading), and the bracket would be false for the second. ACCEPTED.
- **JQ25 Fuzz-22 (r2 I2).** a hand-set `helper.role = "fuzz"` is skin evidence and joins Fuzz-5's list: under Auto the set stays Advanced Skin with a `skin_albedo` diffuse, as today (DY). Why: the owner's rule is about the shared SUFFIX being a weak identifier; a role the user wrote by hand is a statement, not a suffix. The test's Auto row asserts the shader and the diffuse type, not only the G channel. Cost if wrong: one evidence item.
  - Mechanism: `FileScopes::hand_fuzz` (filled in the prepare step beside `skin_typed`) and its test in `pack::plan::set_is_advanced_skin` (the plan's Task 2 item 5), on a member or a file shared in; it needs no fuzz suffix (DY).
- **JQ26 Fuzz-23 (r2 I3a, clarifies Fuzz-1a).** a mesh majority of `Default` counts as "nothing else gives a shader": on a fuzz-map set the pick is PBR, in the GUI with a scan database as in `sdc convert`. Why: nearly every source mesh votes Default (PGPatcher turns it PBR later), so a Default vote says nothing about the textures, and the owner's words name "other maps", not meshes; it also keeps the GUI and the CLI the same in the owner's normal setup. A mesh majority of any OTHER shader wins as today (the fuzz map is unused and listed); the CLI sees no meshes there, which joins Fuzz-11's known limit. A test passes mesh votes for both cells. Cost if wrong: one condition in `pick`.
- **JQ27 Fuzz-24 (r2 I3b).** an explicit Advanced Skin pick that writes nothing (DU6's `skin_follow` returns `None`: no Diffuse-slot member, or a diffuse whose written type DU6 leaves alone) while the GUI still plans the set skin is a KNOWN LIMIT: the CLI plans that set PBR with `<base>_f.dds`. It joins Fuzz-11's `shortcut:` (same upgrade condition: the day an explicit shader pick is written to `sdc.toml`) and is pinned by a test; spec table 3a's row is corrected. Why not write `rfaos` in the pick's step: it needs a new ledger of what the step wrote so leaving Advanced Skin can clear it, for a corner (a skin set with no diffuse) the one upgrade above removes together with Fuzz-11. Cost if wrong: one task. The owner is told.
  - Done: the controller told the owner.
- **JQ28 Fuzz-25 (C1).** the unused fuzz map (PACK-UNUSED-INPUT with the two new reasons) is a Note: listed under the Notes pill, no button, no mark, never against Ready (JO1: the output is valid). The CHANGELOG says "Problems lists it under Notes as unused". If wrong: a per-reason class hook and a JO1 exception.
- **JQ29 Fuzz-26 (C2).** the switch on open clears only the Diffuse-slot `skin_albedo`; an old skin set's recorded `normal` stays a `normal`: it is the PBR set's `_n` and bakes (JS2, JS4), and it keeps its alpha, since a value recorded before Note JS reads as typed (JS5). If wrong: the switch also clears an untouched recorded `normal` (could drop a wanted alpha).
- **JQ30 Fuzz-27 (C3).** Fuzz-14 stays: an explicit PBR pick in a fuzz-map set retypes an untouched `normal` to `pbr_normal`, as the existing PBR follow does when the diffuse is decided `pbr_albedo` (`pbr_follow::pbr_counterpart`); leaving PBR reverts it. In every set the Normal slot follows JS6. If wrong: undo, or leaving PBR, restores the alpha.

Task 10 lists the page.
- **JQ35 Fuzz-32 (C8).** the two new PACK-UNUSED-INPUT reasons and their fixes go in Texture-Packing.md "Which data wins" (pick Advanced Skin or a PBR shader; remove one of the two fuzz files). Diagnostic-Codes.md stays untouched.
- **JQ36 Fuzz-33 (C9, text fix).** the note's letter also goes on the "GUI Dialogs and Export" index line.
- **JQ37 The `_f` rename's guards (planner's call).** `policy::fuzz_f_output` renames only on cs, only a `_fuzz`/`_sheen` image resolved `pbr_fuzz` whose output is the Discover default `<stem>.dds` (no `[sets]` or output-path override, no input alias), and never beside an `_f` member, where the fuzz map keeps `<base>_fuzz.dds` so the two never clash. A file with a hand role never reaches it.
- **JQ38 A hand-set `helper.role` keeps its meaning under every shader (planner's call).** `fuzz` is the skin mask and skin evidence (JQ25); `none` makes the fuzz map an ordinary file that converts alone. `plan_packing`'s reading gate skips any hand role. Tests `fuzz_follows_shader::a_hand_set_fuzz_role_stays_the_skin_mask_under_every_shader`, `a_hand_set_none_role_is_an_ordinary_file_under_every_shader`.
- **JQ39 Fuzz-1a takes the PBR family member (planner's call).** The Default arm's PBR pick is `shader::pick`'s `pbr_family()`: PBR Grass for a GRAS set, PBR Landscape for a `cs_txst` set, else plain PBR.
- **JQ40 Two fuzz maps in a PBR set (planner's call).** Both would be `<base>_f.dds`; one writes it and the writer clash check (BV2) lists the other as IN-DUPLICATE in `skipped`. Test `fuzz_follows_shader::two_fuzz_maps_in_a_pbr_set_clash_once` checks that one `<base>_f.dds` is planned and that `skipped` holds an IN-DUPLICATE; it does not count the IN-DUPLICATE rows.
- **JQ41 The PBR reading under Auto is a set decision (planner's call; Fuzz-18's mechanism).** `policy::resolve_file_with` resolves an untyped fuzz map the set reads as PBR to `pbr_fuzz` with provenance `set` and `decided`, never a suggestion, so FC1's recorder writes nothing on it; its suggestion list leads with `suggest::FUZZ_PBR_REASON`. The Inspector shows the provenance as Fuzz-21's string (JQ24).
- **JQ42 `fuzz_reading`'s order (planner's call).** `pack::plan::fuzz_reading`: not on cs, no reading (`Unused`); an explicit PBR-family pick reads PBR, an explicit Advanced Skin (`chosen_skin`) the skin mask, any other explicit shader nothing; then skin evidence (`set_is_advanced_skin`) reads the skin mask, ahead of an automatic pick; an automatic pick reads as the explicit one would; with no pick (pass 1) PBR. So an explicit pick beats written evidence for the reading, while a written recipe still picks the packed file (Fuzz-20, JQ23).
- **JQ43 The `f` clone serves the other members only (planner's call).** The 6c `f` clone serves the members other than the fuzz map; the fuzz map resolves against the real set, because the rename guard (JQ37) and the `_f` arm read `set.suffixes.contains("f")`. As built: JQ52.
- **JQ44 The skeleton (planner's call).** `skeleton::FUZZ_SUBTYPE` ("subtype in a set with a fuzz map") makes a sub-type edit in a fuzz-map set (on cs) plan in full (Fuzz-19); a set counts by `pack::plan::has_fuzz_map` or a member image with a fuzz stem (`Skeleton::fuzz_sets`). `f_sets`, returned by `plan_sets` beside `pbr`, holds the sets read with `f`; that `f` goes only into the PBR_CHANGE check's clone, never into the prepared sets a partial runs on.
- **JQ45 `skin_typed` counts only in fuzz-map sets (planner's call).** `FileScopes::skin_typed` (members whose requested sub-type is a skin one) and a written `rfaos` on either packed key are skin evidence in `set_is_advanced_skin` only when `has_fuzz_map`, so no other set's reading changes (a metallic-only set with a written `rfaos` stays `_rmaos`, DQ2).
- **JQ46 The switch's machinery (planner's call).** Fuzz-8 reuses FI3's join: the switch's step sets `reencode_follow` to its merge key, so the recording of the next plan joins it as one undo step. The switch is tried once per load of the documents (`fuzz_switch_checked`), not used up by a plan held back by a running export or the crash restore (JQ56). Its strings are Fuzz-17's (JQ19). Its row is its own (`fuzz_note`), beside FC1's `auto_note`; its Undo survives the ordinary recorder change (JQ56, JY6).
- **JQ47 The encode-version pins moved a key, no value (controller, during execution).** Fuzz-10 renames the typed fuzz map's output, so the fixture key `textures/h/pbr_fuzz.dds` became `textures/h/pbr_f.dds` in `crates/engine/tests/encode_versions/encode_inputs.json` and `identity.json`. Every hash, format and size under the key is unchanged; no `encode_version` arm moved (commit 053d596b).
- **JQ48 A real `_rfaos` file is Advanced Skin evidence, with or without a fuzz map (controller, during execution).** The plan's item 6 would have planned a PBR/skin mix for `_rfaos` + roughness + fuzz map. Test `fuzz_follows_shader::a_real_rfaos_beside_a_fuzz_map_keeps_the_set_skin` (commit c3cd0e27).
  - shortcut (final review A, m5): the test is by path, so a project that exports into its source folder and still holds Tessalith's own earlier `<base>_rfaos.dds` keeps that set Advanced Skin until the file is deleted. Upgrade with follow-up F14: tell Tessalith's own earlier outputs from a user's file (`pack::plan::set_is_advanced_skin`).
- **JQ49 One home for "this set has a fuzz map" (controller, during execution).** `pack::plan::has_fuzz_map(set)`, which counts a member file and a file shared in (the set's suffixes). The Advanced Skin gate, the skin `_f` candidate and `Skeleton::fuzz_sets` read it, so a fast re-plan and a full plan agree (commit a5984751).
- **JQ50 Fallback and Derived shader picks count as automatic in `fuzz_reading` (controller, during execution).** Neither is the user's pick: `fuzz_reading` treats every provenance but `Set` as automatic; Derived is PBR Landscape, which reads as PBR.
- **JQ51 A fuzz map shared between sets that read it differently (controller, during execution; follow-up F10).** shortcut: one file has one job, and every packed source ends its run with a `PackedSource` event, so the lender's reading decides what a shared fuzz map becomes (a known exception to HH1: a shared fuzz map is read by its own set only).
  - Borrower under an explicit Advanced Skin, lender PBR: the borrower's `_rfaos` gets a default G; its PACK-DEFAULTED row (a Note) names the lender set and its shader (`data.why`, commit f525a6a2).
  - The mirror (lender Advanced Skin, borrower PBR): the borrower writes no `<base>_f.dds` and shows no row. No existing code fits and the plan adds none. The GUI does not show the borrower's Fuzz slot filled (the share is listed under the set's shared helpers only): `fuzz_follow::a_mirror_borrowers_fuzz_slot_is_not_shown_filled` passed when the final review added it, so it is a pin, not a known limit.
  - A set whose only fuzz map is shared in gets neither Fuzz-1a nor the PBR marking: the automatic pick and `pbr_read_fuzz` read member fuzz maps only (`has_fuzz_map` does count the share, for the Advanced Skin gate).
  - A GUI Share drop of a raw fuzz map makes a Fuzz-slot borrow, not a `share` line, so the borrower's PACK-DEFAULTED note has no "why".
  - Pin: `fuzz_follows_shader::a_shared_fuzz_map_its_own_set_converts_is_no_borrowers_mask_known_limit`. Upgrade with follow-up F10 (shared fuzz maps read differently by two sets): when a packed source can keep its own job (run events, the summary, the GUI's source counts) and one file can have a second job. The `shortcut:` in `pack::plan::plan_packing` names both cases.
- **JQ52 Fuzz-1a's PBR marking never touches the real set (controller, during execution; replaces the plan's "insert `f` after packing").** The helper-fill step resolves a filled target again, so an `f` on the real set made a filled fuzz map lose `<base>_f.dds`. As built: 6c resolves the members other than the fuzz map against a clone with `f`; the fuzz map resolves against the real set; the real set never gains `f`; only the `set_resolved` event's `pbr` and the skeleton's PBR_CHANGE check read a copy with `f`. One helper, `api::member_set(set, file, f_sets)`, gives the set a member resolves against, and 6c, the helper-fill step and `map_members` (the shader map check) all call it, so a filled diffuse stays `pbr_albedo` and a kept DDS fills its PBR slot (final review A, I1 and I2; tests `a_filled_diffuse_in_a_fuzz_only_set_stays_pbr_albedo`, `a_kept_dds_in_a_fuzz_only_set_fills_its_pbr_slot`). Commit eda22831's subject says "the set gains f after the fill step": the subject is wrong, this item is the rule. Test `fuzz_follows_shader::a_filled_fuzz_map_keeps_base_f`.
- **JQ53 Fuzz-4 covers a kept `_f.dds` (controller, during execution; final review A, I3).** A kept DDS is a member and fills its slot (GY4), so it is the set's PBR fuzz texture: the fuzz map is listed unused and the reason names the DDS. Test `fuzz_follows_shader::a_kept_f_dds_beside_a_fuzz_map_wins_under_pbr`.
  - Except when the kept DDS is the same file (`dds::same_file`) as the fuzz map's own `<base>_f.dds` output: a project that exports into its source folder finds its earlier output there. Path only, as CO102 decides. There the plan converts the fuzz map, and the run's existing-output check decides: a file with no manifest record is kept (OUT-UNKNOWN-EXISTING) under the default and re-encode modes, and replaced only by `sdc convert --on-existing overwrite`.
  - One predicate, `group::is_fuzz_own_f`: `pack::plan::pbr_f_members` (the packing gate) skips such a member and `api::pbr_read_fuzz` checks the same iterator; policy's Fuzz-4 test and `fuzz_f_output`'s guard read the copy of the set `api::member_set` gives the fuzz map, which drops that `f`. Tests `an_in_place_second_plan_keeps_converting_the_fuzz_map` (including a fast re-plan), `an_in_place_run_keeps_its_own_f_up_to_date`, `a_hand_made_f_at_the_fuzz_output_is_never_overwritten_unasked`.
  - The fuzz map's own output is the path it really writes: its `output_paths` name when one is set, else `<base>_f.dds` (`group::is_fuzz_own_f`). Among `pack::plan::pbr_f_members`, the reason names a resolved `pbr_fuzz` job (including a rule-typed image or a re-encoded DDS) or a kept `_f.dds` first, then orders by path. An `_f` resolved as another type is named for that type, even when a rule supplied it. The gate and `fuzz_f_output`'s guard still count every `_f` member, because an `_f` typed away keeps the `_f` path. The unused reason says what that member is: "{f} ({type}) is written at the set's _f path", "{f}, the set's _f, failed to load", "… was cancelled", "… is skipped" (F15, N-42). Only `sdc convert` exports into its source folder: the GUI refuses that folder (HQ3, HQ5) and writes a CS PBR set under `textures\pbr\` (P14), so a kept `x_f.dds` beside the fuzz map is never the GUI's own output; `has_f` counts it, the map is listed unused, and `sdc convert` into the same export folder plans the same (F16). Tests `a_named_fuzz_output_leaves_a_hand_made_f_the_sets_f_in_place`, `an_f_typed_away_from_pbr_fuzz_is_named_for_what_it_is`, `an_f_that_fails_to_load_is_named_as_failed`, `an_f_that_is_a_duplicate_is_named_as_skipped`, `fuzz_follow::a_kept_f_beside_the_fuzz_map_is_the_sets_f_in_the_gui_and_the_cli`.
- **JQ54 Fuzz-9 in the GUI, as built (controller, during execution; commits f85955cb, 57dbc239, a701adb2).**
  - The `pbr_fuzz` write, the PBR counterpart write and the leave step skip a member in a locked slot, as `pbr_follow` and DW1 do; a member pinned after the pick keeps the value and its `auto_pbr` key (final review B, M1). R5's own arm is unchanged.
  - Advanced Skin then a PBR-family pick: DU7's stored value is mapped to its PBR counterpart only when Tessalith recorded it. It counts as recorded when it is non-empty, the recorder is on, and it is what the recorder writes on a colour member: the current pick itself, or `diffuse`/`pbr_albedo` while the plan suggests `skin_albedo`. A hand value equal to the recorder's is the recorder's (DU8). shortcut: under High a hand-written `diffuse` on a diffuse the recorder would have typed `pbr_albedo` before the skin pick is treated as recorded; upgrade when DU6 stores whether the value it replaced was untouched.
  - Leaving a PBR-family pick, for Auto or any shader outside the PBR family, reverts when the set still holds a fuzz map, or when a member listed in `auto_pbr` still holds what only this step writes (`pbr_fuzz` on a fuzz map, `pbr_albedo` on the Diffuse-slot member). A value changed since (an edit takes its key off `auto_pbr`) stays.
  - Removing the fuzz map from its set clears the `pbr_fuzz` the step wrote (if it still holds it) and its `auto_pbr` key in the same step (final review B, M7). The file in no set is a set of its own holding only a fuzz map, so Fuzz-1a still reads it PBR (the owner's rule: a set holding only a fuzz map reads PBR) and it converts to `<base>_f.dds` with nothing written.
  - Known leftovers: the leave step cannot tell its own `pbr_normal`/`pbr_emissive` from `pbr_follow`'s Switch writes (a rare chain leaves a `pbr_normal` in a vanilla set). `auto_pbr` can list the Diffuse-slot key. A fuzz map taken out of its set still writes `<base>_f.dds` (the M7 item above). Explicit PBR then a vanilla shader in a set with no fuzz map keeps FC1's PBR types on members other than the Normal slot (follow-up F13, the general R5 gap; JQ59 covers fuzz-map sets only; the Normal slot: JS6).
- **JQ55 Fuzz-5's predicate, as built (controller, during execution; commits eceedffa, 727d37a1).** `AppModel::fuzz_recorded_skin`: beside the spec §4 list, a hand-set `helper.role = "fuzz"` on a member or on a file shared in is skin evidence (Fuzz-22, which the plan's condition list left out); the written recipe is read at the key built from the Diffuse-slot file (`<its folder>/<base>_rfaos`), as the engine's `set_is_advanced_skin` does, because a set view's key is an output path; the fuzz map must be an image (a kept `_fuzz.dds` does not count, as in the engine's `untyped_fuzz_map`). The code also asks for the cs target, the set whose own Diffuse slot holds the file (Fuzz-30), an unlocked Diffuse member and a set that is not shared skin.
  - A hand-set fuzz role counts on an image only (a kept DDS takes no role, as the engine's `hand_role`), and the skin types are `Subtype::is_skin()`, the engine's list (final review, lines 43 and 46).
  - Known limit (follow-up F10): a set whose only fuzz map is shared in from another set is not switched. An automatic edit of `sdc.toml` is not widened into shared fuzz maps while F10 is open. Pin `fuzz_follow::an_old_borrower_of_a_shared_fuzz_map_is_not_switched`.
  - Known limit: under accept off, Auto → explicit Advanced Skin → explicit PBR keeps an old recorded `skin_albedo` under the PBR pick (the Advanced Skin pick writes nothing and makes the origin `Set`), so `sdc convert` plans that saved text as skin; the switch on open normally clears the value first.
- **JQ56 Fuzz-8, the switch on open, as built (controller, during execution; commit 011cb838 and its fix round cd6a9df9).** `switch_fuzz_sets`, `finish_fuzz_switch` and `reset_fuzz_switch` in `model/auto_record.rs`: the first two are called from `finish_plan`, `reset_fuzz_switch` from `open_folder`, `reload_documents`, `check_sources` and `resolve_external_change`.
  - **Gate.** The switch does not run when `sdc.toml` cannot be written: `sdc_toml_writable()` is an error (a load or parse error, a plan blame, or the file's read-only flag). That is wider than FC1's recorder, which checks `read_only()` only, so on a flagged file the recorder still records in memory and the switch does not run. A flag cleared mid-session switches at the next load of the documents. A flag set after a switch leaves the switch in memory, and Save reports the read-only flag.
  - **Once per load of the documents.** The switch runs on the first settled plan after `open_folder` and after every re-read of `sdc.toml` (`reload_documents`, the changed-on-disk reload in `check_sources`, and `resolve_external_change` taking the file on disk; one function, `reset_fuzz_switch`): a reload is treated as an open, as FC1 re-records after one. `cancel_open` goes back through `open_folder`, so the previous folder switches again. Within one load, an Undo of the switch does not make it run again.
  - **A switch the user undoes and saves runs again at the next load.** The spec has no key that stores "the user refused"; the row's hover names the way to keep a set skin (choose Advanced Skin as its shader).
  - **Used up without switching:** a non-cs target, or an unwritable `sdc.toml`, on the first settled plan. **Deferred, not used up:** a running export, a crash-restore step on top, a superseded plan.
  - **The switch is an automatic change (FI4).** A switch, with the automatic recordings joined to it or after it, on a document that was clean before, raises no close, open or reload dialog, writes no recovery file, and lets a hand edit of `sdc.toml` on disk be taken silently (JY6). A user edit after it prompts. Undo of a switch restores a subtype and sets the type/alpha mark even when it returns the text to disk. Close prompts and recovery keeps that choice until Save or reload.
  - **Save hover.** Automatic-only changes leave header Save disabled with `Nothing to save.` JY6 gives the explicit first write on a new project.
  - **One undo step.** The switch, a format cleanup it causes and the joined High recording are one step (FI3's join: `reencode_follow` plus the step's merge key). The row stores the switch's own step and offers Undo only while that step is on top.
  - **Undo and Redo clear the row** (`apply_snapshot`), as they clear the automatic-types row; a Redo of the switch shows no row.
  - **The row** is a codeless Info row right after the automatic-types row and is treated as that row is: under Notes, in the default list only while it has its Undo, never counted against Ready, not hideable (`can_hide` needs a code). A switched file with no job when the row is built gets a line with no arrow. The switch keeps its Undo (Fuzz-15, JQ17, JY6).
  - **The row lists a switched file once.** Under High the recording that joins the step also wrote the switched diffuse; its line for a key the switch already names is dropped, and its lines for other files stay (final review B, I1).
  - **Known limit (follow-up F12):** the switch's edit clears an encoding-cleanup note (JG3) built in the same plan tail, so an old project that needs both loses the cleanup's Info rows (the cleanup's step stays). FC1's own recording clears it the same way, and a cleanup the switch causes loses its note again when the High recording joins (final review B, M2). Also F12: Undo of a user step above the switch drops the switch's row while the switch stays applied (M4), and `cancel_open` brings back a history whose old switch step's Undo does nothing (M5). F12 is one rule for both automatic rows, the recorder's and the switch's.
  - **Pin, not proof:** `fuzz_follow::the_switch_row_shows_on_the_frame_after_it_is_built` passes without `invalidate_views()` (Note FL6's `same` branch lands the read in the same poll); the call stays (Fuzz-29).
- **JQ57 What this note supersedes and amends.** The 1.x wording R-fills-1 ("`_rfaos` when a fuzz source exists") and DR2 ("or a `_fuzz`/`_sheen` raw map") stays in the frozen DECISIONS-1.x.md; the digest lines on `packing.recipe`, the skin `_f`, M7's prediction and skin detection carry the new rule and cite JQ, which supersedes it. JQ5 extends FC1 R5 (DECISIONS-1.x.md, not edited); the digest line on the GUI's automatic decisions cites JQ. JE2 says how a Fuzz-9 `pbr_fuzz` is cleared (JQ54). A1-2 and A1-3 are withdrawn (JQ1). HH1 is amended by JQ51 (a shared fuzz map is read by its own set only; a `shortcut:`, nothing archived). The S-shader-6 digest line gains JQ11's PBR pick at rule 12; the S-shader-10 and FI4 digest lines cite JQ26 and JQ56.
- **JQ58 PBR types for untouched members (planner's call).** `pbr_member_type` (`model/sets.rs`) gives an untouched `diffuse` on the Diffuse-slot member `pbr_albedo` (an `_i` elsewhere stays `diffuse`), an untouched `normal` `pbr_normal` and an untouched `glow` `pbr_emissive`, because `member_subtype` has no such arm under a PBR-family shader and changing it would re-type every explicit PBR pick, `--set` included (JQ16). It runs only for an explicit PBR-family pick in a set holding a fuzz map, and skips a member in a locked slot (JQ54). The leave step clears what it wrote only for a member not in a locked slot (JQ54, B-M1).
  - shortcut: there is no `_s` arm, so an untouched `subsurface_tint` in a fuzz-map set stays under the PBR pick. The `_s` suggestion is Low confidence and the recorder writes High ones only (accept is `None` or `High`; there is no Low or Medium mode), so today this bites only a value written another way. Upgrade: add the arm with `engine::suggest`'s rule for `_s` (follow-up F13).
- **JQ59 Leaving PBR for a vanilla shader in a fuzz-map set (controller, final fix wave; review B, I2).** `shader::member_subtype` keeps a PBR type under a vanilla shader (JQ16), so after Auto read a fuzz-map set PBR and FC1 recorded `pbr_albedo`/`pbr_normal`, an explicit Glow left them written and `sdc convert` planned PBR members. In `untouched_for_shader`, for a set holding a fuzz map:
  - The controller's ruling: an explicit pick outside the PBR family from a set read PBR gives an untouched recorded member, not in a locked slot, its vanilla counterpart: `vanilla_member_type`, the inverse of `pbr_member_type` (Diffuse-slot `pbr_albedo` → `diffuse`, `pbr_normal` → `normal`, `pbr_emissive` → `glow`).
  - Ruling (controller as the owner's delegate, 2026-11-18): Advanced Skin is left out of that pick, because DU6-DU7's `skin_follow` owns the diffuse there and stores the value it replaces.
  - Ruling (same): its way back (`vanilla_back` in `untouched_for_shader`). Auto after a chosen vanilla shader clears an untouched `diffuse`, `normal` or `glow` (not in a locked slot), so the next settled plan records what Auto reads (FC1 R5's Auto rule).
