Without it, Glow then Auto left the set read PBR with a written `diffuse` (the Glow → Auto row of the RED test failed).
  - Ruling (same): neither write joins `auto_pbr`. The value is what the new shader's recorder writes (DU8), a later PBR pick maps it back through `pbr_member_type`, and listing it would make the leave step read a vanilla value as the PBR step's own.
  - Tests `fuzz_follow::high_auto_pbr_then_pbr_then_glow_drops_the_recorded_pbr_types` (RED before the fix), `pbr_then_explicit_glow_gives_back_the_glow_types` (pin).

## Note JT — Checks for rules that were prose only (main team, 2026-11-18)

Rules from CLAUDE.md, `docs/DOCUMENTATION.md`, the mistakes logs and the `egui-ui-rules` skill that a machine can see are checked by a test, a clippy lint, the doc lint or the pre-commit hook, and the prose names its check. A pointer never promises more than its check does: where a check covers part of a rule, the rest stays prose. The rulings are the controller's.

- **JT1 Two rules stay prose.** "Build hover text lazily" has no check: in egui 0.37 `on_hover_text` fills the AccessKit description and `on_hover_ui` does not, so the rewrite a `format!` scan would force drops descriptions. A `///` on a schema enum variant is not scanned in the source, where most doc blocks sit on data-carrying enums or enums in no schema; JT4 checks the committed output instead.
- **JT2 Two clippy lints, in the main team's crates only.** The root `Cargo.toml`'s `[workspace.lints.clippy]` sets `exit` and `undocumented_unsafe_blocks` to `warn`, which `-D warnings` makes an error. Only `engine`, `texcore`, `sdc-gui`, `sdc`, `bcenc` and `encoders-sys` opt in with `[lints] workspace = true`; the NIF team's crates opt in on their own branch, so their unsafe blocks stay unchecked until then. Each deliberate `process::exit` (`crates/cli/src/cancel.rs`, two in `crates/gui/src/crash_log.rs`) has an `#[allow(clippy::exit)]` with its reason on that one call, so a new exit in the same function still fails. `clippy::exit` does not fire in a `main` function.
- **JT3 Linear and ease-in motion is disallowed in the GUI.** `crates/gui/clippy.toml`'s `disallowed-methods` lists `egui::Context::animate_bool` and `animate_bool_with_time` (linear), `emath::easing::linear`, and emath 0.37's seven ease-in curves (`quadratic_in`, `cubic_in`, `sin_in`, `circular_in`, `exponential_in`, `back_in`, `bounce_in`). The two `animate_bool*` reasons point to `animate_bool_with_time_and_easing` with an explicit duration and an ease-out curve; the curve reasons name an ease-out curve. The `*_in_out` curves and `animate_bool_responsive` stay allowed: they are not linear motion. Clippy reads the file for `sdc-gui` only, the one crate that animates. An entry catches a call and a function passed as a value: `egui::emath::easing::linear` given as the curve of `animate_bool_with_time_and_easing` in `crates/gui/src` failed `cargo clippy -p sdc-gui -p sdc -- -D warnings` with "use of a disallowed method `emath::easing::linear`" (measured 2026-11-18).
- **JT4 A unit enum is never written as `oneOf` in a committed schema.** `schemas::a_unit_enum_is_never_written_as_one_of_in_a_committed_schema` walks every committed `schema/*.json` and fails on a `oneOf` whose members are string `const`s, or string `const`s with `type: string` `enum` members (one `///` on one variant is enough to make schemars write that); it lists every hit in one run. Four enums of `event.schema.json` are allowlisted: `Need`, `RecipeKind`, `RecipeStatus` and `SkipKind`; an entry whose enum is no longer `oneOf` fails, so it goes when its enum is fixed. Their committed schema stays byte-identical: switching their variant docs to `//` changes it, and that is the owner's call. An enum in no committed schema is not seen.
- **JT5 The whole-GUI name checks allowlist the hits they found.** `accessible_names.rs::audit_names` runs on the nine screens its tests audit and fails on an input named only by its hover text and on two or more inputs that share a name. Two shared names are excepted, both set by egui: a Slider with its own value box, and splitters alone (egui names every panel splitter "Resize panel", and the app cannot set it). `SCREENS` lists the nine screens: `audit_names` fails on a screen not in it, and a self-test fails on an allowlist entry whose screen is not in it. `SHARED_NAME_ALLOWED` holds each shared name found when the check was built, with its screen, the sorted roles of the inputs that share it and a reason; `HOVER_ONLY_ALLOWED` is empty. Roles are compared exactly, so a new input under an allowlisted name, a gone one, a changed role and an entry whose hit is gone all fail. No name changes with the check, because another branch is renaming the set editor's controls; fixing the real pairs is product work.
- **JT6 This note's letter.** It is JT because JR and JS are used by two unmerged branches. The repo rule is the next free letter on `main` at merge time, so the letter is checked again at the merge.
- **JT7 The doc lint's history rule.** `tools/doc-lint.py` fails "since" followed by a version, with or without a leading `v`: the literal "since 1.x" and any number, such as "since 3.0" or "since v3.0.0" (also a decimal such as "since 1.5 GB"). A legitimate sentence of that shape is reworded. "used to" after is, are, be, was or were is not a hit: "the slider is used to set" says what a thing is for. "now" and "no longer" are not checked, because most of their uses in the docs are legitimate; "was renamed" is not checked either.
- **JT8 The process-state scan sees the repo's wrappers.** `it::no_it_module_changes_process_state` reads every module of the `it` binary and each module those pull in with `mod`, `pub mod` or `pub(..) mod`, and fails on `set_var(`, `remove_var(`, `panic::set_hook`, `take_hook`, `crash_log::install` and `watch_device(`, each where it does not continue an identifier (`offset_var(` is not a hit); a `mod` it cannot find a file for fails too. `it::process_state_needles_match_the_files_kept_apart` names the `[[test]]` files kept apart for process-wide state, asserts that the manifest's `[[test]]` list is that list plus `it` and `ui_drive`, and that each listed file holds a needle on a line that is not a `//` comment, so a state change the needles miss fails there.
- **JT9 Each check and where it runs.**
  - `cargo test` (the gate's test step and CI):
    - `sdc-gui` lib, `crates/gui/src/look.rs`: `look::tests::status_colours_are_defined_in_ui_status_only` (`const RED`, `AMBER`, `GREEN` or their RGB values under `crates/gui/src` outside `ui/status.rs`) and `look::tests::scroll_bars_use_thin_scroll` (a scroll preset outside `look.rs`, a whole scroll style set to anything but `look::thin_scroll()`).
    - `sdc-gui` test binary `it`, `crates/gui/tests/it/main.rs`: `no_it_module_changes_process_state` and `process_state_needles_match_the_files_kept_apart` (JT8), `kittest_checks_are_never_switched_off` (`with_accessibility_check(` or `allow_missing_glyphs(` in `crates/gui/tests` or `crates/gui/src`), `every_early_return_in_a_test_says_why` and `the_return_rule_tells_a_skip_from_a_checked_exit` (LH3, in the module `return_scan`, `crates/gui/tests/it/return_scan.rs`) and `the_walker_skips_only_an_absent_optional_root` (the walker both scans share); `crates/gui/tests/accessible_names.rs` through `audit_names` (JT5).
    - `engine` test binary `schemas`, `crates/engine/tests/schemas.rs`: JT4's test, its matcher pinned by `the_unit_enum_matcher_sees_the_mixed_shape_and_not_data_enums`.
    - `engine` test binary `alloc_budget`, `crates/engine/tests/alloc_budget.rs`: `enum_ids_do_not_allocate` (LH4).
  - Clippy with `-D warnings` (the gate's two clippy steps and CI): `clippy::exit` and `clippy::undocumented_unsafe_blocks` from the root `Cargo.toml` (JT2), `clippy::allow_attributes_without_reason` and `clippy::ignore_without_reason` (LH1), `clippy::format_push_string` and `clippy::assigning_clones` (LH2), and `disallowed-methods` from `crates/gui/clippy.toml` (JT3).
  - `tools/doc-lint.py` (the gate's `doc-lint` step and CI; by hand `py -3.13 -I tools\doc-lint.py`): history words over README.md, docs/gui.md and docs/wiki (JT7); "as above" and "as below" in docs/wiki; the `###` headings of each released CHANGELOG section (Main first, then Engine, GUI, CLI, Common; 0.8.0-alpha.1 allowlisted); wiki page budgets and `_Sidebar.md` coverage; at most two IMPORTANT lines a wiki page; over README.md, docs/gui.md and docs/wiki, no byte-order mark and no image with empty alt text. Six or more top-level bullets in a row print a warning and do not fail.
  - `.githooks/pre-commit` (at each commit where `core.hooksPath` is `.githooks`; neither the gate nor CI runs it), on the staged change, beside fmt, control bytes and duplicate note headings: a `.dds`, `.bsa`, `.ba2`, `.esp`, `.esm` or `.esl` outside `crates/render3d/shaders/cs/`; with `core.autocrlf=true`, a staged `.rs` whose working copy is LF (not one marked `-text` or `eol=lf`) and a CR byte in a staged text blob not marked `-text`; a `## Note` letter missing from the `## Index` block of `.claude/DECISIONS.md`; a `.claude/mistakes/*.md` entry not in the `- YYYY-MM-DD <what> → <instead> (<where>)` form.

## Note JR — Sets and slots: links, outputs, labels, titles (review E plan 3b, 2026-11-16)

Review E's sets plan (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-16-review-e-sets.md`; spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-16-egui037-review-e-design.md`, items E1, E13, E17, E24 and C14, and owner additions A1 and A2 of 2026-11-17). "Controller ruling N, round M" quotes the spec's controller rulings (acting for the owner; the owner may overrule); "controller ruling, 2026-11-18" marks a ruling made while the plan ran; "planner's call" marks the plan's own choice. JR8 and JR9 govern feature keys, JR17 governs owned and borrowed slot paths, and JR14 governs which entries reach the exported PGPatcher JSON (controller rulings 3b-F5 and 3b-F7). Encoded DDS bytes stay the same. Code: `crates/gui/src/model/{sets,txst_map,set_preview,pack_fills,bottom,export_scope}.rs`, `crates/gui/src/ui/{set_editor,sets_column,pgp_fields,group,xpmf,problems}.rs`. Tests: `crates/gui/tests/slot_links.rs`, `set_cards.rs`, `packing_fills.rs`, `slot_alpha.rs`, `set_editor_width.rs`, `set_preview_model.rs`, `fast_replan.rs`, `problems_notes.rs`.

- **JR1 A `slotN` with a non-empty value is a link (E1).** It is shown only on a set that writes a PGPatcher entry (`pbr_export::writes_entry`: a JSON set whose statics keep the entry, JR14). A link sits on the row of its game slot (`link.row`): the source item's kind when that kind's game slot is the link's slot; else the kind the set already has on that slot; else the slot's default kind (1 Diffuse, 2 Normal, 3 Emissive, 4 Height, 6 RMAOS, 7 Coat, 8 Subsurface; `sets::link_row_kind`). On game slot 5 with no kind of the set there it has no row. So a slot-7 link is on the Coat or the Fuzz row, never both, and a path to another kind reads on the slot's own row (controller ruling, 2026-11-18). One precedence rule, `sets::slot_shown` (the one display reader; `v.slots` keeps its meaning): a link on the row, then an own or borrowed file whose game slot holds a link on another row or on none (`Replaced`), then an own or borrowed file under `slotN = ""` on a set that writes an entry (`Cleared`), then own or borrowed, then the record, then empty. The row shows what the JSON carries.
  - **The row.** A link reads as filled: "{file}" and the link "from set {short}" that opens that set (AccessKit "Open set {short} ({slot label})", hover "Open the set this file comes from."), or "{file} · set by path"; the button **Unlink**; no format, alpha or channel-source controls and no status dot. Both sides stay inside the File cell: the file name is cut at the end, the link side takes at most half of the cell and is cut at its end, each with its full text on hover, and a linked row never makes the File column wider (controller ruling, 2026-11-18). On a shared game slot (3 or 7) a linked slot's row belongs to the link's kind, whether or not the set has an own file there, and shows **Unlink**, never Assign… (`sets::view_row`, which `editor_cells` and the status line's slot reasons build on). "Match on this" is not drawn on a linked slot 1 or 2; the Matching group keeps it. **Unlink** and **Use this file** are off on a pinned slot, with the slot menu's reason (`AppModel::slot_command_off`). A link to a slot that has no row (no shader pick and no own file there) draws no row (`// shortcut:` in `view_row`).
  - **Under a link.** An own file under a link for another kind (slot 7: an `_f` under a link to a `_cnr`) does not read as filled: the row shows the link and "{own} still converts to DDS. The JSON uses the linked file for slot {n}." Its hover says "Unlink to use {own} in slot {n}." The displaced file draws no chip, and the 3D preview does not serve it (controller ruling 2, round 2: "when a link holds a game slot, an own or borrowed file of a different kind in that same game slot is not in the JSON, so it does not read as filled"; controller rulings, round 3: (1) "a Replaced slot draws no chip, the linked kind's chip carries the link"; (2) "when a game slot is linked its shared row belongs to the link's kind, and it shows Unlink"). On a set that writes an entry, a `slotN = ""` over an own or borrowed file reads "Left out of the JSON: you cleared this slot." with **Use this file**; on any other set no JSON carries the clear, and the file shows as itself (JR14). The Assign menu keeps **Clear**. Unlink and Use this file each remove only the stored `slotN` (`SlotCommand::RemoveSlotKey`, 3b-W1). One Undo restores the command. An underlying borrow and sets sharing through it keep their lender. From this set and Clear retain their separate share-removal behavior. A From another set pick that changes the document and resolves to a lender logs one Info line to Messages ("{slot label} of {set short}: {file} (from set {from short})"). Picking the same link again makes no edit or message.
  - **The chip.** A linked chip is filled and named "{letter}, from set {short}" ("{letter}, path {file}" for a typed path), hover "{slot} linked to {path}", with the link mark painted in a 10 px square at the chip's top centre, between the two lock cells, in every lock state (controller ruling, 2026-11-18); a typed-path chip also paints its locks. Two rings on a backing fill clear the letter and both lock cells in either theme. A cleared slot's chip is the dim letter box "{letter}, cleared", hover "{slot} (cleared): {file}". An own file whose slot is linked (of any kind) or cleared draws no trailing thumbnail, and `chip_rows` estimates the height before drawing. Mixed borrowed and extra members and docked helpers can make the estimate differ from the drawn rows. The cleared chip and the typed-path box sense a click for the slot menu, so they are `Role::Button` and Tab stops (egui-ui-rules 8.5).
  - **The missing-map check.** A `SHADER-MISSING-MAP` warning for a slot that holds a link with a row is dropped (`link_slots`): the JSON names a file for it.
  - **Resolving.** `txst_map::link_slots` finds a link's source in the index of every planned output, by the game location of the file each settled job writes (`sets::written_game_location`, JR14) under PGPatcher's slot path rule (JR14), so the opened folder does not matter; the first set in display order that writes a path keeps it. The JSON's slot paths are unchanged (`slotN` already won); the feature keys follow JR8.
- **JR2 `SetView.short`.** The shortest unique tail of a set id, of at least two parts, compared folded. Split at either `/` or `\`, trim end separators for the comparison and keep the id's own separators in the displayed tail (an id of one part is itself, and ids equal after folding keep the whole id; `sets::short_names`). The card titles (JR5), the "from set" texts and the linked chip's name use it.
- **JR3 Output files lists the set’s conversion jobs (E13).** The 2D set editor lists each distinct planned output once. Own slot files come first in slot order, then all remaining member jobs in member order, including a displaced own file, a second file of one kind and outputs without a slot. A borrowed or linked file belongs on its producer’s list. Each line uses the editor row title for that kind (the file name for an unslotted job). Paths are relative to the export folder where possible and absolute outside it. An elided path has one full-path hover. **Show in Explorer** appears when the file exists and clears the floating scrollbar. The closed header reads "{n}, all on disk", "{n}, none on disk", "{k} of {n} on disk" or "none". A nonempty open group explains "The count is of the files at these paths now, whichever export wrote them." An empty group reads "Nothing is written for this set." The 3D view keeps the room for its viewport. The `→ prefix` arrow appears only on a PBR set. A prefix error shows on every JSON set.
- **JR4 Values have their own column headings (E17, A2).** The headings reserve no space inside the values. File names in source pickers keep their suffix when space runs out (owner addition A2, rulings A2-1 to A2-4, A2-6 and A2-7).
  - **Heading.** The slot rows are a 6-column grid (Slot, thumbnail, File, Format, Flags, Status), so the labels are one heading line above them ("Slot", "File", "Format", "Alpha"; the thumbnail and status columns have none). It is drawn only when some row has a format combo, and "Alpha" only over a Diffuse row's alpha combo, with its left edge at the combo's: on a set whose Diffuse row draws the Match toggle first, the heading cell starts after the toggle's width, using the same button measure as Match. Blank heading cells reserve space without an empty accessible label, so Match has no heading (controller ruling, 2026-11-18). Every combo paints its bare value; the labels are in each combo's AccessKit name ("Normal _n format", "Slot alpha type: {label}").
  - **Widths.** The format combos keep `COMBO_W` (96) as their minimum and widen into the room left in their column and the row (`combo_w`; the slack inside the column is `(format_base - COMBO_W).max(0)`); a format value that is still cut has its full value on hover. The alpha combo stays `ALPHA_W`, which holds "Cutout + Blend". `ROWS_MIN` stays 724, so the labels cost the side columns no additional width (opening a set still narrows them to the floor, HP6). Budgets calculated from the constants, not measured breakpoints: 143 (Textures) + 133 (Sets) + 724 = 1000 fits 1024; with the default side columns (380 + 266 = 646 px) the editor is at that floor up to a 1370 px window. The BC4 one-channel value fits at 1280 and 1536 px. The leaf and JSON-set alpha fixtures fit under their headings with Confirm at 1280 and 1600 px. At the 724 px floor the longer R8 value can be cut. Its enabled hover gives the full value and a disabled hover adds the refusal on the next line. An uncut, enabled BC4 has no value tooltip (`packing_fills`, `slot_alpha`).
  - **Channel lines.** The channel lines of a packed output span the Format cell and the row's empty Flags cell; every line has a role-label column as wide as the widest role of that output, so all its pickers start at one x, and every picker is `SOURCE_W` 130 wide (probe P2b: `…_roughness.png` needs 126.1 px; ruling A2-4 keeps `COMBO_W` 96). This supersedes DY18's `SOURCE_W` (1.x). A source file name longer than the picker is cut at the front (`front_cut`, `…_roughness.png`), so its suffix shows, with the full name on hover and in the AccessKit value; a picker with no file paints "value" and its hover reads "No file: the box sets this channel (0 to 255).". On a read-only `sdc.toml` a disabled picker's hover is two lines, the full name (or the Value sentence) and then the read-only reason (ruling A2-6); the value box and **Reset** give the reason on hover too. The grid passes its measured Flags width to the channel lines (A2-7). A saved fill's disabled Reset gives its reason. The painted "Source: " is gone; the AccessKit value keeps it (A2-2). File names are user text of any length, while formats and alpha modes are short fixed strings.
  - **Match.** The Match toggle is the painted link mark alone (`MARK_W` 13, the chip's mark), with a distinct AccessKit name for each slot (JR15). Each click is one Undo step. A disabled mark on a read-only sets document gives that document’s refusal on hover; the word "Match" is not drawn. Controller ruling, 2026-11-18: spec E17 asks for "a visible label or a named tooltip" and the toggle has the named tooltip; drawn from 900 px up, the word would take 41 px from File and move the Format column off `look_036`'s pin. The owner may ask for the word. No emoji glyph in any label. This supersedes in part FB2's (1.x) "icon-toggle flags "Match on this" and "Opaque"" (Opaque is JI5's dropdown).
- **JR5 Floors and card titles (E24).** The centre's floors hold only while the Selection tab is in front: the 2D set editor's (`ROWS_MIN`, 724 px) and an expanded texture's (`ui::centre_floor`: `PREVIEW_MIN` 480 + the open settings' 280 + 16 = 776 px in a window 1280 px or wider, where the settings start open; 480 + the 24 px strip + 16 = 520 px below). Behind Presets, the JSON editor or Settings the floor is `CENTRE_MIN` (360), so the side columns keep their widths behind a centre tab, and Selection in front of a docked JSON editor keeps its floor (controller ruling, 2026-11-18, for the preview's floor). This supersedes in part (1.x) FB2's "while the 2D set editor is shown the centre's floor is `set_editor::ROWS_MIN`", FC8's "from 1280 the centre's floor is `PREVIEW_MIN + SETTINGS_WIDTH + 16` (776)" and CE15's 520 px floor: each holds only while Selection is in front. The 3D settings window reads the front tab after the centre is drawn, so it never draws a frame over a tab (AG132). Card titles keep the set's unique tail (JR2): the middle forms are used only when they end with it, the last form is `…/<tail>` only when it shortens the id, otherwise the whole id, a title that does not fit beside the status pill takes its own line, and it wraps only when that last form does not fit on its own line either (HN6, HP7; controller ruling, 2026-11-18).
- **JR6 A feature's state shows once (C14).** The four feature groups with a Features tick (Coat, Emissive, Glint, Fuzz) carry no on/off word in their header: the tick shows the state when the Features group is expanded and the key is available. Grass filters unsupported keys and their groups. A group whose state shows only in a control inside its own body (Subsurface's mode selector, the XPMF card's checkbox) says that state in its header while the group is closed and nothing while it is open; "closed" is the real collapse state (`group::group_closed_value`), not the default. A feature that is on but blocked keeps its amber reason. Subsurface in Off mode has no blocked header. An active blocked header reads `{state}, blocked by {feature}`, open or closed (CC10). This narrows "a closed header is never silent" to headers whose state shows nowhere else (controller ruling, 2026-11-18). FM33's kept group follows it. This supersedes in part (1.x) CC8's header values (Subsurface "Off, Standard, Foliage"; Coat, Emissive, Parallax, Glint, Fuzz "on" or "off"), FB2's "A feature's state shows once, in its own group header ("on" / "off")" and AJ4's kept Coat group "reading "off"".
- **JR7 The 3D preview serves a link (E1, 3D).** `set_preview::member_slots` serves a linked slot's file at the link's path, keyed by the row the link fills (`link.row`), and does not serve that row's own fill. A rowless link (slot 5) can remain in the candidate without contributing local bytes. For an unresolved typed path the own fill keeps its prefix path and the scene can load the requested path from game data, including a BSA. A linked Coat replaces an own or borrowed Fuzz in slot 7. Both source and encoded A/B copies of a resolved link survive an unchanged resync. Local link overrides require `writes_entry`, including the statics rule. The simulator is given the set's candidate entry (`pbr_export::json_entries`, read by `set_preview::fingerprint`), so a set whose statics drop its entry is still previewed; on such a set the candidate carries no stored `slotN` link or clear, as its written result would not (controller ruling 3b-F9; `slot_links::the_preview_of_a_set_that_writes_no_entry_simulates_no_stored_slot_command`). The member key is not `link.kind`, because `Encoder.members` is keyed by kind and a link to another slot's kind would evict the set's own fill of that kind (controller ruling, 2026-11-18).
- **JR8 A linked slot counts for the features (owner, 2026-11-16, "4 yes").** Height and parallax by controller ruling 3, round 2: "A linked height turns `parallax` on … make the `checks.rs` `filled` reader … treat a linked height as filled"; slot 7 by controller ruling 2, round 2, quoted in JR1. A slot linked from another set (resolved to a source set, an item and a kind) counts for the feature defaults (`auto_flags`) and for the coat, fuzz and subsurface blocks as the kind of the row it fills (`link.row`, JR1), in place of the set's own and borrowed fills of that game slot: the game reads a slot by its number, so the feature is the slot's (controller ruling, 2026-11-18). A typed path counts on the same terms as a menu pick: it must resolve under JR14 to an indexed local output with a kind. The first writer in display order supplies the item even across different mod roots. A counting link with no row (game slot 5) removes the slot's own fills and adds none (`// shortcut:`). The readers take `sets::feature_slots` (`SetView.features`, built by `link_slots`): the PGPatcher JSON writer, `checks.rs` and `pgp_fields::context_of`. A link changes the input of the feature defaults (`auto_flags_for`), not the keys themselves: it defaults a key on as an own file of that kind would, `emissive` (`_g`), `parallax` (`_p`, height), `multilayer` and `coat_normal` (`_cnr`), `fuzz_on` (`_f`, written as the `fuzz` object), and for `_s` `subsurface` or `subsurface_foliage`, or `coat_diffuse` while multilayer is on. A default never replaces a key the set writes itself (`false` included), a feature another feature blocks gets none, and on a PBR grass set only `_s` switches a key on (AS8); so the exported JSON gains the key only where a default would apply. A linked height counts as filled for the checks: a keyed Landscape or PBR Landscape set with one joins CHK-PARALLAX-SCALE's comparison of displacement scales, which reads no height pixels. Changing or clearing the effective height can add or remove the actual Problems row on the set and its keyed peers. The no-albedo PBR exception (HL5) applies to the PBR pick alone. For `_s`, Standard or Foliage follows the destination’s tree evidence or path fallback. With multilayer active the default is coat diffuse. A link whose source set is gone no longer counts. This supersedes in part AJ7 ("member textures switch features on") and AJ11 ("a filled coat slot", "a filled fuzz slot") (1.x; the digest's coat line says so).
- **JR9 A cleared slot turns no feature on.** Controller ruling 5, round 3: "an own file under `slotN = ""` turns no feature on, which changes exported JSON for such sets: the controller's call, for the owner to overrule". An own or borrowed file under `slotN = ""` is left out of the JSON, so `feature_slots` drops every kind of that game slot and the file no longer defaults its feature on (`emissive` for a cleared slot 3, for example). An explicit false overrides the default. Subordinate properties stay stored in the project. The writer can omit the disabled group’s properties or an explicit default false. Reactivation emits applicable stored values again, including emissive scale or nested fuzz weight. This holds only for a set that writes a PGPatcher entry (`features` is computed where `writes_entry` holds, as `linked` is: the statics decision included, JR14); a set without one writes no `slotN`, so its clear changes nothing. A clear counts only where it removes an own or borrowed fill. For such JSON sets the other `feature_slots` readers follow: a cleared height takes a Landscape or PBR Landscape set out of CHK-PARALLAX-SCALE's comparison, and a cleared `_cnr` or `_f` blocks nothing. The exported JSON changes for a set whose cleared slot (3, 4, 7, 8) held a file that defaulted a feature on; no pinned test changed.
- **JR10 No recipe combo (owner addition A1, 2026-11-17: "pbr is always rmaos, advanced skin is always rfaos, can be removed").** Rulings A1-1, A1-4, A1-5, A1-6, A1-7, A1-8, A1-9 and A2-5; a fuzz map follows the set's shader (owner: "fuzz is grayscale only for advanced skin, it should be treated according to which shader it is in", Note JQ).
  - The set editor has no RMAOS/RFAOS combo; the shader and the maps pick the recipe, and the packed row's slot title (RMAOS or RFAOS) shows the result. `packing.recipe` stays in the config, the engine, the CLI and the schemas. The selector's rules went with it: R-review I2's RFAOS option, its disabled reason and its re-click rule, and R-fills-2's "the recipe choice" (1.x); the digest's fills and skin-follow lines (cites R-fills-1, R-fills-2) stay.
  - **The model's guard stays.** `AppModel::set_packing_recipe` refuses RFAOS on a set that cannot build it (no roughness, glossiness or AO source, `rfaos_unbuildable`): it writes nothing, moves no fill and returns the reason, so the fills stay on the planned `_rmaos` key. Its one product caller is the sub-type edit's follow-on (`edit_subtype`, Note DQ: a diffuse made Advanced Skin takes its set's packed output to `_rfaos`, one taken off it goes back, in one step). There the sub-type change still lands, the refusal shows no message, and the packed row's title keeps RMAOS. The title carries the `KEEPS_RMAOS` hover and AccessKit description only on cs when the set's resolved shader is Advanced Skin; on a set whose shader stays PBR (an explicit PBR shader wins), it has no such hover. Test `packing_fills::rfaos_on_a_metallic_only_set_is_refused_with_the_reason`.
  - **PACK-UNUSED-INPUT** no longer says to change the recipe: "A packing input isn't used by this set — remove it, or change the set's shader or the file's type" (controller ruling: the combo the old sentence named is gone; `model/code_text.rs`).
  - **Clear recipe.** PACK-RECIPE-CONFLICT and PACK-RECIPE-UNBUILDABLE offer **Clear recipe** only when a recipe other than `auto` is written on either of the set's packed `[files]` keys (`_rmaos`, `_rfaos`) and the target is cs (`AppModel::recipe_written`); otherwise the row has no fix and keeps its sentence, as a recipe from the project, a preset or `--set` is not the button's to clear (controller ruling, 2026-11-18). The button and the effective file override use the same parser for a stored recipe. Its hover reads "Remove packing.recipe from this set's packed entries in sdc.toml. A project or preset recipe can apply next." The button removes `packing.recipe` from both packed entries in one undo step and prunes a `packing` table and a `[files."…"]` entry it leaves empty; fills stay, with their own CFG-FILE-UNMATCHED row where they have no file (A1-8).
  - **SHADER-EXTRA-MAP.** The row that names the set's packed output offers **Clear recipe**, then **Change shader…**, under the same condition (A1-7); otherwise **Change shader…** alone. It never offers **Remove from set**: a packed output cannot leave its set, and a button that can never work is noise (controller ruling, 2026-11-18). **Go to set** opens the set editor for the packed member named by the warning. An unslotted extra `_m` has no row registered for scrolling or outlining. The recipe fix still requires a matching RMAOS/RFAOS key. A non-packed extra member keeps **Remove from set**, then **Change shader…** (JO15).
  - On a read-only `sdc.toml` **Clear recipe** is disabled and its hover gives the read-only reason (A1-8); the command itself also returns early for an unrecognised packed key, a non-CS target, or a missing or read-only project document (A1-9). Undo restores the recipes removed from both keys. A metallic-only Advanced Skin set that keeps `_rmaos` (DQ2) says why on hover of its packed row's title (`set_editor::KEEPS_RMAOS`).
  - The standing exceptions stay: DQ2, a pinned diffuse (DW1), a pre-packed image (CO109), a plain `diffuse` picked by hand on an Advanced Skin set (DV3), and a written recipe still wins over an explicit shader (A1-7, Fuzz-20: a written `rfaos` under PBR keeps `_rfaos`, a written `rmaos` under Advanced Skin keeps `_rmaos`).
- **JR11 A set filter follows its set (follow-up F7; planner's call and controller rulings, 2026-11-18).** `bottom::view_of` (over `view_lookup`, which holds the lookup order once) finds, after the key, the key-less id and the folded id (JP5), the key-less set whose `engine::sets::set_key(sets root, id)` is the stored value, so a Problems filter opened by a set's key follows the set when the set loses every file and with it its key. When the output root changes, the filter's stored value (`Set`, `SetNotes`, `SetChecks`) is rekeyed to the new root as the selected set is (JI2; `AppModel::rekey_filter` in `model/export_scope.rs`) when it names a set by key, and left as it is when it names a set by its id. Which it names is decided by the lookup under the plan before the change (`view_lookup`, `AppModel::set_filter_names_an_id`: the second and third lookups name an id), since the text cannot tell a typed id from a key. Selection and the filter use the same old-root membership rule, independently. An explicit ID survives even when another selected set changes key. Export scope has its own rule for keys outside the old root. Opening another project folder drops set-specific Problems filters, including on a cancelled switch. Reopening the same folder keeps them. Non-set filters survive a folder switch. The filter's chip resolves through the same lookup (`bottom::scope_label`). Tests `problems_notes::a_keyed_sets_filter_follows_the_set_once_it_loses_its_key`, `a_set_filter_keeps_its_set_when_the_root_moves_to_an_ancestor`, `a_set_filter_keeps_its_set_when_the_root_changes` and `a_key_less_ids_filter_is_not_rekeyed_when_the_root_changes`.
- **JR12 The new view fields hold after a partial plan as after a full build (planner's call).** `short` is carried over by `splice_views` (a splice never changes the list of set ids), `outputs` is set in `finish_view`, which both paths run, and `linked` / `features` are built by `link_slots`. `build_views` runs it over every view, and `splice_views` runs it over the views it rebuilds. `splice_views`' mapped-project shortcut is also taken when any sets-doc entry has a non-empty string `slotN`, so the splice path only meets `slotN = ""` clears; the shortcut's comment says to upgrade "when a splice can name the sets a link reads" (controller ruling, 2026-11-18). `view_reads` includes the job key and the settled output’s exact OS string. A change of case or an extra dot component refreshes the displayed spelling even on a carried view. A moved output rebuilds its links without a second mapped-job-key check. Pinned by `fast_replan::views_after_a_partial_equal_a_fresh_build`, `…_with_a_linked_slot` and `…_with_a_cleared_slot`. 3a's `sets::editor_cells` is built on `view_row`, so the editor's rows and the status line's slot reasons follow a link's kind on slots 3 and 7 together (JO7).
- **JR13 What JR supersedes in the 1.x record.** R-review I2: its selector clauses (the RFAOS option, its disabled reason, the re-click rule) are superseded and its model guard is restated as a live rule in JR10; the skin-detection digest line no longer cites I2. In part: AJ7 and AJ11 (JR8, JR9); AJ4's kept group "reading "off"", CC8's header values and FB2's "own group header" clause (JR6); FB2's icon-toggle flags (JR4); FB2's editor floor, FC8's 776 px and CE15's 520 px preview floors (JR5); DY18's `SOURCE_W` (JR4); R-fills-2's recipe selector (JR10). The digest lines that cite them state the current rule.
- **JR14 Export reads what the JSON carries (3b-F3, F12, F13, F18, F19, F23, F35, F41 to F45).** The no-link, no-clear guard pins the export blocks and written JSON bytes for its project (`partial_export::with_no_link_and_no_clear_export_blocks_and_writes_what_it_did`). Membership identifies a pack's owner even when the set borrows slot 6. Mapping that pack to its default path creates no collision with itself. A consumer borrowing the pack can name the lender's legacy output name. JR17 gives the slot path and the checks Export makes before writing the entry.
  - **Path identity and the index.** Export, From another set and the index use the same projected game path (JR17). The index includes each set member that writes its own output, including extra files of the same kind and unslotted files. The first producer in display order wins. An unslotted complex `_m` supplies a file without turning on a feature. PGPatcher folds ASCII case and adds `textures\` when the stored string lacks it. The index uses that rule to look up a stored `slotN`. Path… validates a relative DDS path and stores backslashes. From another set offers members with a game location and logs a changed pick (JR1). The menu waits for planning and scan reads to finish and for the displayed plan to match the current settings. A failed plan holds the picker and Export. The menu caches the settled check until its inputs change and the click repeats it before storing the path. The menu and index each read the output roots once per list. When a new plan installs different game paths, the index resolves the links again.
  - **One answer to "does this set write an entry".** `pbr_export::writes_entry` is `exports_json` (CS, a JSON set, a match that applies) and the statics rule keeping the entry (`pgp_entry_wanted`; a CS set with statics set to Never writes none). The link display, the feature defaults (`features`), the missing-map suppression and the cleared state read it through `link_slots`, and `written_json_entries` filters by it. On a set that writes no entry a stored link or clear changes no feature default and drops no missing-map row, and an own file under `slotN = ""` shows as itself (no dim chip, no "Left out of the JSON" sentence): `slot_shown`'s `Cleared` needs the file left out of `features`. `link_slots` asks `writes_entry` only of a set with a `slotN` string. The one exception is a record fill (controller ruling 3b-F13, withdrawing 3b-F9's record-fill clause): a stored `slotN` of any value keeps a TXST record from filling that slot (Note HG) on every set, a set that writes no entry included, as on main, because that decision feeds the engine's `borrowed_normal` input and so the roughness bake, and encoded bytes do not change. So on a statics-Never mapped set a typed path or a hand-edited `slotN` over a record-provided fill hides that fill in the editor, as on main (`slot_links::a_stored_slot_command_keeps_a_record_fill_out_as_on_main`, `txst_map::a_stored_slot_key_keeps_the_record_normal_out_of_the_engine_inputs`). Readers that promise the written result read `writes_entry` too: the no-albedo Info row; the 3D preview's candidate entry leaves the command out (JR7).
  - **Validation.** Export's blocks read what the JSON names (`sets::json_files`, over the fields the feature keys read: `feature_slots` and `linked`), in three states: a local file (an own or borrowed fill no `slotN` overrides, or a link that names a set's output, on the row it fills), a typed path no set writes (it fills its game slot for the JSON and is never missing), or nothing (a cleared slot). So "{feature} is on but the set has no …" counts a linked or typed-path height, coat normal, fuzz, glow or subsurface and does not count a cleared one; "_cnr and _f both use slot 7" and "_g and _sk both use slot 3" need two own or borrowed files the JSON keeps, so a `slotN` on that slot (a link, a typed path or a clear) clears the clash; and the "has no output path" check skips a slot whose `slotN` the JSON writes, a clear included (`slots_of` writes the string). One helper answers "does the JSON keep this fill" for the clash, the output-path check and the editor's conflict line with its Remove buttons (`sets::json_keeps_fill`, read by `view_row` without allocating), so on every set the editor shows a conflict only where the JSON keeps both fills: on a set that writes an entry, in the run or not, that is its effective slots (a `slotN` on the slot leaves no conflict); on a set that writes none (its statics drop it), its own and borrowed fills. Export checks a set in the run in its blocks, and a set left out of the run only in `prepare` and only when it has a stored slot command (its entry is held with the reason); a set left out with none is not checked (controller rulings 3b-F8, 3b-F12 and 3b-F17; `set_editor_ui::a_slot_the_json_overrides_shows_no_conflict_as_export_has_none`, `slot_links::export_takes_a_typed_path_over_a_clashing_slot_7_or_3`). On a set that writes no entry these read its own and borrowed fills, as before. The typed-path state changes a block for a set whose `slotN` names no set's output: Parallax on with a typed slot-4 path no set writes no longer blocks, and a typed slot-7 path over an own `_cnr` and `_f` no longer clashes.
  - **Dependencies and scope.** A link that names a set's output is a dependency exactly as a borrow is; scope and dependencies come from the link as `link_slots` resolved it, so a `slotN` string that names no settled output (a typed path, or a path the producer no longer writes) is an external path with no host and no dependency (controller ruling 3b-F5). Export set, Export group and Export N sets include local lenders under the same excluded-group rule. A direct queued export expands the selected keys after planning settles. If Export in Review triggers a source rescan, it keeps the keys expanded before the rescan. A newly resolved lender can stay outside the run and its consumer is held when the file is missing (3b-F20, 3b-F36). The finalizer's dependencies of a JSON entry are the local files `json_files` names (`pbr_export::prepare`). Export compares the emitted local slot with the producer's projected game path (JR17) and matches the run row by its manifest job key. For an excluded local lender, Export checks the job's physical output at its exact spelling, including Unicode. A failed row holds the entry even if an older file exists. A Vanilla borrow copy checks its captured source path (3b-F19). A linked file is checked like a borrowed one (written or up to date in this run, or on disk for a set left out on purpose, HF9) and the entry is held with the same note when it is not. An own or borrowed file that a `slotN` (a link, a typed path or a clear) overrides is not in the JSON, so it is no dependency of the entry; the set's own conversion jobs and a borrow's scope are unchanged. So a clear over a borrowed file never exported lets the entry in (`partial_export::a_clear_over_a_never_exported_borrow_lets_the_entry_in`). An entry of a set left out of the run gates nothing (HF9). A set with a stored slot command is written only when its own checks pass (the clash, missing-texture, feature-clash, prefix, slot-path and output-path blocks, `pbr_export::set_blocks`); otherwise it is held with "not in JSON: {set} ({reason})" and the run goes on. A set with none is not checked, as on main: its files on disk are accepted and its entry is written (controller rulings 3b-F7 and 3b-F12; `partial_export::an_entry_left_out_of_the_run_is_held_when_its_own_checks_refuse_it`, `…::with_no_slot_command_an_excluded_set_is_written_as_main_writes_it`). Tests `partial_export::a_scoped_export_treats_a_linked_lender_as_a_borrowed_one`, `an_excluded_linked_lender_holds_the_entry_as_a_borrowed_one_does` and `a_borrow_under_a_link_is_no_dependency`.
  - **The no-albedo Info.** Decided from the JSON the writer produces: an explicit `slot1` (a link, a typed path or a clear) is what the JSON names for slot 1, so the mesh keeps nothing and the "No albedo in this set; the mesh keeps {path}…" row (HL6) is not raised; `pbr_export::no_albedo_slot1` is `None` then, and `slots_of` writes the same JSON as before (the explicit `slot1` won already). Test `slot_links::with_a_linked_slot_1_the_no_albedo_info_is_not_raised`.
- **JR15 Accessible slot and fix names (final wave, Group 5).** A member chip exposes the file, pin, PGPatcher lock and link target in its description. A cleared button describes the file without an “empty” value. A typed-path chip paints its locks and its accessible description gives only the linked path. A linked file label says “PGPatcher reads {path} for this slot.” Its “Open set {short} ({slot label})” button says “Open the set this file comes from.” The Match controls are “Match on this (Diffuse)” and “Match on this (Normal _n)”. A cut format exposes its full value. Output files names its button “Show {full path} in Explorer”. Problems names its fixes “Clear the packing recipe of {out} ({code})” and “Change shader for {id} ({code})” using the output's leaf name and the set ID respectively. Two outputs with the same leaf name and code share a Clear recipe name. Two warnings with the same set and code share a Change shader name.
- **JR16 Reuse text on unchanged frames (final wave, Group 7).** Resolve the Problems chip label when its filter or views change. Build Match help on hover. Measure a card’s whole title once for its id, unique tail, search, Body font and pixel scale. Measure shorter candidates only when needed. The fit at the label’s actual available width supplies both the title and its wrap mode. Resizing reuses candidate widths and preserves the inline threshold. Output files polls cached file stats and reuses its count text until the count changes. Open rows share the export root and reuse the displayed path, absolute path and Explorer names. Format value and width share one fit. Source pickers reuse the fitted suffix, full accessible value and channel labels. Include the button font, pixel scale and combo chrome in the fit. Construct the disabled reason on hover. The drawing caches expire after the controls leave the frame.
- **JR17 Written slot paths (3b-F35, F39 to F43 and F45).** The landed plan supplies the game directory from the accepted TXST mapping, the owning PBR set's output prefix or the source job's game path. The job supplies the filename it writes. A mapping to `textures\armor\textures\stone_p.dds` keeps both the `armor` directory and its inner `textures` directory. The JSON can omit the leading `textures\` when PGPatcher resolves the string to the same file.
  - For an owned PBR albedo with no slot command, Export compares the output and default paths using Unicode lowercase. With `match1 = 'stone\É'` and `rename = 'textures\pbr\stone\é'`, the JSON omits `slot1`. An explicit height can keep its source game path `textures\armor\rock_p.dds`, and a renamed albedo keeps the interior dot in `textures\stone\.\wall.dds`. If a TXST requests `textures\İ\rock_fuzz.dds` and the job writes `rock_f.dds`, the lender and borrower both name `textures\İ\rock_f.dds`.
  - For an emitted slot supplied by this project's own file, a borrow or a stored local link, Export checks the string PGPatcher reads against the producer's projected location. A mismatch holds the entry. The producer also needs a completed or up-to-date row, or its physical DDS on disk when left out of the run. A failed row holds the entry even over an old DDS. A typed or retained external path has no local producer and passes through as written.
  - Export can still publish a slot whose file is absent when a sibling `textures` folder makes a separate export drop the output-root prefix or when a PBR rename is exported through a junction alias of the input `textures` folder (3b-F46).
- **JR18 Unlink and Use this file (3b-F35).** Both buttons remove the stored slot command and replan. If removing slot 2 uncovers a TXST normal from another set, the next export uses the settled plan with that borrowed normal.

## Note JU — Tests for UI invariants and the codes page (main team, 2026-11-18)

- **JU1 Every code has its row on the codes page.** `engine/tests/wiki_codes.rs` reads the rows of the rendered tables of `docs/wiki/Diagnostic-Codes.md` whose first column is headed `Code` (outside fenced blocks, with or without outer pipes) and fails when a code in `Code::ALL` has no row, has two, or has a severity other than the engine's, when a row names a code that does not exist, when a data row of such a table does not start with one code in single backticks, and when the page has no such table. Of Markdown it knows the two fence markers (a fence of either opens or closes a block, whatever its length) and not HTML comments or indented code blocks. `SET-BAKE-RATIO` keeps its code under HD2 so an `allow` list naming it still loads; its row says it is no longer raised.
- **JU2 Which rules are tests.** The `ui_proof` walk (JU4) checks five `egui-ui-rules` rules (JU5 to JU8, JU11), and `wiki_codes` checks the codes page (JU1). A rule a test checks keeps, in the skill, kittest.md or a mistakes entry, its number, how to build it and the egui cause, then one pointer, "checked by <binary> <function>; limits in Note JU<n>", plus in a few words any limit that leaves the reader something to check by hand. How a test works and its verdict live only in this note.
- **JU3 The screen list.** `crates/gui/tests/ui_proof/screens.rs` lists the screens the walk visits, each opened the way its doc-shot scenario opens it, without the GPU, on the `demo` or `helpers` temp-dir fixture with the export folder inside the screen's own temp folder, or with no project (`NO_PROJECT`) for the start screen; each has a marker, and no entry's marker may hold on another entry's retained middle-size snapshot. A screen that needs the GPU (the 2D previews), a real scan or a fixture neither holds is left out and named in the file's header: the cube panel is out because no temp-dir fixture holds six face files, and no cube fixture is built for the walk. The list lives in the `ui_proof` binary, because its harness redirects the app-data folders for the whole process; each screen's temp folder sits beside them in the one process-wide temp folder, and the walk's harness activates the Meshes view with no MO2 launch detected.
- **JU4 One walk.** `invariants::the_walk_finds_nothing_new_on_any_screen` visits every screen at 1100x800, 1280x800 and 1600x900 with the pointer out of the window, runs `checks::run_checks` once per inspected screen and size snapshot and every invariant of `FRAME_INVARIANTS` and `OBSERVING_INVARIANTS` on it, then the key step once per screen at 1280x800, and then lets the screen go; a new invariant joins one of the two lists, never a second walk. A visit that cannot be made, a frame of another size than asked for, a modal dialog open on a screen not marked as a dialog, a marker that fails (a needle counts only where its node or text is shown; each marker names its screen's own body, which `screens::an_editor_marker_needs_the_body_and_not_only_the_header` proves for the four set-editor entries) and anything an invariant cannot observe is an observation error of that screen and size. The walk does not see the 2D and 3D previews, tooltips, a running worker, error states, the light theme or windows under 1100 wide, and a node the tree lacks is no error: it is not judged.
- **JU5 Window edge and clipped text.** `nothing_past_the_window_edge` reports an interactive node whose rect intersects the window and, where its clip is known, intersects its clip, and that runs past the window's left or right edge by more than 0.5 px (any other is only counted), and `no_clipped_text` a partly visible Label that sticks out of its clip cut to the window (the window alone when the clip is unknown) at the left or right by more than 0.5 px. A cut at the top or bottom, a widget past its column's edge inside the window, an interactive widget with no AccessKit node, and text painted inside a button or by a painter with no Label node are not reported.
- **JU6 Cut text.** `cut_text_keeps_its_value` reports a painted text cut by egui when no name, value or description of the smallest named node holding it contains the whole painted text, a text egui cut whose own text has an ellipsis that fails the next rule (a hand cut cut again), and a text showing an ellipsis when no such field holds a longer text it is cut from (compared as painted, with only the decorations in `TRAILING_DECORATIONS` and `LEADING_DECORATIONS` stripped) and its whole text is not in `AUTHORED`. `AUTHORED` lists the app's labels that end in an ellipsis by design, matched on the whole text with no screen, count or px; an entry must be painted on some screen of the walk, or it is stale. The check reads the node, not the tooltip, a hand cut spelled like an `AUTHORED` entry passes, and the node is chosen by geometry (containment, area, depth), not by the text's paint layer or the node's clip.
- **JU7 Keys never write.** `press_keys` presses Enter, Escape and Space with nothing focused on every screen at 1280x800 and reports `keys_never_write` for a net change between the trees taken right before and right after the key (a file or folder added, changed by content or deleted) under the screen's temp folder or the harness's app-data folder, and for a started export (the running flag on the key-down frame and after settling, or the export's record and the count of exports started, before and after); the trees are keyed by native relative paths. A folder it cannot read, a settle that times out, an export already running and a screen that clearing the focus dismisses are errors; when a key has closed the screen, the entry is replayed before the next key only (none after the last), as part of the same visit. It cannot see a write outside the two folders, a change undone before the after tree, a key with a modifier or inside a focused field, or an export that starts and ends within the key's frames and leaves its record and that count unchanged.
- **JU8 Scroll bars.** `scroll_bars_are_visible_and_clear` reports, with the pointer out of the window, a vertical scroll bar whose own paint (its layer, inside its clip and strip: one track with at most one nested handle, the track being the tallest that spans the strip and, at equal height, the one that holds the other, then the wider) has no alpha above 0, and the interactive nodes of the bar's layer whose visible rect (the rect cut by its clip where known) overlaps the track's height, starts left of the track's right edge (a node starting at or beyond it is not judged) and ends more than 0.5 px past the track's right edge less the bar's width; a Sets card has only to stop short of the track, panel splitters are exempt (egui's resize handle on the panel edge, not a control of the app), and a node of another layer floats over the area on purpose. It does not see a bar's look beyond alpha above 0, a shape in the bar's own layer nested in its track like a handle, an overflowing area whose `ScrollBar` node is gone (no node gives no finding) or a local hover style wider than `look::thin_scroll()`'s. A bar's panel and the Sets column's edge come from `ui::panel_ids::panel_rect`, the panel's last stored rect, which does not say whether this frame built the panel, so a stale panel rect can name a bar or place that edge.
- **JU9 Allowlists and the verdict.** The walk fails on a hit no `ALLOWED` entry matches, on an entry a hit did not consume, on a stale `AUTHORED` entry and on every error; a hit an entry matches passes. An `ALLOWED` entry in `invariants.rs` is a hit the tree has today, a fault not yet fixed or a known non-fault (an ellipsis the author wrote inside a text), with its exact screen, invariant, name and place among same-named hits, its window size less than 0.5 px off in each dimension, its px within 2.0 inclusive (exact px fails on a font-metric or path-length shift), a count of one and a reason; a scroll bar's clearance entry also lists each widget by exact name with its own px within 2.0 inclusive, and the bar is named "scroll bar K of N in the P panel". These rules are for `ALLOWED` only; delete an entry when its hit is gone.
- **JU10 What the walk takes on trust.** Some settles inside a fixture or a step line drop their timeout, so a worker still running there is an error only when the entry's own settle after it also times out. The px of every `ALLOWED` entry were measured with the host's fonts (`fonts::install_system_fonts`), so another font, a fallback glyph or `SDC_FONT=embedded` can wrap a row or move an overrun by more than 2.0 px and fail the walk.
- **JU11 Status dots.** `status_dots_have_names` reports a filled circle whose unmultiplied RGB equals `ui::status::RED`, `AMBER` or `GREEN` (alpha ignored) and whose disc its clip shows with positive area, when the innermost node of its layer that holds the whole circle and whose visible rect shares a positive area with the disc has no name (a Label's text counts, an input's value and the ancestors do not); a node whose clip is unknown keeps its place in the snapshot's order among the candidates, and is an error only when counting it in would change the winner. It does not see what a dot's name says, a name of spaces only, a status in another colour, or a status disc drawn as a mesh or a path instead of a circle.

## Note JS — Normal sources for roughness baking and shared specular alpha (owner, 2026-11-18)

The owner: "if a normal is only ever in a pbr set, its alpha should always be drop without a message, a normal in a shared set should always be data. A person can add the 'data' treatment to pbr normals incase they plan to share it with an existing texture, or they want one normal for advanced skin/regular skin sets (none msn ofc)"; of B3's note and button: "remove the note and the button, if a normal is only ever in a pbr set it's un needed, if a normal is in both sets it could misdirect"; of a landscape PBR normal: "is the alpha in a landscape pbr normal used? if it is then keep it, if not it should be retyped to pbr normal" (it is not: Community Shaders' PBR terrain reads roughness, metalness, AO and specular from the layer's `_rmaos`, and the normal's alpha reaches only `glossiness`, which a TRUE_PBR material does not read); and asked whether the shared-alpha step runs only on the user's own steps or also as an automatic step: "1" (only the user's own steps). Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-18-normal-bake-and-shared-alpha-design.md`; plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-18-normal-bake-and-shared-alpha.md`.

- **JS1 The rule.** A "PBR set" is one whose shader is PBR, PBR Grass, PBR Landscape or Advanced Skin (`Shader::is_pbr_or_advanced_skin`). A `pbr_normal` defaults to a silent alpha drop (P25). A plain `normal` keeps specular alpha by default, even in a PBR-only set. The normal shader-follow rules use the recorded type to identify an untouched normal (JS5–JS6). A shader change preserves a stored tangent type with no listed automatic word. JS6 covers the older `auto_pbr` leave step. An eligible user step writes Data for a newly shared normal (JS7). A user-written alpha policy takes precedence and suppresses the fallback Note. The engine never changes a normal's alpha policy because of its shader. Its suggestions are unchanged, so `sdc convert` reads the same normal settings from `sdc.toml`.
- **JS2 The bake (N-7, N-12, N-13, N-15, N-22).** For a roughness bake, `bake::plan::own_normal` prefers `pbr_normal` under any shader. A picked or automatic PBR, PBR Grass or PBR Landscape shader also accepts `normal`, then `landscape_normal`. Advanced Skin also accepts `normal`. An authored `landscape_normal` on Advanced Skin gets SHADER-EXTRA-MAP and supplies no bake. A regular or derived pick accepts only `pbr_normal`. Within a type, the first usable image by path wins. A borrowed Normal slot uses the same priority among the lender's normals, with the borrower's shader deciding the eligible types. When the lender has several normals, the slot can show one file while roughness bakes from another. The borrower can bake from the lender’s own normal even when the lender’s slot shows a borrowed one. The bake's own `renormalize_bake` (provenance `set`) is no CL40 opt-in (`gloss_bake_opted`). `profile_sha256` hides `renormalize_bake` for `landscape_normal` too. An explicit `mips.normal = renormalize` still opts out (R6). On `cs`, the missing-roughness check applies to the selected own normal, including a plain normal under an eligible pick. It skips shared roughness and a normal lent to a borrower with a writable roughness member. With no roughness companion it reports SET-MISSING-COMPANION (Info). A DDS-only companion has its own code and an image companion outside the batch is silent. Messages: "has no normal job that bakes (… or its type does not bake under this shader)", "has no usable normal job". Tests in `normal_alpha::a_borrowed_slot_shows_the_first_normal_but_bakes_from_the_lenders_priority_pick`, `bake_plan`, `bake_run::a_plain_normal_that_starts_baking_re_encodes_only_its_roughness`, `gloss_bake::the_bakes_own_renormalize_bake_never_opts_into_the_gloss_bake`, `manifest::tests::a_landscape_normals_bake_hashes_as_renormalize`, `shader::tests::a_derived_pick_never_bakes_a_plain_normal`.
- **JS3 Identity (N-11, N-23).** No `encode_version` arm and no engine version moves. Under `if-changed`, a roughness member that starts baking gains a bake source and re-encodes once. This includes eligible automatic PBR Landscape sets that start using a plain or landscape `_n`. A DDS job or explicit `renormalize` opt-out does not start a bake. A set already baking from the same `pbr_normal` gains no new source. A `landscape_normal` with a user-written `renormalize_bake` hashes as `renormalize` (same bytes, CL40 never read it). Retyping the `_n` (JS5–JS6) or changing its effective alpha policy to Data (JS7) re-encodes the normal once when its identity changes. The alpha policy also contributes to the identity of each roughness output that actually bakes from it. Turning the bake on or off alone leaves the normal up to date (`bake_run::the_n_stays_up_to_date_when_its_bake_turns_on_or_off`).
- **JS4 Roles and the removal (N-8, N-9).** PBR's and PBR Grass's `_n` take `normal` and `landscape_normal`; a plain `_n` raises no SHADER code, and two tangent normals in one set raise no EXTRA (as on PBR Landscape). SHADER-NORMAL-SPECULAR and Use PBR normal are removed; they were pushed, never tagged (`git tag --contains 13fcc0e0` is empty), so there is no migrate hint, and `EVENT_SCHEMA` stays 2 (a dropped enum value). The Also export's `legacy_n` on PBR and PBR Grass prefers `pbr_normal`, then `normal`, then `landscape_normal`; PBR Landscape keeps its first `_n`. The vanilla `_n` role and its warnings are unchanged.
- **JS5 `auto_normal` and the recorder (N-17, N-19, N-20, N-26, N-27, N-35, G1).** `[project] auto_normal` records the tangent-space sub-type word Tessalith listed for each key. Untouched = listed with the word its `[files]` sub-type still holds, `alpha_free` (no matching `[files]` policy, or only `data` in the exact table listed in `auto_data`), in no locked slot, not a kept DDS. Each settled recorder pass builds the `auto_normal` list, the `[files]` index and the slot lock map once, then looks up each candidate. The one-input destination rule in JS6 applies throughout.
  - With writable `sdc-project.toml`, the recorder lists the tangent-space sub-types it writes. On `cs` it records a never-recorded `_n` in the set's own Normal slot as `pbr_normal` when the pick `bakes_any_tangent`, the alpha is free, the slot is unlocked and the DDS is not kept. This includes a suggested `landscape_normal`. The follow pass moves an untouched listed normal to `pbr_normal` when its settled pick qualifies. Ordinary recordings update the baseline. An established normal's shader follow gets its own Undo and row. A recording joined to Re-encode stays in that user step (JY6). Before writing the PBR type the recorder verifies that `auto_normal` holds the new word. With a read-only sets document or an `auto_normal` value that cannot store the entry, the recorder can still record the engine's suggestion in writable `sdc.toml`, including `landscape_normal`. A follow alone leaves the stored type in place if it cannot list the change.
  - A user's sub-type edit leaves the list alone (a different value already reads as the user's, so the edit stays files-only and keeps the fast partial plan, FK and FL); only a pick of the listed word takes the key off. Tessalith prunes entries whose value changed when it writes the list.
  - The ownership list is automatic for FI4. The sets document reconciles automatic list changes against its baseline (JY6). Save writes it when its live text differs from disk. Close, open and reload ask only about user edits, so the prompt leaves out a sets document whose only change is the list. The five save-if-clean sites (export folder, target mod and three hash records) save it. Automatic edits alone produce no recovery files. With both documents dirty and a user edit in `sdc.toml`, recovery includes both documents so the restored normal types keep their ownership list.
  - For normal shader following, an unlisted stored tangent type counts as the user's even when it equals the suggestion (N-20, with the `auto_pbr` exception in JS6). Saving presets still uses R5: if a suffix alias changes the suggested type, the recorder replaces a stored type that still equals the old pick, even without an `auto_normal` entry. The preset change wins if the same key also qualifies for PBR follow in this pass. A follow-only Info row omits the High-confidence explanation, including when suggestion acceptance is off. shortcut: an automatic change (a pick leaving the PBR family, a membership change) never reverts an automatic `pbr_normal`. shortcut: list keys are not renamed with `[files]` keys, so a renamed file reads as the user's. Tests in `normal_alpha`.
- **JS6 The pick and the Switch (N-17, N-19, N-28, N-31, G2-G4).** When a normal follows its set’s shader, Tessalith changes its settings only if the entry controls one input. The same check applies to **Keep alpha**, a shader pick, the diffuse’s **Switch** and **Revert**, and **Reset all**. A stem table can qualify. An ambiguous destination or a table that would also retype another input holds the change back. Re-encoded DDS inputs and cube faces count when checking the destination. If a cube face is read through `wall.png.tga`, recording its type can also mark `wall.png` as a cubemap. A mesh scan can write an alpha setting for `wall.png` that also applies to `wall.png.tga`. If the second file is a shared normal, **Remove** can drop its alpha without a **Keep alpha** row, as before this release.
  - An explicit pick acts on the set's own untouched Normal slot. A PBR-family pick writes `pbr_normal`. A regular pick restores the engine suggestion (`landscape_normal` when the path has a `landscape` folder, else `normal`) except when R5 supplies Eye's `msn`. Eye writes `msn` once and parks the listed tangent word. When R5 returns that free `msn` to a tangent type, the new shader decides the word and `auto_normal` lists it. Auto clears the Eye type and its listing, even when clicked before Eye's plan lands. Both the no-change check and the outgoing-shader comparison use the live pick (N6-c, N6-d, NF-6). Auto from an explicit PBR-family pick clears an untouched `pbr_normal`. Both Auto and the diffuse's Revert read the current pick from the sets document, even before the plan lands (N6-g, N6-h). The key leaves `auto_pbr`. Other stored tangent types are the user's, except for the leave step clearing a key an older pick listed in `auto_pbr`.
  - The diffuse's Switch to `pbr_albedo` or `skin_albedo` can retype any free inferred `normal` or `landscape_normal`, or an untouched listed tangent member. An authored alpha policy protects even an inferred normal. Listed members stay in `auto_normal` with the new word, inferred ones join `auto_pbr`, and a model-space member keeps `msn` (IA3). Revert keeps an untouched `pbr_normal` under an explicit PBR-family pick, including Advanced Skin. Otherwise it restores the engine type and the recorder may follow once more (N-31). A shader pick clears the follow marker only when it moves the key away from `pbr_normal`.
- **JS7 Shared alpha (rule C, N-10, N-27, N-33, N-34, N-37, N-38, N-39; N-36 withdrawn by the owner).** The follow-on answers which owner normals regular sets explicitly share (slot 2, chains followed, own-first as JN1). For duplicate set IDs with the same folded spelling, the first matching entry supplies the borrow, as in the slot resolver. Both the automatic Data write and the clear check the lending owner's slot 2 lock, including when the row displays another file.
  - It runs only at the end of the user's own steps on `cs` that changed `sdc.toml` or `sdc-project.toml` and plan again (`edit_docs`, and once per user `batch_edit`); never in an automatic step (the recorder's, the fuzz switch's: `AppModel::automatic_step`), never in a step that does not plan, and never after a settled plan. Both documents must be writable and the starting views must be fresh. It compares the share before and after the step, both read inside it; nothing is stored between steps but `[project] auto_data`. The step reads current borrows, membership edits, chosen shaders and `[files]` values. Each set's automatic shader and Normal-slot member come from the last plan.
  - When the borrower's membership changed and the chain reaches an owner, that owner's normal is `unsure`. When a lender's membership changed anywhere on the path, traversal stops before identifying the owner. The step is then `blind` and holds back every listed clear, including an unrelated Unshare in the same batch.
  - Both the follow-on and the Keep alpha row require a resolved tangent-space sub-type, including types assigned by a rule. A model-space normal stays outside this workflow. A newly shared owner normal with no `alpha.policy`, a job, not a kept DDS, not locked, with a varying alpha or none known yet (N-39: a known constant or absent alpha has nothing to keep) and not already `data` (read after the step: a sub-type the step changed answers by its own built-in) gets `data` and joins `auto_data` only when the destination governs this input alone. The step uses the exact full-name `[files]` table, else the single Unicode-folded full-name match, else a new full key if no table matches. It abstains on a stem-only table, an ambiguous match or a full key that also governs another input (even when creating it).
  - On every run the step checks every listed key. A clear touches only the exact listed table still holding `data` after its last regular share ends. A lock, an uncertain share or a key that now governs another input keeps both the Data policy and its exact `auto_data` key through Unshare and Save. A set whose files are all gone reads no texture: its borrow shares nothing and holds back nothing. A view is read as empty only while no plan is out (N-40), so a set the running plan does not know yet (New set, Duplicate into, a rename's new id) stays `unsure`. A fresh writable step drops a list entry whose exact table is gone or no longer holds `data`, leaving the file value alone; the user's Data pick unlists only the exact table on which that pick wrote Data. An entry for another input that matches through a stem stays listed (N-34).
  - The landed plan's shader picks and resolved Normal slots determine the Note. A regular set must read a PBR-family set's own normal through a borrowed slot, including a TXST record fill. The row names a normal that has a job, is not a kept DDS, has varying alpha, is planned other than `data`, and holds no `alpha.policy` the user wrote ("… its alpha is not kept"), with **Keep alpha**, which writes `data` as the user's step and which the model refuses on a lock, a read-only `sdc.toml` or while the row is waiting for a current plan. A current partial plan can enable Keep alpha while the full plan is still running. Its accessible name is `Keep alpha for <project-relative path>`. Keep alpha uses the same one-input check and full-name precedence. With no full-name match it can also use the matching stem table if it governs this input alone. It creates a full key only if no table matches, and refuses an ambiguous match or a key that also governs another input. Activation rechecks the displayed table against the current row and refuses a changed destination. That covers a family the next plan changes, an owner normal the recorder retypes later, a locked owner and a project opened in that state.
  - shortcut: a leftover automatic `data` on a normal only PBR sets use (a sharer turned PBR by a type decision) stays until a fresh, writable user step can safely clear it; there is no row (in game the PBR path does not read it). shortcut: a set with no shader until the next plan (New set, Duplicate into, a rename's `retarget_auto`, a set sent back to Auto, a set with no card) makes its pairs `unsure`: that owner normal is neither written nor cleared in the step, and the plan's row or a leftover shows the result.
- **JS8 Data on a `pbr_normal` (N-18).** The Alpha row offers Data on a `pbr_normal`; it sticks through the user's retypes and shader picks. Test `normal_alpha::data_is_offered_on_a_pbr_normal_and_sticks`.

## Note JV - One selection per set, key or no key (F11, controller, 2026-11-18)

- **JV1 One selection names one set.** A keyed selection holds its engine key. A key-less selection holds its document id in `Selection::SetId`, including an empty id. A known id resolves by folded id even when it spells another set's key. The card, editor, group and slot reveal, Rename outputs and navigation use that identity (Rename inputs is a Textures-view command, LJ3). A card click, Edit match… (LJ2), a Problems row's set link and Go to set open their source set. Go to set outlines the named slot or member when the 2D editor draws its row. The 3D editor consumes the request without an outline (JO8). A deferred card click follows the source document id to its current key or id after the field commits, and clears selection if the card disappeared. Show opens the set that owns the written JSON entry in the main Textures editor, including from popped JSON over Meshes. Fix path opens the owning field in the main 2D Material editor, expands its groups and focuses it even with JSON popped. The editor reads reasons from the resolved view. Clicking a row reason passes the card's typed target to Problems so Problems shows its own row and applies JO7's focus rule, even for an empty id or one identical to another card's key. The row list, filter chip and Checks/Notes pills keep that distinction. The editor's Slot reason uses a keyed plan warning. A "from set" link goes to the set that owns the planned member output.
- **JV2 A full plan follows the selected set.** On an output-root move, JI2 rekeys the active engine key and every selected set key before pruning and carries a document id verbatim. After the views are carried and the keyed multi-selection is pruned, a surviving keyed member takes priority without rebuilding views. If none survives, the active set is resolved as a single selection in the landed views. A known key matches the engine key or the key a surviving document-only set would have. The selection takes that view's current key or id, or clears if the set is gone, even when another card has the same text in the other namespace. Partial plans keep their membership guard. Within a view revision the editor and preview reuse the resolved selection, including a miss. A same-root gain or loss of key preserves a Problems filter's string. On a root move JR11 rekeys a key-based filter and preserves an id-based one. An ordinary filter uses the old plan's key-first lookup to decide which it holds. A filter opened from a row reason keeps its key or id tag. Opening another folder clears a set filter. Renaming a key-less set while its filter is open can leave that filter stale.
- **JV3 A set with no engine key is selected on its own.** A click with any modifier selects a key-less target alone. A modified click from a key-less active set to a keyed card also starts a single selection. Toggling a keyed group down to one set opens its editor. Right-click preserves a group that includes the clicked set and brings a single card's editor forward. Primary and secondary card navigation let the old field commit first. Ctrl+A from either kind of set selects the eligible keyed cards in display order (JV6). An empty eligible list leaves the selection as it was. Keyed ranges, groups' Select all sets and the palette retain their keyed order.
- **JV4 A change of selection returns the centre to Selection and the editor to its top.** Gaining or losing the engine key changes the session identity and triggers the same reset. The 3D preview and its settings follow the selected set or the keyed owner of a selected texture. The fingerprint reads that set's candidate entry (JR7). When selection changes between polling and drawing, the centre shows Preparing until the polled fingerprint belongs to the active target. A set change drops the previous viewport content and frame while the new scene prepares. The 2D scroll reset waits for the scroll area to draw, including after a selection in 3D or reopening a cleared selection. Mesh choices belong to distinct in-memory targets. A set that gains a key gets its keyed choice, and opening another project clears choices for document ids. Material settings use the resolved view's cached CM card eligibility and TXST block.
- **JV5 Selection is session state.** Renaming away the selected identity clears it on the full landing. F11 preserves the saved settings format, project format, undo record format and exported bytes for the same inputs. Fix path from 3D saves the 2D preference. Revealing a key-less card in a folded group can save the unfolded group, and revealing hidden Problems kinds can save their visibility. Leaving an untouched Rename field preserves the project text and undo history. JV1 partially supersedes JO7's reason-cache key and key-less row attribution, and JP5's scope-key rule for editor reasons. JV1 and JV2 supersede JP5's B3-20 limit. JP5 and JR11 still govern ordinary Problems filters, including badge clicks and "+k more in Problems". Such a filter resolves a key before a matching id, and an empty string names no rows.
- **JV6 Ctrl+A and Shift+click ranges skip folded groups.** Both follow the Sets column's display order and take only keyed cards that pass the filter and are outside a folded group (HF6). Ctrl+A with no eligible cards keeps the current selection. Folding a group keeps its existing selections. The `sets_filter` tests are `ctrl_a_skips_folded_groups`, `shift_range_skips_folded_groups` and `folded_groups_keep_existing_selection`.

## Note JW — set cards and dragging (main team, 2026-11-20)

- **JW1 Empty slots (A4, owner “Floating strip”).** A card at rest draws no chip for `SlotShown::Empty`. Missing “?”, cleared, linked, record, borrowed, own and packed chips stay. Full builds and rebuilt splice views compute `empty` and `empty_line` from the final `view_row` after `link_slots`, even when there are no links. The card and its off-screen stand-in expose the cached description (for example, “Empty: G, P, CNR, S”). The status pill lists the same letters on hover.
  - A `DragItem` or `DragBorrow` over a card reveals its empties in a single painted Foreground strip.
