Its dim letters sit at the filled chips’ bottom-left offset in 32 px boxes, wrapped at `chips_per_row`. The strip starts `STRIP_GAP` (2 px) below the card, flips above it or clamps inside the Sets view. It takes no input. The card owns the strip and the gap to it. Read-only cards can show it. Only editable cards outline themselves and accept a release. A covered card or new-set zone draws a plain frame. Empty slots add no rows to `chip_rows`, so a drag leaves card heights alone (FM32).
  - For an in-app drop, the position of the **last release event, any button**, selects the receiving zone. Test the previously painted strip and bridge before the previous card body. Dispatch a strip or bridge release to its recorded owner even if scrolling made that card a stand-in on the release frame. A later pointer move cannot redirect it. The destination is the set, whichever letter the pointer covers (SC-27, SC-30, SC-31). JW6 governs whether a texture drop there can skip Choice.
- **JW2 Files from Explorer (A4-2).** While `hovered_files` is non-empty, winit sends no pointer moves. Sets reads the OS cursor through `ui::drop_pos`, checks the card’s intersection with the scroll clip, and requests a repaint after 50 ms on every hover frame, even between cards. The previous strip and bridge keep their owner. `ui::os_drop_blocked` blocks both the strip and delivery under a modal. Once the drag ends the strip clears and the hover polling stops (SC-34).
  - A file drop uses `drop_pos` alone. With a viewport inner rect, a failed native cursor lookup means the position is unknown. Without that rect, `drop_pos` falls back to egui’s pointer. An unknown position or a drop on a Sets card, strip or bridge opens the files’ folder (Item 17, spec §5, SC-32 and SC-33). An uncovered editor slot keeps its own drop. Receiving files into a Sets card would need a separate owner ruling.
  - Unit tests for `image_drag_at` and `image_over` put the OS cursor and egui pointer on different cards. The Windows kittest runs with no viewport inner rect and pins the hover wiring only.
- **JW3 Shared outline (A5-1).** A borrowed chip (CD8) and a record fill written by another set (HG7) use a 2 px `status::shared_stroke(vis, width)` and the linked chip’s 10 px panel-backed, top-centre mark between its locks. A shared-helper row (HH7) uses the same stroke at 1 px and a 12 px mark before its name. The function reads the theme’s strong text colour. `status::AMBER` keeps its value for “needs a decision” (`columns::border_of`) and unusable slots (V8).
- **JW4 Dragging a shared chip (A6).** Only a displayed `SlotShown::Borrowed` starts `DragBorrow { item, from }`. A drop shares that file directly into the receiving card or strip owner’s document id, including a keyless card (JV1). Each card zone takes the payload type in flight, since egui 0.37 consumes the payload before checking its type. `member_acts` handles `remove` separately from `ChipDrag`: only own members offer “Remove from set”. Explicit linked chips have no drag payload. During a share the new-set zone draws a plain frame so egui cannot highlight it. Releasing there does nothing. Supersedes the CD8 and K13 no-drag rule and amends DU5.
  - After checking effective links and clears, a drop on the source set reports “Nothing to share: {name} is already in {set}.” For a displayed borrow matching both item and owner, directly or through a chain (JN1), it is “{set} already shares {name} from {from}.” Neither adds an undo step.
  - An explicit link effective in the displayed or resulting view gives “{set}'s {slot} is linked to {target}: Unlink it first.” An effective clear gives “{set}'s {slot} is cleared: use Use this file or Unlink first.” Both leave the command, both documents and Undo intact (SC-1). A Share that would remove an automatic destination set’s last owned texture is refused on release, before Replace (SC-11).
  - An occupied local, borrowed or record slot otherwise asks Replace. Release and confirmation validate current document membership, the lender and the exact numbered-slot item, using the same own-first lookup as the displayed borrow (including JN1 chains and slot 7). If membership differs from the last plan, release or confirmation gives “The shared source changed. Drag the chip again.” before checking the occupant. This includes a destination membership edit while Replace is open. A stale source gives the same message. Confirmation then checks the approved slot kind and occupant, `Own(item)`, `Borrowed(item, lender)` or `Record(path)`. A mismatch in that confirmation check gives “The destination slot changed. Drag the chip again.” Neither refusal writes (SC-8 to SC-10). A chip release or borrowed Replace gives the shared-source message while planning, after a failed plan, or when the inputs differ from the plan. Ordinary Replace reports “Tessalith is still planning. Try again in a moment.” while planning and “The plan must run again first.” after failure or an input change (SC-38, SC-44). Ordinary Replace, including an Explorer drop on an editor slot, checks the approved kind and owned or borrowed occupant (SC-37). A successful Share retargets downstream shares in the same Undo step (JN3) and preserves the JS7 normal edit.
- **JW5 Helpers offer Move and Share (A7-1).** `drop_choices(item)` gives those two choices, in order, for every `ItemKind::Helper`. `choice_allowed` refuses Duplicate even on a direct call with “a helper cannot be duplicated; share it instead”. A raw helper’s Share uses the file itself (HH5).
- **JW6 Remembered answer (A8).** `DropState.last_choice` holds the last Move, Share or Duplicate answered in Choice that the model accepted, including a choice leading to Replace. Cancel, Escape and a refused answer preserve it. It lasts for the app’s run across folder opens and is never saved.
  - In offered order the body buttons read “Move it here”, “Share (borrow its finished output)” (“Share (use this file here too, no copy)” for a raw helper), and “Duplicate the source for this set” when offered. A refused choice is disabled with its model reason on hover and in its AccessKit description. Share checks the exact numbered-lender item for a finished texture, whether an automatic destination set would lose its last owned texture, and whether a raw-helper file share has a destination member (SC-5, SC-13, SC-39, SC-42). Choice caches the offers and reasons per opening, refreshing when the item, destination, model generation, views or editability changes (SC-17). `choice_refused` and the write in `drop_choice` both use `drop_precheck`.
  - A remembered choice offered by `drop_choices` with no refusal uses `selection.bg_fill`, the name “{label} (default, Enter)” and the hover “Your last choice. Enter picks it; hold Shift as you drop to skip this question.” The line below the buttons reads “Enter picks the highlighted choice. Hold Shift when you drop to skip this question.” With no usable remembered answer there is no highlight or hint and an unfocused Enter picks nothing. A focused, enabled answer button takes Enter itself.
  - Default Enter requires a previously open Choice and no focused widget (`dialogs::modal_keys`). Both Enter and Escape require Choice to be the top modal and no other dialog pending in the model, including one requested that frame. Escape wins over Enter in the same frame and leaves files, documents and Undo alone (SC-14, SC-16, SC-19).
  - Shift skips Choice only for an available remembered answer, rechecked at the drop, with exactly one primary release in the frame carrying Shift inside the owning card body, strip or bridge. Read that event’s modifiers and position. A later Shift change has no effect. With zero or multiple primary releases, or the Shift release outside the receiving zone, an ordinary drop that needs Choice still asks (JW1, SC-15, SC-20, SC-28, SC-30). Shift held before the press also works. A Textures tile range-selects on a Shift-click only, so a drag selects nothing. A pending dialog or active modal blocks in-app texture drops on the body, strip, bridge and new-set area (SC-40).
  - **Amends FC5, K1 and K5 for the set-drop dialog only.** Replace always asks and needs a click. The documents change when the drop completes. Duplicate creates its copy at that point. If Replace opens, both wait for confirmation. Duplicate creates the destination exclusively and removes an incomplete copy on failure (SC-41). Duplicate and Redo hash the bytes they write. Undo removes the copy only if the file at its path still matches that SHA-256 (SC-45). If the hash differs, Undo keeps the file and refuses the step before restoring either document. JU7’s disk-write keyboard proof still applies on other screens.
- **JW7 Helpers are never large (A9, owner “Never large”).** Use the corners returned by `dock_cells`. One free corner can hold just `+K`, zero free corners give no dock, and linked chips take none. Suppress a helper’s trailing tile only when a visible badge or accessible overflow list represents it.
  - The visible button reads “+K” for up to nine distinct folded helpers and “+” from ten up. Its AccessKit name gives the full count, “{k} more helpers fill {slot} in {set name}”, singular “1 more helper fills {slot} in {set name}” (SC-23). Its hover lists the grouped files and ends with “Click to list them.” The closed button exposes that list as its AccessKit description. Group hidden channels by helper in dock order. Cache the groups by item and set-document revisions, set, slot and hidden slice. A checks-only completion keeps the groups. Format their display names only for an open popup, hover or active accessibility (SC-22, SC-24 to SC-26).
  - The popup has one “{file}, fills {fills}” button per folded helper, naming all its hidden channels on this chip. Click selects, double-click expands and dragging starts a `DragItem`. The list stays open on selection or drag and closes on Escape or a click outside. The row’s ✕ is named “Remove {file} from set” and is disabled with the reason on hover for a read-only document.
  - A helper left in the trailing row has a 16 px `BADGE` thumbnail in a 20 px `BADGE_HIT` tile with no painted tag. Its name and hover keep “{file}, {role}”, including “unused: …” for a dimmed unused helper. Amends HN1, HN2, HN3, HP9 and HP12.

## Note JX — Shift+click opens a texture from a set card (owner, 2026-11-20)

- **JX1** Clicking a texture thumbnail without Shift follows the card's existing click rules (JV3). Shift+click opens the texture's settings, even if the texture is already selected behind another centre tab (FB7). This applies to slot chips, docked helpers and trailing members. Shift+click on empty card area still extends the set selection.
- **JX2** On a focused thumbnail, Enter or Space opens the set. Holding Shift with either key opens the texture. Both routes wait for a focused edit to commit (FC8). The thumbnail's hover and accessible description give the Shift+click hint.
- **JX3** Dragging a thumbnail still uses its usual payload (JW4). Double-click expands the texture with or without Shift. Right-click opens the slot or member menu. A plain click in the Textures column selects the texture. Go to set in Problems opens the indicated set.
- **JX4** Clicking a helper in the open `+` list opens that texture without Shift (JW7).

## Note JZ — BC1 NVIDIA three-colour midpoint (main team, 2026-11-20)

- **JZ1** The trial scored three-colour fits and solid blocks in packed endpoint order. No candidate met the quality rule. The shipped encoder keeps its legacy midpoint lookup (scored before the endpoint swap), rgbcx's NVIDIA table and the byte-exact default Dual path at (1, 1) (HY18). See the [midpoint measurements](../docs/research/encoder-benchmark.md#bc1-nvidia-three-colour-midpoint-2026-11-20-note-jz).
- **JZ2** Adoption needs a separate owner instruction and a port to current main. Require fresh quality and speed results for each affected mode. Keep cutouts, perceptual, fast and BC3 on their legacy paths until each qualifies separately from plain BC1. For changed bytes, bump the affected `encode_version`, re-record the goldens and document the identity change in DECISIONS.

## Note LA — Authored normal length through the mip chain (owner, 2026-11-20)

- **LA1** A resolved `normal`, `landscape_normal` or `pbr_normal` with `mips.normal_base = keep` requires XYZ storage because BC5, RG8, BC4 and R8 discard Z. Resolve raises CFG-INVALID even for a one-level chain with FMT-BC5-NORMAL acknowledged. Raw chains start from unit directions under LA7. Under `if-changed`, the failed normal keeps its output and record. An eligible CS roughness member losing its sole normal reports SET-MISSING-COMPANION and rebuilds without the bake (JS2, JA1).
- **LA2** In the Normal domain, these three standalone outputs with Keep store the direction `d` of the box-mean authored vector at radius `min(mean authored length, 1 / max(abs(d.x), abs(d.y), abs(d.z)))`. A mean vector of length at most 1e-8 uses +Z: zero inputs emit zero RGB, cancelling nonzero inputs emit +Z at the capped mean length. Only the emitted texel is capped. Larger footprints use the unrounded vector and length means with the same box weights as alpha, including recursive overlap on raw odd sizes. A standalone PBR Grass normal and a derived legacy normal use their resolved profiles, and a twin follows its producer. ComplexGrass and derived GrassStack keep base-only handling on the bottom half and the same detection verdict. SkinDetail follows LA6. Other outputs keep their chains, subject to the XY base rule in LA7.
- **LA3** The roughness and explicit gloss bakes normalize each post-resize base texel before averaging or fitting (amends R1, R5 and HD2). A kept XYZ coat normal carries authored length through its RGB chain, and its active data-alpha self-bake averages unit directions separately. HD1 excludes mip 0, and the 0.999 noise floor, clamp and roughness-channel math still apply. R4's member-owned decode, R6/R7 and JS source selection, CL40's explicit opt-in and the R12 identity tuple (including `normal_base`) stand. A source's external bake toggle alone leaves its own profile hash unchanged. A one-level normal can supply a mipped member. Shared roughness, DDS inputs and one-level members gain no bake.
- **LA4** The authored-length change sets the encode version to 3.0.1 for Normal, LandscapeNormal, PbrNormal, CoatNormal, Rmaos, SkinRfaos and ComplexMaterial (DM). Under JA1 and JA2, `if-changed` keeps an otherwise matching older output and its record until `--ev` covers that output. Omitting `mips.normal_base` still selects Renormalize.
- **LA5** The GUI keeps the Normal base level row, its two choices and its normal-category lock. The row is locked for Advanced Skin detail (LA6) and for tangent normal outputs in formats that drop Z (LA7). Its help distinguishes the four XYZ outputs from base-only handling. Project-Config owns the option table, and the release entry gives the scoped JA commands.
- **LA6** Advanced Skin detail normals start from unit length under both base modes (NL-14, amends LA2 and LA5 for SkinDetail). CS rebuilds BC5 Z from XY (`Lighting.hlsl:1686`), which would flatten detail encoded from a short vector, and normalizes the BC7 direction (`Lighting.hlsl:1690`). BC5 remains valid with project-wide Keep, and the SkinDetail encode-version arm is 3.0.1 (DM, JA2).
- **LA7** Tangent normal outputs in BC5, RG8, BC4 or R8 start from unit directions under either base mode because these formats discard Z. Only the three base types in LA1 reject Keep at Resolve. DetailNormal joins the seven types in LA4 and SkinDetail in LA6 at encode version 3.0.1. SkinWet stays at 3.0.0 because all its writable formats store XYZ.

## Note JY — Export, Review, header and start (review E 3c; controller for the owner, 2026-11-16)

- **JY1 The export preference belongs to the project.** `[project] export_without_review = true` in `sdc-project.toml` enables it. Off removes the key and is the default. A change survives Undo and Redo without adding a step. A clean document saves the pick immediately. A dirty one keeps it for the next Save and says so. A repeat pick changes nothing. Test `export_shell::export_without_review_is_per_project_and_off_by_default`.

- **JY2 The header opens Review by default.** `Review {n} files…` opens the whole project, including from the Ctrl+K palette's Export action. There is no Ctrl+E binding (3c-15). Review's Export writes. By controller ruling, Export set, Export group and Export n sets open Review for the selected sets and follow slot borrows and resolved slot links transitively. A lender in an excluded group is refreshed only if explicitly selected. Otherwise the borrower uses its existing file (HF11). In either scope the preference permits an immediate write only on a settled plan with files to write, an open gate, no undecided textures, no writable NIF edit, no edited PGPatcher JSON, no applicable Also tick and no counted Problems row. Checks, Notes, queued and intended rows do not count. Tests `export_shell::the_header_click_opens_review_on_a_clean_project`, `partial_export::export_set_opens_a_scoped_review`.
  - **Header hold (3c-14, 17, 18, 21, 22, 24, 28, 34, 36).** Label, count and accent always describe the whole project, even over a scoped Review. Review counts every pending NIF edit. Only `Export {n} files now` holds `settled_export_files` during `full_plan_pending()`. The Review count stays live. Accent requires a write-now answer or an open whole-project gate and a positive combined file and NIF count. The header calculates one answer per frame. File, undecided, counted Problems and blocker counts use revisions. After the cheap checks pass, the full PGPatcher JSON state and scope-blind Also writability share a one-second display cache. Dispatch refreshes the filesystem blockers, derived Problems and edited JSON.
  - **Before a write (3c-23, 25 to 27, 29 to 33, 35, 37 to 41).** Every export entry, deferred callback and queued delivery checks for a dialog first. A dialog drops the click with a warning before arming a write or clearing scope. A deferred press or queued Review confirmation drops on a changed scope revision. A scoped click during a pending plan or with changed sources opens its scoped Review immediately. Only a whole-project click can arm a write. An immediate or landed write walks the sources, then reads eligibility fresh once. A click can request one source replan. A second change opens Review, and a reload of a hand-edited `sdc.toml` may start its own plan.
  - A waiting click carries the original scope revision, destination, resolved destination, target-mod revision and **Re-encode every output** setting through its replan. Only the replanned bit changes. A changed destination, target or Re-encode setting, another scope or an open Review sends the click to Review, even if the file count still matches. A plain directory created meanwhile at the same resolved path counts as the same destination. Resolution compares the canonical nearest existing ancestor plus missing components, and an indeterminate result never matches. Edits, Undo and Redo during the wait take effect on the landed plan. It writes only if it still qualifies at the displayed count, otherwise Review opens. Known limits (3c-42): a plan, picker or dialog can change in the frame before a deferred callback checks it, so the count or destination can differ from the press. Replacing a dangling destination link with a directory while the plan runs can compare equal. A share can change during an immediate source walk.

- **JY3 Two controls set the same preference (3c-6, 9, 13, 43).** Review offers **Export without review next time** beside Close. By controller ruling, Settings offers **Export without review** in This project so the choice can be reversed outside Review. Neither box appears without a project, including NIF-only Review. Both explain a read-only project document. While the first open can still be cancelled they are disabled, and the setter changes nothing. The hover and attempted change say `Export without review can be set once the folder has finished opening.` Cancel returns to the previous folder without a question. Inputs have distinct accessible names independent of hover text. Tests `review_tab::reviews_opt_out_tick_sets_the_project_key`, `settings_tab::settings_turns_the_opt_out_off`.

- **JY4 Review lists the files the export touches.** The output list covers the primary DDS files and JSON. **Also updated:** names the settings saved first, ownership records (`sdc-project.toml` and `_sdc/generated.json`), output manifest (`<out>/_sdc/manifest.jsonl`), borrower copies, NIFs and their edit records when applicable. Listing the generated ownership record is a controller ruling. Each entry explains its role on hover and preserves the full path when shortened. One Also folder entry covers every file under it. A manifest append or compaction is one path. Tests `export_shell::review_lists_every_file_the_export_changes`, `export_shell::review_writes_is_cached`.
  - **Exact and run-dependent entries (3c-48 to 53, 55 to 62, 66 to 69, 72, 76, 116).** Use the writer's own predicate wherever the pre-run inputs settle the answer, including JSON bytes, ownership and overwrite answers, NIF Keep, and Save's predicates for both documents. A decision that needs bytes the run may produce is listed and marked, together with each affected record. Its hover adds `It changes only if this export's new files change it.` A planned primary DDS is conditional until the currency evidence in JY8 settles it. An identical JSON may be rewritten atomically to adopt its hash. Its identical bytes need no entry, but its changed records do.
  - Follow primary outputs, Also folders and borrower destinations in writer order. If a source, an earlier copy destination, or a required CM or XPMF DDS can be produced by the run, mark its dependent entries even if it exists now. A copy into a run-produced destination marks the copy and both ownership records. An unreadable or non-file copy destination contributes no dependency of its own. An unreadable regular file can still satisfy XPMF's file-presence check. Resolve parent directories once per build through the deepest existing ancestor. An unresolved path, including a dangling directory or final file link, is a possible match. A borrower and both records stay marked.
  - Count four bookkeeping changes under the listed entry. A completed initial document save may remove its own `.recovery` sibling. A JSON finisher that actually ran may remove its owned old spelling in the same parent, with its recorded pre-run hash. This excludes `_sdc/generated.json`. One new move-aside beside a listed NIF record must hold that record's old bytes. A listed NIF can remove its autosave under the session's `nif-edits` store. A single case-only replacement on a case-insensitive volume is one change. Distinct case-sensitive twins remain distinct. Every other changed path must be covered, and a listed but unchanged file must be marked.
  - The list and its display strings are cached by revision, including evidence, with a one-second disk expiry. A hit does no file query, path build or row hash. Borrowers share a successful owner hash and length by source path within a build, and failed reads are retried. An external change inside the expiry can briefly leave the list stale. Known limits are a Keep answer whose cached NIF row omits changed edit bytes (3c-54) and lexical Also-folder grouping that can show a primary DDS again through a junction (3c-73).

- **JY5 Options in Review (3c-7, 78, 79).** A fresh Review shows blockers, undecided textures, the file list and the write button. **Options** starts closed and toggles on its heading click. It contains Also export as, folder names, CM settings, skipped sets and Recipe settings. An Also blocker opens it and names the reason under Blockers, where it stays visible if Options is closed again. The closed heading names the active Vanilla or Vanilla and CM folders and adds the skip count only while an Also tick applies. The saved skip list survives. The footer names the extra folders even when Options is closed. With Re-encode on it says `Re-encode every output is on: every file Tessalith recorded is written again.` Test `review_tab::an_also_blocker_opens_options_and_names_the_reason_in_blockers`.

- **JY6 Save, the title dot and prompts share the user-edit state.** Automatic type and alpha recordings alone create no ordinary Undo step, offer no **Undo this change**, and never light Save or the dot, prompt on close, open or reload, or create crash recovery. They reach disk with a real Save or Export's save-first. Save can commit a focused field. A never-saved folder with pending document writes shows `New project: nothing saved yet.` and **Write project files now** (controller, subject to the owner's call). Choosing an export folder alone keeps the line. Any `AppModel::save()` that leaves `!needs_write()` clears `never_saved`. Tests `export_shell::write_project_files_now_puts_the_automatic_types_on_disk`, `export_shell::a_real_save_clears_the_new_project_line`.
  - **Undo snapshots (3c-83, 86, 89, 93, 98 to 100, 105, 107, 108).** Each ordinary automatic recording advances the live baseline. `History::apply_auto` patches the value into every before/after snapshot on both Undo and Redo stacks. It fills an absent value, or for an R5 re-type replaces only the previous value. Undo and Redo therefore keep ordinary automatic writes, including a mesh remove, without waiting for another plan. `History::auto_written` is removed. Replay touches only the batch's keys and prunes a listing only when that snapshot's own subtype invalidates it. Expand joined cube recordings to all six faces. Identical texts share one patch per call, on the UI thread, bounded by 200 commands. `apply_auto_timing` measured 88.5873 ms in release for 5,000 file entries and 200 steps (250 ms budget). A provably absent new key in a simple flat layout takes a byte-identical append. Existing or alternate key spellings, inline tables and dotted keys take the TOML editor path.
  - **Joined commands.** An FI3 recording joined to a user's Re-encode stays in that step without advancing the automatic baseline. The Fuzz-8 switch keeps its single joined step and its own Undo row and advances the baseline on open (3c-1, JQ17, JQ46, JQ56). An established normal's shader follow is a separate top Undo command, clears Redo and is never replayed into older steps. Its row loses Undo when the step is undone. A recording that pushes no command keeps the replaced note's Undo only while its associated step remains on top.
  - **Dirty and recovery (3c-84, 87, 90, 92, 95, 96, 109, 110, 113, 115, 116).** On writes and restores, reconcile `user_dirty` with `baseline_text`. A frame reads flags. Automatic recordings and export hashes advance only their fields in the baseline, even over a pending user edit. Copy and CM JSON hashes restore in the same order. Undo back to baseline is clean immediately for ordinary fields. Close, open and reload exclude text still being typed and initiate no draft commit before a prompt. An ordinary focus-loss commit still runs. A still-focused draft can be lost on close. The JSON editor shares its `json-name` buffer across folders. If the field stays visible and focused across a switch between writable CS projects, leaving it with Tab can apply the first project's name to the second. When the names differ, the second project gains a `JSON name` Undo step and prompts to save on close (3c-96).
  - **Type and alpha claims after the normals merge.** A user write, Undo or Redo of a `subtype` or `alpha.policy` sets a persistent-in-session mark on `sdc.toml`. This includes a pick, Switch, Keep alpha, Reset all, bulk edits and following shared alpha. Recovery sets it only for a restored type or alpha difference from disk. Only successful Save or reload clears it. Even Undo of an unsaved fuzz switch back to its disk text prompts and keeps recovery. Save uses the existing stamp and conflict check. An ordinary retype edits the files document only. Automatic pruning of a stale listing advances its sets baseline too. Save writes `sdc-project.toml` whenever its text differs from disk so a saved claim stays user-owned after reopening. An unreadable sets document offers no Keep alpha without the slot-2 borrow. A temporarily unreadable ownership list can reverse a saved claim. After the planner rejects and stashes an edited document, a successful retry can hide the claim from close and recovery (3c-114). Tests `fuzz_follow::undoing_the_switch_before_save_returns_to_disk`, `auto_accept::a_mesh_remove_is_recorded_and_survives_undo`.

- **JY7 Read-only engine currency preview (3c-118, 119).** `Engine::currency_preview` returns one verdict per scoped ready output key using the run's manifest, identity, version and existing-output rules. It reads the manifest without compacting it. It reports write, current, kept (both keep reasons can apply to one key), protected unknown existing, or explicit skip. Cancellation and manifest read errors return errors. The ready loop checks cancellation before its lazy existence check. For an unrecorded destination the writer's final step refuses to replace a file that appeared meanwhile and reports `SkipKind::UnknownExisting`. Recorded destinations retain atomic replacement. Test `currency_preview_agrees_with_the_run`.

- **JY8 Status, Review and results name their units.** Status counts physical discovered files, planned textures and carded sets, e.g. `57 files → 68 textures (11 packed or generated) · 7 sets`. Omit the parenthesis at zero and use singular for one. The ` · {s} sets` suffix is the planner's call. Another planner's call limits header count = progress total = sum of result categories to the primary PBR folder when an Also tick is on. Each secondary phase and Vanilla copy count is reported separately. Tests `export_shell::the_status_line_counts_files_not_plan_items`, `export_shell::the_header_number_is_the_progress_total_and_the_result_sum`.
  - **Preview (3c-121, 126, 129 to 131).** A cancellable worker checks the effective destination, scope, policy and settings. Discard a stale reply. While pending, show `checking which changed…` and conditional writes. On failure keep the conditional list and Export available. On success group **Will be written**, **Up to date**, **Kept** and **Protected** by path. A WillWrite is definite only with an existing directory parent and an absent, non-link destination. Check cancellation per destination. Neither currency nor planning adds a disabled reason to Export. A key mismatch requests one reconcile replan per Review opening. Reload, edits and plan landings do not renew that allowance. Input changes still refresh the preview. A second mismatch keeps the settled list until Review reopens.
  - **Results (3c-121 to 125, 127).** Progress starts at the settled primary count including lenders. The denominator covers the union of reviewed keys and actual terminal-event keys, including when Export beats the preview. Count a key finished on its first terminal event. Later events for that key can change its outcome without incrementing the finished count. An unkeyed failure counts in failures and the denominator. Written, up to date, failed, kept, protected and cancelled account for the pass. Count distinct skipped source paths separately in each phase. Files uses the same terminal classification as the result, including a `kept:` reason. Files and Messages show the same result even alongside `Export failed: …`. The writer runs its full request if the preview missed a key.

- **JY9 NIF problems explain their share of the count (3c-4, 132).** In the default Problems worklist, group visible counted NIF rows under `From the NIF you are editing ({k})` at the position of the group's highest severity. Keep severity order within the group. Actionable Info notes stay outside it. The tab hover explains the default count even under a set filter. At zero omit heading and hover line. Build the collapsed worklist only when hovered. Test `export_shell::nif_edit_problems_are_named_as_such`.

- **JY10 Start without a project (3c-134, 135, 137 to 140).** A bare launch opens the Textures start card with Open folder and Recent. Restore the previous view only when reopening a project. An explicit folder takes precedence over the device-loss restart folder. Resolve the supplied path to absolute and read its whole directory listing. On failure show the path and OS reason in status and Messages, keep it out of Recent and show the start card. Tests `app_shell::a_launch_with_no_project_shows_the_start_card_even_after_meshes`, `app_shell::reopening_a_project_restores_the_saved_meshes_view`.
  - Meshes without a texture project keeps the MO2 list and a banner with an Open folder button named `Open a texture folder from the Meshes banner` for accessibility. Hide Undo, Redo, Save, Export and the bottom strip. The GH8 controller exception restores Undo, Redo, Review and the strip for pending NIF edits in Meshes, with `Review {k} NIF edits…`. Selecting a NIF alone is insufficient. Save still requires a texture project. Commands opening a hidden tab are disabled with a reason and preserve unread entries. Messages marks entries read only when drawn. The existing NIF footer can still open an invisible Review with neither project nor edits (3c-138).

- **JY11 One next step after opening (3c-142, 143).** Publish after the last opening plan settles, using whole-project counts even in a scoped Review. In order, name textures needing a type and Undecided, counted problems and Problems, the missing destination and Export to, or the ready header button and what pressing it does. Cancel or failure disarms the note. A later replan retires this sentence without clearing a replacement or Undo note. Changing the opt-out recomposes the sentence once if still displayed. Test `start_shell::after_open_one_line_says_the_next_step`.

- **JY12 Tools and the destination menu (3c-144).** With a project open, PGPatcher JSON editor…, Make marker cube… and the one-shot Re-encode every output move to **Tools ▾** after Preset. Ticking Re-encode keeps Tools open and refreshes an open Review. Choosing a command closes Tools. With no project, Tools is hidden and the marker stays under More ▾. A disabled JSON editor names the missing requirement (CS mode or a loaded sets document). Both **Open export folder** buttons give a short inline reason so users can tell why the folder cannot open, including when a file occupies its path. The full reason is available on hover and to screen readers. Test `header_layout::tools_holds_the_json_editor_marker_and_reencode`. [Main window menu listing](../docs/wiki/GUI-Main-Window.md#export-to)

- **JY13 One header row down to 1055 px (3c-3, 145, 147).** Move Tools, then MO2…, then Preset into `⋯`, once the destination is cut to its readable form (LC1). Settings… stays outside. Wrap the destination, Save and Export together only when the fixed controls cannot fit. Size each Undo/Redo slot to the view's longest label, caching on view, labels, font, scale and padding. In Meshes the short label is **Undo NIF edit** or **Redo NIF edit**. Both directions keep the full step in the hover and accessible name. Tools and Preset are keyboard-accessible submenus through `look::menu_button_036`. Preset shares `preset_entries` with the regular picker. The submenu and Review's Choose preset popup scroll in short windows. Any enabled choice closes either picker, including the current value. Test `header_layout::the_1055_header_fits_long_fields_pending_nif_edits_and_launch`.

- **JY14 Folder picker starts (3c-149, 150).** The Export folder worker takes the first existing directory among the chosen project destination, the last accepted export pick (`gui.toml`'s `last_export_folder`) and the project root or most recent project's parent. Dispatch passes unchecked candidates. A current accepted pick wins even if equal to this instance's baseline, preserving unrelated concurrent settings edits. Cancelled, stale, refused and programmatic choices leave last-pick memory alone. Also location, Target mod and Save report start at the project or recent parent. Save report retains its filename and filters. Open folder keeps FI10. The app-wide destination still serves GH8. Test `export_folder::the_export_picker_starts_in_the_last_export_folder_then_the_project`.

- **JY15 Open a tool in the current view (3c-151 to 155).** Docked Settings, Presets and PGPatcher JSON cover Meshes in place. Pop out, Close and **Back to meshes** uncover it. Dock brings the tool back. Selection opens Textures. Mesh and NIF navigation returns to Meshes. The overlay keeps the banner, scan feedback and MO2 errors visible. Header, shortcut and palette Undo/Redo target the visible tool and never queue an edit for the hidden NIF. Back, a view-tab click and in-tool navigation such as JSON Show commit the focused field once before navigating. Cap NIF preview height only when it is visible. Recipe settings opens the selected PBR set's Also tab in 2D, falling back to the first unskipped PBR set, then the first PBR set, including a document-only borrower. A key-less borrower explains the absent Skip control. Export eligibility still uses the engine pick. Tests `app_shell::settings_opens_in_the_meshes_view_and_back_returns`, `app_shell::json_show_commits_the_focused_name_before_selecting_the_set`.

- **JY16 One row for the default preset.** The shared picker lists the target's default first as `{id} · default`, selected for either an implicit or explicit default. Selecting it clears the project override. The closed combo shows the tag when following the target. Presets… opens on the project's preset or the target default and keeps an existing draft. Test `header_layout::the_default_preset_shows_once_with_a_tag`.

- **JY17 Window size follows the GPU surface limit (3c-156, 157).** Cap the initial request at 8192 physical pixels per side using the largest of the saved and successfully reported monitor scales. A failed DPI query contributes no scale. When the real GPU limit is known or scale changes, send `MaxInnerSize` in points rounded down. Shrink an oversized inner window and log the clamp once. An unknown limit sends no size command. A restored maximized window on a monitor over 8192 pixels, or unreported higher DPI, can need first-frame correction. Test `app_shell::a_surface_clamp_logs_once_and_sends_no_repeat_commands`.

- **JY18 Marked warnings in Files (owner F3, controller 3c-5, 11, 12).** The Files row's warning count and **Has warnings** both use `bottom::open_export_warnings` and `export_warning_marked`, using the same mark lookup as the badge and hovers. `FileFilter::matches` reads that count. Info and Note-class export warnings still count. Only a mark removes a warning. `FilesIndex` keys on item and sets-document revisions, so marking takes effect immediately. If all warnings are marked, `Ui::weak` shows `{k} marked` in the count's place with `warnings_hover`. Planner's call, accepted by the controller: share the `has_mark_of` code prefilter with `marked` and document the visible row's per-frame scan with `shortcut:`. Test `problems_notes::a_marked_warning_leaves_the_files_count_and_filter`.

- **JY19 Export details in Files and the report (owner F4, controller 3c-10).** Files heads its nonempty list of what Export did besides converting `Export details (n)`. The matching Markdown section is `## Export details`. The Problems tab keeps its **Notes (m)** pill. Tests `export_drawer::the_files_tab_heads_its_export_notes_export_details`, `export::the_markdown_report_lists_the_export_notes`.

- **JY20 Header and history feedback (controller, 3c-162 to 3c-164).** The header keeps whole-project views in their own cache across scoped Review frames and disk refreshes. Its Export hover uses the same whole-project scope and file count as the label. With a tool docked over Meshes, a successful project Undo or Redo names the step in the status line as `Undone: {label}` or `Redone: {label}`.

- **JY21 Supersedes and amends.** Supersedes the clean-export rule in CA1 and FB1, FB1’s start folder, FC1’s automatic Undo step and action, FI4’s Save rule, AG39(a), FB7’s switch to Textures, AM12’s default row and CA8’s status-only JSON rows. Amends CA6, FI5, AG17, CD18, CD4, BH7, DT27, FI10, FI14, AG126, CD2, CN74, CN80, CN81, FB10 and GH8. Amends FM24, FP3, HF10, HR2, JA11, JA12, JF1, JI1, JL7, JO4, JO5, JO11, JP6, JQ9, JQ17, JQ46, JQ56 and JS5.

## Note LB — A borrowed slot over an own file warns (owner, 2026-11-20)

- **LB1** The owner chose “Warn, don't skip” (BS-3). A skin material, TXST record, PGPatcher default name, path stored in a NIF or earlier JSON can still name the borrower's own DDS. The GUI raises the engine warning `SET-SLOT-BORROWED-UNUSED` as a Problem because an unused file under a borrow often points to an accidental share. It counts against Ready until marked as intended. `SHADER-EXTRA-MAP` stays a Note (JO1).
- **LB2** The GUI records producers by set from every engine job before it merges items with the same key. Two sets can pack the same first source and show only one pack in the GUI (HS2, BS-4). Each job supplies its slot kind, Ready status, physical output and ordered pack sources or single input. A full plan replaces the list. A partial plan replaces the replanned sets' entries and keeps the rest.
- **LB3** W1 limits the warning to slots the set's Community Shaders writer reads. A PGPatcher entry must pass the writer's entry rules and use a slot from 2 through 8. An Advanced Skin material can use only `controls` (Rmaos, 6), `wet` (SkinWet, 9) or `detail` (SkinDetail, 10).
- **LB4** W2 requires the writer to use the explicit borrow. A TXST record fill is excluded. For PGPatcher, the borrowed kind must win that slot number (coat and fuzz share 7, Emissive and SkinSk share 3). A `slotN` string supplies a link or a clear (JR1), and a lock that omits the slot leaves the mesh's path in place. A boolean `slot4 = false` lets the borrow through. For skin, the material must select the lender's physical output for that kind.
- **LB5** W3 requires a Ready job of the same kind in the borrowing set with a different output from the lender's. This covers a plain file as well as a pack and excludes jobs that cannot write. The GUI only looks up the writers once it has a candidate.
- **LB6** W4 rules out each own output that any planned PGPatcher entry or skin material uses. Every written PGPatcher slot from 1 through 8 counts, including another slot in the same entry and sets outside the export selection (JN1). Only the written string goes through `txst_map::slot_path_key`, which folds ASCII case and supplies a missing `textures\` prefix. The job's comparison key uses its mapped game directory, then the game directory of its DDS relative to the first containing output root (active PBR folder first, then the ordinary export folder), then its planned game directory, and its final output filename, folded to ASCII lowercase. An output without a game projection cannot match a PGPatcher string. Skin compares the selected physical paths directly.
- **LB7** One warning per set and slot names the first qualifying own output in case-folded output-path order. “and N more” counts the rest and the hover lists their paths. The shown line names the slot lender, the own filename and its sources in order. A shared pack source reads “which this set shares from {owner}” when its owning set ID is known, or “which this set shares” otherwise. Other pack inputs and a plain file's single input read as own sources.
- **LB8** The warning appears on the producing set's Problems row and counts toward its card badge. **Go to set** opens that set at the borrowed slot. The card hover lists the first ten warnings, member warnings first. It includes the remedy when it lists this warning. The Problems row hover always reads “Remove the share or the own file, or drop the borrow. If a mesh or another mod uses the file, mark this row as intended.” The set owns the warning, so the texture inspector has no row for it. The row offers only **Go to set** and **Mark as intended**. BS-5 leaves out fix buttons because an automatic edit can retarget a downstream borrower (JN4).
- **LB9** **Mark as intended** uses the warning code, `set:{id}` and `slot {n}` on both the row and the card. The row moves to Notes and stops counting against Ready. The mark follows a set rename and applies again if the row disappears and returns (JO).
- **LB10** Planning, DDS bytes, JSON, item and `[files]` keys, the sets document, Output files (JR3), Review, the CLI and Vanilla keep their existing behaviour. Skipping files, fix buttons, warnings for displaced files under links, and checks for XPMF, TXST records, meshes or other mods are parked. The hidden-pack identity and Review count are a separate follow-up (BS-4), as is the chained slot-6 export block (BW-2).

## Note LC — The export path gives way before header controls move (owner, 2026-11-20)

LC is provisional. At merge the controller rechecks the next free main-team letter and renames LC and its references throughout the affected files if needed.

- **LC1 The path gives way first (amends JY13).** The owner’s question about which part of the path to keep is open (2026-11-20). The default ships with the drive, `…` and the whole last folder, with no length cap (`C:\…\My Mod`). Keeping the parent folder, capping the last folder or using the 12-character floor would each change only `header::readable_path` and its test cases. A cut ignores trailing separators, keeps a root intact and replaces even a one-letter middle folder with `…`. `HeaderWidths::fixed` reserves the narrower of the full and readable destinations. JY13 governs overflow and wrapping. After all optional controls move, the path can shrink to 12 characters. On a wrapped row, `set_row_height` centres the destination on Save and Export. Tests in `header_layout`: `readable_path_keeps_the_drive_and_the_last_folder`, `long_destination_is_cut_to_its_last_folder_before_an_optional_control_moves`, `a_long_export_path_keeps_tools_mo2_and_preset_at_1440_and_1600`, `tools_stays_when_the_full_path_is_narrower_than_the_readable_form`, `one_letter_folder_keeps_tools_when_the_cut_path_fits`, `short_leaf_keeps_tools_at_the_drive_and_leaf_boundary`, `short_leaf_wraps_only_when_the_drive_and_leaf_no_longer_fit`, `the_first_resize_frame_matches_the_settled_header_in_both_directions` and `a_cut_destination_trims_the_painted_separator_but_keeps_it_on_hover`.
- **LC2 Review’s JSON lines are cut at the footer’s edge.** Like the file rows, a JSON row shows the whole line on hover and in its accessible name. A long JSON path stays inside the footer, so **Also updated** still cuts its list in the middle. Test `export_shell::a_long_json_path_stays_inside_the_review_footer`.
- **LC3 GUI tests do not depend on the TEMP length.** A test that needs a long path builds one. Checks for keys in the project document match a key line with `support::toml_has_key` so a folder name containing the same text cannot satisfy the check. To reproduce, run the full non-render `it` suite under normal TEMP, then repeat with the header-path plan’s Task 2 `Use-LongTemp` command. It sets TEMP and TMP to the same 120–150 character path containing `padding`.

## Note LD — Meshes and NIF edit, review E (main team, 2026-11-21)

LD is provisional. At merge the controller rechecks the next free main-team letter and renames LD and its references if needed.

- **LD1 Draft location and save.** A project owns `<project>\_sdc\nif-edits\<data path>.sdc-nifedit.toml`. Without a project the draft is `%LOCALAPPDATA%\sdc\nif-edits\<sha>.toml`, keyed on the ASCII-lowercase Windows data path. A change marks the operations unsaved before saving. A drag saves on release. Opening an untouched edit or choosing an export folder does not create a draft. Export saves the draft first if it has an unsaved change or its stable ID has yet to reach disk. Review lists that write under “Also updated”.
- **LD2 Export record and ownership.** Only Export writes `<export folder>\.sdc\nif-edits\<data path>.sdc-nifedit.toml` (`edit_doc::doc_path`). After any required backups and draft save, Export writes the pre-record, the NIF and the final record, in order. The pre-record preserves the prior snapshot for retry. The final record carries the source, operations, NIF hash and `owner`, the draft's stable ID. IDs read from disk must be 16 ASCII hex digits. An older record can lack an owner. A different owner makes even a matching installed NIF foreign. Review binds Overwrite to the installed bytes. Copying a project copies the ID, so the copy alone does not trigger that question. A shared ID gives no permission to erase divergent operations. The source-copy exclusion (GQ3) still applies.
- **LD3 Migration, badges and Review.** If no draft wins recovery, an ownerless export document with operations and no NIF hash can supply an old draft. The user's next successful draft save removes that ownerless file only if its fingerprint still matches. This migration is the sole exception to the Export-only rule for the export folder. An ownerless record with an exported hash and pending changes can also supply edits, but stays as a record. A pre-record is never a legacy draft. Disk badges use the project draft's operations and the selected folder's export state when the owners agree or the record has no owner. With a different owner the draft keeps its own export state. With no draft the badge uses the record alone. Without a project the open edit can show a badge for its recovered autosave. An unseen pending record gets a byte-for-byte backup before replacement. Review freezes `<stem>.<record mtime in Unix seconds>.toml`, then `-2`, `-3` for collisions. With no Meshes model, Review keeps the first backup name supplied for each opening record, even if the name arrives on a later refresh. An export that skips Review freezes the name at its start. “Also updated” includes the NIF, record, required draft, displaced record/draft/kept backups and any autosave or legacy file the save may remove. Export checks the listed paths and reserves the backup without replacing an occupant. A changed inventory or a failed required backup, draft or pre-record stops that NIF before its write. A failed backup or replacement gives the session no ownership of the displaced file.
- **LD4 Leaving and recovering an edit.** On a project change the old project's session ends when reopening would recover its current operations. The selected NIF can reopen from the new location. Select and Close edit use the same durability check, before and after a needed save. Undo or redo history alone needs no write. An unknown recovery candidate cannot establish durability. A renamed destination can qualify only through the same draft ID and recoverable operations. Saving under an existing project creates its subdirectories one level at a time. On a failed project draft write while leaving, Tessalith tries a kept autosave named `<sha>.<id>.toml` and reports its path through Messages (Info). Only a matching draft discovers it. A missing project root can still block departure after a kept save. When reopening would lose the edit, the session and reason stay, export is blocked, and the next edit or project request retries. A pending switch retries only for the current project. Returning to its own project keeps the session and its undo history when the save succeeds. A touched no-project edit can carry into the next project under the recovery rules in LD12. Choosing an export folder rereads its record and changes no file. HS12 still blocks exporting over the source.
- **LD5 Footer paths.** “Writes {data} to {export}” fits the available pixels, shortening the export path first. The hover and accessible name give both full paths and the source-file sentence. “Draft saved in {path}” and **Show in Explorer** point to the last draft or autosave the session loaded or saved. A pending save retains the last path. A failed save hides it if the operations differ, and undoing to the saved operations restores it. With no path the footer reads “Draft not saved yet”. The disabled button explains the first edit, a pending drag, a failed save or a missing draft location. When the NIF is in place but its record still needs saving, the footer asks for another export. If Undo removed the last operation and export is blocked, it asks for Redo when available or for the edit to be reapplied when history is empty. The Writes and draft lines cache their text by paths, width, display scale and font. Egui's own label copies are accepted. External changes or deletion are not watched between saves or opens, so the line can be stale (E6-Y7). Reveal checks the path at the click.
- **LD6 Panel height.** The footer draws first and clips the editor above it. The scrolling head gives up space for a 200 px tab area while room permits. The tab area's outer bounds include its scrollbar. The open Diff list yields from 160 px to 40 px. Below about 300 px of panel height the lower editor can be clipped. Its controls cannot receive clicks through the footer.
- **LD7 Mesh list width.** Paths shorten in the middle to fit their pixel width. The hover and accessible name keep the full path. The mod column fits the widest mod name plus padding, capped at a quarter of the available row width and 180 px. The path takes the space left by the mod and chips columns (GK3).
- **LD8 Edit button.** The framed **Edit** button beside the selected NIF's name shows its selected state while the editor is open. Clicking it again closes the edit subject to LD4. A retained session keeps this button beside its own NIF name if a scan reload drops its row.
- **LD9 Scan source.** The MO2 popup says “Scan: choose an instance first” or “Scan: choose a profile first”. Disabled **Choose…** and **Scan a folder…** explain a running scan (AM1). The list reads “From the scan of {root}. Scan data last written {date}.” An unavailable part is omitted. Only enabled scan origins count. The root is the overwrite folder's parent for MO2 or the sole mod origin for a folder scan. An external overwrite directory can therefore name the wrong root. The UTC date uses the later available modification time of `scan.db` and its WAL when the database time is available. Any later store write can advance it. These approximations stand until modscan records the instance and scan time.
- **LD10 Mesh labels.** The owner's Meshes labels override the “keep” entries for this panel in review E, textures. “Editing: Root” and “Editing: {shape}” put the block class in the hover and accessible description, including the full editing line when cut. Known shader types show their name (“Face Tint”), with the NIF number on hover. The editor also explains that changing type changes the trailer fields. Unknown types read “type {n}”. “This shape has no alpha settings.” explains NiAlphaProperty on hover. **Texture wrap** shows 0 “Clamp both”, 1 “Clamp U, wrap V”, 2 “Wrap U, clamp V”, 3 “Wrap both”. Other values keep their read-only “Other ({n})” entry until the user picks a named mode. **Shader flags 1** and **Shader flags 2** retain SLSF1/SLSF2 on hover. The field names also govern Fix, Undo and pending rows (“BodyMesh: Shader flags 1”). “This texture set has no textures” explains TXST on hover. **Winning** identifies the selected mod's copies the game loads. Hover explanations are available to assistive technology.
- **LD11 File ownership.** On the owner's authority the main team changed these NIF-team files under `crates/gui/`: `src/model/meshes.rs`, `src/model/meshes/{edit_doc,export,nif_edit}.rs`, `src/ui/meshes.rs`, `src/ui/meshes/{edit_fields,edit_panel,txst_preview}.rs`, `tests/meshes_view.rs` and `tests/nif_edit.rs`. The other changes are GUI integration and tests under `src/{app,crash_log}.rs`, `src/model/{also,mod,review}.rs`, `src/ui/{header,review}.rs` and `tests/{also_export,export_folder,export_shell,header_layout,meshes_txst_preview,mo2_button,review,review_json_answer,review_tab,support/mod,ui_proof}.rs`. No NIF crate changed.
- **LD12 Recovery and file identity.** Open and carry compare the draft, the matching kept autosave and the plain autosave by modification time (GD11). A project session must be recoverable from its own files. A carry retains the project's existing draft ID. When that changes the live ID, the session reads the export record again to establish its current owner. Equal durable operations need no rewrite. Otherwise the carry saves the selected operations before removing an adopted plain autosave. The session records each fingerprint where it adopts or writes operations. It preserves unknown bytes in a timestamped sibling or refuses the write. An equal record can be adopted at its current path. A proper prefix of the session's operations can permit the export without conferring ownership. Each draft save checks the kept recovery candidate, including on retries. A winning kept copy stays until the draft write succeeds. A known future-dated loser must be preserved before it can displace the new draft on reopen. A recovery file retimed externally between open and save is not watched continuously. A losing kept file can be preserved in its timestamped sibling even if the external change made it newer (3e-11). The fingerprint check and subsequent filesystem action have no cross-process lock. A change in that interval, removal of the project root during a save, and simultaneous writers of one kept file or export folder are accepted races (E6-M).
- **LD13 Export eligibility and repair.** Export compares session operations, source hash and installed NIF with the chosen folder's record. An absent record clears that folder's derived state. A source change makes an exported edit pending in memory. An edit drag reuses the installed NIF hash while its path and metadata match. A committed edit or the end of a drag invalidates the cached hash, so the next classification reads the installed NIF again. Idle ownership refresh uses record length and modification time, including after a read error. A replacement preserving both can leave the row stale (3e-9). Export always reads the record and checks the installed bytes before writing. An unreadable record blocks the row. With zero operations an unrecorded installed NIF gets a refusal and reason. A final record failure leaves a pending repair even though the NIF was written. The live session can recognise its own partial NIF by hash. After reopening, Review can ask about the installed file. Keeping an externally replaced NIF can bring back the repair hint at the next open. Review and the viewport's quick export use the same ownership guards. The Review inventory uses the existing export list and refreshes when the edit or its classification changes.

## Note LE — Variance bake for shared roughness (owner, 2026-11-21)

LE is provisional. At merge the controller rechecks the next free main-team letter and renames LE and its references if needed.

- **LE1 Shared roughness (amends R7 and M4-final-1).** A set joins the bake group through its own or borrowed roughness slot. A JSON slot-6 link or clear leaves that membership intact. The normal check follows slot-2 links and clears. A shared slot-6 output bakes once on its producer when every set using it resolves to the same eligible normal image, including a borrowed normal. Different, missing or unverifiable normals leave the roughness unbaked and put SET-SHARED-ROUGHNESS-NORMAL on the producer's job and in Problems. Explicit bake exclusions and an explicit renormalize setting on the normal still win. Mip 0 stays authored (HD1).

## Note LF — Allocation budgets (main team, 2026-11-21)

Four tests count allocation calls against a budget: a warm full plan, a one-edit re-plan and a skeleton patch (engine, `alloc_budget.rs`), and one settled frame of the demo project in the real app (GUI, `alloc_frame.rs`); a fifth, `enum_ids_do_not_allocate` (LH4), pins zero allocations for an enum id. A change that makes any of them allocate far more fails a test; the plan changes no encoded byte.

- **LF1 Plan allocation budgets.** `crates/engine/tests/alloc_budget.rs` counts allocation calls with a process-wide counting allocator, for a warm full plan and for one `replan_sets` edit of the fast fixture, and fails above `WARM_PLAN_CALLS` or `REPLAN_CALLS`; `PATCH_CALLS` bounds the skeleton patch alone (LH5). Every request runs with `jobs = 1` so the count is the same from run to run. Each const is the largest of 5 measured runs times 1.10 (owner, 2026-11-21: 10 % headroom, since the counts repeat exactly), rounded up to two significant figures; to raise one, re-measure 5 runs of its named test with `--exact --nocapture` (never the whole binary), apply that rule, and give the reason in the commit.

- **LF2 GUI frame budget.** `crates/gui/tests/alloc_frame.rs` counts the allocation calls of one settled frame of the demo project in the real `SdcApp` (`h.step()` after `app_harness::quiet`), on the test's own thread only, with a per-thread counting allocator; worker threads are left out. A global allocator is process-wide, so the file is a `[[test]]` kept apart from `it`, and `process_state_needles` has a `global_allocator` needle for it. The budget is `FRAME_CALLS` in that file, set by the LF1 rule; for this test one run's count is the largest of its five frames, the `largest` it prints.

- **LF3 Splits without allocating.** `group::split_suffix` and `group::split_vocab` compare bytes through a private `cut_tail` and allocate only the returned base (and suffix), and `sets::no_set_paths` builds its `KeyIndex` only when an edit is a `SetEdit::NoSet`; every result is unchanged. The budgets were lowered at the time; LK1 gives the current values of `WARM_PLAN_CALLS` and `REPLAN_CALLS` and their latest measured counts, and `FRAME_CALLS` is 3,100.

## Note LG - Validation after encoding is opt-in (owner, 2026-11-21)

The owner: "Probably turn validate into an optional setting flag in the gui, and an opt in for conversions using sdc, validate already exists so can be reused." GUI default: "Off, like sdc". Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-21-optional-validate.md`.

- **LG1 The switch.** `BatchRequest::validate` is off in `BatchRequest::new`. `sdc convert --validate` (a plain flag on `ConvertArgs` only) and the GUI's `gui.toml` key `validate_exports` (default off, saved at once, no re-plan; `export_request` copies it into `req.validate`) turn it on. With `[export] also`, all three phases follow it.
- **LG2 A record that was not validated.** The record keeps its `validation` object with `passed: true`, `levels` the header's mip count, the other fields empty and `skipped: true` (`ValidationSummary::not_run`; `skipped` is omitted from JSON when false, so a validated record and every `sdc validate` report read as before). The field stays because readers need it: `currency` treats an absent `passed` as Stale, `ev_covers` reads `validation.levels`, Also export deserialises the whole `ManifestRecord` (a 3.0.0 engine requires `validation`), `FileDone` would lose a field (`EVENT_SCHEMA` stays 2 while fields are only added), and the GUI and CLI log read `passed`. `passed` keeps its meaning, "no Error-severity warning was found".
- **LG3 No byte, pin or arm moves.** `validate()` takes the encoded bytes and returns a report; the same bytes are written either way (`optional_validate::skipping_validation_writes_the_same_bytes`), so no `encode_version` arm, `encode_versions` pin or profile hash changes. The guard and the goldens run with validation on: the engine harnesses (`golden.rs`, `encode_versions.rs`, `encode.rs`, `packing.rs`, `derive_run.rs`, `bake_run.rs`, `resize_relative.rs`, `api.rs`) set `validate = true`, and the CLI ones (`cli_convert.rs`, and `cli_edges.rs`'s `partial_outranks_validation_failed_and_fatal_outranks_partial`) pass `--validate`.
- **LG4 `if-changed` and a record not validated.** A `skipped` record has `passed: true`, so `currency` keeps it as any passing record and needs no code change. A record whose validation failed (from an encode or from a check in place) is encoded again on the next run. This amends JA1.
- **LG5 The `done:` line and exit 4.** Without the flag the line reads `..., N failed, validation off, N cancelled`, or `N failed validation (complex types only)` when an always-validated output failed; with it, as before. Without the flag exit 4 comes only from the always-validated types (LG9). Note K's injected failure (`SDC_TEST_FAIL_VALIDATION`) fires only on an output the run checks: with `--validate`, for an always-validated type, or in a check in place.
- **LG6 The GUI setting.** App settings has five groups, with **Export** after Checks, holding **Validate exported DDS** (accessible name "Validate exported DDS", static hover). It covers the plain and Also exports and Review's export. **Copy CLI command** adds ` --validate` when it is on, and the Markdown report says "not validated" (or "validation failed N (complex types only)") when an export ran without it, and adds "checked in place N (M failed)" when it ran with it. This amends JI1.
- **LG7 Version (owner, "MINOR anyway").** The change ships in a 3.x MINOR release as an owner exception to DM8. Under DM8 alone it is MAJOR: a script that relied on exit 4 for a type other than complex material or complex grass now needs `--validate`. The CHANGELOG bullet says so. FC7 is cited for comparison only.
- **LG8 Preview and `sdc validate`.** `Session::preview` still validates its in-memory DDS for the GUI readouts; it writes nothing and is not a conversion. `sdc validate` is unchanged: it has no encoder input, base coverage or source peak.
- **LG9 Always validated (owner, "Always validate those two").** `validate::always_validated(subtype)` is true for `ComplexMaterial` and `ComplexGrass` only, by resolved subtype and never by the `_m` suffix (a derived `EnvM` writes `_m` and follows the switch). `process_file` validates when `req.validate || always_validated(subtype)`, so these records never say `skipped`, and the complex-material card's smallest-mip alpha lines keep working.
- **LG10 Checking in place (owner, "Check them in place"; OV-R1 from the controller).** A validating run under `if-changed` reads and checks an up to date output whose record says `skipped: true`, without encoding it (`pipeline::check_in_place`, then `commit_check`), and appends its record again with only `validation` and `ts` changed. `Manifest::append` is the path, after a second cancel and output-stamp check; the DDS is never opened for writing. An output kept for an older encode version is not checked (an older engine built its encoder input). The event is `FileSkipped { UpToDate }` with the reason suffix `; checked in place`, or `; checked in place: failed validation`; `BatchSummary` and `BatchDone` gain `checked` and `checked_failed`, Also export's sum adds them, and `exit_code` gives 4 for `validation_failed + checked_failed > 0`. A read or prepare failure is a failed file, not up to date. A check still queued when the run is cancelled counts as cancelled, not as up to date (before this note an up to date output was never queued). `--dry-run` returns before the manifest is opened, so a dry run checks nothing. In the GUI a finished check counts as work in the files/s rate and the ETA, though its row ends as up to date (it refines AG35). This amends JA1.
- **LG11 What a check in place leaves alone (controller, OV-R3 and OV-R4).** A packed output is checked only when its recorded `pack.*` provenance equals what `prepare` would assemble now. Only a `Current` output is checked, so an output kept for a lower quality target and every Vanilla copy (`copied_from`) stay not validated until re-encoded, and no verdict passes from a twin to its copy. JA7's copy path is unchanged. A derived output (derived diffuse, legacy `_n`, `EnvM`) is checked like any other: its `DeriveJob` carries the `[export.legacy]` numbers (`params`), `identity_sha256` hashes them, `recorded_sources` records that identity on each source, and `currency` compares it, so a changed recipe value makes the output stale and never reaches a check. A check in place that ends as a failed file (the output changed during the run, a source unreadable) leaves a failed row, so the PBR JSON export refuses that dependency (HF9); no DDS was touched.

## Note LH - Rust audit follow-ups (main team, 2026-11-22)

The recommended actions of the AI Rust patterns audit of 2026-11-21 beyond Note LF: suppressions state their reason, two allocation lints, a scan for silent test skips, enum ids that do not allocate, a measured allocation budget for the re-plan's skeleton patch, the repo copies of the Rust CLI skills removed, and a repo skill for code on per-item paths. No encoded byte changes.

- **LH1 Every suppression states its reason.** The root `Cargo.toml`'s `[workspace.lints.clippy]` sets `allow_attributes_without_reason` and `ignore_without_reason` to `warn`, an error under the gate's `-D warnings`, so in the six main-team crates every `#[allow]` and `#[expect]` carries `reason = "..."` and every `#[ignore]` is `#[ignore = "..."]`, each saying why the code is right as written. The first lint checks `#[expect]` too (probed 2026-11-22). An allow was removed only where its lint fired in no configuration (lib and test builds, with and without `test-hooks`) and no including binary. A file-wide `dead_code` allow on a test module stays only where several test binaries share the module; `bcenc/src/hits.rs` keeps its conditional `cfg_attr(not(test), allow(dead_code))`, since its counters are read only in the unit tests. The NIF team's test helpers that the CLI and GUI tests compile through `#[path]` keep their own allows; the including module allows `clippy::allow_attributes_without_reason`, with its reason, above its `#[path]`.

- **LH2 Two allocation lints are gated.** `format_push_string` and `assigning_clones` are `warn` in the root `Cargo.toml` (errors under `-D warnings`). Text is appended with `write!` into the string (`let _ =`, since writing to a `String` cannot fail) instead of building a `format!` and copying it, and a value is cloned into an existing place with `clone_from` or `clone_into`, which can reuse its allocation. The text and values are unchanged.

- **LH3 A test that returns early says why.** `it::return_scan::every_early_return_in_a_test_says_why` reads `src` and `tests` of the six main-team crates (each `src` must exist; a crate's absent `tests` root is skipped, its `src` is still read) and fails on a `return;`, `return }` or `return Ok(());` in a `#[test]` function (an attribute wrapped over several lines still counts as part of the block) unless one of four positional checks holds: the test is `#[ignore = "..."]`; a print macro is on the return line or within three lines above it, and no line after the print line goes below the depth at the end of the print line; a listed skip helper that prints its own reason (`render_state()`, `skip_byte_pins()`; matched as itself, and not directly after a `!`, though `!(helper())` still counts) is on the return line or on a line directly above that ends in `{`; or the statement before the return starts with `assert` (a checked exit). The checks read positions, not control flow or text: a print says nothing about whether it is the reason, a print whose block closes on its own line passes, a helper behind `||` on the condition line passes, a helper on a `{ } else {` line above the return passes although the return sits in the branch where the helper did not fire, and `helper() == false` passes (only a prefix `!` is excluded); a match arm `=> return,` is not read as a return at all (two `shortcut:` comments in the scan). `it::return_scan::the_return_rule_tells_a_skip_from_a_checked_exit` pins the matcher. A printed reason shows only with `--nocapture`, since libtest drops a passing test's output; the exception is `render_state()`, which writes "skipped: no GPU adapter" straight to stderr on its first failed initialization and caches the failure, so later tests in the process skip without printing again.

- **LH4 Enum ids are names.** `RawMap::id`, `Subtype::id` and `Format::id` return the serde name as a `&'static str` from a `match` (as `PackRecipe::id` does), not a `String` serialised through `serde_json`; the generic `serde_id` is gone. `config::tests::ids_are_the_serde_names_of_every_variant` reads every name from the schema and compares, and `alloc_budget::enum_ids_do_not_allocate` pins that an id allocates nothing. No allocation budget moved under the LF1 rule by this change (measured 2026-11-22 after Task 10: warm plan 106921, re-plan 25691, frame 2738; measured again after Task 11 and the merge of main, 5 runs each with `--exact`: warm plan 106931, frame 2738 in all five frames of all five runs). The owner lowered the warm plan and re-plan budgets afterwards (LK1).

- **LH5 The re-plan shares what it does not change.** `Prepared`'s `registry`, `user_config`, `files`, `grouped`, `joined`, `sets`, `hand`, `typed`, `dup_idx`, `gen_replaced` and `packed_outputs`, the skeleton's own indexes (`set_of`, `key_sets`, `pbr_sets`, `fuzz_sets`, `f_sets`) and `FileScopes::forms` are `Arc`s, so `Skeleton::patch`'s copy of the skeleton shares them with the session's skeleton; a set whose `typed` members change is rewritten through `Arc::make_mut`, which leaves the kept skeleton as it was. Copies remain: the `[files]` documents, the request and the `[files]` layer, which patch writes (a `shortcut:` in `patch`); a clone of `typed` per patch for its checks, kept in a fresh `Arc` even when no member changes; a copy of `grouped` at the join check; and a copy of `sets` in the shared-output check, with `Arc::make_mut` cloning the set vector on the first suffix write. `alloc_budget.rs` pins the patch alone at `PATCH_CALLS` 20000 (measured 17888; 20446 before). The one-edit re-plan, which goes through `patch`, measured 23132 calls (5 runs, `--exact`: 23132 four times, 23133 once; 25691 before, LH4); `REPLAN_CALLS` is 26,000 (LK1): the LF1 rule gives 26,000, which is not below the old count (25691), so it would not fail the old code, and the owner lowered it to that anyway; `fast_replan.rs` shows a partial plan still equals the full plan.

- **LH6 The Rust CLI skills come from the plugin only.** The 16 `.claude/skills/rust-*` copies of the `rust-cli-skills` plugin were removed (owner, 2026-11-22: "they can be removed"); the saved workflows load the plugin's names. Codex (through the repo's `.agents\skills` junction) and the sister apps Texalith and FaceGen Composer (through their `.claude\skills` junctions to this folder) read this folder, so they have no Rust skills from it; the `egui-*` skills stay.

- **LH7 Code on a per-file or per-frame path follows `rust-hot-paths`.** `.claude/skills/rust-hot-paths/SKILL.md` is the repo's own skill, not a plugin copy (LH6 is about those); Codex and the sister apps read it through the junctions LH6 names, and its examples, test paths and goldens rule are Tessalith's. It names four shapes found by the 2026-11-21 profile and Notes LH4-LH5: a `String` built only to compare it, an index built before anything needs it, a value serialised for its name, and a whole clone per re-plan. For each it says what to write instead, keeping the Unicode fold where Note BF requires it. Its rules: copy the intent of nearby code, not its cost; change a type (shape 4) only when the task does; never raise an allocation budget to pass; no float rewrites; no scope widening. The SDD constraints template names it for changes under `crates/engine/src/` or `crates/gui/src/ui/` and model code a frame calls. A plan's task that adds or changes code run once per input file or per frame says so in its text, and plan review flags one that does not. Why (owner, 2026-11-22: "Yes, on a branch"): a scratch trial with Sonnet implementers, one task, 3 runs an arm, using an earlier draft of the skill without the examples. A new matcher placed beside an allocating one copied its `format!` loop in 3 of 3 runs from the brief alone (61 allocation calls per miss). It allocated nothing on a miss in 3 of 3 runs when the brief named the skill, and equally in 3 of 3 when the brief only said the function runs once per file. So the trial does not show the skill doing better than that one line. No run changed the old function's logic. Only the first shape and only Sonnet were tried.

## Note LI - BC3/BC4/BC5 Max search, and Slowest for radius 10 (owner, 2026-11-22)

- **LI1 Names (owner, 2026-11-22; the quote is in LI5).** `slowest` is the radius-10 search; `max` is the new one; no alias. The GUI ladder is Fast, Default, Slowest, Max on BC3, BC4 and BC5. A single-texture Effort pick undoes as "Change Effort" on every format (JI4).
- **LI2 The search.** The radius-10 search, then every endpoint pair within 16 of the block's lowest and highest value, then (BC3 alpha) the coverage guard at the texture's alpha-test cut, not 128. It lives in `bcenc` as the `_max` functions. It has no C++ counterpart: this amends HY1 and HY7, and the reference is `search_max_reference`, a literal port of bench 87c5539f `bc4ref.rs:304-340`.
- **LI3 Proof.** The bench-derived byte pins (`max_bytes_match_the_bench`: the pad-16 witness, the incumbent tie, the two improving ties, the two fallback blocks), the mutations they catch, the reference equality, the window property, the pair-counter check for the early return, the regression pin, the hits, and determinism across thread counts (`max_output_is_identical_for_any_thread_count`).
- **LI4 if-changed.** Ranks fast < default < slowest < max. A record at `max` whose `engine_version` is at most 3.0.0, or missing, not a string or unparsable, ranks as `slowest`, so a job at `max` encodes it again; a 3.0.1 dev build from before this note also wrote `max` for radius 10, and such records read as the new `max`.
- **LI5 Versions.** No `encode_version` arm moves. This is an explicit, narrow exception to DM1: the same serialized `max` (same profile hash) now writes different BC3, BC4 and BC5 bytes. Why: the owner ruled that serde `max` is the new search, with no alias, because "a person using max already wants the best possible result", and LI4 already re-encodes old `max` records once through the record age, so moving the arms would only report every other radius's output as written by an older encode. `rgbcx_patch`, the pins and every profile hash are unchanged (no built-in preset stored `max`); semver MINOR (compare LG7).
- **LI6 Quality and time.** The bench quality table, cited not copied: over the shipped Slowest, +0.033 dB on BC4 (`mask_real`), +0.020 dB on BC5 (`normal_real`), +0.076 dB on BC3 alpha over all BC3 sets and +0.211 dB on the cutout set (both against the guarded search BC3 ships; bc4-family.md:153-162). The 0.020 dB whole-BC3 figure is against the unguarded search and is not quoted. A complete block takes 2.65 times Slowest's time on BC4, 2.61 on BC5 and 1.06 on BC3 after the pair-search pruning (HY24); before it, 4.90, 4.76 and 1.18 (`bcenc`'s `max_speed` example on 20 sampled PNGs, `Path::best()`, median of 3 rounds, on the quiet Linux test VM with an i9-13900K host and 6 pinned vCPUs; docs/research/bc4-family.md#max-near-exhaustive).
- **LI7 Supersedes FJ8.** FJ8 (1.x) declined the near-exhaustive `max` search and kept `max` at radius 10; that is no longer the rule.

## Note LJ - Sets menu: Copy JSON settings, Edit match, Rename inputs (owner, 2026-11-21)
