This note was written as LG on the branch (its commits Note LG1 to LG3 say so) before main took LG for another note.

- **LJ1 Copy JSON settings (owner SM-O1 to SM-O3, SM-R1).** The set menus offer **Copy JSON settings from {set} to {n} selected…** (a dialog with one tick per row, in six groups), **Copy JSON settings** (puts the set's rows on a session clipboard) and **Paste JSON settings from {set} to {n} selected…** (the same dialog from the clipboard); the source is the last-clicked set and the targets are the selected sets. Only material values are rows: identity, path and control keys never copy. A stored value the source's writer would not export is no row: a wrong type, an XPMF number outside its field's range (that writer refuses the profile), or a raw `glint` / `fuzz` switch (PGPatcher rows use the storage keys `glint_on` / `fuzz_on`). A number's row value is what the source's writer exports (a CS block's value as it is; a plain PGPatcher value clamped, as the PGPatcher writer emits it), and it is converted at the destination: a texture set takes it as it is, a PGPatcher key stores it clamped to the panel's range; a colour follows the same rule. Whether a target has a block is read from the sets document once per target. A mapped CS value is listed once and reaches a target's block whatever its PGPatcher eligibility; on PBR grass without a block, the grass view's keys are skipped. Glint ownership is decided once per target before the first write: a material object that follows its texture set (both blocks, synced, BM16) takes the texture set's glint and skips its own glint rows; any other material object, a lone one included, takes its own rows and is never pulled along. CM `export` and XPMF `pbr` are each target's own; PGPatcher's `disabled` copies. No block is created: a missing block is a skip with its reason (SM-O2), a skin table is not a block, and an automatic set's `[[sets]]` entry is created as by any panel edit. Skin values are written exactly and the target's other skin keys stay; skin rows skip a shared set and a set whose diffuse is borrowed, as the skin writer does. A destination blocked by a hand edit (a plain value where a table goes, a table where the value goes, or a top-level `sets` of another shape) is skipped, never written past or counted. A row whose write would give the target two exclusive features (hair beside multilayer; subsurface beside subsurface_foliage; any pair the engine's exclusivity check names) is skipped with the target's own feature named (W14a, the panel labels, never storage keys). One apply is one undo step and one plan; undoing it clears its result line. The report goes to the bulk panel, Messages (one line per row, headed by its group) and the status line (one summary toast per apply). The Copy / Paste clipboard is a session snapshot, kept across a project switch, never saved. Copy needs exactly one set; Paste includes the source when it is selected (a revert to the copied values). Feature clashes a copy creates show as Problems rows (FN5). Tests: `set_json_copy::the_rows_are_material_values_only`, `raw_glint_and_fuzz_switches_are_never_rows`, `a_value_of_the_wrong_type_is_not_a_row`, `every_group_copies_material_values_and_keeps_the_targets_own`, `a_cs_source_value_copies_as_is_and_clamps_in_the_pgpatcher_output`, `a_plain_pgpatcher_source_copies_its_exported_value_to_cs`, `a_mapped_colour_row_carries_the_source_writers_value`, `cs_values_reach_a_block_even_without_pgpatcher_json_and_glint_stays_synced`, `pbr_grass_takes_no_ignored_key_even_a_mapped_one`, `glint_ownership_is_decided_once_per_target`, `a_material_object_alone_takes_glint_rows`, `a_copy_never_creates_a_block_and_says_why`, `skin_values_copy_exactly_without_pruning`, `skin_skip_reasons_follow_the_skin_writer`, `xpmf_values_copy_within_bounds_and_each_profile_keeps_its_own`, `a_hand_edited_value_in_the_way_is_skipped_not_counted`, `hair_is_skipped_beside_multilayer`, `subsurface_is_skipped_beside_subsurface_foliage`, `foliage_is_skipped_beside_subsurface_and_hair_beside_any_engine_pair`, `one_apply_is_one_undo_step_for_every_write`, `a_skin_write_and_another_groups_write_share_one_undo_step`, `undoing_the_copy_clears_its_result_line`, `a_dropped_target_is_reported_and_counted`, `one_apply_raises_one_summary_toast_and_logs_each_row`, `the_menus_offer_copy_json_with_counts_and_reasons`, `copy_on_one_card_then_paste_on_another_writes_it`, `paste_writes_the_snapshot_even_onto_its_source`, `the_clipboard_survives_opening_another_project`, `the_copied_line_survives_a_plan_that_settles_after_it`, `an_all_skipped_paste_on_one_set_says_why_and_changes_nothing`, `the_dialog_names_each_row_with_its_value_and_applies_only_ticked_rows`, `copy_skips_with_reasons_in_one_undo_step`, `two_exclusive_rows_of_one_apply_do_not_both_land`, `a_report_line_names_its_group`, `copy_and_paste_entries_say_why_they_are_off`, `copy_on_the_card_menu_shows_its_own_reason_while_editing_is_off`, `copy_json_settings_from_the_set_menu_writes_the_other_set`, `an_unmapped_colour_row_is_clamped_like_a_number`, `a_block_just_added_takes_a_mapped_row_before_the_plan_lands`, `every_value_of_a_mapped_key_has_a_texture_set_form`, `an_inline_table_in_the_way_is_skipped` and `the_result_lines_survive_a_partial_plan_and_the_full_plan` (all in `set_json_copy.rs`).
- **LJ2 Edit match… opens Matching.** It uses the Fix path focus request (FC3) inside the deferred card callback (JV1), with the set's first match, or brings the Matching group into view when there is none. It is off with W18 ("This set has no PGPatcher JSON settings, so it has no match rules") on a set without the JSON panel, by the same predicate (`shows_json_panel`) the set editor uses to draw that group. Amends JV1. Tests: `set_cards::edit_match_opens_matching_and_focuses_the_first_match`, `edit_match_with_no_match_row_opens_the_group_in_view`, `edit_match_is_off_with_its_reason_on_a_set_without_json`, `edit_match_waits_for_a_focused_field_and_uses_the_view_after_it`.
- **LJ3 Rename inputs only from the Textures view (owner: "it's very destructive"; SM-O4, SM-O5).** The card submenu, the multi-set menu, F2 and the dialog's mode switch offer no Inputs over a set selection; F2 over sets with no PGPatcher PBR set opens no dialog and says where (W19). Clear input alias and the set editor's Input name box stay. SM-O6, SM-R2: a Textures-view rename of one member keeps its set's settings and borrows (G1, AB1, HF4, HS13). Both tests passed on the base code, so they are pins, with no fix commit: the loss the owner saw came from the Sets-view route this note removes. They are `rename::a_textures_view_rename_of_one_member_keeps_the_sets_settings_and_borrows` (a diffuse renamed alone: its entry, borrows and format follow it) and `a_textures_view_rename_of_a_non_diffuse_member_keeps_the_sets_settings_and_borrows`. An input rename from the rename dialog moves the set's Also export opt-out with its entry, as the Input name box does (extends FT1 to `apply_rename`). The whole rename mapping is applied once to the original skip list, so a chain moves each opt-out once, and a skip rename edits the `skip` elements in place, keeping `[export]` comments (the Input name box and the set rename too). Tests `rename::open_rename_refuses_inputs_over_a_set_selection`, `rename::a_textures_view_rename_moves_the_also_opt_out`, `rename::a_rename_chain_moves_each_opt_out_once`, `backlog_b4_input_name::an_input_rename_keeps_the_export_comments`. Supersedes AG13 in part (Rename inputs under the set menu's Rename submenu); amends HJ1 and JV1.

## Note LK - Warm plan and re-plan budgets (owner, 2026-11-22)

- **LK1 `WARM_PLAN_CALLS` is 120,000 and `REPLAN_CALLS` is 26,000.** Each is the LF1 rule on its measured count (5 runs, `--exact --nocapture`: warm plan 106930 to 106932, re-plan 23133), rounded up to two significant figures. Both are lowered although neither would fail the earlier code they were measured on (warm plan 112683 at LF3, re-plan 25691 at LH4): the owner, 2026-11-22: "Lower the warm plan budget to 120000 anyways, to allow less regression" and "Lower replan to 26000 aswell", so a later regression has less room to hide. `PATCH_CALLS` and `FRAME_CALLS` are unchanged. Amends LF3, LH4 and LH5.

## Note LL - Tooling from the 2026-11-22 retro (main team, 2026-11-22)

- **LL1 `rustfmt.toml` sets `newline_style = "Native"`.** Measured with rustfmt 1.10.0 (on a tracked CRLF file, with a formatting break inserted as a CRLF line): under the default `Auto`, rustfmt rewrites a file that needs formatting as all LF, and leaves a file that needs none untouched, so a clean CRLF tree passes `cargo fmt --all --check` and only a reformatted file turns LF. Under `Native` the reformatted file stays CRLF. A file with mixed endings (first line ending LF) passes `--check` under `Auto` and fails it under `Native` ("Incorrect newline style"); `cargo fmt` then writes it back as CRLF. CONTRIBUTING.md (Conventions) says a Windows working copy is CRLF (`core.autocrlf=true`): with an LF working copy `cargo fmt --check` fails.
- **LL2 The gate writes `<Out>\progress.txt`.** One line per step start, PASS or FAIL, skip, and the final result. Through the gate's own `Step`, a native child's stdout and stderr lines were readable with `Get-Content` 8 s into a 20 s step (12 bytes) and the final log kept all four lines in order, so `Step`'s redirection is unchanged. The 0 bytes seen on a long run were not reproduced; a stale NTFS size is a guess.
- **LL3 The long-run results rule is in `CODING_STANDARDS.md`, "A long `#[ignore]` run keeps its per-item results".** The `bcenc` 10k corpus test is not changed here; Plan E's bcenc track, which already writes per-file hashes from a scratch test, brings it under the rule.

## Note LN - BC7 weighting gains Alpha ×4, ×32 and ×64 (owner, 2026-11-22)

- **LN1 `Bc7Weights` has `alpha4`, `alpha32` and `alpha64` (owner: "add in bc7 weight modes alpha modes x 4, x 32 and x64").** They give bc7e `[1, 1, 1, w]`, are accepted wherever `alpha16` is (`sdc.toml`, overrides, presets, `--set`) and reach bc7e only, as the others do (CP2, CP4, CP5, CP8). Engine order (`Bc7Weights::ALL` and the "expected one of" text): linear, alpha2, alpha4, alpha8, alpha16, alpha32, alpha64.
- **LN2 No existing byte changes.** No preset, sub-type or rule sets the new values, so the goldens, `encode_versions` and every profile hash are as before.
- **LN3 The hover texts are proposals pending the owner's reading.** They live in `WEIGHTING_OPTIONS` in `crates/gui/src/model/fields.rs`. The ×16 text no longer says "the strongest", which ×64 now is, and the ×32 text's small gain is the measured +0.3 dB of alpha over ×16 on `alpha_real` (docs/research/bc7e-tuning.md).
- **LN4 The dropdown's options, in order (`WEIGHTING_OPTIONS`):** Linear, Perceptual, Alpha ×2, Alpha ×4, Alpha ×8, Alpha ×16, Alpha ×32, Alpha ×64. Perceptual is `encoder.perceptual`, the rest are `encoder.bc7_weights`. An older version refuses an `sdc.toml` that uses the new values.

## Note LM - Textures: types, bulk edits and words (review E, 2026-11-16, controller rulings 2026-11-21)

LM is provisional. At merge the controller rechecks the next free main-team letter and, if needed, renames LM and its references: this note, the index lines, the digest and item cites that name LM, the archive's ARCHIVED lines and `tools/decisions-archive/2026-11-22-LM.tsv`.

- **LM1 One type table.** `model::labels::type_info` holds every type's label, one-line description and the file ending that gives it, in one exhaustive match. `subtype_name`, the type picker, the type combos, the texture panel's undecided card, the bulk panel's card, set-card pills and members and the Messages lines read it, so they agree; set-card chips use the same words through `slot_type_label` (LM5). A type's hover in the picker and the type combos is its description, then "File name: {ending}" ("No file ending gives this type (picked by hand)" and the like where no ending gives it), then `OWN_SET_HOVER`, "A file alone under its base name becomes a set of its own once it has a type." The picker draws each description in small weak text under its label; its search ignores case, reads `_` as a space and matches the label, the description or the stored id. The descriptions state the shipped rules (controller ruling on the type facts, worded from `docs/wiki/Texture-Types.md` where it already says them): a PBR normal drops its specular alpha by default and Data on the Alpha row keeps it (JS7, JS8); the endings of PBR fuzz, Detail normal and Skin detail name the shader or set that reads them. The undecided card's combo is named "Type for this texture", with the shown type as its AccessKit value. Ids, the `subtype` key and stored values are unchanged. Tests `type_words::the_table_reads_like_the_wiki`, `type_picker::the_type_menu_shows_descriptions_and_finds_by_them`.
- **LM2 Plain words in the texture panel and Settings.** Normal handling's bake entry reads "Bake normal detail into roughness or gloss" (`RENORM_BAKE_LABEL`) with `RENORM_BAKE_HOVER` on hover: the bake reads unit directions, the smaller mips gain roughness where they vary, and on a plain normal in a complex material set it also reduces gloss in the smaller mips of the `_m` green channel. While the bake is the row's active value, the Normal handling row's hover is `RENORM_BAKE_HOVER` then `VARIANCE_BAKE_HOVER`: Keep as authored preserves average length in smaller XYZ tangent-space base and coat normal mips (LA), and the bake uses unit directions with either base setting. On a plain `normal` in a complex material set the bake is the explicit gloss opt-in, so that row shows `RENORM_BAKE_HOVER` before the choice too (CL40); any other row, Normal base level included, shows neither (controller ruling on the type facts). The DDS tag's UNORM entry reads "UNORM (as stored)" with `UNORM_HOVER`. Effort's entries read "Ultra fast", "Very fast", "Fast", "Basic", "Very slow", "Slowest" and "Max" (`labels::effort_label`, in the panel and the presets editor). The built-in layer's provenance reads "Tessalith's default". Settings' Mode combo reads "Community Shaders (cs)" and "Base game (vanilla)" (`code_text::target_label`), with the note "This beats the Target set in a preset." Keys and stored values are unchanged. Tests `texture_panel_layout::the_bake_hover_shows_only_for_the_active_bake`, `settings_tab::the_mode_says_community_shaders`.
- **LM3 "type", never "sub-type", in words a user reads (controller, 2026-11-16; the owner may overrule it).** The window, the engine's messages and so the CLI's printed prose say "type": for example `--set` errors "unknown type 'nope'", "set the type with `subtype =` on the rule, not in overrides", "Change this file's type.", `sdc inspect`'s planned-file line "  type {id}  format …" (`verbs.rs` `print_planned`), the texture panel's Texture type row and the Textures filter "Filter by type". The ruling covers the wiki pages as well; their rewording is separate from this note. Names stay as code: the `[files]` key `subtype`, `subtype:` scopes and `--set subtype=…`, `--subtype`, schema names and doc comments, and the Rust identifiers (`Subtype`, `subtype_name`, `TypeKind::SubType`). No reworded text reaches a stored form: `ManifestRecord`, `ShaderPick` and `ValidationSummary` hold none of it, and the hash records in `_sdc/generated.json` and `sdc-project.toml` (FC11) hash exported files, never a message, so the goldens, `encode_versions` and the manifest tests are unchanged and no encode version moved. No mark key changes: each reworded code is an Error or Blocking row (not markable) or shows the GUI's own sentence, and IN-PACKING-INPUT, the one markable code among them, is shown and marked by its GUI sentence; the engine detail of PACK-DEFAULTED, PACK-CONFLICT, PACK-UNUSED-INPUT, SHADER-EXTRA-MAP, SET-SLOT-BORROWED-UNUSED and SHADER-MISSING-MAP is untouched. Diagnostic events and the CLI's printed output carry the new words, so redirected output differs from earlier builds. `vocabulary::no_stray_words` fails a drawn "sub-type", "Sub-type", "spec §", "Veryslow" or "UNORM (linear)".
- **LM4 Messages cite no spec or ruling.** No engine or CLI message names a spec section or a DECISIONS item: the mip-filter reasons, the format and tag reasons, the suffix-alias errors and the packing-input reasons state the rule alone. The JSON-schema description strings in `schemas.rs` keep "(DECISIONS S6)" and "(Note DY)": they are schema documentation, and the committed schemas are pinned (controller ruling). Tests `policy_checks::{filter_reasons_cite_no_spec, hdr_filter_reasons_cite_no_spec, messages_cite_no_decision}`.
- **LM5 Set cards explain their letters (controller ruling on empty slots).** A chip's slot label comes from `labels::slot_type_label` through `set_slot_label`: the resolved shader first, a document-only card's saved shader next, the member flags last. Each chip's hover, which is also its AccessKit description, keeps its first detail and adds the label: an own file adds "{label}"; a borrowed one reads "borrowed from {set}" then "{label}"; a link keeps JR's "{slot} linked to {path}" and adds "{label}, from set {short}" or "{label}: {file}, set by path"; a cleared slot keeps "{slot} (cleared): {file}" and adds "{label}: cleared, so the JSON leaves this slot empty"; an external record adds "{label}" under its path; a replaced slot reads "{slot} (empty)" then "{label}: the link to {path} supplies the {slot} slot, so {file} is not written here" (the slot by its name, as the other chip hovers name it, never by a number). JX's Shift+click hint follows once. A trailing member with a job adds its type's description on a second line. A resting card draws no empty-slot words: they are in the drag strip, one small "{label}: no file yet" caption per empty slot, and in the "?" legend beside the Sets heading (AccessKit name "What the letters mean", 18 lines, `labels::LEGEND`). Only the strip's letter grid and its 2 px bridge own a release; the captions paint over the card below, and a release over them goes to the card under the pointer. A strip flipped above its card puts the grid next to the card and the captions above the grid. An auto-picked shader's combo hover reads "Tessalith picked this shader from the set's files. Pick one yourself to fix it."; a disabled combo keeps the first sentence. A pill hover's recipe line names the recipe by its table label ("PBR RMAOS: run"). The No JSON predicate is unchanged. Tests `set_cards::the_legend_lists_the_letters_and_the_pills`, `set_drag::{a_centre_drop_on_the_next_card_lands_there_while_the_card_above_shows_its_captions, a_release_over_a_flipped_strips_captions_goes_to_the_card_under_them}`.
- **LM6 A type choice that makes a set says so, with Show (controller ruling on new sets).** A type write through `edit_subtype` (the card's Confirm and the bulk Apply among them; Reset type queues nothing) queues its item with the card ids listed before the first choice of the group (`sets::views`, document-only cards included). The plan that settles the chain (`finish_plan`) compares the cards by root-relative id (`engine::sets::same_id`): a partial or intermediate plan announces nothing, and a card listed before, one that gained a key and one whose output root moved are not new. The new cards that hold a queued item still carrying its own written type give one Info line in all, with Show: for one card "{file} is now {type}. New set {id} (1 texture)." or "… ({n} textures).", for several "2 new sets: {a} and {b}.", "3 new sets: {a}, {b} and {c}." or "{k} new sets: {a}, {b}, {c} and {m} more.", whose Show names the first. The status note reads "New set {id}: see Messages." or "{k} new sets: see Messages."; the post-open next step wins it when due. Undo, redo, an open, a cancelled open and a failed plan drop the queue. A Messages row's Show (accessible name "Show {id}") resolves its project root and card id against the current card list (`engine::sets::same_id` on the root-relative id, so it still finds its card after an output-root rekey, which keeps the id): in another project, or with the card gone, it is disabled with "That set is no longer listed". A click leaves the Meshes overlay, selects the card, clears a Sets filter that hides it and opens its folded group. Tests `messages::{the_final_plan_announces_once_after_a_partial_and_an_intermediate_plan, an_output_root_rekey_does_not_announce_a_card}`, `app_shell::a_show_button_is_bound_to_its_project`, `set_groups_ui::a_messages_show_button_opens_the_folded_group_of_its_set`.
- **LM7 The automatic recording row names its files.** The Messages row of an automatic recording reads "{n} types set automatically: " (or values), n counting the decisions, then the first three distinct file names in recording order (same-named files in two folders are one name), then ", and {m} more" when more remain. Its hover lists each file's line. Only when the batch set a first automatic type (a `subtype` write with no `only_if`, not a guarded retype such as a normal follow) do a blank line and the owner's sentence follow: "Tessalith sets a type on its own only when the evidence is clear: a known file ending such as _n, the folder it is in, or the meshes and grass that use it. Anything less sure stays Undecided for you." A values-only or follow-only row never carries it. The recording stays the JY6 baseline, and the JS5 and joined-follow history is unchanged. Tests `auto_accept::{a_mixed_recording_names_three_files_and_counts_the_rest, the_automatic_row_says_when_tessalith_decides}`.
- **LM8 The bulk panel asks the undecided ones (controller ruling on bulk scope).** With selected textures that wait for a type, the bulk panel draws a card before its groups: "What kind are these {n} textures?" ("What kind is this texture?" for one), the likely-type buttons (LM9), a combo of the types offered to every one of them (`type_offered_to`; AccessKit name "Type for {n} textures"), "Apply to {n}", and "{m} other selected textures already have a type." It asks the selection's type questions (`subtype_question`: not an alpha or green question, not the blocked-type picker) outside a locked slot, whether or not they are in the current Review run; every selected texture with a job and no type question counts in the line, locked or not. The combo starts on "Choose a type"; a pick holds only for the same undecided set and while it is offered to all of them, else Apply is off with "Pick a type from the list first". Apply is also off with `fix_off`'s reason while a plan runs or `sdc.toml` is read-only. `decide_many` reads the selection, the questions, the locks, the offers and writability again at the click and writes the keys still eligible through the bulk `subtype` edit, as one undo step and one plan. Tests `bulk_ui::{apply_to_undecided_is_one_undo_step, the_bulk_type_card_uses_only_selected_actionable_type_questions, the_card_offers_only_types_every_waiting_texture_is_offered}`.
- **LM9 Likely types.** An undecided card, single or bulk, offers up to three likely types as buttons (`model::likely::likely_types`): the button shows the type's label, its hover the reason, its AccessKit name "Use {label}". The sources, in order: the engine's suggestions (one vote per texture per type, most votes first, then id; the reason from the first key in key order); the type written in `[files]` for other files with the same name ending (the stem after its last `_`, lower case, trailing digits dropped; most first, then id; "{file} has this type."); then the pixels, pooled over the textures ("The pixels look like a normal map.", "The pixels are grey."). A candidate is kept only when the target offers it to every asked texture, before duplicates are dropped and three are taken. On the single texture card a click answers as Confirm does: it writes the type and moves on (LM10, ruling 3d-T12-1). On the bulk card a click picks the type in the card's combo and writes nothing; Apply writes. The blocked-type picker has no buttons: its type is decided but unavailable. Tests `type_words::{a_restricted_picker_skips_an_unavailable_likely_type, a_reason_comes_from_the_first_key_in_lexical_order}`.
- **LM10 Confirm moves on.** The single card's Confirm and its likely-type buttons decide and select the next undecided texture after it in the current view order (`next_undecided_after`: a texture the filter hides never counts, and it does not wrap); with none left the selection stays. The bulk card's likely-type buttons and Apply, and the Problems row buttons, do not move the selection. Tests `type_words::{confirm_moves_to_the_next_visible_undecided_only, the_card_shows_likely_buttons, bulk_and_problems_type_answers_keep_the_selection}`.
- **LM11 Each bulk row shows its reach.** A value-independent bulk row's note reads "applies to {k} of {n}", also when k is n. The mip filter, BC7 weighting, Format and DDS tag rows list every option as "{option} ({k} of {n})", where k counts the textures a write of exactly that value writes by `plan_bulk_edit`'s rule (job, field, slot lock, target); a texture that already holds the value counts. Format's options are format/tag pairs, and the tag row offers the tags each selected texture's own format allows. An option with k = 0 is disabled with "None of the selected textures can take this", also while an edit is in flight (waiting would not turn it on); every other option is disabled with "Wait for the plan to finish" while an edit is in flight. These rows' notes group options of equal reach, widest first, two groups at most, then "others vary"; when no option reaches any texture the note reads "applies to 0 of {n}". The note is one truncated line, with the whole text and the skip lines on hover. Reach is counted in `bulk_fields`' one pass per texture and cached with the bulk view by revision. A read-only `sdc.toml` greys every bulk row, so the combo does not open. Tests `bulk_plan::the_per_option_reach_equals_what_the_write_writes`, `bulk_ui::{the_reach_entries_are_off_while_an_edit_is_in_flight, a_zero_reach_entry_keeps_its_reason_while_an_edit_is_in_flight, the_reach_entries_are_off_while_sdc_toml_is_read_only}`.
- **LM12 Status pills start off (controller, 2026-11-16).** The Textures status pills and the Sets pills (Not ready, Ready, No JSON) are not saved: `layout::capture` keeps only `hide-helpers` in `filters` and empties `sets_filters`, which a save then leaves out of `gui.toml`, and `restore_model` restores hide-helpers, the type, format and set combos, sort, tile size and the Sets shader. An old `gui.toml` with pill names loads, a start does not rewrite it, and the next save drops them. The Sets search stays unsaved (CD11). CD17's guard stays: no "No sets match" before the first plan. Tests `textures_filter::status_pills_are_not_restored_but_sort_and_tile_are`, `sets_filter::the_shader_persists_and_the_pills_and_search_do_not`, `window_layout::an_old_file_with_pills_is_not_rewritten_by_a_start_but_loses_them_on_the_next_save`.
- **LM13 Used by N meshes (controller ruling on mesh links).** The list sits under the texture's heading, job key and Stop re-encoding, above the first screen's type row (not in the compact panel); its header is a texture-panel group header (Note LQ). Its header "Used by {n} meshes" ("Used by 1 mesh") counts distinct NIF copies in the scan database, so it matches what a click can open. The cap of 200 (`USED_BY_CAP`) counts copies, "and {m} more meshes, not listed" counts those past it, and the alpha mark is decided over every copy before the cap. Rows keep the (NIF, mod name) grouping: the NIF path (cut in the middle to fit, the whole path on hover and as its accessible name), then per copy its own "{mod} · MO2 priority {p}" or "{mod} · BSA load rank {p}", "(overridden by {mod})" when another copy wins ("(overridden by {mod} at {root})" when the winner has the copy's own mod name), the amber "decided the alpha" tag with its hover, and one link per shape that uses the texture ("Shape {block}" when unnamed; hover "Open this mesh in Meshes with {shape} selected."; accessible name "Open {nif} at {shape} (copy {id}, block {block})"). A group shows no row-level priority or rank. The shape links and "Show all in Meshes" are off, with the reason on hover, while a scan runs or did not finish ("A scan is running or did not finish, so this list may change. These links work once a scan has finished."); while the list is read, after a scan until it is read again, or while Meshes reads its rows ("The mesh list is still being read. These links work once it has finished.", and the last line is the disabled "Reading meshes..."); and when Meshes reads another scan database ("Meshes shows another scan database than this list was read from."). An answer read from another database is never served, and a panicked read keeps the database it read. A shape link sends its store, copy, block and name (`Action::ShowMesh`); "Show all in Meshes" keeps the texture filter. Tests `used_by_meshes::{used_by_sits_under_the_heading, same_name_copies_count_toward_the_cap, links_are_off_while_a_scan_is_unsettled}`.
- **LM14 A shape link opens that copy on that shape in Meshes.** `app.rs` `route_3d` brings Meshes forward for every link. For the store Meshes reads it calls `MeshesModel::show_shape(copy_id, block, shape)`; for another store, or with none open, it sets Meshes' status to "That mesh link came from another scan database than Meshes shows, so it opened nothing." or "Meshes has no scan database open, so that mesh link opened nothing." A request is bound to `store_path` when made and replaces an earlier one; a NIF picked by hand (`select_nif`) drops it, and a waiting request of another store is dropped with the other-store line at the next `reload` or take. It opens once no scan runs or is unfinished and the rows came from the latest read, which succeeded (`rows_current`, which `reload` clears): at once, after the next successful row read, or in `finished` on the rows from before a scan PGPatcher paused. While a read is in flight it waits. When the store is settled, no read is out and the rows are not current (the store would not open, or the mod list or the rows failed to read), it is dropped with "The scan database could not be read, so that mesh link opened nothing." beside the database error. Everything is read before anything changes: the copy among the fresh winners and losers, its shapes, the block and its nullable name (which must equal the link's) and the PGPatcher pair; a copy, block or name the scan no longer holds sets "That shape is no longer in the scan" and changes nothing. An open edit stays only for a link to its own row or that row's pair, with the same data path and a counterpart of the shape (same block and name, else the only shape of that name); its preview stays and the edit's card for that shape is selected. Every other case takes the guarded selection: an edit ends only as LD4's durability check allows, and a refusal keeps the draft, the edit, the selection, the filters, the side and LD's error (LD4, LD12). On success the list shows the row (Any mod, All for a winner and Overridden for a losing copy; a search or texture chip that hides it is cleared and a This project pill that hides it is turned off) on the side of a pair the link names, with the shape's card selected and the Shapes column open; an earlier link's status line is cleared and any other line stays.
  - **The NIF-team file.** In `crates/gui/src/model/meshes.rs` (LD11's list): new public `show_shape`; `select_nif` drops a waiting link first; `poll_rows` opens a waiting link after a successful install and leaves a failed or stopped read's link to the drop above; `reload` marks the rows not current, first drops a waiting link of another store, and drops any waiting link when it stops on the mod list; the test hook `hold_rows_read` marks the rows not current; `load_shapes` still clears `txst_picks` first, before its early return and a failed read, and `install_shapes` clears them too (Note GO; test `meshes_txst_preview::a_failed_shape_read_drops_the_picks`); new `pub(crate)` consts `OTHER_STORE_LINK` and `NO_STORE_LINK`. Everything else added there is private. `tests/nif_edit.rs` pins it with 22 `shape_link` tests, among them the draft and refusal pins `a_shape_link_saves_the_open_draft_before_switching`, `a_shape_link_keeps_an_edit_that_cannot_be_saved`, `a_shape_link_to_a_pair_keeps_an_edit_of_another_nif_that_cannot_be_saved` and `a_shape_link_to_another_mods_copy_of_the_edited_nif_leaves_the_edit`, and the waiting pins `a_shape_link_clicked_during_a_scan_opens_once_it_settles`, `a_failed_row_read_drops_a_waiting_shape_link` and `a_waiting_shape_link_never_opens_in_another_database`. The NIF team's handoff line is written at merge.
- **LM15 Packed and data-alpha tiles draw opaque.** A tile's thumbnail shows alpha as opacity only for a colour map. `TextureItem::alpha_is_data` holds for a packed output and for a job whose alpha policy is Data or Remove (so normal, landscape normal, coat normal and detail normal tiles too: their alpha is specular or a mask), and `ui::thumbs` uploads such a thumbnail with alpha 255, cached per flag. Blend, Cutout, CutoutBlend and a non-packed item with no job keep their transparency. Measured on a generated packed eye RMAOS (roughness 140, metallic 0, AO 255, specular 10): the upload's mean RGB was 5 and its first pixel #05000A0A (premultiplied by the specular alpha, near black) before the flag, and is 131 and #8C00FFFF with it; a source file of the same pixels with Data alpha went from 5 to 131, and a Blend control stays premultiplied. Tests `dds_thumbs::{the_packed_tile_draws_its_own_thumbnail, a_data_alpha_tile_is_not_drawn_black, a_blend_tile_keeps_its_low_alpha_and_the_flag_follows_the_policy}`.
- **LM16 Packed tiles say what they are made from (controller ruling on packed outputs).** A packed output's tile hover adds "Packed output, made from {sources}. Bulk edits change unlocked settings on this output." (the sources of `job.pack`, one per key, sorted by file name: "a", "a and b", "a, b and c"). A source's tile hover, and its helper row's target hover, add "Packed into {j}: changing this file changes {j}." once per output whose sources hold it, in place of the plain "Packed into {j}" status line; a source with no job of its own adds "Bulk edits skip this source because it has no settings of its own." A helper row also carries those lines in its AccessKit description; a no-set item shows none. The hovers describe bulk behaviour as it stands, which this note does not change: Ctrl+A selects packed tiles, an unlocked field such as mip levels writes on the packed output's own key, and a consumed source with no job is skipped. Tests `bulk_ui::{a_packed_tile_says_what_it_is_made_from, a_shared_source_lists_every_output_it_feeds, ctrl_a_includes_packed_tiles, a_packed_output_writes_its_own_mip_levels}`.
- **LM17 A re-plan says it is re-checking.** `PlanCause` picks the plan bar's words. An open says "Planning…" and then "Analysing {done} / {total} files…". A re-plan after the user's own edit (`replan_after_edit`) says "Re-checking after your change…" and then "Re-checking after your change: {done} / {total} files…". Every other re-plan, Undo, Redo and the recorder's follow-up included, says "Re-checking…" and then "Re-checking {done} / {total} files…" ("file" for one). An open in progress wins over any cause. The partial-plan line, the undo note, the Export-cancelled override, the next-step line and Review's "Planning…" are unchanged. Tests `plan_progress::{an_edit_replan_says_re_checking_after_your_change, a_plain_replan_counts_as_re_checking, an_edit_during_an_open_still_says_planning}`.
- **LM18 Shift-click on Textures tiles selects the range.** A pin of existing behaviour: test `bulk_ui::shift_click_on_tiles_selects_the_range`. JX's Shift+click on a set card's thumbnail is a separate gesture.
- **LM19 Whose words.** The owner settled five Table A descriptions on 2026-11-16: Subsurface tint, Complex grass, Skin subsurface mask, Complex material and Skin RFAOS (`labels::type_info`), and the automatic row's sentence in LM7 (`auto_record::HIGH_CONFIDENCE`). The controller ruled the technical content on 2026-11-21: the type facts (LM1, LM2), empty slots (LM5), bulk scope and new sets (LM6, LM8), mesh links (LM13, LM14) and packed outputs (LM16); and on 2026-11-16 the Sets pills (LM12) and "type" in prose with the CLI output (LM3). Every other new string in LM1 to LM17 is the controller's proposed text, shipped pending the owner's reading at the next report. The strings live at their source: `labels.rs` (`type_info`, `type_hover`, `OWN_SET_HOVER`, `slot_type_label`, `LEGEND`, `effort_label`, the UNORM and bake consts), `sets_column.rs`, `ui/messages.rs`, `new_set_note.rs`, `auto_record.rs`, `bulk_panel.rs`, `likely.rs`, `settings_panel.rs` (`NO_REACH_HOVER`, the used-by reasons), `used_by.rs`, `model/meshes.rs` (`SHAPE_GONE` and the link lines), `columns.rs` and `model/mod.rs` (`PlanCause`). Wording `docs/wiki/Texture-Types.md` already shipped keeps that source.
- **LM20 Supersedes.** In part: FC9 (the "Sub-type labels from one table" bullet: labels as ids with spaces and the id on hover, LM1); FB9 (the texture panel's Sub-type field name, LM3, and the bulk panel's "a selection has no one suggestion", LM8); AG17 (the Textures status pills leave the saved list, LM12); AG50 (the Sort digest line's saved "Filters", LM12); CD11 (the saved Sets pills, LM12); AG96 (the header words, the placement and one row per (NIF, mod), LM13); CD17 (its reason, pills restored from `gui.toml`; the guard stays, LM12); U4 (the "Sub-type row" wording, LM3); S-mips-2 (only the spec cite in the filter reason, LM4); the quoted words of BC11, AG46/AG53, FM10, GU2, IB9 and HI4 (LM3); the link name in the Meshes list digest line (GC) and in JL8 (LM13). The digest lines of U4, BC11, AG46/AG53, FB9, AG17, AG96, the Sort line (AG50) and the Meshes list, and the items FM10, GU2, IB9, HI4 and JL8, are edited, with their old wording in the archive. FC9, CD11, CD17 and S-mips-2 have no live wording that changed, so nothing of theirs is edited or archived; the frozen 1.x record keeps every original text. JR, JS, JV, JW, JX, JY, LA, LB, LC and LD stand.
- **LM21 A tile's AccessKit node carries its hover's base text (controller rulings 3d-F-9, 3d-F-9b).** The tile's hover is built only while hovered (`on_hover_ui`), so egui copies nothing of it into the tile's node. `columns.rs` makes that copy by hand inside `accesskit_node_builder`, with egui's own guards from `describe_for_accessibility`: an existing node only, the description only when none is set, no name on a `Label` or `labelled_by` node. The copy is `tile_access_text`: the key, the status (with the plain "Packed into {j}" line) and the warnings, as the hover read before LM16. LM16's pack lines never go there; they stay on the hover and on the helper row, and building them scans every output, which would run for each visible tile while a screen reader reads the tree. `widget_info` is not used: it would give the tile a role and a short name, which the accessible-names audit and the tests that find a tile by its file name do not expect. Tests `problems_rows::one_line_replaces_the_three_alpha_lines`, `bulk_ui::a_packed_tile_says_what_it_is_made_from`.
## Note LO - BC6H kernel fixes (main team, 2026-11-22)

An audit of the vendored BC6H kernel (the plan's survey, `2026-11-22-bc6h-kernel-fixes-survey.md`) found defects in `ispctc/kernel.ispc`; the fixes are ISPC TC patch 0004. Finding #4 is out of scope: `quantize.rs` `clamp_half` clamps every input first.

- **LO1 Patch 0004 is `ispctc/patches/0004-bc6h-kernel-fixes.patch`.** It is `git diff <base> -- crates/encoders-sys/vendor/ispctc/kernel.ispc` against the post-0003 kernel at `fca6cb41a6703aaad61461700e083670cbcc3bd6`, regenerated whole after each fix (`git diff --output=`, never `>`), and applies on top of patches 0001 and 0003. Every change is marked `sdc patch 0004` and the notice at the top of `kernel.ispc` lists the fixes. The objects are built by `tools/regen-ispc.ps1` with both compilers (ISPC 1.31.0, and 1.32.0 for `kernel_avx512skx.obj`), as for patch 0003; the objects are built with the kernel's asserts live (no `--opt=disable-assertions`), so a violated assert aborts a test run.
- **LO2 #6 `bc6h_bounds`' `rgb_span` holds three floats.** The kernel declared `float rgb_span[0] = { 0, 0, 0 };` and then wrote and read `rgb_span[p]` for p in 0..3; it is now `rgb_span[3]`. ISPC 1.31.0 (avx2, `-DSDC_BC6H_ONLY`) printed no diagnostic naming `rgb_span` or that line. The three ISPC TC objects `kernel_sse2.obj`, `kernel_avx2.obj` and `kernel_avx512skx.obj` differ in bytes from the pre-patch objects after the rebuild. The notice explains it: the kernel with only the notice added and `rgb_span[0]` kept builds objects equal to the committed ones (regen listing 10 MATCH), so the fix itself left the generated code unchanged. `kernel.obj` and `kernel_ispc.h` and all bc7e files match. BC6H output bytes are unchanged: the 18 pins on every path and the 22 measured images on SSE2, AVX2 and AVX-512 hash equal.
- **LO3 #7 `bc6h_enc_2p` clamps the partition count to 32.** `part_list` holds 32 partitions, but `partial_sort_list` and `bc6h_enc_2p_list` took `state->fastSkipTreshold` unclamped; both now take `n = min(state->fastSkipTreshold, 32)`. Tessalith cannot reach it: `shim/sdc_shim.cpp` fills `g_bc6` from the five `GetProfile_bc6h_*` (the largest `fastSkipTreshold` is 32) plus the peak copy, `Bc6hLevel` is closed, and nothing under `shim/`, `src/` or `crates/engine` writes the field; the clamp guards other callers. The field is `uniform int` and the file defines no `min` of its own, so the call binds to the standard library's `min(uniform int, uniform int)` (Note JB5). The three ISPC TC objects differ in bytes from the previous ones: the notice's extra line shifts line numbers, and the clamp adds a few instructions (16 to 32 bytes). BC6H output bytes are unchanged: the 18 pins on every path, and the 22 measured images on SSE2, AVX2 and AVX-512 hash equal at all six levels.
- **LO4 #5 `get_span` returns the float span.** `get_span` stored `span_table[mode]` in a `uniform int`, which truncated 921.6, 204.8, 1843.2, 3686.3, 14745.4, 7782.3 and 1945.6; it keeps the float now. A block reaches the gate (`max_span * margin > span`, kernel.ispc `bc6h_test_mode`) only for mode 2, at a widest-channel span of 99 half codes (204.39 UF16 units, inside (204, 204.8]), where the int kept it out of mode 2 and left mode 0 chosen at Fast (the proof test checks Fast; Basic runs the same mode-2 gate in the non-slow branch and goes on to try mode 1, so the int kept a block out of mode 2 there too); the 18 `hdr_real` images changed at 15 of 18 at Veryfast, 17 at Basic and 18 at every other level; that no other gate is reachable is the plan's reading (their truncation windows are under 0.4 units wide against a 2.06-unit step between half codes), not tested block by block; `mode_2_takes_a_span_inside_its_fractional_limit` pins it (never mode 0, a two-region result is mode 2 with R widest). The other bytes that move come through `compute_qbounds(state, span)` (kernel.ispc, in `bc6h_test_mode`): a Veryfast block takes only modes 10 to 13 (the `bc6h_test_mode` calls in `CompressBlockBC6H_core`), whose float spans 7782.3 and 1945.6 shift the endpoint clamp, since no block span lands in their truncation windows. On the 18 `hdr_real` images at AVX2 every mean delta against the previous kernel is +0.000 (half-width 0.000) and no level loses.
- **LO5 Versions.** `engine::manifest::encode_version` returns `ENGINE_VERSION` for `PbrEmissive` and `HdrSky`, the built-in BC6H sub-types (`every_subtype_has_the_one_encode_version` lists them with the 3.0.1 types). Their existing outputs are reported as older encodes; `if-changed` keeps them unless the run's `--ev` covers them (Note JA), and the CHANGELOG entry ends with the JA10 command `sdc convert <project> --on-existing if-changed --ev --property bc6h_uf16`, which re-encodes only the BC6H outputs. A `pbr_emissive` written as BC7 is flagged older too (ruling BC6H-Q1). Shortcut: a `format = "bc6h_uf16"` set by hand on any other sub-type is not marked; if that is reported, add a BC6H-scoped `ispctc_patch` tag compared like `rgbcx_patch` (in `api::currency`); the `ispctc` and `ispc` tags in `encoders-sys/src/lib.rs` stay (spec D5).
- **LO6 Pins.** The 18 hashes of `encoders-sys/tests/bc6h.rs` (`RECORDED_PRE_AVX2`, `RECORDED`, `RECORDED_AVX512`, `RECORDED_PEAK`) and `crates/engine/tests/encode_versions/identity.json` `textures/h/hdr_sky.dds` (`payload_sha256@avx2`) are re-recorded with each byte-changing fix, on an AMD Ryzen 7 9800X3D (the Intel runners skip byte pins, Note JC), because the objects they read changed. The three Veryfast pins did not move at #5, the other 15 did. #1 moved none of the 18 pins (no block of `hdr_image()` changed) and only `hdr_sky`'s identity hash. #3 moved the 12 pins of Basic, Slow, Veryslow and VeryslowPeak and `hdr_sky`'s identity hash; the six Veryfast and Fast pins did not move. #2 moved all 18 pins and `hdr_sky`'s identity hash.
- **LO7 #1 `block_quant` keeps float errors.** `block_quant` stored each texel's float error in `int best_err`, which truncated it and, above 2^31 (a black texel beside one brighter than about 1344.0), gave `INT_MIN` on x86 that beat every honest candidate; it is `float best_err` now (`best_q` stays `int`). `black_beside_bright_keeps_both` (black beside 8192.0: half and half, and one black texel among fifteen bright) failed first on SSE2, AVX2 and AVX-512: at Slow, Veryslow and VeryslowPeak on the one-black block, and at Slow and Veryslow on the half-black block (guarded in peak mode), every bright texel decoded to 0.6064453. So the shipped VeryslowPeak was exposed on unguarded blocks. The code suggests why the other levels did not show it (read, not measured per call): Slow, Veryslow and the peak level (`slow_mode`) test modes 13 and 2 with margin 0, so their candidates may reach `block_quant` with endpoints far from the texels; Veryfast, Fast and Basic reach it only through margin-gated calls, the 2p search and the 1p encode, whose endpoints probably stay within the block's span; and `bc6h_peak_guard` replaces a guarded block's score with its own float error, so at VeryslowPeak the guarded half-black block never scored through the `int`. On the 18 `hdr_real` images at AVX2 against #5's kernel, every mean delta is +0.000 (half-width 0.000, mPSNR and RMSLE worst -0.0001 dB and -0.001 x1000) and no level loses. The PC timings of these runs measured load (they rose at every level, Veryfast's too, whose bytes did not change); on the Linux VM, #5 and #1 are within noise of the kernel before each (0.997 and 0.996 in total; LO10). Bytes are identical on 18/18 images at Veryfast and 1/18 at Fast, Basic and Slow, 0/18 at Veryslow and VeryslowPeak (the float sum also drops the sub-unit truncation at those levels).
- **LO8 #3 The one-region encode submits its best refinement.** `bc6h_enc_1p` refines its first quantisation `refineIterations_1p` times (2 at Basic, Slow, Veryslow and VeryslowPeak, 0 at Veryfast and Fast), each pass refitting from the previous pass's indices as upstream does, and now submits the lowest-error of the first quantisation and every pass; upstream submitted the last pass even when it was worse. It keeps copies of the best `qep` and `qblock`, because `bc6h_code_1p` swaps endpoints in place (`bc7_code_apply_swap_mode456`). Mirroring the two-region refine (refit from the best so far) was not taken: when pass 1 is worse than the first quantisation, pass 2 refits from the first again, repeats pass 1 and submits the first, which can be worse than upstream's pass 2; the best of upstream's sequence is never worse than upstream or the mirror, at the same cost (survey #3). Proof test `refinement_never_ends_worse_than_veryfast` checks the invariant: Veryfast writes the first one-region candidate that Basic and Veryslow also make, so they can end worse than Veryfast only through a worse submitted refinement. Its premises, read from `CompressBlockBC6H_core` and checked empirically below: at Basic, Veryslow and Veryfast the first one-region candidate is the same (the same mode 10 call, margins and `compute_qbounds` span), Veryfast submits it unrefined, `bc6h_peak_guard` returns its input (`peak_mode` is off at all three), and `block_quant` sums the unweighted squared error over three channels. The test bounds each level's own error from its output bytes, in half codes: per channel, with e = decoded - source, between e^2 and (e+1)^2, because the decoder writes `(u * 31) >> 6` (`err_bounds`). It asserts `E_lo(Basic) <= E_hi(Veryfast)` and `E_lo(Veryslow) <= E_hi(Veryfast)` with no tolerance, on eight witnesses from the ignored `search_refine_witnesses`, over blocks from `candidate(i)` (seeded gradients and random ranges in 0x2000..=0x7000 half codes, drawn widest-channel span 600 to 12288, one block in eight with an outlier texel that can widen it to 20480): 25, 95, 292, 364, 395, 432, 643 and 678. On the pre-fix objects the test failed on every path, 14 times on AVX2 and AVX-512 (Basic at all eight, Veryslow at six; 643 and 678 only at Basic) and 13 on SSE2 (643 passes there); for example witness 364 at Basic and Veryslow: E_lo 2025 > Veryfast E_hi 1383. On the fixed objects the search finds no violation in all 1048576 candidates (9.1 s), which also checks the premises empirically. On the 18 `hdr_real` images at AVX2 against #1's kernel, Basic, Slow, Veryslow and VeryslowPeak each gain mPSNR +0.003 dB (half-width 0.002; no image loses) and RMSLE +0.001 x1000 (half-width up to 0.001; VeryslowPeak loses 0.002 on dreifaltigkeitsberg); peak ratio is unchanged except at VeryslowPeak, -0.002 (half-width 0.004), where dreifaltigkeitsberg falls from 1.008 to 0.971. `hdr_grad` gains at those levels, up to +0.048 dB. Bytes are identical on 18/18 images at Veryfast and Fast and 0/18 at the other levels. These PC timings were taken with two other jobs claiming 16 threads and fell at every level, Veryfast's included, so they measure load; on the Linux VM, #3 is within noise of #1 (0.998 in total, 0.994 to 1.006 per level; LO10).
- **LO9 #2 Endpoints take the decoder's nearest code.** `ep_quant_bc6h` rounded by `ep * (2^bits - 1) / 65535`, but `unpack_to_uf16` rebuilds an interior code q at (q + 0.5) * 65536 / 2^bits, so the bias was not the audit's half step up everywhere: about +1/2 step near code 0, none mid-range and -1/2 near the top. Below 15 bits the kernel now clamps `ep` to [0, 65535] and keeps the code that rebuilds nearest, the lower on a tie; 16-bit mode 13 keeps upstream's rule, already nearest. That code comes from one multiply, the interior code ceil(ep * 2^bits / 65536) - 1 clamped to 1..2^bits - 2 plus one compare with an end code beside it, in place of the first version's three-candidate search, which took 1.135 to 1.226 times as long as #3's kernel per level on the Linux VM (1.180 in total; 1.123 to 1.205 in a second run) (ruling BC6H-T7-3); the bytes are the same (every pin, and the hashes of all 22 measured images at six levels on SSE2, AVX2 and AVX-512), because an interior code rebuilds at the centre of its cell, so only the uncentred end codes 0 and 2^bits - 1 need a compare (`the_one_multiply_rule_takes_the_nearest_code_for_every_value` checks the rule for every integer value, the tie at the top end code and values a quarter either side of every tie, at every precision a mode uses: 6 to 12 and 16 bits). The float `clamp(ep[i], 0.0f, 65535.0f)` binds to the standard library's `clamp(float, float, float)` and the int calls to `clamp(int, int, int)`, not the file's `clamp(float, int, int)` (Note JB5); a float32 sweep of `compute_qbounds` widths gave 15, 29, 7 (13 widest), 15, 15 (29 widest), 244 and 122 for modes 0, 1, 2, 5, 6, 11 and 12, as the old rule and inside each delta field. `one_region_endpoints_take_the_nearest_code` (mode 10 at Veryfast, G and B constant over the block, so their endpoints lie close to the channel's mean, not exactly at it; R's wide span forces mode 10 and the 4-unit margin to the edge between two codes keeps the shift from changing the nearest code; 16 mid-range blocks, and 4 blocks at the end codes: G whose nearest code is 0, B whose nearest is 1023) failed first on the pre-fix objects: all 16 mid-range blocks on every path (32 of 32 on SSE2 and AVX2, 48 of 48 with AVX-512), always the shipped rule's codes (G one high, B one low); block 0 on AVX2: `G half 0x094f, B half 0x757f: G codes 77 77, B codes 969 969; nearest G 76 B 970; shipped rule G 77 B 969`. The 4 end-code blocks added later missed on every path too (8 of 8 on SSE2 and AVX2: G code 1 where 0 is nearest, B code 1022 where 1023 is). Two cases are not covered, as the test's doc says: a value exactly midway between two codes (the kernel's strict compare takes the lower, but a tie is a knife edge for the encoder's float endpoint) and the delta modes 11 and 12, where the two endpoints of a channel that is constant over the block can carry codes one apart (the pre-fix objects do the same). On the 18 `hdr_real` images at AVX2 against #3's kernel, mPSNR changes by +0.067 dB (half-width 0.020) at Fast, +0.066 (0.020) at Basic, +0.064 (0.018) at Slow, +0.066 (0.018) at Veryslow and +0.066 (0.019) at VeryslowPeak, each interval clear of 0. Veryfast loses 0.0034 dB (interval [-0.0054, -0.0013]; RMSLE -0.0024 x1000 [-0.0056, +0.0008]; peak ratio +0.0037 [+0.0004, +0.0070]; `hdr_grad` sky_sun -0.10 dB), a loss the owner accepted (ruling BC6H-O1, "Keep it, accept Veryfast"). With the one multiply, #2 takes 1.027 to 1.064 times as long as #3's kernel per level (1.058 in total), inside the 10 % limit (LO10).
- **LO10 Quality.** Each byte-changing fix was measured against the kernel before it, on the 18 `hdr_real` images paired per image at AVX2 (`examples/bc6h_quality.rs`), under the rule (spec D4). A mean delta below 0 whose 95 % t-interval excludes 0 in mPSNR, RMSLE or peak ratio at Veryfast, Fast, Basic, Veryslow or VeryslowPeak dropped the fix. At Veryslow and VeryslowPeak a non-significant drop of 0.01 dB mPSNR or 0.2 % of the base RMSLE mean or more stopped the task for the owner's word (smaller ones were only reported, ruling BC6H-Q3). So did a VeryslowPeak image whose peak ratio fell from 0.8 or more to below, a `hdr_grad` image losing more than 0.05 dB, and a level's encode time rising more than 10 %. Slow is reported only. Base (the post-0003 objects) against the final kernel, mean mPSNR delta with its half-width: Veryfast -0.003 (0.002), Fast +0.067 (0.020), Basic +0.069 (0.018), Slow +0.067 (0.017), Veryslow +0.069 (0.017), VeryslowPeak +0.069 (0.018) dB. At Fast to VeryslowPeak the RMSLE and peak-ratio intervals include 0, except Slow RMSLE (+0.009 x1000, half-width 0.008, better); at Veryfast RMSLE is -0.002 (0.003) x1000 and peak ratio +0.004 (0.003). The one loss is Veryfast's mPSNR, which the owner accepted for fix #2 (ruling BC6H-O1; `hdr_grad` sky_sun -0.10 dB). Peak mode's cost is unchanged (VeryslowPeak against Veryslow: mPSNR -0.060 dB, was -0.061; worst image venice_sunset -0.696, was -0.674), and no VeryslowPeak image is below a peak ratio of 0.8 (lowest 0.934, was 0.923). Every level changed all 22 images (0 of 18 `hdr_real` and 0 of 4 `hdr_grad` bytes identical at each of the six levels). SSE2 and AVX-512 agree with AVX2 in direction at every level, with means within 0.001. Method, tables and the per-level counts are in docs/research/encoder-benchmark.md, "Kernel fixes (patch 0004, 2026-11-22, Note LO)". Time was measured on the Linux VM (an i9-13900K guest with 6 vCPUs, Wine 11.0, the AVX2 path, `--threads 1`, the 22 images summed per run, the median of 3 rounds in alternating order, no competing process in any run), as ratios against the kernel before each fix. #5, #1 and #3 are within noise: 0.997, 0.996 and 0.998 in total, 0.983 to 1.024 per level, with rounds of one exe up to 1.014 apart. #2's first form, a three-candidate search, was 1.180 in total (1.135 to 1.226 per level), over the 10 % limit, so the one-multiply form with the same bytes replaced it (ruling BC6H-T7-3). That form is 1.036, 1.027, 1.044, 1.045, 1.059 and 1.064 against #3 at Veryfast, Fast, Basic, Slow, Veryslow and VeryslowPeak (1.058 in total), and the final kernel against base is 1.024, 1.037, 1.029, 1.039, 1.051 and 1.054 (1.049 in total). Veryfast's run totals are 0.08 to 0.10 s, so its ratios are coarse. The VM's CPU is an Intel, whose BC6H bytes differ from the PC's, so these runs give times only, never bytes or quality (ruling BC6H-T8-1).

## Note LS - Tessalith 3.1.0 release (owner, 2026-11-23)

- **LS1 Release commit (owner, 2026-11-23: "prepare a 3.1 release").** The workspace version is 3.1.0: the dev cycle was numbered 3.0.1 and ships as 3.1.0, a MINOR release because it adds features (Note DM8). `RELEASED_ENGINE_VERSION` is "3.1.0", so `bumped()` is false for every sub-type and `encode_versions` is strict from here on; `identity.json`, `encode_inputs.json` and `profile_hashes.json` are unchanged. The `encode_version` arms that return `ENGINE_VERSION` (Note LA: `normal`, `landscape_normal`, `pbr_normal`, `coat_normal`, `rmaos`, `skin_rfaos`, `complex_material`; LA6 `skin_detail`; LA7 `detail_normal`; Note LO: `pbr_emissive`, `hdr_sky`) read 3.1.0, as intended: those types changed this cycle, so a 3.0.0 record of them is an older encode (Note JA). The next cycle's version bump writes those arms as the literal "3.1.0" before it moves anything, as the 3.0.1 bump did for the 3.0.0 arms. No preset version moves: `fast-preview` changed this cycle (`encoder.bc1_search = "fast"`) and keeps 3.0.0 by that plan's ruling, so this cycle's entry in `docs/presets-changelog.md` is headed `2026-11-23 — sdc 3.1.0 (presets stay 3.0.0)` and the pin `compact_and_fast_preview_resolve_as_designed` still expects "3.0.0". Records written by 3.0.1 dev builds keep the version they wrote: `api::MAX_WAS_SLOWEST_THROUGH` stays [3, 0, 0], so a 3.0.1 record at `max` reads as the new search (the Note LI shortcut in `api.rs`), and a 3.0.1 record of an arm that now reads 3.1.0 is an older encode, which `--ev` re-encodes. CHANGELOG's `## Unreleased` is `## 3.1.0` in release shape (`docs/DOCUMENTATION.md` section 6) and RELEASE-NOTES.md is headed `## 3.1.0`, its bullets the owner's.

## Note LQ - compact texture panel (main team, 2026-11-23)

Plan 4 of review E (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-16-texture-panel-compact.md`). The texture panel shows a short first screen and groups that start closed. Navigation (opening, closing, Go to) is never gated by a pending plan.

- **LQ1 The first screen and its order.** In the centre panel the first screen is, top to bottom: the texture's heading, its job key, **Stop re-encoding**, **Used by {n} meshes** (LM13), the green line of a normal map, the undecided card while a question is open, the **Texture type** row with **Helper** right under it (HI4), **Format**, the size line, the problem rows (LQ7), and then the groups (LQ2). With the undecided card asking the type, the Texture type row is dropped (the card has its own type picker) and the Helper row stays (the card has no Helper tick). Used by sits where LM13 put it, with a group header (LQ3).
- **LQ2 The groups and their order.** `model::panel_groups::PanelGroup::ALL` is the order the single panel, the strip and the 3D window draw: Alpha, Mipmaps, Normal map, Size, Compression, Cubemap, Channels and "Where these settings come from" (`Sources`); `panel_groups::panel_group` maps every field to its group, and one test pins the whole table. Input content (`color.content`, key unchanged) and DDS tag are in Compression, after Format in `FIELDS` order, and the preview's strip leaves both rows out (as it leaves out Levels and DDS header, JI3). **A header is never disabled:** a group whose every shown row is locked starts closed, still opens, and shows each row with its own reason; its summary reads "not used for this texture" ("not used for these textures" in bulk), and its hover gives the shared reason when every row has the same one, else "Each row below says why it is not used.". `edit_off` (a pending plan, FK3) never touches a header, its summary or its "all locked" state: group headers are navigation. A read-only `sdc.toml` greys the bulk rows, never the bulk headers.
- **LQ3 One header style.** Every texture-panel group header is a full row, 24 px high, ending the scroll bar's width short of the right edge (as ↺ does, rule 3.2), so the floating bar never lies over it, with a "▶" turned by openness, "● changed" and the AccessKit name `{title}, {expanded|collapsed}[, changed][, has a problem][: {summary}]`. The single panel, the 3D window, the preview strip and the bulk panel draw it from the one `ui::group` header, and so do Used by, Cubemap and "Where these settings come from". **TPC-R1:** the summary shows only while the group is closed, following JR6 and its digest line: while it is open the rows show the values, so the header has no summary text, no summary hover and no `": {summary}"` in its name ("Mipmaps, expanded, changed"). "● changed" and the problem dot stay on open headers. Two summaries are fixed: "Where these settings come from" reads "How each value was chosen" and Channels "R {r}, G {g}, B {b}, A {a}". On a cubemap the single panel's Size summary is Resize's no-effect reason, "no effect on a cubemap: Face size sizes it", as in bulk (LQ9). An elided summary's hover is the full summary, a line break, then the hover text. The dot shows the group's worst problem level (a blocker is not drawn as a warning). The column collapse arrows are named "Collapse Textures" and "Collapse Sets"; their hover stays "Collapse the {name} column". The 3D settings window has no minimum width of its own.
- **LQ4 "Changed".** A group is "changed" when one of its rows holds a value the user set (`edited`), not when it differs from the base profile. One rule on both panels: "● changed" shows exactly when a row inside the group shows its ↺. On the single panel that includes a locked row (its ↺ clears a value that no longer has an effect); a bulk row counts only the textures it reaches (`BulkField::edited`), so a group no selected texture can use is never "changed" in bulk.
- **LQ5 Open state.** The open state of every group, "Where these settings come from" included, is saved app-wide in `gui.toml` `[texture_groups]` and is closed by default. Used by (LM13) is not one of these groups: its open state is egui temp data, one flag for every texture, closed at start and kept for the session only. A group opens only by the user's click, by a Problem row (3a's `!is_note()`) once per selection after the job resolves, or by a Go to (LQ10). "After the job resolves" is the selected texture's first draw with no open need of any kind (type, alpha, cube layout ...) and no plan pending (`plan_wait`; a read-only `sdc.toml` does not hold it back), so a problem that answering the question raises opens its group. A forced open is never saved, shows without sliding on every header (Cubemap and Sources included), and ends with the selection; the user's click on a header ends the hold for that group. A replan, an undo, a hand edit, a bulk edit or a value set does not open a group. The 3D window, the strip, the centre and the bulk panel share one open state.
- **LQ6 Late problems.** A problem that appears after the selection's latch filled draws the header's dot and ", has a problem" but does not open the group, so no header moves under the pointer. A row's `also` codes map to groups as the row does.
- **LQ7 Problems on the first screen.** At most three Problem rows are drawn, then a **+{n} more** button that opens Problems filtered to the texture's set. Notes (`is_note()`) are never counted there, only "{m} notes in Problems". The rows leave out `Level::Decide` rows and the codes another first-screen widget already shows (SUG-LOW-CONFIDENCE, IN-GREEN-UNSTATED, CHK-GREEN-GUESS). FMT-HDR-SOURCE is not one: its line is drawn in the Compression group, and as an Info code it counts among the notes. No problem is hover-only.
- **LQ8 Motion.** A group opens and closes with egui's built-in collapse. A keyboard toggle and reduced motion (`ui::motion::reduced`) snap, and a forced group never slides: it snaps open, snaps shut on the user's click, and snaps shut when its hold ends with the selection (the header keeps whether it was held in the last frame, so the next texture's closed group does not slide shut).
- **LQ9 Bulk.** The bulk panel draws the same groups (Alpha, Mipmaps, Normal map, Size, Compression) with the same ids; Texture type and Format stay on its first screen. Its summaries are `{Label}: mixed` where the values differ, built once per input change in the cached `BulkView`; a field no selected texture can use (`applies == 0`) is left out of the summary, and a group nothing can use reads "not used for these textures". A no-effect reason (for example Resize on a cubemap) shows on a bulk header only when every applying texture shares it, so the header reads the same whichever texture was clicked last. Cubemap, Channels, "Where these settings come from" and Used by never appear in bulk. **The bulk panel has no forced opens and no Go to** (TPC-T7-2): both are per item, and a problem's Go to selects its item first.
- **LQ10 Go to.** A Problems texture row whose code is in `panel_groups::FIX_FIELDS` (this plan's one table; Note codes are not in it) opens the field's group (a first-screen field opens none), moves **keyboard focus** to the field's control in the centre panel only, and scrolls it to the middle. A row that is disabled (plan pending, FK3), locked, a six-file cube's layout label, or left out by `fields::hidden_reason` takes no focus: the request is taken or expires, and the group still opens. The request is one-shot: `select_item` and `select_set` (through `go_to_target`) drop a waiting one, and a request not taken in 10 passes is dropped by `ui::draw_with_scan`. The other selection paths (multi-select, restore, reconcile) set `selection` directly and rely on the item match in the centre's context and the 10-pass expiry. A Go to to another texture keeps its latch through the next selection check, and its scroll wins over the new selection's top. **Against JO8 (Go to set):** Go to on a texture row moves keyboard focus to the control, because the spec asks for it, where Go to set moves none; it follows JO8's other two rules.
- **LQ11 JI amendment.** Nothing with a reason is hidden: a closed group still shows its rows' reasons when opened, and rows `fields::hidden_reason` leaves out stay as JI left them. JI's "What works well" item 2 still holds.
- **LQ12 E22 (control kinds).** Distinct enabled control kinds in the centre (`support::control_kinds`), 1280x800, every group closed: decided colour 11, tangent normal map 12, packed RMAOS 11, cube 12, undecided type 12, alpha question open on a decided type 13, bulk of 4 8. Before this plan: E22 39 enabled kinds, replay 44. With every group open (1280x2400, the same counts at 4800): 20 to 51. The engine asks the alpha meaning only after a type is chosen (`Need::AlphaMeaning`), so an undecided type and an open alpha question never meet: the alpha case is the question on a decided type (TPC-T9-1).
- **LQ13 Bulk green (owner addition A3; rulings A3-1 to A3-5, TPC-R2, TPC-R3).** Ruling A3-5: the Green result is the bulk panel's own result line, by the rule every bulk write follows; nothing is written to the window status line.
  - The bulk first screen has a **Green** row while a selected texture takes green (`engine::policy::takes_green`: every Normal sub-type except `msn`). It is a wrapped line under the Format rows: one label, "Green: OpenGL", "Green: DirectX" or "Green: mixed" (unset reads DirectX), the two buttons, and "applies to {k} of {n}", with the skip reasons on hover in LM11's form, "locked on {count} of {n}: {reason}" (TPC-R3; "slot locked", "type not decided yet", "not planned yet", "model-space normal (_msn): no green to flip", "not a normal map"). A3-3, A3-4 and TPC-F-3: the row reaches every selected texture that fills no locked slot and has a planned job that takes green and is not waiting for its type (`!locked && job && !waiting && takes_green`). A texture in a locked slot is skipped first, with the lock's reason ("slot locked", or "slot locked in {set}" for a set borrowing it), as every other bulk row skips it (LM11); one still waiting for its type is skipped with "type not decided yet". A normal that already reads DirectX by a written value stays in reach, although the single panel shows no green line for it. The hover shows while `sdc.toml` is read-only.
  - **Treat as OpenGL (flip green)** and **Treat as DirectX** WRITE the convention (`opengl` or `directx`) on those textures only, as one undo step labelled "Green for 1 texture" or "Green for {k} textures" (A3-2, quoted: "the bulk Green row is a 'set' control: both buttons WRITE the convention … The single panel's Clear is unchanged"). A written DirectX overrides an `opengl` from a `[subtypes.normal]` or `[[rules]]` line, which a Clear would not; the bulk **Treat as DirectX** says so on hover, because the single panel's button of the same name clears. The panel's result line (`bulk.report`) says "Green: {OpenGL|DirectX} written to {k} of {n}", or "No selected texture takes green."; a click that changes nothing and skips nothing leaves it as it was, as `apply_bulk_edit` does.
  - **TPC-R2:** one rule for both bulk writes. Like the type card's Apply (`decide_many`, LM8), the Green write refuses while `fix_off()` is set (a pending plan or a read-only `sdc.toml`: nothing written, no report) and re-reads the selection at the click, writing only to the textures still selected. Its write path stays `batch_edit` around `decide`, because Green is not a field.
  - Green is not in `fields::FIELDS` or `bulk::panel_fields()`, so Copy settings never carries it (Z5).
- **LQ14 Controller rulings TPC-R1, TPC-R2 and TPC-R3** (2026-11-22, on the drift report): quoted in LQ3 and LQ13.
- **LQ15 Test data this plan removed (JT5).** The six "◀" `COLLAPSE` entries and the five `GROUP_ROW` entries ("Alpha", "Mipmaps") of `SHARED_NAME_ALLOWED` in `crates/gui/tests/accessible_names.rs` are gone, because the names they allowed are gone. The groups start closed, so the screens audited before this plan reach no grouped row; three audit screens open every group (a single texture, a cube, and a bulk selection of normal maps with its Green row), and the `ui_proof` texture-panel screen opens them too (marker "Alpha threshold"). Their only shared names are the Sets column's, allowed as on the other screens.

## Note LT - BC6H audit follow-up (main team, 2026-11-23)

A second audit of the vendored BC6H kernel and a Codex audit, each verified adversarially by Opus, left open findings; the owner's decisions of 2026-11-23: R3 and the Q4 absolute window are out, Q6 is in (Task 21), astra #3 and #7b are dropped; the order is diagnostics, then byte-neutral work as patch 0006, then byte-changing fixes as patch 0007.

- **LT1 Diagnostics.** `bc6h_quality diag --root <bench> --out <txt> [--target t] [--threads N] [--gate today|d16] [--centre]` encodes the 22 bench images at all six levels and counts, from the source and the decoded halves, what the audit asked about; it changes no kernel byte. Below, on the Ryzen at AVX2 with the shipped objects, `hdr_real` (18 images, 589824 blocks) and `hdr_grad` (4 images, 65536 blocks). **Q5 bias:** d = decoded half - source half has mean -0.41 to -0.51 per channel over all `hdr_real` texels at every level (median 0, or -1 for Veryfast's blue) and -0.13 to -0.18 over the texels with |d| <= 4; `hdr_grad` -0.36 to -0.63 and -0.09 to -0.40. **Q2 collapsed regions** (decoded texels all equal while the source's are not; a region with distinct endpoints but one shared index counts too): `hdr_real` Veryfast 1144 of 589824 regions (0.194 %, all in mode 12), Fast 3919 of 899018 (0.436 %), Basic 2502 of 888550 (0.282 %), Slow 1945 of 924411 (0.210 %), Veryslow 2037 of 939569 and VeryslowPeak 2037 of 939565 (0.217 %), their squared error at most 0.007 % of the level's; mode 2 holds the most from Fast to Veryslow (1966, 1120, 1020, 1084). `hdr_grad`: 401 (0.612 %), 895 (1.034 %), 639 (0.785 %), 316 (0.380 %), 235 and 235 (0.279 %), their squared error 0.030, 0.264, 0.184, 0.215, 0.157 and 0.157 % of the level's. **Q4 gate:** replicating the Fast and Basic two-region gate in f32, D16 changes the pick of 43728 of 589824 `hdr_real` blocks at Fast and the same 43728 at Basic, and Slow's output is in the family D16 newly picks for 29921 of them (`hdr_grad`: 3135 of 65536 and 1418). The replication matches the kernel's modes: 0 mismatches away from a threshold. Two classes of difference are counted outside the stop rule (the kernel's fast-math `/ 31 * 64` rounds differently from the exact f32 division): 7 outputs (Fast and Basic each) differ from the pick with a span within 1e-3 of a gate threshold, and 9003 of 309194 two-region outputs at Fast (6590 of 298726 at Basic; `hdr_grad` 1327 of 21007 and 788 of 15909) are the neighbour mode of the pick's family, where the two widest channel spans lie within 1e-3 of each other and `max_span_idx` falls on the other channel. **Q6 guarded blocks:** 256 of 589824 `hdr_real` blocks are guarded (none in `hdr_grad`), 111 of them in satara_night and 42 in moonless_golf; Task 21's floor un-guards none of them; Task 5's lift changes no channel in 211 of them. **astra #5:** 5 guarded blocks (all moonless_golf) hold a texel with m below 0x3400 and a channel below half(m)/4096, 2 of them with m below 0x3000. **astra #2 (VeryslowPeak):** 2 guarded blocks (both satara_night) decode the peak texel below 0.9 of its source luminance, one at an anchor texel and one not, and no other index reaches 0.9 for either. **astra #6 (a source-side proxy; Task 22's all-variants build decides):** the widest channel fits the doubled window and the second exceeds the single one in `hdr_real` blocks 44256 (family 2, kernel modes 2-4; 23978 within 10 % of each other) and 45814 (family 6, modes 6-8; 26827), at Slow, Veryslow and VeryslowPeak alike; those levels write family 2 in 127746, 140073 and 140073 blocks and family 6 in 68018, 68781 and 68781. **RED tests committed ahead of their fixes**, both `#[ignore]`, so an owner-data `-- --ignored` run reports them failing until their fix lands: `pca_survives_a_zero_covariance_row_sum` (0(a)) passes at every level on SSE2 and AVX2 on the Ryzen and on the Intel VM (i9-13900K guest under Wine, SSE2 and AVX2), and fails on the Ryzen's AVX-512 path only: Veryfast decodes all 16 texels to (0, 0, 0) in both arrangements, and Fast and Basic decode the two-halves block to (3720, 3720, 7688) and (3952, 3952, 7920) in every texel; Slow and above pass. `constant_blocks_decode_to_their_half_code` (0(b), Q5) fails alike at all six levels on SSE2, AVX2 and AVX-512: 2194 of 4536 constant blocks (h from 0x0001 in steps of 7, and 0x7BFF) do not decode to h, the first being h = 0x0001, which decodes to 0.
- **LT2 Strip offsets.** The kernel forms `(yy * 4 + y) * stride` and `yy * width * data_size` in 32-bit `int`, so a strip over 2 GiB wrapped while `bc6h_compress_rows_on` checked `w * 8`, `rows` and `width` only separately (Codex audit astra #8). It now asserts `rows * width * 8 <= i32::MAX` after the slice-length checks ("strip exceeds the kernel's 32-bit offsets: pass fewer rows"); the input offset dominates the output offset, so one product covers both. The engine passes 4-row strips and never reaches the bound; no kernel or output byte changes. The helper `strip_offsets_fit_i32` has unit tests (4 cases, 3 RED before the fix); the assert's placement at the call site is checked by reading only, because the slice-length check needs a 2 GiB allocation first.
- **LT3 R2 no uninitialised slots (patch 0006).** `ep_quant_bc6h` and `ep_dequant_bc6h` loop over the three channels of each endpoint (`i * 4 + p`) and no longer touch slots 3 and 7 (11 and 15 with two pairs); `block_pca_axis` and `get_pca_bound` scale only the covariance entries `covar_from_stats` wrote, and `block_pca_axis` adds its epsilon to `covar[9]` only for four channels (it was added to an unset entry for three). The four-channel path is as before; the helpers are shared, so BC7's three-channel calls in a build without `SDC_BC6H_ONLY` reach them too; by reading the code that is value-neutral, but that build is neither compiled nor shipped, so it is not tested. BC6H bytes do not change: the `cargo test -p encoders-sys -p sdc` run passes with no pin edited, on SSE2 and AVX2 and with `SDC_TEST_AVX512=1`; the 48 strip hashes (SSE2 and AVX2) and the 72 with AVX-512 equal the baseline; `bc6h_quality run` on SSE2, AVX2 and AVX-512 compared with the base CSVs says 18/18 and 4/4 bytes identical at all six levels; `identity.json` is unchanged. No mutation: R2 changes no value, so no test could fail on removing it. VM time (AVX2 under Wine, one thread, 22 images, median of 5 alternating rounds, none discarded; the A/A run of Task 1 gave ratios 0.989 at Veryfast and 0.999 to 1.001 elsewhere). Median seconds, base / R2: Veryfast 0.09 / 0.09, Fast 0.64 / 0.59, Basic 1.61 / 1.49, Slow 6.83 / 6.56, Veryslow 14.71 / 14.11, VeryslowPeak 14.83 / 14.21, total 38.71 / 37.04. Spread (max/min over the 5 rounds), base / R2: Veryfast 1.023 / 1.037, Fast 1.024 / 1.012, Basic 1.014 / 1.008, Slow 1.006 / 1.005, Veryslow 1.001 / 1.009, VeryslowPeak 1.003 / 1.004, total 1.002 / 1.006. R2 against base: 0.988 at Veryfast (0.09 s, one-hundredth resolution, within noise), 0.917 Fast, 0.926 Basic, 0.960 Slow, 0.959 Veryslow, 0.959 VeryslowPeak, 0.957 in total, so R2 is faster, beyond the noise at every level but Veryfast; kept.
- **LT4 Zero-div guard (patch 0006).** `block_quant` forms `div` per texel; it now takes `div = max(div, 1.0f)` before `proj /= div`. Every BC6H endpoint is an integer UF16 value, so `div` is 0 only for a region whose endpoints are equal in every channel, where 0/0 was NaN, `(int)` made it INT_MIN and the clamp made it index 1; with `div >= 1` the numerator is still exactly 0, so the index is the same without the NaN (the helper is shared, so BC7 in a build without `SDC_BC6H_ONLY` reaches it too; by reading the code its integer endpoints make that value-neutral, but that build is neither compiled nor shipped, so it is not tested). BC6H bytes do not change: same checks as LT3 against the R2 files, all equal (tests pass with no pin edited on SSE2, AVX2 and AVX-512, 48 and 72 strip hashes equal, 18/18 and 4/4 identical at all six levels on all three paths, `identity.json` unchanged). No mutation is possible: removing the guard is the old kernel. VM time, same run and method as LT3. Median seconds, R2 / guard: Veryfast 0.09 / 0.08, Fast 0.59 / 0.60, Basic 1.49 / 1.50, Slow 6.56 / 6.60, Veryslow 14.11 / 14.21, VeryslowPeak 14.21 / 14.29, total 37.04 / 37.29. Spread, R2 / guard: Veryfast 1.037 / 1.048, Fast 1.012 / 1.019, Basic 1.008 / 1.010, Slow 1.005 / 1.004, Veryslow 1.009 / 1.005, VeryslowPeak 1.004 / 1.004, total 1.006 / 1.002. The guard against R2 is 0.988 at Veryfast, 1.017 Fast, 1.004 Basic, 1.007 Slow, 1.007 Veryslow, 1.005 VeryslowPeak, 1.007 in total: Fast's 1.7 % lies inside the exes' own spread (1.012 and 1.019), Veryfast's 0.988 is one-hundredth-resolution noise (0.09 and 0.08 s), and Basic to VeryslowPeak are 0.4 to 0.7 % above R2: a small measured cost (Slow 1.007 and VeryslowPeak 1.005 exceed both exes' spreads, so it is not noise), accepted by ruling BC6H-C6.
